# Collected Letters - To My Disciples (Volume 1, edition 2)

## Collected Letters To My Disciples

**(Volume 1, edition 2)**

**Niranjana Swami**

## Introduction

I have collected some material for the first section of this book, from letters written as far back as 1986. Prior to 1986, I did not use a computer, and therefore all the letters I responded to then, were either handwritten or typewritten. Unfortunately, I did not make any copies of the letters I had written during that time, and therefore there are no letters prior to 1986 in this book.

During the years 1983 until early 1986, just prior to my acceptance of *sannyasa,* I spent most of my time traveling in New England. For those unfamiliar with the geography of America, New England is in the north-eastern corner of the United States, and consists of six states: Connecticut, Rhode Island, Massachusetts, Vermont, New Hampshire, and Maine. Combined, these six states had a population of approximately twelve million people. At that time, we had only one temple in Boston, and one small preaching center in Providence, Rhode Island, about 100 miles from Boston. There were no temples in the other four states, and prior to 1983, there were no devotees traveling in those States, other than devotees who were primarily concentrating on book distribution.

I was the Temple President in Boston from 1979 until 1983. During my tenure as Temple President, I would from time to time receive letters or phone calls from people in these six states, who had either met devotees on book distribution, met devotees in other cities, or had somehow or other, acquired one or more of Srila Prabhupada’s books. There were no developed *nama-hatta* programs during that time. Neither was there itinerant preachers traveling in these undeveloped areas. Gradually these phone calls and letters started to weigh more heavily upon me. As the Temple President, I knew that somebody had to take responsibility for these people who had somehow come in contact with Srila Prabhupada’s books. Having either met a devotee, or having read one or more of Srila Prabhupada’s books, many of these people had questions about Krishna consciousness but, for whatever reason, were unable to make the long drive to visit the Boston temple.

My interest in reaching out to these people started intensifying in 1982, a little over two years after accepting responsibilities as Temple President. I began corresponding with anyone who contacted the temple, and gradually, although not very often, I started visiting people in their homes with prashadaam and a selection of Srila Prabhupada’s books.

In 1983, with the permission and encouragement of my GBC, H. H. Satsvarupa das Goswami, I left the position of Temple President in Boston. I did this in order to devote all of my time to meeting these people, to help them to progress in Krishna consciousness, and hopefully, to engage them in Srila Prabhupada’s mission.

When I first started, it was a completely new experience for me. As Temple President, I did not have to look for engagement. There was always something happening every day that required my attention—the book distributors and Sankirtana leaders would come to meet with me, the Harinama was going out regularly and required my participation, the head pujari would come with questions or requests for assistance, the temple building always needed maintenance work, the professors from local colleges were calling for guest speakers, public relations communications had to be written, legal problems needed to be dealt with, etc., etc. Each morning, right after the morning program, I would coordinate my responsibilities for the day, and then I would sit in my office, as one devotee after another would come in to discuss his/her personal concerns.

However, when I was relieved from all these managerial responsibilities in order to take up my new service, suddenly, I found I had to motivate myself, much more than previously, to be fully engaged from one day to the next. The devotees in the Temple were no longer coming to me for advice or assistance. They had their new Temple President waiting for them in my former office. He had been quickly accepted by them. I was neither getting phone calls from devotees living away from the temple, asking me either for help, for some engagement, or for me to visit them. It didn’t take long for me to realize that if I didn’t go out and preach, nobody was going to either push me out or pull me out the door.

Sure, those potential devotees I either had spoken with or visited as Temple President were out there. However, most of them were working to maintain themselves, or for most, their families as well. If they were not working, they were still involved with so many other affairs. Krishna consciousness was not, at least not yet, their primary engagement in life. Being new to the role as an itinerant preacher, I naively thought that everyone who was corresponding with me was practically ready to take up full time devotional service. It didn’t take long before I learned something different. They had “some” interest, but for most, chanting 16 rounds a day, following the four regulative principles, worshiping deities at home, organizing preaching programs, or going out to preach themselves, was still far from their thoughts. Still, I felt a strong desire to connect them to preaching, knowing that by so doing, they would get Srila Prabhupada’s mercy. But how to reach out and engage them? I had no money, no other means of financial support and at first, no assistance.

Somehow, by Krishna’s grace and the kindness of the new Temple President, I was able to arrange for the use of one vehicle from the Temple. So I just packed my bags, filled the car with as many books as I could fit into it, some devotional paraphernalia, a map, and a list of contacts which I had accumulated as Temple President, and decided that I would go out and preach, although I didn’t quite know how I was going to do it.

I soon found that practically nobody was home during the day. And, in the evenings, during the week, not everyone was available. Moreover, from my list of contacts, I found that everyone lived far from each other. If I visited one home, it was not uncommon to find that the next contact was two hours away. It took quite some time, but I gradually adjusted myself to a schedule, which would provide me the best opportunities for meeting people in the evenings. I scheduled home visitations and programs in advance, and during the day, I would simply use my time for studying, preparing *prashadaam* for programs or for home visitations, distributing books and invitations to programs, and writing letters.

Around that time, most of my letters were typed on a small portable typewriter, which was donated to me by one godbrother, who was living in the western part of Massachusetts, doing a little preaching of his own. During one of my visits to his home, he asked me if I had any need for a portable, electric typewriter. I enthusiastically accepted his kind offering, and I used that typewriter for about 1—2 years until I was able to acquire something a little smaller and even more portable. I would often type letters while visiting people’s homes, or when I stopped in to visit the Boston temple for a few days. Somewhere around 1986, I began using a computer. However, even then, the computers were not portable, and this factor also accounts for the reason why I do not have so many letters to publish from that time. Some letters written during my visits to Boston were done on computer, whereas all those written while I was traveling were still typewritten or handwritten.

Sometime during the year 1984, I began producing a small monthly newsletter entitled “On the Way to Krishna”. I realized that if I was going to maintain a connection with all of these people, and if I wanted to generate the interest of more people, I needed to write more than just personal letters. Therefore, I started writing newsletters as well. The newsletter was produced as quickly as possible during my brief stopovers in the Boston temple. I had to type everything myself. After typing, I had to manually cut and paste the text, the headings and subheadings, different borders for design, and the captions for a few photos, which were also taken by me. With no computer for doing all the work, I remember that it took me a lot more time than I wanted to put into it, so I usually kept the newsletter small, anywhere from two to four pages folded in half. It contained mostly New England news, an article by Srila Prabhupada, and a calendar of events and home programs. I have included here in this book, a couple of small articles, which I found from that time. I still have a few of those early newsletters in my office in Boston.

Gradually other devotees became inspired to assist with the production of “On the Way to Krishna”, and the newsletter took on a more stylish appearance. That newsletter is still being produced today in Boston by Premananda dasa, the congregational director, but on a much grander scale than what I was hastily putting together in the 80’s.

I could write a lot about my experiences during those three years, from the middle of 1983 until 1986 (the year I accepted the renounced order), but this book is not intended to be an autobiography. I am simply trying to set the scene, so to speak, for some of the letters, which I have found and have selected for publication in the first section of this book.

When I accepted the *sannyasa* order in March, 1986, I was still preaching in the New England region. However, in 1985, I had also been asked to take responsibility as the Regional Secretary of New England. I was no longer, so to speak, simply on my own, traveling and preaching. I was also assisting and working closely with the devotees who were maintaining the Boston temple, the Providence temple, the newly re-established Hartford, Connecticut temple, and especially the book distributors, since they, more than anyone else in the region, had the potential to help me expand the preaching in the region. The book distributors were always meeting new people, so I developed a method for gathering names of interested people to whom we could mail “On the Way to Krishna”. The distributors were trained to get the names and addresses of people who showed interest on *Sankirtana.* In time, this collaboration with the distributors became quite helpful to me, since I was thus able to increase the attendance at our programs by sending invitations to all these new names.

I continued to travel and preach and coordinated the preaching in New England as Regional Secretary until 1987, at which time, I was requested by the GBC body to accept the responsibility as GBC for New England as well as another region in North America.

While looking for all the letters I had written during this time, I unfortunately was not able to find many for the reasons stated above. But from those which I did find, some, which I have selected for publication, reflect this mood that I had during that time— to expand what we sometimes called “Contact *Sankirtana*”, and sometimes called *“nama-hatta”* both in Boston and in the New England region. I was still writing letters to people who were contacting the Temple, I was writing letters to people who were met on *Sankirtana*, I was writing letters to devotees who were assisting me in some small but significant way, and I was writing letters to devotees who were engaged in the temples.

Most of the letters from 1987—1989 were personal letters to devotees. As was done in previous compilations of my letters, I have taken out the names and other potentially recognizable information from these letters in order to maintain anonymity. My purpose for doing this is, hopefully, obvious. I do not wish to embarrass anyone by publishing something which might be personal to them—especially without even asking them. In some few rare cases, I have left in names, considering that by so doing, there would be no implications.

After reading through these earlier letters from 1986-1989, I realized that they were written with a certain simplicity. Although very carefully worded, they were not replete with deep realizations.

It’s how I preached at that particular time. If I were given the same situations now in which I was preaching back then, my approach would likely be different. It’s natural that my realizations would be different after an additional 15 years of preaching and close association with senior godbrothers. But I’ll let you, my readers, judge whether my preaching contained in these earlier letters has any value to you now or not. If anything, they will at least give you a small glimpse into my life of service to Srila Prabhupada in the late 80’s.

It is also my sincere hope that these letters might kindle, or possibly “rekindle”, a desire within my readers to get more involved in preaching. Although, as I said, there was a certain simplicity in the way I was preaching back then, looking back to those years, I definitely remember that it was a time when my desire to give Krishna consciousness to others, as Srila Prabhupada had introduced Krishna consciousness to us, began intensifying in my heart. I pray that that desire will come through in these letters, and that my readers will be able to derive some benefit by coming in contact with it.

The other section of this book contains excerpts from letters written during the years 1996—2001. As an additional benefit to my readers, whenever possible, I have tried to include either quotes or sections of the original letters which were written to me. After some thoughtful consideration, I realized that by having the original questions available, it might possibly reduce any chances that my answers will be misinterpreted. Unfortunately, I do not have all of the original letters which correspond to the letters I’ve chosen to publish in the second part of this book. But those which I did have, I’ve taken the sections which seemed, to any degree, relevant, and have included them.

Lastly, I would personally like to thank all the devotees who have been kindly assisting me in producing these books of lectures, letters and instructions in the Russian language. In the course of the past several years, many devotees have been involved in transcribing, translating editing, proofreading, drawing, layout, production, research, and distribution. I do not want to miss anyone, and therefore it’s hard for me to even begin listing everyone who has helped in this endeavor. But still, I am so grateful that I must try.

First, I would like to thank Radha Kunda devi dasi, for her steady and unremitting efforts to oversee the production of Gauranga Press publications. I would also like to thank Karuna Nidhi dasa, for the ongoing support, he has given his wife to continue with this service and for his help with layout and design. My gratitude also goes out to Giriyadava dasa and Ananda Prema devi dasi for funding and proofreading.

Next, I would like to thank everyone, both past and present, who have made Gauranga Press publications possible. Translations are being done by Yugala Piriti devi dasi and Bhaktin Lena, and previously, by Arundhati devi dasi. Transcribing and editing are being done by Jarati devi dasi, Naradi Gopi devi dasi, Rasesvari devi dasi, Kalpataru devi dasi, Madri devi dasi, Sri Radha devi dasi, and Bhaktin Larisa. Previously, Gita Priya devi dasi had worked tirelessly, transcribing numerous lectures until her temple responsibilities no longer allowed her to continue. I am very grateful for all the work she has done. Had she not done so much transcribing when it was needed, I question if some of the earlier publications would have come out when they did. Also, Anandini devi dasi, Parama Karuna devi dasi, Kalavati devi dasi, Yudhishthira dasa, Bhakta Shamil, Kalpataru devi dasi and Bhakta Sergey have helped with transcriptions for previous publications.

I would also like to thank Kunti devi dasi, who has enthusiastically been doing research for Gauranga Press publications, ever since I asked her to take up this service two years ago. I would like to thank Dina Bandhu dasa and Vajraghya devi dasi, for their contributions of artwork. I would like to thank Yoginath dasa for his layout work. I would also like to thank Indriyesha dasa, for faithfully maintaining the tape ministry, which has provided the raw material for many past and hopefully future publications. I would especially like to thank Yuga-avatara dasa, who is always eager to facilitate the production and distribution of Gauranga Press publications.

I would like take this opportunity to offer special appreciation to Atmanandi dasa and Indrani devi dasi for taking up the work, which previously Radha Seva devi dasi, to whom I am also very grateful, was doing on “Mukam Karoti Vacalam”, until her work no longer allowed her to continue. I am grateful that Indrani devi dasi sends her sincere reminders that she’s waiting for material for this journal.

I beg forgiveness of those whose names I may have failed to mention, but who have also assisted in some way. There are probably many more that I have missed. May Lord Gauranga bless all of you, both those who I’ve mentioned and those who I’ve unintentionally overlooked, with His causeless mercy, for only by His mercy can we hope to obtain, in this life, the beautiful vision of Radha and Syama in Goloka Vrindavana.

Niranjana Swami

April 13, 2001

## Letters—1986

## Letter #1 (NS—&gt;Elise) August 8, 1986

Hare Krishna. I am writing in regards to your recent inquiry concerning ISKCON devotees presently residing in Maine. Unfortunately there is no temple there at this time, but there seems to be more and more potential for a regular gathering there similar to the ones that we used to have up until last year. Just recently I also received a letter from someone in State College, PA, (Rich Lanning) who is also moving to Portland this fall. He is also concerned about devotee association. And there are other people throughout Maine who have varying interests in Krishna consciousness to some degree or another. The only reason why I stopped going to Maine was because there was one woman who used to help me coordinate programs, who is now a devotee living at the Gita-Nagari farm in Port Royal, PA. Her assistance was essential because I was not able to make all the necessary preliminary arrangements for setting up gatherings. I have to spend much of my time in Massachusetts. But once she left it became more and more difficult for us to get together due to the lack of commitments of the others who attended. So, maybe between yourself and Rich, we can at least get a few people together once a month to chant Hare Krishna, discuss Krishna conscious philosophy, and honor a delicious feast offered to Lord Krishna.

Why don’t you write to me in Boston, and let me know once you are situated there, and maybe we can begin to make some kind of arrangement to see that this happens. Probably by that time, I will also have an address for Rich, which I can send to you.

Besides that there is one other couple that I can recommend that you get in touch with. They live in Calais, Maine, about two hours north of Bar Harbor. I am sure that they would be more than happy to hear from you, but before I send you their address, I should probably first get in touch with them myself to make sure that they will be available. I have not communicated with them since the beginning of the year. They are a very nice couple, with 5 children, who all chant Hare Krishna and lived in a *gurukula,* until their father had to return to his employment in Maine. Once I hear from them, I will send their address to you.

I hope that this information is of some help to you, and that you keep in touch with us here in Boston.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #2 (NS—&gt;Lucille) August 8, 1986

Hare Krishna. I hope that everything is all right with you. It’s been over a couple of months since I have heard anything from you and I recalled that you mentioned in your last correspondence that you were experiencing turbulent times. It’s times like that, that I can honestly say, bring the Krishna conscious philosophy to life. I am firmly convinced that turbulence and constant change exists in everyone’s life within in this world. But the one Personality, whom we can find complete shelter in is Krishna. He gives His full assurance that He will protect His devotees under all circumstances. Such protection and our own conviction of that protection, enables one to be peaceful in all circumstances. By regularly chanting Krishna’s names, we can gradually develop that complete conviction.

My purpose for writing is really for no other reason than to just make some contact with you and let you know that we are still always available for bringing Krishna consciousness to you and your friends. Sometimes time has a way of taking away a good thing, but when it’s for Krishna, we want to try to prevent that from happening.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #3 (NS—&gt;) September 8, 1986

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am writing in response to your letter as well as a follow-up to our brief conversation last night.

It is good that you are wanting increased association with the devotees. By such hankering, you will develop a greater understanding of how to take advantage of that association when you have it. And through personal association and guidance, you will also get the necessary strength to sustain yourself in Krishna consciousness, when you are not in the immediate association of devotees. You have asked to develop that kind of personal relationship with me, and I will certainly do all that I can to provide that type of association to you. I like that you always come with many questions that are relevant to developing your Krishna consciousness. To make relevant inquiries is very appropriate. And then as you apply yourself more and more to fulfilling the instructions that I give you, you will experience increased reciprocation from Krishna for your increased endeavor to understand Him. “As you surrender unto Me, I will reward you accordingly”, Lord Krishna states in the Bhagavad- gita.

To share what Prabhupada has given is my prime duty in life. To not do so would be totally *kripana,* or miserly. But you must understand that my association can in no way replace the continued association that you must also take from Srila Prabhupada in his books. Just by reading his words, you will get strength to remain steady in Krishna consciousness, and keep to your vows.

But what really makes his words come to life, is when you are also rendering nice service. So some practical service is of the utmost importance. Without practically applying your mind and senses in devotional service, then it is very difficult to maintain Krishna consciousness while you are so-called “living outside in *maya”. It* does not have to be that you are “in *maya”* by living outside the temple. It is all dependent upon your degree of determination to not be in *maya.* Everything becomes spiritualized when you put Krishna in the center. Just as Prabhupada says that when you have so many zeros, they all add up to zero, but when you put a one in front of them, they become a significant number. So, similarly your domestic life must also become spiritualized by making Krishna your topmost priority. I know that your wife offers a lot of resistance, but despite her attitude, you should try to work towards developing some regular monthly gathering, there in southern New Hampshire. To create this facility for preaching Krishna consciousness would be very pleasing to me. We have discussed this many times, and I know that you are trying to do it, but if you make this service your meditation, Krishna will surely help you in your efforts, and advancement will come. That is the real goal of everything that we do; to increase our attachment to Krishna.

Regarding your 3 questions:

1\) The substances used were milk, yogurt, ghee, sugar water, honey and various fruit juices. The first 5 substances are called *pancamrta.* Because cows are very dear to Lord Krishna, the major portion of those things are used to as an offering to Him. The fruit juices are used to bathe Him only for variety. The devotees take great labor to blend numerous varieties of fruits for

His pleasure, and they are just added to increase the opulence of the *abhisheka.*

2\) It is understood that Krishna’s color is like a monsoon cloud. Krishna’s Deity can be made from many substances, (i. e. wood, stone, metal, sand, etc) There is actually no substance available that can compare to the actual color of Krishna’s complexion. So, therefore the color is not as important as the actual carving. Therefore, sometimes Krishna is black, sometimes white, and sometimes yellow, as would be found in metal.

3\) Jesus Christ was *guru.* He was instructing his followers to be chaste and thus establish that their only relationship to Krishna was through him. But it is not that that relationship to Krishna through him is the only relationship for all of eternity by which one can approach God. Whenever there is discrepancy in the execution of religious principles, Krishna either descends Himself or sends his pure representative to set things straight. Just following scripture (i. e. the Bible) is not sufficient. There must be a living representative to teach the principles within the scripture by his own example. Otherwise it is so much room for interpretation according to the fulfillment of one’s own desires. You can try to look for such an ideal follower of the Bible, but why do so when the Vedic scriptures are so accessible my the mercy of Srila Prabhupada, and his representatives are so accessible to connect others to his teachings and instructions?

So, I hope that you find this satisfactory. I am awaiting your phone call on Thursday evening, and I will then confirm whether or not I will be able to accompany you to New York this weekend.

Hoping this meets you in good health and blissfully absorbed in Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #4 (NS—&gt;Len) September 10, 1986

Hare Krishna. I have received your letter dated 7/4/86 and am just now getting the opportunity to return it. I have been traveling extensively over the past couple of months, having been to London, Ireland, Montreal, and the temples in the Mid-Atlantic states at least 2 or 3 times each. That coupled with the fact that I have the unfortunate experience that every time I answer a letter to you, it always comes back to me in the mail, with a notice that you are no longer there. I have basically concluded that my communication to you just isn’t meant to be. But after due consideration, I thought that I would give it one more try—especially when I considered that you had inquired about the whereabouts of... Swami and... dasa, who will both be coming here to Boston next week for the *Ratha-yatra* festival on September 19-20. I thought that maybe this could give you a double impetus to get away for a couple of days to get some of their association. If you think that you can make it, just please let me know in advance, because the temple is going to be quite packed up during those days. I can’t guarantee any lavish facilities if you want to stay over, but I should be able to at least guarantee a bunk for you to sleep on.

As far any change in our relationship because of my having taken the renounced order of life goes, there is absolutely none that I can think of. Spiritual relationships are based on mutual service to Krishna. Whether I am a *sannyasi* or a *brahmacari* does not make any difference except that people tend to take the advice of a *sannyasi* more seriously because he is supposed to be more learned in the scriptures, and renounced from mundane pursuits. As far as whether or not I fit that description, I guess that’s for others to determine by their dealings with me, but as far as I am concerned, I am definitely striving to “fill the role”. I can say though, that by my own relationship with Swami, I can easily perceive that he definitely has developed genuine saintly characteristics. It would be well worth your while to have some association with him.

So please let me hear from you again sometime, and let me know if you can ever make it this way. I still would like to get the opportunity to meet you sometime.

Hoping this meets you well.

## Letter #5 (NS—&gt;Elise) October 9, 1986

Hare Krishna. I received your letter from Maine a couple of weeks ago, but did not respond immediately because I anticipated that you probably had not yet received my letter which was sent to your Florida address. I hope that that letter was forwarded to you and that you received the information about in Calais, Maine.

If not, let me know, and I’ll fill you in about her. As far as Rich Lanning is concerned, he is still settling in, and recently wrote to me that it will be a little while longer before he can become involved with any Krishna conscious activities in Maine. So, therefore, I’ll hold off sending you any information about him until I get the opportunity to meet with him in the near future.

I have enclosed a copy of the story behind *ekadasi* taken from the Padma Purana. It gives quite a detailed explanation, so I won’t get into any further details myself.

Regarding your question about the end of the yogurt fast, that ended a few weeks ago and we are now observing a milk fast until next week, which will be followed by one month of fasting from *urad dahl.* These fasts are observed during four months of every year during the period called *caturmasya.* Originally, this *caturmasya* was observed in India in the Vedic culture, as a kind of austerity to help free one from bodily demands. The practitioners would stay in one place for four months, observing special types of fasts. Generally in ISKCON, rather than undergoing the full austerities prescribed for caturmasya, we only observe a portion of the austerities by restricting our diet from spinach, the first month, yogurt, the second month, milk, the third month, and urad dahl the last month. These different types of foods correspond to the most desired types of food available according to the season. Although ISKCON devotees do observe caturmasya to a limited degree, it is not very necessary for someone who is living outside the temple to observe. More important, is that you offer whatever food you eat, first to Krishna, and only offer vegetables, grains, fruits, and milk products that are free from any types of animal products, such as rennet or enzymes which come from the intestines of cows. And of course, you should try to chant and read Srila Prabhupada’s books regularly. And whenever possible, associate with devotees. If you follow these basic principles of Krishna consciousness, you will be protected from Kali’s influence, wherever you are.

Regarding the mantras required for offering food, I have compiled a booklet, which I am sure you will find very helpful. It describes all these things in detail, specifically those things related to practicing Krishna consciousness at home. Unfortunately, I am presently traveling and I'm writing you from Philadelphia. I don’t have a copy of the booklet with me, but as soon as I return to Boston, which will be some time next week, I will send you a copy.

You should not feel awkward that you did not immediately surrender to in New Ramana Reti. Sometimes it is better that someone does not prematurely jump into Krishna consciousness, without having the necessary philosophical foundation. You have decided to continue with your education, maybe for the time being, but don’t neglect furthering your understanding of Krishna consciousness. Krishna consciousness should never be neglected for without Krishna consciousness there is no meaning to anything. Prabhupada would often give the example that if you have so many zeros, added up they equal zero. But when you put a one in front of all of your zeros, you immediately have a very significant number. So, similarly, you must add Krishna to your present way of life, but to do so does not require that you immediately move into a temple, but you should at least perform those activities which I have previously outlined for you.

I hope that this information is of some benefit to you, and that you will contact me if you should require any further assistance.

## Letter 6 (NS—&gt;Len) October 11, 1986

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have received your letter dated 9/20/86 and have noted the contents. I'm happy to hear that you will definitely receive everything that I send to you. I was beginning to feel a little doubtful. But, it is even more encouraging for me to hear your enthusiasm for receiving these things. So I will try my best to continue to provide whatever association that I can offer.

The following are the answers to your four questions:

1\) The best advice I can offer you for helping your acquaintances at the nursing home, is to see that each and every one of them get *prashadaam.* This is certainly one of the most powerful ways to introduce Krishna to people in a way that they can “digest”. I guess that I can also answer your second question along with this one by assuring you that you can be sent *prashadaam* sweets on some kind of regular basis, but it would be nice if you help cover the cost by sending some donation.

2\) As a matter of fact, I just recently received a letter from someone who is now living in Calais, Vermont (wherever that is). In my response to him, I suggested that if he couldn’t make it to Boston, that it could be possible for me to go up there in the near future. But there’s nothing definite yet. Also, there’s a devotee named Ijya dasa, who sometimes goes to visit his family in Glover, Vermont. He and his wife and child just returned from a visit there, so they probably won’t be going again until for at least a few months, but the next time they go, I'm sure that he would be happy to stop by and visit you.

As far as starting a farm community there goes. We don’t have any immediate plans to do so, although we do have some plans in the making to start a small rural community somewhere in New England by the middle of next year. There are several factors that have to be considered to determine it’s final location. Once plans are a little more solidified, I can fill you in, in case that interests you. There is also a devotee named dasa who hopes to have a small center near the Amherst area sometime next year. That’s not southern Vermont, but it’s closer than Boston.

3\) At present we are a bit cramped in the Boston temple. That’s one reason why we are looking into establishing a rural community so that the married couples can raise their children outside of the city. There are about 30 devotees living in the temple. We used to own the building next door but we had to sell that about 6 years ago for legal reasons. Now we have only the one building on 72 Commonwealth Ave., which is makes it hard to expand and, unfortunately, hard to accommodate an artist who needs a place to keep his oils and easel.

The women live on the fourth floor. On the third floor is my office, a nice homey guest room for any overnight guests. Due to lack of space this guest room is usually shared with another devotee. And two main sleeping quarters. On the second floor is the temple president’s office, a small store with devotional paraphernalia, a men’s and women’s *prashadaam* room, and a guest lounge for reading, watching videos, etc. The first floor is the temple room. And in the basement is a room for the married men, the kitchen and some storage. That’s about it for the external description. Internally, the devotees are still doing the same activities that you probably observed 8 years ago during your previous visit; absorbing their consciousness in developing their attachment to the lotus feet of Lord Sri Krishna. Such an atmosphere is none other than *Vaikuntha,* or the place where there is no anxiety. Of course, we do not make any claims to be completely free from all anxiety, but to the degree that we are actually developing Krishna consciousness, there is no anxiety.

So there are your answers. Since I'm presently writing to you from Philadelphia, I can’t send any sweets to you. But I’ll be back in Boston later on in the week, and I’ll see that a little package is made up for you and the elderlies at the nursing home.

Hope to hear from you soon.

## Letter #7 (NS—&gt;) October 12, 1986

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I’m writing in response to your letter dated 10/30/86.

First, I want to let you know that the contact SKP program is gradually developing here in New England. Although we don’t have any great stories to contribute, …Prabhu has already had some success selling some sets and has averaged between $100—$150 for each of the half dozen sets that he has so far distributed. Right now there are two parties going out. They are working mostly with the names that I have generated over the past couple of years, and are setting up appointments in advance to visit them in their homes. It’s still too early to give you much of an analysis of which techniques are better, but as things crystallize, I’ll see that the news gets to you.

My main concern now is to see that a lead generation program gets underway here, so that there can be a constant supply of leads for the SKP men to follow up on. I have been tempted many times to just go ahead and produce a brochure myself, but to be honest, I just don’t have the resources to put something nice together. I then considered paying the New Vrindavan devotees in the graphics department to do something, and then spoke with a few devotees there to see if it would be possible. If I went there, I could probably get them to do something, but it would cost a lot. What are the ministry’s plans for producing a brochure for bulk mailing? When we spoke in Philadelphia, mentioned something about seeing that a brochure was produced. Are you going to follow up on it? Please get back to me on this point as soon as possible.

Regarding your point about appointing a zonal *Sankirtana* representative, we just had our monthly zonal meeting, and I proposed that Swami be the representative from our zone. Unfortunately, he is in India now until at least the end of the year, so even if he agrees to do it, you may want to hold a meeting before he gets back. So I am prepared to be the zonal representative until he returns, and if he doesn’t want to take the position, I will see that someone does. I do think that it is important to have regular meetings to discuss ideas, but four times a year seems too much. But that can be discussed at the first meeting. As far as when that meeting should be held, if you want to hold it before the marathon, you better call it soon. My main concern at this point is the development of a lead generation program, so that is the topic that I would like to discuss. Primarily I would like to see some standards set for brochures, mailouts, form letters, follow up letters, etc., and I would like to see the BBT produce full-color materials for the temples to purchase.

As far as where the meeting should be, I’ll have to leave that up to you to establish, according to who is interested in attending the meeting. It’s obvious that if the majority of devotees desiring to attend were on the east coast then it should be held on the east coast, or vice versa. I would offer having the meeting in Boston, but as you may recall, we are definitely limited for space. But I suppose that if there were 6 devotees or less attending, it could be possible to have it there.

I like the idea about the self-improvement course. But personally, I feel that the development of this course should be completely secondary to working on my above-mentioned concerns.

I hope that this information gives you a little something to go by. Please get back to me as soon as you have some answers to my questions.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #8 (NS—&gt;Jesse) October 25, 1986

Please accept my blessings. All glories to His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada.

I have received your letters and have read them carefully.

The living entity within this material world is struggling very hard with the six senses, which include the mind. Your situation is certainly no exception. That your service is very regulated now is very good for you. You should see this regulation as your great fortune, for by such regulated service, there will be no gaps for *maya* (i. e. your continual mental ramblings) to enter in. But to accomplish this, you should be on guard to avoid your tendency to serve mechanically, while your mind is somewhere else.

Everyone who joins this movement should do so with the primary motive to become devotees of Krishna. For it is only by devotional service that we can once and for all burn up the false ego, which continues to entangle us in repeated birth and death. But to become a devotee requires that we develop the quality of humility, as Lord Caitanya has instructed in His Siksastaka, “One can chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking himself lower than the straw in the street, more tolerant than the tree, and ready to offer all respects to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly. ” Although you would like to be more recognized, and therefore due to the lack of recognition, you dwell upon your “false ego”, your real glory will only come, when you realize that you are a servant, and not the controller. Krishna will give you all facility to do something wonderful for Him, but first learn how to be His servant, by serving His devotees! See it as your good fortune that you are being given so much opportunity to serve Krishna’s devotees, and use your mind to improve the quality of that service. By genuinely developing this attitude, you will never be the loser, but only the gainer, a million times.

So, in this way, take shelter in your service, and not in your mind. When it starts to ramble, just chant and serve. Don’t give up the struggle. As soon as you do, maya’s got you. And then you’ll just start in again with your fault-finding tendencies. Krishna will only tolerate that for so long. Don’t lose this rare and valuable chance to become a devotee of Krishna, and blow it by committing offenses and losing the association of devotees.

Thank you very much for your invitation to New Vrindavan. I am also anxious to visit there sometime in the future, but exactly when, I cannot say at this time. I have a lot of traveling coming up over the next couple of months, some which is overseas. But the first opportunity I get, I will come.

It is nice that you are relishing reading Japa Reform Notebook. I also greatly appreciate Srila Satsvarupa Maharaja’s writings, and I recommend others to read them as well. But don’t neglect reading Prabhupada’s books. You will become strong if you get regular doses of Srila Prabhupada’s association.

Hoping this finds you in ever-increasing Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #9 (NS—&gt;Romapada Swami) October 31, 1986

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for the name and address of Laslo Derdak. As soon as I received it, I immediately gave it to Dhira dasa who will be attempting to contact him this afternoon for an appointment.

I am anxious to hear from you regarding your valuable assistance for Contact SKP. Since I am devoting a lot of time with some of the devotees here to develop Contact SKP, whatever assistance I can get, is greatly appreciated. Right at this time, we already have two parties going out almost full-time in dhotis. They have been distributing the Introductory Sets purchased from England. During the day they either make phone calls to set up appointments, or they visit the contacts that we are continually getting from various sources. Most people are not home during the day, so the devotees have been leaving nicely packaged sweets with a personal note introducing themselves to the people and just letting them know that while they were in the area they stopped by for a visit. Then when the devotees call them, the people seem a lot more receptive to conversation and a possible visit. They have also been visiting people in the evenings without appointment, but have not had much success in book selling, but have been making some nice contacts for future correspondence.

We haven’t been having any astounding results but… and … have distributed about 8—10 sets since we first got them a little over a month ago. Most of the selling is done by Dhira who has got a lot of experience and he has been averaging between $100—$150 for a set of five books. The other party is … and one new *bhakta. …* is much less experienced than, and has had little results.

But he is continuing to plug away at it.

I am also planning to invest in producing a nice colorful brochure, and try some target mailouts to various mail lists with the hopes of increasing leads. It could cost a lot, so I'm trying to approach it carefully. My hope is to use November and December to generate a lot of leads, and then put the 4 SKP men on contact SKP during the grueling months of Jan. and Feb. for a little enlevement.

So, as you can see, we are already developing Contact SKP, in our own little humble way, and will continue to do so early next year. So whatever help that you can offer would be graciously accepted.

## Letter #10 (NS—&gt;Erin) November 4, 1986

Hare Krishna. I received your card last week inquiring about materials and instructions on individual devotional service, and realized that you once previously wrote making the same inquiry. I was under the impression that I already sent you one of the enclosed booklets, but evidently I was negligent to see that you got one. I apologize for the delay.

I compiled this booklet last year because I realized that inquiries similar to yours were becoming more and more frequent. So many people have had contact with our society by either reading the books by His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada, or by having met his devotees who are trying to fulfill his vision for spreading Krishna consciousness to every town and village. I sincerely hope that this booklet will be of use to you, and that it will help you to develop Krishna consciousness even while living in Waterville, Maine.

There are many people who regularly correspond with me from all over Maine, and there is a possibility that we could be having a gathering sometime around the beginning of the year. Where in Maine it will be cannot be exactly determined at this time. Maine is certainly a big state. But one possibility is that there will be something at Bar Harbor. If you like, I will notify you as soon as there is a more concrete plan. There is a girl who is a student in Bar Harbor, who, like yourself, wants to pursue devotional service while continuing with her education, and I am sure that she would like to know that there are others near her who want to practice Krishna consciousness.

Please feel free to write if you should require any further information or if you should have any further questions on any aspect of Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #11 (NS—&gt;Alison) November 14, 1986

Hare Krishna. Enclosed is some literature that will be helpful for you to get a basic understanding of the philosophy and activities of the Krishna Consciousness movement. You will also find a brochure and forms for membership, should you wish to take advantage of the benefits provided in our membership program.

The Krishna Consciousness movement provides a means for people from all walks of life to gradually realize their full potential as parts and parcels of the Supreme Lord Sri Krishna. By nature we are all meant to experience full knowledge and bliss eternally. But having forgotten our eternal relationship with Krishna, we are now trapped in the pursuits of temporary happiness, only to find our desires for complete happiness unfulfilled. But through service to Krishna we can attain a glimpse of a superior pleasure; a pleasure which carries us to the topmost perfection.

So we invite you to inquire how to add this most important ingredient to your life. It is not necessary to leave one’s position in society, to practice Krishna consciousness. It begins by hearing and reading the books of the Krishna Consciousness Movement. Then service to Krishna will naturally follow. His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada, the founder of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, would often say that if you have so many zeros they add up to zero, but when you add a one before them, you have a very significant number. So, similarly, we may have so much wealth, friendship, and positions in society, but when Krishna is added, it gives everything a purpose.

So please look over these introductory literatures and let us know if we can be of any further service. We have over 100 volumes of literatures available to help increase your understanding of Krishna consciousness. More information can be sent upon your request.

## Letter #12 (NS—&gt;Elise) November 26, 1986

Hare Krishna. Thank you for your donation for the booklet. Every donation helps us so that we can continue to provide people like yourself a way to stay in touch with devotees and gain further insight into the practices and benefits of Krishna consciousness.

Regarding you inquiry about New Vrindavan, I am not aware of such a program, although that certainly does not mean that it does not exist. But such an arrangement could also possibly made in the Boston temple, depending on when you wanted to come and what kind of work you would like to perform during your stay. If you are interested in more information, I suggest that you let me know the answer to these questions and we can take it from there. Of course, if you are more interested in spending time in a rural community, the Boston temple is not the place for you. In that case, you will have to write to New Vrindavan or possibly Gita-nagari farm in Pennsylvania and they can provide you with more information.

Don’t give up on the chanting. It may seem difficult, but there is no greater cure from our material disease of forgetfulness of Krishna. We don’t have the taste for chanting in our neophyte condition. But the example is given that just a person with jaundice finds sugar to be extremely bitter, still, it is the only cure. The more sugar he eats, the more sweeter it begins to taste. Similarly, chanting may seem bitter due to our covered condition, but the more we chant, the sweeter it becomes. This can be practically experienced by perceiving our decreased attraction for temporary material pleasures.

So Krishna will help you, but you must show Him that you want His help, by praying and chanting. Chanting Hare Krishna means “O Lord, energy of the Lord, please engage me in your service”. Krishna will certainly reciprocate with one who chants with sincerity. But without chanting it is virtually impossible to develop the required devotional mentality to gain Krishna’s favor.

Regarding your inquiry about tapes, there are many tape ministries. It depends on whose lectures you are interested in hearing. I recommend Srila Prabhupada’s tapes to everyone. But you may also be interested in hearing from other devotees as well. If that is your particular interest, you will have to specify whose lectures you want to receive, and I will tell you where to send for a list. If you want a list of Prabhupada’s lectures, you will have to write to:

Sankirtana Movement Inc.

3764 Watseka Ave.

Los Angeles, CA 90034

You can also probably get a calendar from this address as well.

If you have any further questions, please feel free to inquire.

## Letter #13 (NS—&gt;Jane) November 26, 1986

I am writing in response to the inquiry you made on the enclosed card that we received last month.

We are not bothered at all by the fact that you do not wish to practice the teachings of Krishna Consciousness. It is not that we are taking a stand that we don’t care if anybody even practices it. But it is more that we are happy when people begin approaching Krishna consciousness in a purely intellectual manner. Satisfying the intelligence is the primary prerequisite for practical application.

We are not interested in blind following. God has given us intelligence for a purpose. This aphorism of the *Vedanta-sutra* states *athato brahma-jijnasya,* “Now that one has this human form of life, he should inquire into the nature of the Absolute Truth”. In other words, we should ask questions such as “Why am I here? Who am I? What is the purpose of this life? Who is God? Is there a God at all? ”, etc., and we should be prepared to determine who can give the most logical answers. That is the gift of the human form of life. If we are only concerned about how to be happy and comfortable in this world, without even knowing who we are, then we are no better than animals, who simply eat, sleep, mate, and defend. If one approaches this Krishna consciousness philosophy in this spirit of inquiry, he will find the most logical explanations to these questions. In this way, one will find the philosophy of Krishna consciousness very intellectually stimulating.

Everyone accepts some kind of authority. But one must first establish the qualification of such an authority. For instance, you wouldn’t immediately subject yourself to surgery or dental work, if you were not confident that the doctor was authorized to practice. So, in a similar way, one should not submit himself for spiritual guidance, unless he can determine the “credentials” of his teacher. There must be a standard criteria for determining someone who is qualified to teach spiritual knowledge. For this we rely upon scripture. We don’t rely on some book that is manufactured by some fallible person’s fertile brain. But rather, we accept knowledge from God—handed down in disciplic succession without any interpretation. Who could be a greater authority than God Himself?

So, I invite you to stimulate your intelligence by reading the *Bhagavad-gita As It Is.* I can send you a copy for just the cost of postage, if you don’t already have one. And if you do have a copy then why don’t you read it and let me know what you think? Then you can determine for yourself whether or not you want to practice it. But don’t even consider practicing it, without out making an honest attempt to understand it.

If you have any further questions, you can feel free to direct them to me at the above address.

## Letter #14 (NS—&gt;Isha) December 11, 1986

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am writing this short note to ask what kind of response you got to your attempt to engage people in making things for the Deities. If you got a real positive response, it may be something that you can do on a more regular basis in the future. Of course, the real proof will be seen when and if these people return with their completed assignments. But I am hopeful for some positive results.

I saw you going around to people on Sunday during the feast and was inspired to see your enthusiasm to engage others in the service of Sri Sri Radha Gopivallabha. I just want you to know that you have my full support in all such endeavors; as well as all of your wonderful preaching endeavors. Your enthusiasm to engage others in Krishna’s service and your strong desire to always speak about Krishna is a wonderful encouragement for all the devotees in the temple.

Thank you very much for your continued inspiration

## Letters—1987

## Letter #15 (NS—&gt;) February 5, 1987

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am glad to hear that you are feeling encouraged in your Krishna consciousness. I can easily see why you have such appreciation for the devotees here in Boston. Personally, I feel very fortunate to have their association. They have so many good qualities that it often embarrasses me when I'm confronted with my own lack of such qualities.

I am 100% convinced that without devotee association we don’t stand a chance against the onslaughts of the material energy. As difficult as it may seem at times, we must always situate ourselves in such a way that we can favorably associate with devotees by continually hearing and chanting the glories of Krishna and His pure devotee, Srila Prabhupada. If that continues then automatically service will be there, and advancement is guaranteed. There’s always the struggle, but Krishna knows our desire. And when he sees us struggling for Him, He promises to preserve whatever we have and to carry whatever we are lacking. You already know these things, but sometimes it helps to hear it from others.

It is easy to see that Gopiballava is rewarding you for your determination to maintain the principles of Krishna consciousness, by allowing you to once again render personal service to Him. Please continue in your steady service and surely Srila Prabhupada will pour his blessings upon you.

Hoping this meets you enthused in Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #16 (NS—&gt;David) February 27, 1987

Hare Krishna. I first want to introduce myself to you. My name is Niranjana Swami and I am presently the regional secretary overseeing the Krishna conscious activities for the International Society for Krishna Consciousness here in New England. Part of my responsibilities, as a preacher of Krishna consciousness, is to provide a means for persons, such as yourself, to grow in their understanding and application of Krishna consciousness.

I recently received your letter to Jiva Goswami dasa, and he asked me to respond to your question regarding future correspondences.

The request you have made brings pleasure to those of us who have dedicated ourselves to bringing the message of devotional service to Lord Krishna into the lives of those who are willing to hear it. It is said that the pleasure of a devotee is experienced proportionally to the pleasure of the Lord. And Lord Krishna has certified that in this world there is no one who gives Him more pleasure than one who tells others about Him. In recent history, that most dear personality was undoubtedly Srila Prabhupada and we are humbly trying to follow in his footsteps by making Srila Prabhupada’s teachings available to the world.

Srila Prabhupada asked his followers to see that his books were widely distributed and that the society of devotees demonstrates to the world, by their own example, how to apply the teachings found in his books. Of course to show others requires that we associate and help to nurture the tiny devotional seeds in the hearts of aspiring transcendentalists, such as yourself, so that one day they can grow into blossoming flowers of love of Godhead. As you will see in your readings of Srimad-Bhagavatam, this process of associating with devotees, by hearing from them about Krishna, and, chanting with them the holy names of the Lord, is the very means for spiritual elevation.

So please don’t feel that your request is in opposition to the discipline required for spiritual growth for the devotees here in Boston ISKCON. On the contrary, it is the very means by which we obtain spiritual advancement, for without pleasing Srila Prabhupada we have no means by which we can hope to please Krishna, which is the perfection of life.

So as soon as you feel the need to inquire, please feel free to send your questions to me c/o the Boston temple. I will try to answer your letters as quickly as possible but please forgive me if, on occasion, I take a few weeks to respond. My service often brings me to far away places, but I regularly return to Boston and answer all of my mail. For instance, I will be leaving on March 12 for 3—4 weeks in India, so I will need to receive your inquiries by the end of the first week of March in order to respond before I leave. Otherwise, I will not be able to do so until the second week of April.

Hope to hear from you soon.

## Letter #17 (NS—&gt;Caroline) April 17, 1987

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have received your card and have carefully read and considered the contents.

I am encouraged to hear that you are now more determined in your desire to be Krishna conscious. It is undoubtedly very difficult to maintain Krishna consciousness while living at school. It is not impossible, but certainly living in the association of devotees is superior.

I am also aware of your family’s resistance to your taking to Krishna consciousness. I don’t want to discourage you from doing what you are now convinced is best, but I do want to caution you to be discreet in your dealings with your family. Don’t do something that will only make it more difficult for you to be a devotee in the future. I strongly suggest that you deal with your parents. Don’t just move in to a temple and try to make believe that they’ll just go away, or that they don’t exist. The mature approach of dealing with them head on about your intentions and convictions prior to moving in, is always far superior and even though they may still vehemently protest, you have a much better chance of gaining their acceptance in the future then if you are insensitive. Don’t give your parents the opportunity to say that you just cut yourself off from them. By dealing with them it becomes far more evident that being a devotee does not mean that you have to reject them. They may reject you for doing so, but you don’t have to reject them. Of course, I am not aware of the particulars in your case, but in general this is always the best policy. You should take more detailed advice from your immediate authorities when you are finally prepared to move into the temple.

Regarding your desire to move into the Philadelphia temple, I must say, in principle, wherever a devotee serves in ISKCON, he/she can advance by serving in that temple and by following the instructions of the local authorities. Of course as you have rightly expressed, one must also accept a bona fide spiritual master in disciplic succession. Krishna can act through many devotees to inspire you in service to Him. But still, you must choose, at some point, someone in whom your faith is the strongest, and seek initiation from him. But that can take time, and you should not feel rushed. Especially since you have expressed an attraction to moving to Philadelphia. You may find yourself relying more upon local inspiration than mine.

## Letter #18 (NS—&gt;) July 20, 1987

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Although we spoke briefly on the phone today, there were still some thoughts on my mind that I felt were inappropriate to discuss over the phone, and I have thus chosen a more preferable format for expressing them; the letter. As you are quite aware sometimes I feel more comfortable using this method of communication on potentially sensitive issues, because it gives the recipient a greater opportunity to consider the points raised without forcing a response under artificial pressure. That reason is there to some degree in this letter, but it is also equally valid to say that my thoughts on this matter have further crystallized since having left for reasons which I will also explain. 1) I am actually feeling a bit more Krishna conscious as a result of having increased opportunities for preaching while traveling \[(i. e. classes every day, and some outside preaching engagements) (I have often told you that if I don’t preach enough, I practically turn into a demon, and I feel that I was not preaching enough during my last extensive stay in...; though I have no one to blame except myself)\] And 2) Just being out of and not (yet) under managerial pressure has enabled me to think more about your situation there in

Therefore while my mind is clearer on the issue at hand, and while I am feeling a little Krishna conscious, I want to share my thoughts with you rather than waiting until I return to..., at which time I could easily be clouded over again. I certainly will not deny the possibility of that happening.

After our *isthagosthi* last Monday, your brief visit to my office left me feeling that in no uncertain terms, you want out of the temple presidency in I think that I can count several times that you have intimated this to me, because each time you do so, it leaves me with a marked impression. I realize that all along I have been dealing lightly with you on this issue. It is sensitive, because the person who tends to bear the burden the most is obviously me. But I am also feeling that now, more than ever, I also bear the burden to preach to you plain and simple Krishna conscious philosophy. I know, only too well, that I must take that responsibility that’s given to me. If I don’t preach I’ll ultimately suffer the consequences, and if I do preach (in an unmotivated way, which I am praying to Krishna that I become qualified to do so) then we both stand to prosper by our increased Krishna consciousness and service attitude to Krishna; for certainly, to some extent, preaching Krishna consciousness is my service to Prabhupada and Krishna.

Now, I am feeling that if I don’t try to help you to become Krishna conscious throughout this whole ordeal, then who will? I know that Maharaja has the potential to do so, but I think that you will agree that for the time being he has distanced himself from such “local” concerns. He would tend to take this more as a managerial affair and would place the matter into the hands of local authorities.

I am also fully aware that our society as a whole has suffered from individuals artificially assuming roles as great Krishna conscious preachers and thus utilized their positions to preach to their “subordinates” to “surrender to Krishna” while they have appeared to not be able to teach the same by their example. These weeds of material desire have left many devotees scarred, for now many have felt exploited by their superiors. We are all presently experiencing the aftermath. In the wake of these previous disasters, I lack the conviction that I can be a real, shining example to you, but to the extent that I am, I am confident that my words will bear a greater effectiveness. I often grapple with the idea of whether or not we can still preach “surrender to Krishna”, but, philosophically I know that we must, otherwise how will we keep Srila Prabhupada’s teachings intact.

My main point of contention in your approach to the issue is your insistent appeal of “lack of qualification” for being the temple president. I find that the utilization of this approach simply becomes an opportunity to hide behind the “lack of desire” which is the real issue at hand. What is our qualification for doing anything in this movement? In Srila Prabhupada’s example we learn that his qualification was that he simply repeated what he had heard from his spiritual master. And because his desire was to please his spiritual master, everything else followed accordingly. Ultimately our qualification for doing anything for Krishna is that we are conscious that we always dependent upon Him for all the results in the execution of our duties. In a purport to Srimad-Bhagavatam 3. 9. 29 Srila Prabhupada states:

“Any person authorized by either the Lord or by His bona fide representative is already blessed, as is the work entrusted to him. Of course, the person entrusted with such a responsibility should always be aware of his incapability and must always look for the mercy of the Lord for the successful execution of his duty. One should not be puffed up because he is entrusted with certain executive work. Fortunate is he who is so entrusted, and if he is always fixed in the sense of being subordinate to the will of the Supreme, he is sure to come out successful in the discharge of his work. ”

We must feel that we are incapable, for if we were to feel too materially qualified, we could easily forget our dependence upon the Lord. Material qualifications are not the criteria for devotional service. It is all dependent upon our desire to please Krishna.

So the question is not “Are you qualified? ” but rather more appropriately it is “Are you desiring? ”. If your desire to continue your service is not strong, then it can be philosophically concluded that you will cultivate the desire for something else. But even if that is the case, then you still must depend upon Lord Krishna for the fulfillment of your desire.

Consider Kardama Muni’s desire for a wife of like disposition. He practiced severe penances under the command of Lord Brahma for 10, 000 years and propitiated the Lord by worship through devotional service. Understanding the desire of His devotee, Lord Krishna arranged for the fulfillment of his desires. In the purport to Srimad-Bhagavatam 3. 21. 28 Srila Prabhupada explains this interesting point.

“The Lord awards all benedictions according the heart’s desire of a devotee, so the Lord informed Kardama Muni, ‘The girl who is coming to be married with you is a princess, the daughter of Emperor Svayambhuva, and so is just suitable for your purpose. ’ ” Only by God’s grace can one get a nice wife just as he desires. Similarly, it is only by God’s grace that a girl gets a husband suitable to her heart. Thus it is said that if we pray to the Supreme Lord in every transaction of our material existence, everything will be done very nicely and just suitable to our heart’s desire. In other words, in all circumstances we must take shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and depend completely on His decision. Man proposes, and God disposes. The fulfillment of desires, therefore, should be entrusted to the Supreme Personality of Godhead; that is the nicest solution.... If we depend on the choice of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, we will receive benedictions in greater opulence than we desire.

So, it is not my intention to grind you down into the ground by attacking your weakness from every angle of vision. There are certainly other areas that we could cover, but I felt compelled to deal mainly with that particular argument; qualification vs. desire.

So please consider these points and how you can ultimately apply Srila Prabhupada’s words to the situation you’re in. If we increase our Krishna consciousness, then what can possibly be the loss? And the gain is immeasurable.

I am also not indicating by this letter that I feel that you are being negligent of your duties. I am pleased and will always be grateful for your steadiness and sincerity. I have indicated in the past, though, that you should make a priority to do some preaching, and in this area, I feel that you have been negligent to some degree. You can speak the philosophy nicely and your unwillingness to do so is simply miserly on your part. If you do make some time for preaching, your consciousness will become much less affected. Nobody can just manage and not be affected.

My plans for return are still the same. I will be returning from Montreal on August 2, at which time, by Krishna’s grace, we can associate more together.

Hoping this finds you in good health and in Krishna conscious spirits.

## Letter #19 (NS—&gt;Gail) Boston. August 24, 1987

Hare Krishna.

It’s been over a month, but I just realized that I never wrote back to thank you for your encouraging letter and donation. All the devotees deeply appreciated your realizations and strong support through these difficult times. Your letter was, and still is, posted on the bulletin board for all of us to regularly read and be reminded that although we face much opposition, there are still so many people like your good selves who have a deep appreciation for Krishna consciousness and our movement. Although you are not able to live within Krishna conscious communities, you are living proof that one can still remain Krishna conscious while living in the secular world.

We are indebted to your consistent support.

I have enclosed a 15-point Master-Plan that the North American GBC conceived in our meeting in Dallas earlier this month. I thought that you would be interested to see some of the goals that we are enthusiastically and cooperatively working towards. Some of them may appear to be wishful, but we are convinced that if we set our goals to cooperatively do something wonderful for Prabhupada and Lord Krishna, that He will be all the more inclined to benedict us with wonderful results beyond our expectations for His pleasure.

You will also be happy to know that despite the court decision in June, the Boston temple is still functioning strongly, and the devotees are going out daily to distribute the holy name, books, and Krishna *prashadaam. Ratha-yatra* was a wonderful success, and hundreds and hundreds of people came to our Janmashtami festival, and voiced their support.

Hoping this meets you in good health. Please offer my respects to Frank, and tell him that I sincerely hope that someday soon, Krishna will allow me to visit.

## Letter #20 (—&gt;NS)

A Newsletter Article

Niranjana Swami has been maintaining correspondence with Phil, a friend of the movement. In Phil’s most recent letter, he has written:

... “I felt ‘coerced’ to join ISKCON, of course. You people believe in what you are doing. I have been similarly confronted by other groups of people from Catholics to Baptists to insurance salesman to college representatives. Although it is crude, I have to agree with my father when he says, ‘Everybody’s a salesman’.

To extend the metaphor, I think that I'm ultimately shopping for the best lifestyle. If there is a God then service to Him certainly seems like the ticket (to the best lifestyle).

... Anyway, I like being in touch with you since I am constantly pursuing something like what ISKCON claims to be ‘selling’ (offering). It does add an exotic touch to my more mundane self. ”

## Letter #21 (NS—&gt;Phil; response to #20) Boston. October 7, 1987

Hare Krishna! As you begin reading this letter, you must be thinking that I have some nerve returning your letter 2 months after receiving yours. I don’t blame you. I don’t like it either. I have never been this negligent in answering my mail. I have written other letters in the last two months; in between trips to L. A., Dallas, Montreal, Allentown, Indiana, etc. etc. But most of those letters were just quick responses that I could just sit down and write. I didn’t want to do that with you, because there were some philosophical points that I wanted to address, and I wanted to properly formulate them. So instead, I have had to just always think about taking the time to answer your letter. Tonight, by Krishna’s grace, those thoughts are now coming to fruition.

I particularly wanted to respond to your comment about everyone being a salesman. I tend to partially agree with that, but I feel that further clarification is required. There is an exception to the ordinary exchange of material goods for material profit. Certainly in the mundane sense, a person desiring money, fame, power, etc. sells his wares (material goods, dogma, ideals, etc. ) for fulfilling his goals. And, no doubt, his apparent success is always at other’s expense. Others may sacrifice, but their return will always fall short of real fulfillment, thus only receiving temporary rewards.

The corporate magnate increases his wealth by the exploitation of others. Advertising agencies are successfully convincing the consumers that totally useless material commodities are as essential to life as the air we breathe. Just the other day, I was marveling how powerful the illusory energy is, by allowing people to think that by smoking Newports one becomes “alive with pleasure”. On the bottom of the billboard the surgeon general is noting that smoking cigarettes can cause death, and interminable suffering, but somehow when you stick some guy and a girl together, clasping each other in erotic poses, smoking cigarettes becomes “alive with pleasure”.

Our political leaders constantly sell themselves to the public, hoping they’ll buy their latest stop-gap solutions as permanent assets for economic recovery, or for solving whatever problems that exist as a result of the previous administration. They get their high paying positions and simply tax the people to death, stealing as much as they can for their own selfish gains.

And even so-called “religionists” sell their dogmatic sermons with ulterior motives of fame and fortune. Certainly, Jim and Tammy Bakker are a perfect example of lavish living in the name of religion. There are certainly a lot more of Jims and Tammys around for everyone. Srila Prabhupada would often say that this world is a place of the cheaters and the cheated. If people want to be cheated (by thinking that they can advance in spiritual life by simultaneously indulging in sensual pursuits), then they will certainly encourage lots of cheaters who will promote false promises for material gain.

If we take a close examination of ourselves and others, we should be able to see that every living being must live at the expense of others. Even the air that we breathe, the water that we drink, and the food that we eat, is available by the superior background arrangement of Krishna. And the Bhagavad-gita describes that one who takes even the basic necessities of life without first offering them in sacrifice is a thief and is to be punished by the stringent laws of material nature.

But Lord Krishna also declares that those who recognize Him as the supreme object of sacrifice and austerities, the Supreme Lord of all planets and demigods, and the friend of all living entities, can attain peace from the pangs of material miseries. Others may exploit for personal gains, but what need is there in Krishna, since He is already the proprietor, enjoyer, and controller of everything and everyone? He’s God and He simply enjoys, but still He comes to this world to display His divine, loving pastimes to attract us to the real world of eternity, knowledge and bliss. We have forgotten that relationship and instead we have chosen to seek permanent happiness in a temporary place fraught with illusion and suffering. Although we have forgotten Krishna, He does not forget us, and thus He is always making arrangements for our deliverance by coming Himself and imparting His instructions, or by sending His pure representative to deliver His message without any motive for personal gain. He wants only our love and nothing else, for nothing else can attract Krishna. He already has everything. And He wants to give us everything by giving Himself. Is He a salesman by asking for our love for our eternal benefit? You could say yes, if you think that He is just an ordinary person. But who else could make such a claim? Only God is capable of fully satisfying all of our needs and desires.

Well you might now say that OK, maybe I can accept that that is the position of God, but what about everyone else who’s trying to sell Him to me? To that I will respond that if someone is found to be giving Krishna to others, whose only desire is to please Krishna, and who accepts everything given to him on behalf of Krishna, then he is a true representative of Krishna. That is the test. Certainly such a devotee of the Lord is not the slightest concerned of material gains, for all of his needs our fulfilled directly by Krishna.
