Just because there are so many counterfeit coins, does not mean that there is not a genuine one. Granted, everyone claims that theirs is the real thing, but if there is conflicting views, how do you know who’s really delivering the goods? As the saying goes “the proof of the pudding is in the tasting”. You must see for yourself. There is a genuine standard for godly life. One must be genuinely renounced and detached from all worldliness, otherwise how can he purely deliver God’s message without exploiting others? That standard is given by Krishna Himself. You must first learn of that standard by hearing from the right authorities.

Srila Prabhupada would often give the analogy of the person desirous of purchasing a diamond. If he were to enter the jewelers without first knowing the how to recognize the diamond, he could easily be swayed into purchasing a piece of broken glass for thousands of dollars. Therefore, the onus is on him to first learn how to recognize the characteristics of a genuine diamond by first hearing from an authority on diamonds. Similarly we should not blindly accept someone as God’s representative without first hearing from scripture, how to recognize His true representative.

So don’t be negligent. If you’re genuinely searching, then you should not pass up the opportunity to genuinely search in Srila Prabhupada’s books for the real standard of life. I know that you already have some of his books, so start reading. And if you need some more then I’ll be more than happy to send you whatever you like. Read, inquire, and chant Hare Krishna. You’ll get a heck of a lot more than your 2-week free membership at “Living Well Spa”.

Hoping this finds you in good health and “good consciousness”.

## Letter #22 (NS—&gt;) Boston. October 9, 1987

Hare Krishna. I realize that communications between us have dwindled to nil over the past year. I’m not sure that I fully understand why, but whatever reason was there, I thought that I would at least try to open them up again, having recently heard that you are under great physical and mental duress. It’s not that I’m foolishly thinking that I have any more ability than others to help you during these times, but at least I am thinking that maybe you’ll find solace in knowing that there is someone out here who cares.

I just recently had a similar experience myself, when I received a package of Ayurvedic medicine from a very senior devotee in Europe. He has hundreds of disciples and oversees so many temples all over Europe, but still he thought of sending me this medicine that he purchased for me in India, knowing that I have been suffering from bronchial attacks over the last few years. Needless to say, I felt encouraged.

So now I have heard from... that you’ve developed some terrible disease, and that your marriage is not working out as well. Of course, in such a desperate state, one naturally seeks solutions to his immediate problems. But in the course of your search, please don’t forget all that you have heard from classes and from Srila Prabhupada’s books, regarding the nature of this material world and the solutions to suffering. We must seek solutions, but without Krishna consciousness, they will only be temporary.

Although, I am not sending any medicine for your ill health, I hope that this little reminder will brighten your hopes for a positive future in Krishna consciousness.

Hoping this finds you in improved health and increased Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #23 (NS—&gt;) Boston. November 24, 1987

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for thinking of us here in Boston and sending us a little news about yourself. We were all concerned that we had not heard from you, and were wondering where you landed.

I apologize for not writing sooner. I probably don’t have to explain the reason why since you are aware of all the traveling that I have to do. I have not been in Boston very much during the last month, and was not able to get to sit down and correspond during my travels either. I usually save everything for Boston.

Even now, I am pressed for time. Although I just returned to Boston last night, I must leave for Washington tomorrow morning, but I felt compelled to at least get something out to you to let you know that we care and hope that you will be able to come back into the association of devotees at some time in the near future.

Just now all of Satsvarupa Maharaja’s disciples are eagerly preparing for his arrival tomorrow. It will be his first visit to Boston in almost a year. He will stay for 4 days and move on to the mid- Atlantic. Why don’t you try to get to see him in Philadelphia when he is there in about a week?

You don’t sound very happy in your letter. As a matter of fact, you explicitly state that you are not happy. You’re right! There is no real happiness in the material world—only temporary relief from suffering. The devotees are experiencing real happiness. And the more that they tolerate the nonpermanent appearance of material happiness and distress, and continue to serve despite all obstacles, the more they can sustain this real happiness. Krishna is waiting for us to turn to Him, and he wants nothing more than to fulfill all of our desires. Don’t you forget that.

So keep sending us letters and keep associating with devotees.

Hoping this finds you in Krishna conscious spirits.

## Letter #24 (NS—&gt;) December 14, 1987

Just a few days ago, dasa, a member of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness met you in a parking lot in the Springfield area and had the opportunity to briefly introduce you to the basic tenets of our devotional practices. You gave him your name and address and requested to have members of our society visit your home to provide a further elaboration of our philosophy and lifestyle.

Since your interest was keen, I am sure that you have taken the opportunity to review some of the literature that has given you, and you may already have questions pertaining to the application of Krishna consciousness.

To be honest, ordinarily I don’t write a personal letter to someone who has just been briefly introduced to our movement. Usually, they are put on our mailing list to begin receiving this newsletter and personal correspondence begins after they initiate it with letters of inquiry. But Ananta dasa insisted that I contact you, because you were so eager to learn more. So that is my reason for writing.

I have been a member of the Krishna consciousness movement for 15 years and I spend much of my time introducing people to Krishna consciousness in temples, churches, schools and homes. Often friends of our movement invite their friends and acquaintances to their homes for an evening of Krishna consciousness. For instance, this Thursday I will be visiting a friend in Hadley who has invited many of his friends for an evening of chanting and an informal discussion. If you receive this letter in time, and you would like to attend, you can call Bob. He can give you more information.

Otherwise, I can also visit you; especially since I am staying in Hartford for the next week. There are volumes and volumes of Vedic literatures that can be made available to you.

I can be reached at our Hartford temple at (203)289—7252 between now and December 24. Or, if you just want to send questions to me, my mailing address is on this letterhead.

I look forward to hearing from you soon.

## Letters—1988

## Letter #25 (NS—&gt;Seth) January 10, 1988

Hare Krishna. Today was the first opportunity that I’ve had in months to sit down and write several letters. While determining who I needed to write to, I thought that I would send a note of encouragement to you. You encouraged me by your constant participation throughout our visit in Hartford, so I thought that I should at least reciprocate.

Although I realize that Radha’s imminent departure is on the minds of many of the Hartford devotees, we can all learn from this experience how this material world is a dangerous place. We may try to look for material formulas to solve this most foreboding problem of life... death and disease, but there is none that exists. Krishna consciousness provides the only viable solution. But somehow we always seem to think that we will escape and “Krishna consciousness” falls on the last of our list of obligations. But none of us will escape and we must be prepared for death’s imminent beckoning by always keeping ourselves fit for full remembrance of the Lord. Now that you have taken serious steps for that spiritual development, please don’t slip back and give up this most important of all spiritual commitments, regularly chanting of the holy names of the Lord. Just as many of you so seriously chanted for Radha when you thought she was ready to leave, we should all think that we could just as easily leave at any moment. There’s no telling how much time we have in these bodies.

So please continue to chant and encourage to hold morning programs, just as you encouraged us by your participation. Although it certainly is more difficult with fewer devotees, kirtana can be performed with as few as two devotees. And the more that come the better it is. Maybe you could pick certain days of the week that would be most suited for you and at least commit yourself to those days. Then you could tell and he could also plan to hold the morning program at a suitable time for you.

I also realize that he is planning on leaving for India for several weeks within the next week or so. But you could still start before he leaves and then you could pick up where you left off once he returns. It is going to take some work to build up a momentum, but if you and he are determined I know it can work. He has also expressed to me his desire for having you at the morning program on several occasions.

As for myself, I will be leaving for Florida tomorrow morning and from there I will return to Boston for the first week of February before departing for India myself. Unfortunately, I will probably not get another opportunity to spend some time with you until I return. But, in the meantime I thought that I would at least write to share some of my realizations with you.

Hoping this meets you in good health and blissful devotional spirits.

## Letter #26 (NS—&gt;) Montreal. April 8, 1988

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

This afternoon I was checking the date on my watch and realized that tomorrow evening is the 2nd Saturday of the month. I lamented that another Nashua program is supposed to be scheduled, and again I am not available to participate. It seems that it’s become impossible for me to schedule my other responsibilities around that program every month. But, nonetheless, I'm grateful when thinking how dedicated you’ve been to see that this program goes on every month. Please don’t think that I'm not noticing or not interested, because I do not attend. This program is the steadiest program in New England, outside of the temples’ Sunday feasts. And they only go on, due to your determination.

I realize that sometimes it can become discouraging when the participation is small, and when people don’t come forward to give more support. We have to always tax our brains how to engage more and more people in Lord Caitanya’s *Sankirtana* Movement, and sometimes the Lord may not manifest results just to increase our determination. On the other hand sometimes, by using our intelligence we must make adjustments in preaching strategies. But we must always be careful to make sure that our adjustments are done for Krishna’s pleasure and not for ourselves.

I would like to discuss the Nashua programs with you during my next visit to Boston. To be honest, I am writing this letter with no knowledge whatsoever of what has been happening in Nashua since January. Last I heard is that dasa was asked to do last month’s program while I was away in India. I never heard about it, since I have not spoken with him since returning. And I also did not get a chance to speak with you during the Sunday feast I was in Boston. I believe I got caught up in preaching to a particular guest, and by the time I was finished you were gone.

Can we get together sometime during my next trip to Boston, besides during the Sunday feast? I am presently planning to return to Boston somewhere around the 13th of this month and will be staying only until the 18th. Can you call me in Boston around Wednesday and let me know if this will be possible? I am planning to visit someone in Tewksbury, MA one of the evenings during that time period. Maybe I can get together with you just after you get out of work on the same afternoon, and from there I can go to his home. I should know what night I will be visiting him by Wednesday.

If I don’t hear from you by 3: 00 PM Wednesday, I’ll call you at work.

Hoping this finds you well and determined in spiritual life.

## Letter #27 (NS—&gt;Jon) May 1, 1988

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have wanted to write, but I am sure that you understand that it has been difficult for me to easily find the time. But now, I have just received your 2nd letter dated 4/9 with ...’s letter enclosed. I want to respond mainly to encourage and to help give you direction.

Regarding desire, Prabhupada would often say “first deserve, then desire”; that is—before you can even intensely desire to serve Krishna you must first deserve to have that desire. As intense as you say that your desires are to serve Krishna, I’m sure that you will find that there are so many other conflicting desires constantly getting in the way. That is our fallen position. Spiritual life means the purification of desire—to revert our desire for personal enjoyment back to it’s original state; pure love for Krishna.

The process of *bhakti* is the method by which we can develop the real desire you describe.

*atha cittam samadhatum*  
*na saksnosi mayi sthiram*  
*abhyasa-yogena tato*  
*mam icchaptum dhananjaya*

“My dear Arjuna, O winner of wealth, if you cannot fix your mind upon me without deviation, then follow the regulated principles of bhakti-yoga. In this way you will develop a desire to attain to Me. ” (BG 12. 9)

Although that pure desire is actually there in your heart, it is still lying dormant.

*nitya siddha krsna prema sadhya kabu naya*  
*sravanadi suddha citte karaye udaya*

“Pure love for Krishna is eternally established in the hearts of living entities. It is not something to be gained from another source. When the heart is purified by hearing and chanting, the living entity naturally awakens. ” (CC Madhya 22. 107)

You have heard about Krishna from the right source and that has awoken you to the need to pursue Him. Association with Him is certainly the highest pleasure for the hankering soul. But, as you say, this unseen enemy does not allow you to pursue Him through bhakti. If you actually want nothing but Krishna, karma cannot hold you back. By surrendering to Krishna, all one’s sinful actions are completely destroyed.

So you must develop the desire to attain Him by following the regulated principles of bhakti-yoga. Don’t rely on time to bring you to Krishna. Krishna says that He appears as time, the destroyer of all worlds. Time destroys everything material and that means it will take stains away, too. But it won’t take the karma away. Only Krishna, personally will do that, when you make the endeavor to approach Him.

That constant endeavor to approach Him is the means by which one develops the desire. What is this foolish Pantanjali yoga you talked about in your previous letter? This will not give you the desire you are looking for. You are being misled again by reading from those who are not pure devotees of Krishna. Stop reading those nonsense books and just read Srila Prabhupada’s books!!! The pure devotee of Krishna can give you Krishna, and the more you hear from the pure devotee, the more you awaken your dormant Krishna consciousness. If you make any endeavor at all, then you should make the endeavor to read only Srila Prabhupada’s books, or you’ll continue to come up with these philosophical misconceptions that will enable you to justify depending on time instead of depending on Krishna. Reading Srila Prabhupada’s books is bhakti-yoga. Reading those written by nondevotees is clearly not bhakti-yoga.

Stay on the right path, and don’t get diverted. Continue to chant, offer your food, read Srila Prabhupada’s books, and associate with devotees. You will not be rejected. I give you my word. The endeavor to do these basic and simple devotional practices, will be the deserving activity that will gradually evoke the desire that you’re looking for.

I still would like, very much to have you accompany me whenever your schedule would allow it. I don’t know what you’re even doing now. Are you working again? Are you going back to school? Why don’t you let me know? I just returned from driving to Ottawa, to Toronto, Montreal, and back to Boston; without any driving assistance. I could use your help whenever you could offer it. Please consider my request.

I am leaving for Los Angeles on May 3 for 8 days. I’ll be back in Boston on the 11th, at which time I will stay for only a few days and will be back on my way to Canada. Maybe you could get back in touch with me some time after the 11th?

Regarding the letter you sent from, I will be discussing the matter with his spiritual master in Los Angeles. Thank you for your concern. It was the right thing to do.

Hoping this finds you in good health and enthusiastic to pursue the lotus feet of Krishna.

## Letter #28 (NS-&gt;) Detroit. May 24, 1988

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have received your letter dated 5/5/88, and have noted the contents.

Although your letter expresses an unsettled state, it was good to hear that at least your desire to become Krishna conscious is still there. If you associate too much with nondevotees, that desire could be lost. Therefore, my inclination is to encourage you in any ambition that will bring you into the association of devotees. So, your idea of traveling to Europe and then to India is approved by me, providing that you do not make it into a vacation tour. Stay only in ISKCON temples. And while you are in the temple, you must attend the full program and render service under the direction of the temple authorities.

Before you plan your trip, though, you should first consult with me regarding which temples to visit in both Europe and India. I know devotees throughout all of those countries, whose association I would like you to take advantage of.

Once you have saved sufficient funds, and you are prepared to purchase your ticket, you should contact me so that we can meet and go over these details.

One other thing that you may want to consider is that I am tentatively planning a trip sometime late fall to some parts of Europe and onward to India. You could possibly accompany me for parts of the trip, depending upon how you feel about that. It’s not mandatory that you travel with me. If you feel that, for now, it’s better that you don’t, that is also acceptable to me. I will leave that up to you.

I am very pleased that Mr. Patanjali is back at the library. Please continue to read Srila Prabhupada’s books only.

If you would like to visit me sometime, I will be back in Boston from approximately June 6—18.

## Letter #29 (NS—&gt;) May 29, 1988

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Last week I had a very interesting experience at a home program in Grand Rapids, Michigan. Sridhara Maharaja was with me at that program and while reflecting on that experience with him this morning he encouraged me to send it along to you for publication in the *Sankirtana* Newsletter.

This is one of those classic stories of the unlimited potency of Srila Prabhupada’s books. But being ever-fresh the more we hear about them, the more relishable they become.

I was invited by Apurva Prabhu to attend a series of programs organized by himself along with other devotees from Detroit. This particular program was held in the home of Murari Krishna dasa, a disciple of Srila Prabhupada who’s now living and working in Grand Rapids. Murari had advertised his program with posters, along with a small ad in the in a local newspaper. He promoted it as an evening of chanting, feasting, and a philosophical discourse on the *Bhagavad- gita.*

Unfortunately we arrived a little later than scheduled, having been delayed by the previous afternoon engagement, conducted by Sridhara Maharaja. Maharaja’s lecture was so enlivening that the Indian families would not let us leave without us giving some kind of assurance that they would be able to hold more programs in Grand Rapids on a regular basis.

Anyways, we entered Murari’s house, and I quickly sat down to introduce the chanting and led a *kirtana* for about 15 minutes. Then I proceeded to speak to the 8—10 guests who had gathered for the program. During the questions and answers, one rather “straight” looking gentleman in his late 30’s raised his hand and proceeded to ask me if he was pronouncing the Hare Krishna *maha-mantra* correctly or not. He recited the whole *mantra,* clearly enunciating each syllable and then continued to say that he had been chanting rounds every day for the last 10 years, but never heard the proper pronunciation from a practicing devotee. Astonished, I asked him why he had never heard the chanting before, to which he responded that he had been reading Prabhupada’s books since 1978 but never could get himself to visit a temple since there was never one in Grand Rapids. He saw the poster for the program in a grocery store, and decided that maybe it was time to pay a visit to the devotees.

After the lecture, Maharaja spoke to him for a while and asked him how he liked the *kirtana.* He said that chanting was much better and more powerful in a group. While other devotees were speaking with him, he concluded his visit by saying that this was all too overwhelming for him to absorb in one evening. Saturated with an evening of *Krishna katha,* and *Krishna prashadaa,* he left with a smile saying, “You’ll see me again”.

I'm convinced that there’s so many people out there taking shelter of Prabhupada’s association. Experiences like this completely convince me that our main business is still seeing that Prabhupada’s books continue to enter into the homes of misguided souls. Then for extra added encouragement, when we go out and preach, we’ll meet more and more souls, who’s devotional creeper was planted from the lips of Srila Prabhupada.

Hoping this finds you in good health and blissfully absorbed in *Sankirtana yajna.*

## Letter #30 (NS—&gt;) July 26, 1988

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your latest letter in hand and want to respond to the different points that you have raised.

First, regarding Maharaja, and his engaging you in helping with street fairs, I just spent a couple of days with Maharaja in Toronto. He is not going to be in New York for quite some time. He is planning to travel across the States and then down into Latin America. So, it looks as though this facility for service may not be available to you—at least by Maharaja.

I'm glad to hear that you had an opportunity to preach to many people during your recent trip to the country. As long as you always try to give as much Krishna conscious association to everyone you meet, as much as possible, you will always be protected in all of your endeavors. But be careful. Granted, since you are working within the materialistic society, you must oftentimes associate with nondevotees. But to the degree that you subject yourself to their whimsical desires, you will find it very difficult to remain fixed in a service attitude to Krishna. We should never want to forget Krishna, not even for a moment.

Regarding your request for me to come and preach in New York, I appreciate greatly your desire to help with my preaching. If you were closer to Boston, then I would immediately jump at the opportunity. But considering my ongoing itinerary, repeated visits to New York would be virtually impossible for me. As it is, I hardly ever get to drive much further south than Hartford, CT. I'm spending more and more of my time in Canada, due to my responsibilities there.

What’s happening with your attempts to return to the Boston area. Has your lawyer made any progress? Of course, I realize that you already have plans to attend nursing school in New York this fall, and that should be disrupted. But, nonetheless, if you do work things out in such a way that you could get back into Mass., then the possibility of transferring to a nearby school could be considered. Then you wouldn’t have to go through all the problems of lack of service and association with devotees. You know that there’s always so much service for you to do here, and the devotees would warmly receive you.

As far as initiations go, this time I am only initiating two devotees; Bhakta John, who’s living in the temple, and Charran Mahadeo, whose address you already have. One thing, though is that I was misinformed as to the date of Balarama’s Appearance Day. It seems as though it falls on the 27th of August and not the 28th. I hope that that won’t affect you plans to come to Boston for the function.

By the way, I still have not received the sweater from Land’s End. I hope that you did not pay for it yet. It seems as though somehow it must of gotten lost in the mail.

So, I hope that all is well, and that you are remaining enthused to increase your devotion to Krishna. Don’t forget, don’t dwell upon your problems should maya try to test you again. Chant Krishna’s name, and always expect His mercy. You will be able to pass over any obstacle that maya will place before you as long as you keep trying to remember Krishna.

## Letter #31 (NS—&gt;friend) August 15, 1988

Hare Krishna. In the enclosed article entitled “Ratha-yatra, The Lord of the Universe Beckons” you will get a glimpse into the unique and enjoyable experience of Ratha-yatra. In a sense, that experience is available to anyone who even slightly comes in contact with this event—whether it be passers-by en route to the festival grounds, or one who simply relishes one morsel of Jagannatha *prashada* (foodstuffs offered in devotion to the Lord).

Even more relishable is the opportunity to assist the Lord in this pastime of benedicting thousands of Bostonians by His merciful presence in the form of Lord Jagannatha. This is His unique way of providing all of us with a way to increase our attachment to Him through devotional service.

There’s so much to be done for *Ratha-yatra* that all the devotees begin making plans at least 6 months prior to the event, and each successive month reveals more and more to be done in preparation for Lord Jagannatha’s ride.

Now we are in the final month prior to *Ratha-yatra* and as those final days begin approaching, volumes of service gradually increase to unlimited proportions. Here at the temple, we’re already tasting the nectar of Lord Jagannatha’s mercy, but within a short time there will be more service than we will be capable of performing without your help.

Don’t miss out! Please read over the list of services on the other side of this letter, and pick one or more that you can perform for the Lord’s pleasure. Then call us and let us know when you can come so that we can coordinate these activities in the most efficient way possible.

When you call, please ask for myself of for Swami.

Hoping this finds you in good health.

You can help most effectively in the following ways:

1\) Donating—The estimated cost is over $7, 000. Thus far we’ve raised about 1/3 of this. If you can help in this area, please contact Dhananjaya Pandita dasa.

2\) Kitchen help—Preparations for cooking begin on Wednesday, September 7 and go right up to Saturday morning. Services vary from vegetable cutting, pot-washing, puri rolling, etc., etc.

3\) Garland making—Garland production will begin on Friday September 9th. Thousands of flowers will be strung to decorate the cart, so be prepared to work through the night, since garlands cannot be made too early.

4\) Cart construction—(Sept. 5—9) The construction will begin on Monday, September 5, and will continue till Friday. The first day requires a lot of manpower to lift and transport the cart to the construction site, so this day is most vital for extra help.

5\) Security—(Sept. 5—9) After cart construction begins on Monday, 2 men are required stay and guard the cart each night for the rest of the week. Since this is done in shifts (one person sleeps while the other stands guard) it is essential to get outside help for this.

6\) Prasadam distribution—(Sept. 9—10) Last year we distributed between 3, 000-4, 000 plates of prashadaam.

7\) Set up of Festival Site—This begins around midnight on the night of the 8th and goes through the following morning until approx. 10: 00 AM. We also need security guards to keep an eye out while setting up the paraphernalia.

8\) Clean-up—This begins around 5: 30 PM on the 10th of September. If you’re already there for the event, please stay on to help for this part. It doesn’t take long, but it takes a lot of helping hands.

9\) Cart disassembly—This takes place during the same time as the clean-up. After a long day at the festival, it’s hard to find help for this essential service. The city requires the cart to be completely removed by morning. Rather than pulling it back 2 miles to the construction site, it’s dismantled on the Common and loaded into a truck.

10\) Accommodations—There are some devotees coming from out of town, some with families who require temporary accommodations. If you can open your home for one or two nights, that will enable more people from outlying areas to come and participate.

Ratha-yatra: The Lord of the Universe beckons

On September 9-10 we will be celebrating Boston’s 11th Annual *Ratha-yatra* (Festival of the Chariots). This ecstatic festival is a very ancient tradition in Eastern India, as old as recorded history, and yet its observance never becomes obsolete or irrelevant.

As eternal spirit souls who are presently imprisoned in material bodies of this transient realm, our unfortunate condition is that we generally forget the fact that a loving relationship with t he Supreme Being is always available to us. Krishna is so unlimitedly kind, however, that He personally incarnates in various attractive ways, thus reminding us of our eternal opportunity to reciprocate His unbounded love.

One such incarnation is Lord Jagannathaa. Literally translated, jagan means “universe” and natha means “Lord”.

(Encaged in our bodily tabernacle, we cannot see the eternal form of God. With these eyes composed of matter, it is not possible for us to actually envision forms, which lie beyond this mundane world. Therefore Krishna, out of compassion for our fallen condition, agrees to come in a form which we can perceive with our present eyes. In Sanskrit language this form is called *daru-brahma, or* the Supreme Spirit manifest in a form made of wood. ) Krishna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and because He is Jagannathaa, or the Lord of the Universe, He has the omnipotence to manifest Himself, partially or fully, through any of His multifarious energies. We may differentiate between material and spiritual energies, but He is the absolute controller of both. This transcendental form of the Supreme Lord is eternally situated at Jagannathaa Puri (the city of Jagannathaa) in East India, along with His brother, Balarama, and sister, Subhadra.

Each year Their Lordships (Jagannathaa, Baladeva, and Subhadra) personally come out of the temple to extend a very special chance for all His devotees to take part in His loving pastimes. In the *Bhagavad-gita* (Ch. 9, verse 29) it is stated:

In the same way, the *Jagannathaa Ratha-yatra* is also designed by the Lord to bring us off the *karmic,* mundane platform of this material world. This is the manifestation of the Lord’s special mercy to His devotees. Let us all, therefore, enthusiastically cooperate to glorify and serve Lord Jagannathaa, thus transforming the quarrel and hypocrisy of modern society into *Vaikuntha,* the spiritual world, where there is no anxiety, but where the eternal knowledge and bliss of Krishna consciousness predominate.

—Note—

The following letter was written to a former Temple President, who under great stress from his responsibilities, withdrew from his services to recuperate without notifying anyone of his intentions. As you can see from my letter, I felt a lot of affection for this devotee. By Krishna’s grace, this devotee later returned and continued associating and serving with the devotees in this temple.

## Letter #32 (NS—&gt;) September 20, 1988

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. After having a couple of days to analyze the situation that you have been subjected to, it has become increasingly apparent to me why you needed a break from the strains of both temple and restaurant management. Ultimately, how you left is really of no concern to me. It only makes it all the more obvious that something should have been done to ease your burden, and in some sense I must accept some of that responsibility.

But, my reason for writing is not just born from a sense of duty. Sometimes the word “duty” seems to imply an obligation towards someone without their personal interest at heart; a sort of “must” from a “higher authority”. That’s there to some small degree, with Prabhupada and Krishna’s expectations of me forcing me to reach out to you. But even more than that, my affection and appreciation for the personality “ dasa” compels me to convey some of my thoughts with the hope that maybe you will feel inclined to reciprocate my concern with a phone call or letter. I trust that you can appreciate that this concern is not something that has not been artificially conjured up by the loss of ...’s temple president. I can honestly say that my involvement with the temple since after my first couple of visits last year, was born just as much from a desire to help you with your managerial struggles, as it was from “higher authorities”.

Within the past couple of days, I have witnessed a gradual unfolding of both your external and internal struggles. Externally, I have seen someone who desperately started a lucrative business to offset the lack of income to the temple, and to relieve his godbrother from his burden of collecting thousands of dollars a week by an unpalatable means. How you spoon-fed the money into the temple while simultaneously expanding the business is practically incomprehensible to my feeble mind. And during this unfolding process, as more temple debts were revealed I became awestruck at the stamina you manifested for as long as you did. I know fully well, that these debts were not a result of your mismanagement.

I’m also fully confident that you must have wanted to improve the legal standard of the restaurant, but how could you do it right away? You had to generate Lakshmi immediately and you did everything within your means and inclinations to do so. In time, these things would have been tended to, but for the time being, so much Lakshmi is needed—and besides..., who is going to get it? The Indian community has been much too unreliable.

Then there was *Ratha-yatra.* Again, no small undertaking. Thousands of dollars needed, and you were already juggling so many responsibilities. It wouldn’t have been as bad, if there could have been more cooperation from the devotees, but that was limited, too. Your health was again deteriorating, and your leg had not time whatsoever to heal from the surgery. And, I'm sure that the incident with didn’t help either.

Internally, I’ve learned more about the dedicated and honorable ... who left everything behind, (even his toothbrush), to improve his Krishna consciousness and his health. How can you think and remember Krishna against such overwhelming tests? And you can’t even take the time to take part in the most integral programs designed to help you to remember Krishna. (Of course, that sacrifice could be seen as too much, but your motives for doing so were unquestionable. You were trying to think of your service, but the tendency is always there to forget who we are doing it for. )

But I'm not going to try to venture too deep into your internal struggles, both then and now. Now your internal struggles are between you and the Lord in your heart; and remembrance of the instructions from your spiritual master. Ultimately, your spiritual master wants you to be Krishna conscious, and all his instructions are meant to culminate in that goal. I remember Prabhupada saying in one lecture, “So sometimes it is understood that if it is too difficult for us to do a particular service, then, under the direction of a spiritual master we can do another service, and that service is equally accepted by Krishna” \[paraphrased for lack of the exact quote\]. Your spiritual master will never reject you for giving up a particular service, but that you are serving Krishna is his ultimate concern. Other devotees within whom you have faith that they are representing Krishna can also provide guidance to help you become rightly situated.

I'm confident that, in time, you will follow the right direction from the Lord in the heart to come back into the association of devotees. But when you finally make that decision, who will you approach so that you can be best situated? I fear that you may be wary to immediately approach Maharaja, for a variety of reasons, although certainly you will at some point. If that is the case, then please seek out the association of someone you trust, and confide in him.

Please consider my humble appeal. If I can help in any way, I can be immediately available to speak with you from wherever I am. We can also get together, if you like. I would arrange to bring you wherever I happen to be at that time. I would also arrange to pay for your travel expense, so don’t let that concern you.

My itinerary is as follows:

9/22-9/23—book distribution party in Western Mass. (Sunset View Campground in Monson, Mass.)

9/24—Boston temple

9/25—Flartford temple

9/26 (4: 00 PM on) Rockville, MD \[(301)762-1864\]

From there I will be driving to Dallas, Texas for the North American GBC Meetings. It would be great to have your association for the long drive.

9/30-10/I Dallas temple (meetings)

10/2 in transit from Dallas to Atlanta temple

10/3-10/4 Atlanta temple

10/5 in transit to Washington DC

10/6—10/7 several places in Washington area. You could try 301 762—1864 and ask for Henry (Agrahya dasa). He may know how to get in touch with me.

10/7-10/8 in transit to Boston

10/8-10/11 Boston

Beyond that I will be traveling to Toronto, Ottawa and back to Montreal but the dates are not yet certain.

## Letter #33 (NS—&gt;) October 5, 1988

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

First I wanted to offer my deepest apologies for not responding to your letter sooner. My life is such that it seems that I can’t prioritize my activities sometimes the way I would like to. I’ve wanted to answer your letter since *Ratha-yatra* was over, (about 3 weeks ago) but not until now have I had the time to do so. Even now, I am writing this letter in Gaithersburg, MD, in the house of a devotee that I stopped to visit on my way back from Dallas. While he’s working on something for me on his computer, I'm grabbing the time to start my first of 20 letters that need to be written ASAP.

Anyway, with that explanation now behind me, I do want to say that I'm glad that you have taken the time to these events in your life to me. This is a genuine Vaishnava exchange (to reveal one’s mind in confidence), and I am honored that you have some trust in me. I hope that I can fulfill my side by offering some Krishna conscious association in return. I am also expecting Swami to be visiting here late November or early December, and I will let him read your letter, I’ll encourage him to contact you as well.

Although your letter did not really seem to solicit any type of response, I feel compelled to respond to some of the points that you have made; especially since your complaints were presented so humbly. It’s a fact that the humility that one experiences from *Sankirtana* does bear a permanent and positive impression upon us.

As in any experience, your complaints can be seen from different angles of vision. There are usually internal and external considerations of all matters, but sometimes it’s hard to differentiate between them; especially when one is not, as you say, “following the principles so strictly”. It is a fact that one’s perspective is affected by “pouring fuel on the fire”, so to speak, otherwise known as indulging in different forms of sense gratification. But even though there is some effect, because you’re sincere and not offensive, theoretically you can understand that spiritually there was no loss for your surrender in Krishna consciousness.

In the first Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam, there is a verse: “One who has forsaken his material occupation to engage in the service of the Lord may fall down in an immature stage, yet there is no danger of his being unsuccessful. On the other hand, a nondevotee, though fully engaged in his occupational duty, does not gain anything. ” Internally, it can be understood that Krishna will never forget the sacrifices you made for Him, and therefore your ultimate success in spiritual life is assured. In that sense you should not feel some remorse that Krishna stripped everything away so quickly. You do have minute independence to choose, even though it may seem that the devotees back then gave you very little room to make the choice to surrender to Krishna. You could have gone back to classes on the following Monday, but there was something that drew you to take fully to Krishna consciousness instead. That is because it is natural experience for the soul to be attracted to serving and loving Krishna.

Externally, it now seems that if you would have continued in your musical pursuits, you could have gotten the training you now desire, and would not feel so dissatisfied. Yes, you may have gotten the training that you so much want. And if you were properly encouraged, it seems as though, you could have pursued it without feeling so guilty about not surrendering to Krishna. However, you must still know that the “satisfaction” you’re now wanting is not necessarily available simply by meeting those criteria. That’s the strength of the illusory potency. We are always thinking that “If I only did it this way”, or “If it only happened that way”, etc, “then I wouldn’t feel the way that I'm feeling now”. That’s the illusion! This is called bhoga/tyaga, or sense gratification and artificial renunciation. Real satisfaction can’t come from material arrangements; even if we get what we want!

But, again, on the external platform, there is another angle that I have increasingly come to realize, and which could apply to your circumstance. That is, that one should take fully to the devotional path (i. e. forsake his material occupation, etc. ) voluntarily. Not that you didn’t, but it seems that there was a lot of pressure. One’s intelligence should be fully satisfied that “I want to surrender to Krishna, and I am doing this because I want to, and not because he (whoever that ‘he’ may be) is forcing me to. ” Arjuna voluntarily surrendered to Krishna. Krishna instructed him accordingly, and then Lord Krishna concluded by saying to Arjuna, “Thus I have explained to you knowledge still more confidential. Deliberate on this fully, and then do what you wish to do. ” He enlightened Arjuna with knowledge, and thus there was no question in Arjuna’s mind as to the proper course of action.

I can’t say whether or not that was how Prabhu convinced you to stay and live in the temple. But I personally feel that it is much better that when a person gives up everything, he should come to that conclusion himself once having sufficiently tested the philosophy and its application. In other words, it should be more on the strength of his own philosophical convictions, and not just by application of pressure from Krishna’s representatives. Of course that conviction also comes through the association of devotees. Had you associated a lot more before voluntarily giving everything up, things may have been different. Maybe you would have never even done it, but that is the risk a preacher must take. He must elicit voluntary surrender to Krishna by his words and by his own exemplary behavior. That’s how Srila Prabhupada was so effective in inducing others to take to the process so quickly. But you did not even get much of his association from even his books, before making your decision.

But the best realization to come to is that even now, Krishna has not forgotten the service you rendered. You are an initiated disciple of Srila Prabhupada, and Krishna may just not let you become so successful materially. It’s completely up to Him whether or not you can become successful.

As far as no one giving you any real direction, help or time, I can fully sympathize with your complaint. You should not be led to believe that you were simply some expendable commodity, and now that you don’t produce anything for the interest of the society, there’s no need to pay any attention. I don’t feel that way at all about any of my godbrothers who gave any portion of their lives to Srila Prabhupada’s mission. But sometimes you must also understand that it is not so cut and dry as far as the direction you’re asking for goes. It’s one thing when you have complete faith in the person you’re submitting yourself to. But it’s quite another thing, when one’s faith isn’t so strong. I trust that you have respect for... Maharaja and myself as you say that you do. I hope that my actions and participation in this glorious movement will continue to be a source of inspiration for you for so many years to come. But I am not so inclined to present anything so conclusively to someone who is in a situation like yours. Rather, I strongly feel that it is much more appropriate to provide a broad picture of all options that one has available so that he can decide for himself what his best course of action is. By trying to keep the most Krishna conscious perspective possible one can gradually rule out various options, leaving the most suitable position remaining, for both service to Krishna and one’s psychophysical needs Then one can more determinedly proceed with full confidence in Krishna.

Because our Krishna consciousness movement is still in such a fledgling stage, there is a great lack of fulfilling one’s cultural needs within the ISKCON umbrella. It seems as though it will take much time, and maybe even lifetimes before our society can come to that.

You are not alone in that regard. But in the meantime, what to do? That is the question.

I honestly feel that the niche that you’re looking for within the context of ISKCON, is just not available. And neither does it seem as though ISKCON is in a position (both spiritually and materially) to facilitate your entrance into college to become a composer. (Not that I'm implying that you are expecting that) However, ISKCON should always be in a position to provide Krishna conscious association for anyone desiring to have that. Srila Prabhupada elaborately explains in the *Upadeshamrita* that that was his primary purpose for starting ISKCON. In that sense, I don’t see any problem whatsoever, with your desire to enter into college, providing you continue to fan your desire to associate with devotees in Krishna consciousness; and, providing that there are devotees who are in a position to provide you with Krishna conscious association. Our numbers are not expanding so rapidly, but nonetheless, I strongly feel that that condition is still readily available through a variety of mediums.

I really haven’t provided a broad spectrum of options within this letter, primarily because I feel that discussion may be a more suitable medium for that. But on the other hand, I really have not provided anything definitive for you either, except that your desire to go to college is not bad! There are many devotees who are making similar pursuits, in so many fields. They are also hoping that, in time, they can suitably employ their developed skills in the service of the Lord, if not directly within the context of ISKCON, at least by using their socially advanced positions to influence others towards Krishna conscious objectives in life.

So I hope this letter will be of some help for you. Please don’t hesitate to contact me again, by either mail or phone. I must forewarn you, though, that my travel schedule is such that it will probably take some time before your letter catches up to me, what to speak of the circumstances that I described at the beginning of this letter. But I’ll try by best not to dally with the response.

Hoping this finds you in good health and Krishna conscious spirits.

## Letter #34 (NS—&gt;) October 7, 1988

Please accept my blessings. All glories to His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada.

I have received your letter of 9/9/88 and have noted the contents with great attention.

Please be assured that I have no doubts whatsoever of your sincerity and intentions. In a sense, I do agree that I have not been providing sufficient attention to you, but it is by no means for the reasons you have suggested in your letter. Because you are humble, you are naturally thinking that maybe you did something to cause my lack of attention.

Just yesterday, I was speaking with a godbrother in defense of another godbrother of mine about whom this first godbrother was presenting a complaint. I was defending this other devotee, by describing how sometimes when one gets a little bit overextended, we tend to not be able to give the same attention to those who we previously gave more attention to. I was describing how I found this lamentable. I went on to explain that when our field of responsibilities are smaller, we are able to give attention (or in other words, association) to those who are requiring it, before their expressed need arises. This is always superior because others can more readily appreciate that we are always maintaining their best interests by anticipating their needs, whereas the lesser position is to have to always hear it from them first. In such a predicament, we tend to rely upon mediums, or other persons, by which we can serve a greater number of devotees, which sometimes breaks down in that it may seem to create a sense of impersonalism. In a spiritual movement, that should not happen, but nonetheless many deficiencies may arise in the process.

To some extent that is also happening with me in that my responsibilities have so quickly expanded that I have tended to rely upon other persons to help me in my attempts to give attention to others. In your case, to some extent, I have relied upon to work with you, and guide you in your service—and to provide me with anticipated needs for yourself. Not that that is an impersonal system. But unfortunately, it could be perceived in a way that you are now describing.

I don’t like being overextended to the point that I have to prioritize my time in such a way that I can’t speak with you on the phone when you call. But, nonetheless, it seems as though I have done that, and for that I am sorry.

Just imagine how wonderful Lord Krishna is that He can provide attention to each and every one of His *bhaktas* in such a way that they are thinking that they are getting Krishna’s exclusive attention. Only Krishna can fulfill that with all living entities throughout His whole creation. But as we become more pure, we can become a more suitable medium by which we can extend more and more of Krishna’s mercy to others. Of course, we can never fill the same position as Krishna. Krishna is always The Supreme Personality of Godhead, and we are always his tiny servants. But nonetheless, He can empower His devotee’s to accomplish such wonderful feats, far beyond our expectations. I pray that, in time, I can become such a suitable instrument for Him whereby I can more effectively distribute His mercy to others.

In the meantime, I am finding that the best medium to most effectively communicate with as many devotees as possible is through correspondence. To be honest, you can ask anyone that knows me well, and they’ll tell you that I tend to shy away from phone conversations. I try to rely on them for only immediate practical concerns, because I find them much more impersonal than written correspondence. I very rarely initiate long phone conversations. Other than that, we can associate at other times when I am in the Boston area. I have already expressed this desire to you on several occasions, but unfortunately, it never seems to come about. Maybe I can try harder to rectify that.

Please continue with your steady service, and feel free to write to me whenever to find the need. But, you must also understand it may take a little time to get a response. Sometimes I don’t see may mail for weeks after it has arrived in Boston, and even them sometimes it takes several more weeks before I can get chance to sit down and answer the letters, such as this letter that I am writing right now. I had to stop over in Hartford, just to go off to some secluded room to catch up with some of my correspondence.

Hoping this meets you in good health and enthusiastic Krishna conscious spirits.

## Letter #35 (NS—&gt;Karen) December 19, 1988

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I received your letter (undated) around the first of this month, and read it thoroughly that same day. I am sorry that I have not been able to reply until now, but, as you may already be aware of, December is usually an extremely busy month for me. It’s the time of year when I set aside many of my other responsibilities, so that I can devote as much attention as possible to assisting the book distribution party during the marathon. Therefore, it has been difficult for me to take the time to give the proper attention to the points you raised in your letter. I hope that this has not disappointed you, but I like to be able to give full attention when answering my correspondence. And sometimes that means that I have to wait for the right opportunity to do so; which is something you should be aware of for the future.

I understand that you have revealed much of your past to me because you feel some trust that I will not abuse this information, but instead will use it to give you personal guidance in service to Krishna. You’re right that you must approach someone you trust, and seek direction how to overcome the obstacles that await you on your path back to Godhead. And you’re also right to not give in to pressure to surrender to someone that you do not have even that level of trust in. Trust will increase to the level of surrender, only by applying instructions given, both in lectures and letters, from whomever you approach to cultivate that relationship with. Krishna reveals Himself by our surrender to Him, through the instructions of guru, and that revelation is needed to increase our faith in the person who acts as the transparent medium in disciplic succession.

So you are wanting to accept shiksha from me, and it is my duty to give you whatever guidance I can in your efforts to advance in Krishna consciousness. Actually, I really have no qualification for doing so accept that I am trying to please Srila Prabhupada by following his instructions.

In your letter I also appreciated your analysis of material relationships, and I can understand that the frustration you experienced with those relationships has helped you to seek shelter from Krishna. It seems that, in one sense, He appeared to you through Mother Isha, who took so much care to nurture your devotional creeper in your beginnings with Krishna consciousness. She is a very kind soul who’s always preaching about Krishna. I often think of what a wonderful, Krishna conscious example she is, and I feel proud to have such a godsister like her. It was her kindness that enabled you to see how superior it is to associate with devotees, and for that you should feel eternally indebted.

As far as your difficulties with Prabhu are concerned, it seems as though now there is no need to get into the details of this. Accept it that Krishna has now made another arrangement for you to take direction from Prabhu. I have great confidence in him, and I expect that you will now feel sufficiently encouraged to continue to serve Sri Sri Radha Gopiballava by performing duties within the temple and developing your artistic skills. I also expect that you will accept him as an authority and that means that you may have to sometimes do something that you may not feel so inclined to do, but because you already have some confidence in him, you will follow his instructions. Without submission, it is very difficult to make progress. And for anyone who approaches me for spiritual guidance, I always insist that they take instruction from their immediate authorities, although I am always open to hear if there some problem arises with that.

Regarding your artistic pursuits, I fully approve of this, providing that the temple has a facility available for your use, and that you try to find some means (in consultation with Prabhu) for covering any major expenditures that may arise for your projects. I am always aware of the lack of space for peripheral projects in the Boston temple and sometimes it’s hard to provide space for anything other than immediate administration, *pujari* services, and *ashrama* space. I am assuming that since you mentioned that you have already been working on your art in the Boston temple for some time, that this is not a problem. I would also be happy to see your painting when it’s done, and I would also like to hear from you how you would like to see it used in Krishna’s service.

I hope that this letter meets you well and enthused in Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #36 (NS—&gt;) December 19, 1988

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have the letter you gave me in Philadelphia in front of me, and I will attempt to answer each of your questions in the order you asked them.

First, though, I will say that it seems as though Krishna has made a different arrangement for you in the Boston temple, which may change your whole outlook for your immediate future there. By now, I am sure that you have been told that Prabhu is going to be your immediate authority, and if you have not heard this yet, I am sure that you will in the very near future. But although there has been some adjustment, I still strongly feel that every devotee has something to learn from every experience they have in Krishna consciousness. Therefore, I will address your inquiries with that theme in mind, so that hopefully you will learn something from all of this, and will not foolishly think that the whole problem existed due to dealings and nothing else.

You asked if it is your own inability to accept instruction that makes you rebellious. It’s a fact that having been cheated for so many lifetimes, it’s hard to surrender to an authority. But it’s not so cut and dry that that is the only reason why we do not surrender. We should only rebel towards a superior devotee if we are asked to do something, which is against devotional principles. If we were really Krishna conscious we would never feel inclined to rebel no matter how we were dealt with. I have often referred to several examples from the Bhagavatam to elaborate this point, but one of the most profound examples is the verse spoken by Shamika Rishi in regards to the behavior of Maharaja Parikshit.

*tiraskrta vipralabdhah*  
*saptah ksipta hata api*  
*nasya tat pratikurvanti*  
*tad-bhaktah prabhavo 'pi hi*

“The devotees of the Lord are so forbearing that even though they are defamed, cheated, cursed, disturbed, neglected or even killed, they are never inclined to avenge themselves. ”

This verse refers to mistreatment from anyone, what to speak of an apparent mistreatment from another devotee. You may think that this is a platform, which is beyond your means to ascend to, as you cited in the beginning of your letter, but nonetheless we must aspire to develop these qualities. If we always justify everything by placing the full blame upon others, we will never be able to come to that platform, although it can be achieved by any sincere devotee.

You asked, “How can someone continue to be enthusiastic about developing personal relationships—even with devotees, when it seems every interaction is an offensive mistake, performed with intention? ” I'm sorry but your question is unclear. An “interaction” implies that at least two people are involved. Are you referring to your offensive mistake, or the offensive mistake of another upon you? Assuming you mean the latter, then you need an adjustment of vision. It may seem that way to one who does not see Krishna. But for a devotee, he learns to see Krishna in all of his dealings. Of course, it is difficult to become Krishna conscious if we don’t surrender to Krishna, but our surrender to Him comes by accepting a *guru.* In one letter, Srila Prabhupada explains this point very succinctly:

(Letter to Jadurani)

“In the *shastras* it is said that Spiritual advance of life is achieved thru the combined mercy of the Spiritual Master and Krishna. Those who are sincere souls, Krishna helps them to have a bona fide Spiritual Master, and the bona fide Spiritual Master helps the disciple approach Krishna. This is the process. ”

And in one other letter he further elaborates on this point:

(Letter to Mahapurusha)

“To stick to the principles advised by the spiritual master and serve Krishna under the direction of the spiritual master is the only hope of our advancing in Krishna consciousness. The Spiritual Master and Krishna are two parallel lines. You have to make progress on these two parallel lines, you cannot avoid one in preference of the other. The train, on two tracks, moves forward. The Spiritual Master and Krishna are like these two tracks, they must be served simultaneously. Krishna helps one to find bona fide Spiritual Master, and the bona fide Spiritual Master helps one to understand Krishna.

If one does not get bona fide spiritual master, then how he can ever understand Krishna? You cannot serve Krishna without spiritual master, or serve just spiritual master without serving Krishna. They must be served simultaneously. ”

You asked how you can avoid fault-finding when you don’t see good examples from older devotees. I must once again point out a defect in your vision. There are so many wonderful examples of surrender in the Boston temple, but you have to develop the eyes to see them. Unfortunately we sometimes develop the tendency to see only the faults in others; especially those who are in a position to give us immediate instruction. By focusing on the faults, it becomes easier to justify our not having to surrender to their instructions. It’s easy to find faults in anyone if that’s what we’re looking for, but if we develop the habit of looking for the good qualities instead of the faults, Krishna will give us the vision to see them.

You ask how you can serve me in a way that would be pleasing to the temple authorities. My response would be by simply doing whatever you are asked to do by them. I am always concerned that things are harmonious in the Boston temple. There is so much potential there for spreading Krishna consciousness by providing a harmonious atmosphere for others to see. There are so many things that need to be done for the service of Sri Sri Radha Gopiballava and when the devotees are ready to do the needful for Them, this increases that harmonious atmosphere. The services that you are already performing are quite satisfying to me; especially when they’re performed in full consciousness of Sri Sri Radha Gopiballava. Your desire to more intimately serve Them at some point in the future is also a very nice meditation, and is, by no means, a sign of laziness.

But you are also right that by distributing Srila Prabhupada’s books, I can be pleased. This was Srila Prabhupada’s foremost desire, and I am always anxious to fulfill it by enthusing others to do it. But now is not the right time for you to take this up. I strongly feel, though, that everyone should learn how to do it at some time in their devotional career, for at least some small period of time. But it shouldn’t be disruptive to the services required for the Deities. And it seems as though you are already depended upon for so many of these services. Also there should be a suitable arrangement for you to get training, and that is not available at this time either. But if you find a little time when you could go out someplace nearby and just attempt to distribute a few books, this would be nice. But don’t do it independently. Do this only in consultation with Prabhu, and only do it if he feels that it can be done. If he determines that at this time it is best that you just don’t do it at all, then I completely accept his judgment, and there is no need to report this information back to me.

I hope that my answers to your questions have helped clear up your bewilderment.

Hoping this meets you well and enthused in service to Sri Sri Radha Gopiballava.

## Letter—1995

## Letter #37 (NS—&gt;) Moscow, Russia. September 26, 1995

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter which was forwarded to me by dasa, and tried my best to understand the contents.

Usually dasa’s translations are understandable to me. However, his translation of your letter left me feeling uncertain of its content. But I’ll try my best to address the concerns you’ve placed before me.

If you want to act in an authoritative way, then you must determine for yourself from where you want to get that authority. The material world is full of so many authorities, and when one loses all sense of being a servant of Krishna, then one must become a servant to so many so-called authorities.

Friends, family, society, our bodies, senses and mind—they all become authorities, but authorities that can never be satisfied. Satisfaction can come only when we become submissive to Krishna’s authority. You need a good definition of the authority which you must follow.

You say that you want to be my servant. Then it seems that you are approaching me as an authority. It’s a fact, the spiritual master must be our primary authority in life, otherwise, we have no other hope to establish our connection with Krishna’s authority.

A grihastha should accept some honest and simple means of livelihood. He does not want to accumulate fortunes, but works as a matter of duty, accepting whatever Krishna provides. If Krishna sends fortunes, he accepts it and uses it for the service of Krishna. If Krishna sends practically nothing, a devotee continues to execute his duties, always faithful that Krishna knows what is needed. There is no need to worship the goddess of fortune independently from your worship of Krishna. Lakshmidevi is always found with Narayana. You are..., meaning a servant of Lord Narayana and Lord Narayana is worshiped by offering the fruits of one’s labor for His satisfaction. Moreover, as stated in Srimad-Bhagavatam 4. 30. 36, “The Supreme Lord, Narayana, is present among devotees who are engaged in hearing and chanting the holy name of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Lord Narayana is the ultimate goal of sannyasis, those in the renounced order of life, and Narayana is worshiped through this Sankirtana Movement by those who are liberated from material contamination. Indeed, they recite the holy name again and again. ” Therefore you must also satisfy Narayana by taking part in congregational chanting, as well as personally reciting His holy name again and again.

I had therefore previously encouraged your proposal to work in the bakery, an honest means of livelihood by which you could support yourself and family as a grihastha. Moreover, I had recommended that you maintain regular gatherings with other devotees who worked in the bakery by which you regularly hear and chant about Krishna with like-minded devotees. For whatever reason, these things did not manifest, and now you still have no means of livelihood, and no regular attendance to the established programs for increasing your Krishna consciousness.

However, because you have grihastha responsibility, I cannot give you exact detailed instructions about what to do to support a family. I can only give you guidelines, which is what I’ve tried to give in this letter. If you study Srila Prabhupada’s life in the Prabhupada Lilamrita, you will see that he did not abandon his support to his family, until he actually took sannyasa in 1958. He received the order from his spiritual master in 1922, during their first meeting. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta did not get involved with Srila Prabhupada’s household affairs. He simply gave him inspiration and orders to preach the message of Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

You are spiritually very weak, and this is primarily due to lack of strong association. It is for this reason that you seem to tenaciously cling to the association of You had very little to say about your devotee association, but you had so much more to say about I don’t want to speak critically about him, especially since all I know of him is what you’ve told me in the letter. But the fact that you took so much effort to expand his glories, leads me to believe that you were trying to convince me that his association was good. But a serious devotee will establish the criteria of what’s “good” and what’s “bad” based upon what’s good for his service to Krishna.

Anakulyasya sankalpah, pratikulyasya varjanam. A devotee must accept everything favorable for the execution of devotional service and he must reject everything unfavorable. This should be the criteria for everything that we do. But despite all this association, you still have not been able to determine your duty and service to Krishna. I am therefore trying to give some definition to your duties, and based on this definition, you MUST then base your association on whether or not it directly supports you in these duties or not.

In your weakened state, you do not have the strength to vigorously preach Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s message beyond the execution of your duties as a grihastha. Therefore, seek strength in the association of those who are fixed in Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mission—not in all of the extrasensory nonsense. This will not bring you any closer in your service to Krishna, but will only distract you. Do not get so entangled in a means of livelihood that you become distracted from the primary goal of life and forget Krishna. Live simply, honestly, and use whatever available time that you have for increasing your Krishna consciousness. This will make you happy, your spiritual master happy, and ultimately you will make progress in fulfilling the topmost perfection in the execution of all your duties—you will satisfy Krishna.

This letter addresses all three of your concerns in a clear and concise way.

Please take this matter seriously.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letters—1996

## Letter #38 (NS—&gt;dd) Hartford. July 31, 1996

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

First, before I begin answering your letter dated June 13, 1996, I thought that I should tell you that I do not see any reference in this letter to the question you asked me in Gita Nagari. You had asked how to develop the feeling that your spiritual master saved you, in a similar way to the way Srila Prabhupada referred to this point to his disciples. This letter of June 13 has no such question even similar to this point. I am therefore thinking that its possible that you had sent me another letter that I never received. If so, you should know that I never received it.

Nonetheless, to answer your question... one must have enough appreciation that one has actually been saved in order to feel appreciation for the person who was instrumental for the saving. What does it mean to be saved? It means to have the opportunity to engage in devotional service. Devotional service is beyond being saved, just as we so oftentimes hear that it is beyond liberation.

You may have so many duties to perform, but who is it that has brought you to the point where you have been able to realize (or at least catch a glimpse) that the highest duties in life are those aimed for the satisfaction of Krishna.

Even though you may oftentimes forget about this, still, having once contacted Krishna through devotional service, ultimately you can never forget. Devotional service is so powerful that even one moments engagement in devotional service can open the door for eternal engagement. That person who has inspired us for devotional service is therefore our savior. That person who is meditating on how to bring us to the lotus feet of Krishna is our savior. That person who is giving us the opportunity for devotional service is our savior. Without his blessings we have no hope for devotional service. Therefore, when we actually catch a glimpse of the glories of devotional service, we can begin to appreciate how wonderful is that person who is giving us the opportunity to engage in devotional service. Without the mercy of a devotee we can never understand devotional service.

Therefore, although I am emphasizing the need to accept your duties related to family, beyond that I am always impressing the need to maintain a consciousness of Krishna. This duty lies beyond all other duties—beyond your marriage, beyond..., and beyond, as you say, maintaining your sanity. Krishna will maintain your sanity. Without Krishna consciousness, then all other duties are simply a waste of time. You must become just like the woman who is always thinking of her paramour. She is attentive to her duties, but in her heart she is thinking of her lover. Similarly, you must develop the art of remembering Krishna. That is the best way to maintain your sanity. If you try to maintain your sanity without Krishna, then you will be destined to failure. It is simply not possible. When the pressure builds up, take shelter in any one of the nine processes of devotional service. Sanity will automatically come. If you take shelter in something artificial, then it wont lead to sanity. Things may seem to get better, but that is only an illusion.

## Letter #39 (NS—&gt;) October 11, 1996

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

To become a better devotee does not necessarily mean that we must only follow the rules and regulations. That can certainly help us to become a better a devotee, but it doesn’t mean that Krishna conscious. This a far better display of devotion aimed to capture the attention of the Lord, than a forced appearance in the temple, executed without the conscious effort to remember Krishna.

Of course, by saying all this, it is not my intention to simply give you a justification for “not” going to the temple. Certainly, every visit to the temple you make, when performed in the proper consciousness will only serve to help you become a better devotee. But, the point that I am simply emphasizing is that that visit to the temple is not an end in of itself. It is not the goal. Krishna is the goal *(vasudeva para gatih),* and He can be achieved only by those who cultivate Krishna consciousness under the guidance of the Lord’s devotees.

Therefore, please take advantage of these points to enhance your day to day “cultivation” of Krishna consciousness, and be assured that Krishna will help you.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #40 (NS—&gt;) Vrindavana ISKCON. October 18, 1996

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have been carrying your letter dated May 5, 1996 with me ever since I arrived in the United States at the end of July. Now that I'm taking some time in Vrindavana for increasing my hearing and chanting, I'm also taking the time to pull out all the letters I’ve accumulated over the past couple of months.

I'm sorry to hear that the vehicle you are using for *Sankirtana* was destroyed in an accident, but nonetheless I am relieved that it was only the automobile and not the passengers within. As you may know, last month we lost two Moscow devotees when their explains, is interested to hear. He never abandons hearing because there is too much other service. Hearing also means learning the philosophy and developing one’s power to distinguish between what is devotional service and what is service mixed with *karma or jnana.* First one must become situated properly in ones prescribed duty, and then from that platform make progress. Not that we just do “any” service. We must perform our prescribed duty and never abandon this hearing, chanting, and remembering which is our most important duty in all stages of life.

So these are a few relevant instructions given with the hope that you will now make further progress.

I hope that this letter meets you and your husband in good health.

## Letter #41 (—&gt;NS) May 12, 1996

My dear older God-brother acting as GBC and/or guru's:

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada who brought the Holy Names and Srimad- Bhagavatam around the world like some transcendental warrior. All glories to you who are following in his footsteps.

Most of you don’t know me from a hole in the wall, and thus I hesitate to write. What can I who am like a fig say to you who have been boldly taking up the forward charge on the front lines of Prabhupada’s Divine Society? I have always acted as an assistant and it is in this mood I write today.

I think Ravindra Svarupa Prabhu, Niranjana Swami, and Romapada Swami have the misfortune of knowing me. I was initiated by Srila Prabhupada in 1976 and mostly served in his temples as *pujari/cook,* moving out with a family in 1988.

Since then I’ve been actively involved with the temple in Philadelphia, as treasurer, vice-president, acting president, and presently just give regular classes at the temple and local preaching center. Just see Srila Prabhupada’s potency and the potency of the Holy Name, while growing up in my house, relatives would say “There will be no talk of God in this house... ” Just see the potency.

Anyways, I’ve been thinking the past year about something which has come to the point where I feel impelled to write you all. Tonight one devotee was describing needed improvements in a US temple, saying that still the congregation wasn’t receiving proper ministering, and I replied just as I had before on this point, that such “proper ministering” is largely dependent upon devotees of sufficient caliber and training being available to do it. How anything will be properly done without quality of workers, without enthusiasm?

Various Godbrothers have spoken to me about various deficien-cies they see in terms of lacking leadership, lacking enthusiasm, lamenting how the temples often seem like revolving doors whereby interested *bhaktas* come, and then go.
