We as older devotees, as disciples of His Divine Grace, have certainly been trying, and continue to desire to do that which we know should be done, in manifesting Srila Prabhupada’s desires, in manifesting Lord Chaitanya’s desires, and remaining before us there is still an ocean of service left to do.

Prabhu’s, although I think there is some formal movement going on now, please take this as another encouragement that it seems really crucial now, at this stage of our movement, that we reconsider, or deeply consider, Srila Prabhupada’s occasional admonishment and advice to us that we “boil the milk”, that we take as much care, if not more care of the devotee’s already come to the shelter of Krishna, as those who haven’t.

Is it not so that commensurate with the inner strength and spiritual understanding of the resident devotees, there is that much more qualification and ability to carry out all the service so urgently needed to be done in manifesting Lord Chaitanya’s *Sankirtana* Movement? Are we acting sufficiently, in an organized way, to cultivate and nurture that inner strength? What anyone can do if they are not a fixed up? What the devotees will preach without some learning and realization? Who we will attract?

In the very first days of leaving home and going to Calcutta our Guru Maharaj advertised in a local newspaper “Wanted: Young men and women to learn *Bhagavad-gita* and be trained up as preachers. ”

From the very beginning Srila Prabhupada’s immediate and initial desire was to develop a society, a *brahminical* society, of persons fixed up in Krishna’s teachings contained in the Gita. Do we have that now? Is there in place within our temples a systematic study program? With weekly and monthly testing? Are the temple presidents *Bhakti-sastri* qualified? Are we ourselves *Bhakti-sastri, Bhakti-sarvabhauma, Bhakti-Vaibhava, Bhakti-vedanta* certified? Should we not be? Would this not serve to insure the potency, to preserve the strength, to enhance the quality and tenure of our world-wide society?

Our father, our beloved Guru Maharaj has instructed us that books are the basis, is not part of that basis our own studying and learning, inside and out, these Transcendental Books? Have we paid enough attention to this? We wonder. Srila Prabhupada has instructed us that only by carefully reading, following the principles and chanting Hare Krishna will enthusiasm to keep up the austerities of spiritual life be maintained. And yet devotees sometimes appear confused why there are various deficiencies and issues to be dealt within the Society, as if it were some mystery.

As a concerned Uncle I care about my godbrother’s children, I know the various *gurus* are concerned whether their charges are being protected properly from *maya,* whether they are learning that which they need to know to be sheltered from the onslaughts of *kama, lobha, krodha,* etc.

I know the G. B. C. cares, is really striving to protect the Society in general. Maybe it’s time for a renewed effort, in concert, by the G. B. C., *guru's,* and Temple Presidents to further establish another piece of Srila Prabhupada’s program by insuring that the devotees in our care, who have come to Krishna, have the shelter they need we don’t want to see them fall back in the grips of *maya,* to risk yet another miserable birth, we do want to see our movement ever more vibrant and vigorous with ever new found enthusiasm. Some Prabhu’s think we should take care to institute, nicely, but quickly, a firm program along the lines listed below:

1\) Every temple president should be at least *Bhakti-shastri* certi-fied.

2\) A standardized weekly study and testing program for Nectar of Instruction, *Bhagavad-gita* and Nectar of Devotion should be developed and quickly instituted. The VIHE seminars are nice, but do not appear suitable to fulfill this needed purpose alone.

Our Guru Maharaj writes in the preface to NOI that it constitutes the very first instructions of spiritual life, and that by carefully following them a devotee is sure to be successful, yet hardly any of my nephews is knowing Nectar of Instruction!!! Is it any wonder there exist some of the difficulties which go on, on both individual and societal level? Let us reflect what purity, what spiritual strength, what success might we be able to offer Sri *Guru* and *Gauranga* by carefully seeing to the “boiling of the milk”?

3\) A general and concerted effort to promote studying and dis-cussing of Srila Prabhupada’s books, i. e., readings during *prashadaam,* in the *Sankirtana* Vans, in the resident rooms, tapes in the kitchen, in the altar, etc., etc.

4\) We think it would be spiritually fruitful to insist on studying of Srila Prabhupada’s conversations and lectures. Every temple should have a set. Our nephews and nieces, our own selves, will surely benefit carefully hearing and discussing amongst ourselves the exact manner in which Srila Prabhupada preached, answered questions, and dealt with specific topic of discussion, would this not insure purity? Would this not be yet another way to keep Srila Prabhupada right where he belongs in the front of our Movement leading us forward into the 21st Century? How else the devotee will know how to preach? I believe there should by testing, of each devotee, whether they are aware how, generally speaking, Srila Prabhupada answered questions and presented the philosophy.

5\) Do our own temples have sufficient stock of books? Does every temple have available sufficient quantity of Gita’s, NOD’s, NOI’s? A library containing a minimum of all Srila Prabhupada’s transcribed lectures and conversations? Is this not at least an equal priority as distributing books to the non-devotee population? While we distribute a million books this Christmas do our own temples have Srila Prabhupada’s books amply available to the residents? Why not a book marathon to install any and all needed books in our own temples?

I'm convinced, because it is a spiritual goal, our householders and congregation will be very much inspired to financially support such a Society-wide attempt at self-preservation, and all around strengthening.

I think, as do several godbrother’s I’ve spoken to, that this deserves equal attention as distributing a million books, a million plates of *prashada,* etc., etc., as much effort as we place on our internal strength as much as that is fixed up, surely in the long run we cannot estimate how much spiritual potency will be unlocked and practical service made manifest. So much potent preaching and new leadership is sure to manifest as a natural consequence By endeavoring as the spiritual family we are to strengthen and ever-increasingly make manifest the sort of *brahminically,* shastrically qualified society Srila Prabhupada hoped for, from the beginning of his preaching efforts, we will surely invoke great blessings from him, from our glorious Brahma-Madhva-Gaudiya Sampradaya, ultimately from our Lord Sri Krishna Chaitanya Mahaprabhu Himself.

This is not thought to be a “magic” panacea, certainly it is ultimately up to each *jiva* to embrace the means of deliverance being offered, otherwise even the best of efforts to help will fail. Neither is this an attempt to make “*pandits*”, rather it is simply a mood to insure the development of what Srila Prabhupada confidentially desired, more and more, namely a strong, pure, *brahminically* qualified spiritual society that can give direction to the world. That the devotees are properly trained and sheltered.

By continuing to move in this direction we will do good to the world making the members of Prabhupada’s Society ever more qualified to do that which we have been asked to do by him, who struggled so hard, who wrote all the books he did, really just for us, as the pure servant of Bhagavan Sri Krishna, Sri Paratha-sarathi, the Transcendental Speaker of Sri *Bhagavad-gita.*

Hari-Guru Vaishnava-Bhagavad-gita ki-jaya! Srila Prabhupada ki-jaya! ISKCON Gaura Bhakta Vrinda’s ki jaya!

As you Vaishnavas have saved me from a most miserable life and future, I'm just feeling fortunate to be continuing on the path of trying to give back something in return... Hare Krishna.

Offering this at the feet of my older brothers, trying to remember our Father,

P. S. One may ask, and rightly so, what really am I doing? Or am I just another back-seat driver? Well, every opportunity I get, upon meeting devotees, I try and talk something about Srila Prabhupada’s books, and I look for chances to promote the aforementioned ideas. I work for IBM and am a little computer literate so I'm learning to write software programs to develop computer based study modules in what little spare time I have. I'm also developing multimedia publicity and advertisements. Most important, I am trying to slow down a bit and study a little more myself. Let me beg some blessings I may become more engaged in serving the Society as my heart desires...

—Potent quotes—

(Have we sufficiently embraced this advice of Srila Prabhu-pada??? That is what I'm wondering... note the date!!! )

9 May, 1972 (Letter to Rupanuga) Honolulu.

“... Now I want that we shall concentrate on making our devotees Krishna conscious and ourselves becoming Krishna conscious, and not be so much concerned with expanding ourselves widely but without any spiritual content.

Just like boiling the milk, it becomes thicker and sweeter. Now do like that, boil the milk. ”

Hoping this will meet you in good health.

A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami

“If you become conscious of Me, you will pass over all the obstacles of conditioned life by My grace. If, however, you do not work in such consciousness but act through false ego, not hearing Me, you will be lost. ” (Gita 18th Chapter)

CC: Various *sannyasi’s,* temple presidents and senior devotees.

## Letter #42 (NS—&gt;; response to #41) Kiev, Ukraine. December 10, 1996

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhu-pada.

Thank you very much for your letter addressed to many senior leaders throughout ISKCON. I appreciate the content, but even more I appreciate the sincerity with which it was presented.

I suppose, however, that you would feel better if I wrote some assuring words that your concerns “are” being addressed. To some degree, I would say that they actually are. There are numerous discussions going on both the top and secondary levels of ISKCON which directly relate to what you have presented. I am also per-sonally witnessing many active efforts to train up our leaders both philosophically, as well as practically, so that ISKCON can push on into the 21st century with a bright future for not only the devotees within but the nondevotees without. But, I must be honest and admit that, in my humble opinion, we are still falling terribly short of the need. Letters like yours help motivate people like me to take this matter all the more serious, and I thank you for doing this to me.

At this point, I honestly do not have that much time for an in-depth letter on The subject. It’s a very broad topic, and I'm in the middle of too many things.

However, as a final note of encouragement I thought that I would share with you the contents of one letter that I wrote last December to our *nama-hatta* leader in Although there is still a long way to go, by sending you this letter, my hope is that you will get a glimpse that there is at least one more of us “out here” thinking about these concerns than you were aware of when you sat down to write your letter.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #43 (NS—&gt;)

PAMHO. AGTSP.

Thank you very much for your report. It definitely gives me the impression that the program is going steadily.

As far as advice towards improving the *nama-hatta* program is concerned, my main concern for *nama-hatta,* as well as for all temple preaching is that the devotees who are being cared for are being responsibly trained in Krishna conscious philosophy. “Training” is the keyword in practically every discussion I have concerning the preaching in the FSU. And I am expecting more and more that the senior devotees take up this responsibility of training. Naturally I see it as my duty to train also, and whenever I see someone who has potential to be trained, I try to give them whatever I have learned in philosophy and practical management. For instance, I am always preaching to and reminding dasa that his real duty as Temple President is to take care of the spiritual needs of the devotees. Without strong local spiritual leadership, the devotees become weak, they lack philosophical discretion, and they lose their enthusiasm for accepting austerities in service to *guru* and Krishna.

The devotees need to feel cared for, and it’s not just the duty of their spiritual master to give them this care.

Therefore, please always emphasize this point in your own service.

Systematically train the devotees who attend the *nama-hatta* programs in philosophy. Instill within them the urge to learn the philosophy. I strongly recommend that you acquire a course outline of the 3-month *bhakta* training course, and systematically teach the whole course throughout your *nama-hatta* region. When that is finished, you can simultaneously begin a new course for newcomers, along with beginning a more developed course for those who have already gone through the 3-month training course. Then *nama- hatta* can become something very dynamic, as you systematically begin to train devotees as preachers, who, in time, will also expand *nama-hatta* preaching.

We should always be careful to not always be thinking that expansion means to increase the numbers. Expansion also means increasing the Krishna consciousness of those to whom we are preaching. Then our preaching will expand naturally.

Regarding the recommendations for the two *bhaktas* on your program, you can do both. You can test them, and give their recommendations.

I look forward to your continued reporting of *nama-hatta* devel-opments.

## Letters—1997

## Letter #44 (NS—&gt;) Kiev, Ukraine. January 18, 1997

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am now rereading your letter I received on November 18, 1996. With regards to your “teen-age” identity crisis, I seriously believe that it is not as out-of-place as your peers may perceive it. I hope that my disciples can mature enough to realize that Krishna manifests Himself through the association of those devotees who bind Him with love. It is not that just because Srila Prabhupada invoked Krishna’s presence within ISKCON, that He (Krishna) will always remain bound within every aspect of the institution after Prabhupada’s departure. Krishna is independent, and He goes where He pleases. He’s bound by love, but I do not necessarily see that this love pervades the whole institution of ISKCON, and therefore by being connected to any temple listed in Back to Godhead, one is automatically connected to Krishna. Our association and service within ISKCON is meant to give us a vehicle by which we can come in contact with Krishna, but whoever holds that love is He who can give Him to us. Therefore, service to “ISKCON” is not tantamount to service to Krishna. At least I do not see it that way.

Therefore it seems that your awkwardness is due to the fact that you want to preach to please *guru* and Krishna, but you want your preaching to be more than just preaching about the activities within ISKCON. Lord Caitanya has given the order to everyone. “Just tell others about Krishna. Give the instructions given by Krishna in *Bhagavad-gita* and Srimad Bhagavatam. ” Krishna’s instructions are not bound to any institution. They are transcendental to time and space. They are the Absolute Truth. If preaching about ISKCON is making you feel some lack, then you don’t have to do it just for the sake of social pressure. But do not use this justify no preaching at all. There is never any excuse for that. And neither should this be a justification to preach against ISKCON. There may be many problems in ISKCON, but it is still the institution which my spiritual master left for me to serve in, and in my humble estimation it is still the most potent institution on the planet for carrying out Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mission.

Not long ago I had written the following in a message to one godbrother:

“At this point, I am inclined to think that ...’s followers should serve in their own institution, and that they should face their own problems that will inevitably have to be faced when expanding Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mission in institutional form. In that respect, I have no doubt that Srila Prabhupada’s vision, expertise, and training of his followers, by far surpasses anything else on this planet. I hope that doesn’t sound like a vain statement. But it’s based upon my uncompromising faith in Srila Prabhupada’s preaching vision. ”

With all of ISKCON’s faults, I'm still convinced that ISKCON is being watched over by Lord Caitanya and He will protect Srila Prabhupada’s sincere followers. Therefore, I remain a dutiful citizen of ISKCON, although I do not demand that my disciples must see ISKCON and Krishna as nondifferent.

For instance, read the following segment of a letter which was written last year to a disciple in the former Soviet Union.

\*\*\*

I can understand why you are bewildered. On one hand you are being taught a philosophy that teaches that we should learn to depend upon Krishna for everything, including our maintenance.

I preach these points all the time. You surrendered yourself to the temple in that spirit, became dependent upon the temple, by equating your dependence upon the temple with dependence upon Krishna. And the temple encouraged that dependence by engaging you in a service, and by assuring you that you would have all that would be necessary to maintain yourself. Then all of a sudden, “Krishna” says that you are costing too much to maintain. However, that was never previously explained before in the philosophy, and all of a sudden you seem to be back where you were before you knew anything about depending upon Krishna.

This is exactly one reason why I always try to emphasize the need to depend upon Krishna “beyond” the institutional dependency. Yes, it is true, that surrendering to the institution is an indication of our surrender to Krishna. Moreover, your spiritual master is also within that institution. Therefore your surrender to him, in one sense, also binds you to surrender within the institution. But if we actually want to cultivate pure Krishna consciousness we must associate with Krishna from those who are giving us Krishna. If we get Krishna’s association, (Who is always beyond any institutional boundaries), then the institution doesn’t become everything for us. The institution is supposed to provide the association that’s required to support our Krishna consciousness. If that support is there, then we can function in any sphere of existence—whether we are being economically maintained by the institution or not.

If the institution is not providing you the support that you need for your Krishna consciousness, then you must get that support from wherever you can find it. I have therefore always tried my best to give you Krishna consciousness, while I simultaneously take an active role within ISKCON. It is my duty to serve the institution of my spiritual master. I cannot whimsically abandon that duty.

Therefore, I dutifully instruct my disciples to work within ISKCON, to cooperate with temple authorities, and to associate with ISKCON devotees. But beyond all that, I try to give Kri-shna to everyone, so that whether they are closely connected to ISKCON or not so closely connected, they will still keep con-nection with their spiritual master, and will hopefully appreciate that I am helping them to become Krishna consciousness.

So, I believe that I’ve said enough on that point. Regarding the points you raised about ...’s letter, any duty that we accept from scriptural authority, or an authority which represents Krishna’s in-structions to us is purifying if we execute it with Krishna’s pleasure as the goal. I do not agree that unless it is applied to our psychophysical nature it will not purify. That is not what Srila Prabhupada taught us. It is true that simply executing our duties out of social pressure will not bring about the desired result. And it is true that we find that many devotees have this experience. But whatever social duties we perform are meant to be combined with hearing and chanting about Krishna. That is what acts to purify our hearts, while we simul-taneously execute our duties within society. Without *Krishna katha,* whatever duties we perform will not bring us to the desired goal.

This whole topic reminds me of a question one disciple asked me last week. She asked if our desires need to be fulfilled in order to advance. I answered her by telling her that all our desires must be fulfilled, otherwise whatever desire remains in the heart which is separate from Krishna will bring us back to the material world. It’s true. But Krishna is the most expert in fulfilling desires. He knows how to fulfill in such a way that we won’t come back and ask for their fulfillment again. The point is that although our desires do need to be fulfilled, we should let Krishna fulfill them rather than attempting to artificially fulfill them ourselves. Therefore a person is best situated for purification who depends upon Krishna for the fulfillment of all of his needs.

...’s point is that it’s all artificial unless the full realization is there. Srila Prabhupada’s point is that it is all natural if we simply strictly follow Krishna’s perfect representative. Everything else is artificial if its devoid of Krishna consciousness and that is a fact! Do you think that its natural to relate as husband and wife in bodily consciousness? Do you think that association between male and female is natural? Natural means according to one’s nature. And according to our inherent nature we are meant to be established in a relationship with Krishna. Unfortunately, Prabhu needed help with his own Krishna consciousness, and that is why everything seemed so artificial to him.

Given your own situation, I would simply impress on the same point. Without Krishna consciousness, then there is no doubt that the perfect execution of all of your household duties will not bring you any closer to Krishna. Therefore within every instruction to you, I have always left you with windows to Krishna consciousness beyond the duty of mother and wife, by stressing association, reading, service to the Deities, preaching, chanting, etc. These are the duties meant to evoke Krishna consciousness not simply being a mother and wife. If you abandon your spiritual duties, then everything else will certainly be artificial and a waste of your time. But if you take strength from your spiritual duties, then you will be able to hurdle beyond your family duties in due course of time. Krishna will fulfill all of your needs and will give you the strength to remain patient in the execution of all your prescribed duties.

In *Caitanya Bhagavata* there are many nice pastimes illustrating this point. When Lord Caitanya was chanting in Shrivasa Pandita’s home, during a *kirtana* Shrivasa Pandita’s son left his body. When Shrivasa Pandita saw his family members lamenting, he chastised them all, and said that they should immediately give up their false lamentation for the departure of his son. “If Lord Caitanya’s ecstasy becomes disturbed by all this lamentation in this house, ” he said, “then I will tie a rope around my neck and drown myself in the Ganges. ” He was thinking only of the Lord’s pleasure. Everyone entered back into the *kirtana,* and it wasn’t until many hours later that Lord Caitanya detected that something was wrong. When he inquired, he learned the events that had transpired, and feeling so indebted to Shrivasa Pandita for his unmotivated love, he vowed that He would never leave Shrivasa Pandita’s association. He said, “How can I leave the company of a person, who out of love for Me, does not even know grief for his own son? ” Moreover, He vowed that both He and Lord Nityananda would become the sons of Shrivasa Pandita. There are many other details, but the point is that the Lord can fulfill all of ones needs if we act for His pleasure.

Thank you for your invitation to a cabin retreat. If something is prepared, I will certainly take advantage of it during my visits to the West.

I hope that this letter meets you well and steady in Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #45 (NS—&gt;) February 1, 1997

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter (undated) and have noted the contents.

I really do not know what you are expecting from me. Your letter states that you have no faith in the process of Krishna consciousness, you have no faith that a spiritual master can help you, you’ve stopped chanting *japa,* and you are still attached to your former habits of taking intoxication. Of course, I can accept that you must have at least a little faith that I can help you, otherwise you would not have written to me. But what more can I say than what I would say in any lecture that you might have heard me give in Odessa? Faith begins and develops from hearing.

Just this morning I was using the example in class that Vaishnavas must sometimes act like physicians who must tell a patient who has an illness that could lead to death, that they have such an illness. The patient may not be able to accept it, but still the physician has to tell them. Usually he has to describe the dangers and consequences of the patients disease before the patient can realize just how serious their illness is, and from that point, they can be prompted to undergo whatever is necessary to become cured. It seems you have not realized how serious the disease of material life is. Otherwise you would be more serious about taking the treatment.

Material life in the form of sense gratification and intoxication leads to an endless repetition of suffering. There is absolutely no end, unless one stops committing the sins which are the cause of future suffering. Lord Caitanya appeared to distribute the most potent medicine for curing this disease, and Srila Prabhupada and his followers have proven that the medicine works. You may ask, “Where is the proof? ” Everyone is still is still growing old, suffering and dying? But the suffering of the devotee and the suffering of the nondevotee are simply not the same. The devotee who volun-tarily accepts suffering as the Lords mercy, experiences the Lords protection even within that so-called suffering condition. Just as Queen Kunti prayed to the Lord for calamities to happen in her life again and again, because when the calamities appeared, she could only remember Krishna. And by only remembering Krishna she was completely calm because she knew that she would no longer see repeated births and deaths within the material world. Someone may doubt that such freedom from repeated birth and death will come, but a devotee experiences “relief” in this life. He need not wait until death. The devotees relief comes from his remembrance of Krishna, and his confidence in Krishna’s constant protection. There is no safer place than in Krishna consciousness. Just like a child who cries for his mother—when the mother picks up the child, the child naturally feels safe simply by knowing they are in the mothers arms. Or, just like the story Srila Prabhupada tells of his son when he was in householder life. Prabhupada was riding on the train with his young son. The conductor ap-proached them and asked the son for his ticket. The son said that he didn’t have a ticket. The conductor, attempting to have a little fun with the boy, looked at him very seriously and said, “Oh, you have no ticket? Then you must immediately get off the train. ” Prabhupada’s son looked very frightened, but within a moment, the fear immediately left his face and he turned to the conductor and said, “Oh, there’s no problem. He (pointing to Srila Prabhupada) is my father. ” It was spontaneous and natural. All fear went away just by knowing his father was there. Similarly, a devotee who constantly feels the Lords presence can be relieved from all anxieties of this material world. Therefore, if one does not practice with faith the activities which invoke remembrance of Krishna, one will not experience this relief. It is simple as that.

So it is up to you. When you are in difficulty, what will you do? Will you look for relief in intoxication? Or will you look for relief in Krishna consciousness.

You must practice this in your day-to-day life. Do not think that it will automatically happen at the end of life without practicing now. Krishna wants to see just how much you want to be Krishna conscious. If he sees that your desire is strong and determined, then He will help. Be assured of this.

So you should begin chanting in this spirit not as some ritual. Chant with faith that Krishna is present in His name and He is listening to the sincerity of your chanting. If He sees that you really want to remember Him, He will appear to you in His name, and He will appear to you in your mind. Relief is as close as your desire to remember Him always.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #46 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated February 2, 1997, and have noted the contents.

Although I understand why you are feeling despondent, I do not see your situation as hopeless as you have expressed it.

If you desire to live alone, and have the means to live alone, it is not prohibited. In fact, in some cases, it is better for devotees to live alone. It enables them to maintain a healthy appreciation of devotees whenever they associate.

The fact that you attend the temple programs everyday, and that it seems that you intend to continue to attend the temple programs everyday is also a very healthy sign. This means that you are taking full advantage of what the temple should offer to everyone—a place to come and hear and chant about Krishna. I am far more pleased by those devotees who live outside the temple depending upon the temple for Krishna consciousness, than those devotees who live “inside” the temple but are more interested in having their bodies maintained than their Krishna consciousness. It is very disappointing when devotees live in the temple but are not interested in Krishna consciousness. This is not the reason why Srila Prabhupada established temples.

What is not clear, and this is what seems to be making you so despondent, is that you do not have any clear service connected with the temple. Please do not be so despondent about this. I have definitely acknowledged the generous donations you have provided me for my service on so many occasions. Although you may not be directly serving the temple, you have still assisted me in my preaching. For this I am grateful, and it is definitely a viable way to render service according to your means. For now, please just continue with this program of service, remain steady in practicing your *sadhana,* associate as much as possible with devotees, and I am confident that your sincere service will not go unnoticed by Krishna.

You have my blessings to go to the *Holy Dhama* this year, but please travel with other devotees and do not travel independently.

Also, please take full advantage of the programs that have been arranged for all devotees attending the festival. As much as possible, you should be hearing and chanting about the Lord and His pure devotees in the association of devotees. This will certainly make your visit to the *Holy Dhama* the most rewarding for you.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #47 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I received your letter dated March 4, 1997 on March 4, by E-mail, however, I did not answer it because,

1\) I was in India, attending GBC meetings at the time, and was doing a low level of correspondence while I was in India, and,

2\) My computer broke while I was in India.

Having returned to the United States during the earlier part of this month, I was able to get my computer repaired, and I am just now answering a lot of old correspondence.

Private instructions are those, which are given through corre-spondence or verbally according to time, place and circumstance.

Although private instruction is helpful and in some cases essen-tial, a disciple, who nonetheless, may not have so much private instruction can still realize private instruction by strict adherence to the general instructions. This realization appears in the heart by the mercy of both the *guru* (externally as the spiritual master) and *chaitya-guru* (internally, as the indwelling Supersoul with the disciple’s heart). For those with firm faith in *guru* and Krishna, all instructions are gradually revealed in this way. However, without firm faith in the *guru's* instructions, and without a strict following of the *guru's* instructions, one cannot expect that Supersoul will reveal everything to the disciple.

Regarding the fanatic attitude towards rules and regulations, the point is that one should not forget what is the goal of our acceptance of all rules and regulations. Rules and regulations are meant to help us become Krishna consciousness. This means that Krishna consciousness is the goal. Not simply following rules and regulations. In *Upadeshamrita* this is called *niyamagraha,* or following the rules and regulations for the sake of following them but not for the sake of making spiritual advancement.

Srila Prabhupada would often quote the verse, “*smartavyam satatam visnur, vismartavyo na jatucit.*.. ” “Krishna must be remem-bered at all times and He must never be forgotten. All the rules and all the prohibitions given in the scriptures are meant to be ser-vants of these two principles” Therefore, certainly we must follow rules and regulations, at all stages of our progressive development in devotional life. But we must always remember what the purpose is behind them. “All the purposes of a small pond can be served by the great reservoirs of water. Similarly, all the purposes of the Vedas can be served to one who knows the purpose behind them. ” (BG 2. 46) In other words, all the purposes of following the rules and regulations given in scripture can be fulfilled if we are conscious of the purpose behind them. *Vedesu yajnesu tapahsu caiva* / *danesu yat punya-phalam pradistam...* “A person who accepts the path of devotional service is not bereft of the results derived from studying the Vedas, performing austere sacrifices, giving charity or pursuing philosophical and fruitive activities. Simply by performing devo-tional service, he attains all these, and at the end he reaches the supreme abode. ” (BG 8. 28).

Regarding your last question about choosing a service, I have given so many instructions and so many lectures on this topic.

I am surprised that you are not aware how I would answer this question. You should contact the tape ministry and ask them to send you some tapes on this subject. That would be the most effective way to get an answer to this question. However, to be brief, a “servant” doesn’t choose a service according to his liking.

A “servant” renders service according to the request of the master.

Devotional service must come down through the authorized sources. If your authorities are telling every devotee to choose their own service, then I say that you should give this one life for distributing Krishna consciousness. That is the best way to please Lord Caitanya in this dark age of Kali.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #48 (NS—&gt;) Vinnitza, Ukraine. June 8, 1997

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter, dated May 10, 1997, and have noted the contents. Thank you very much for writing to me, which brings me to your last question that I will answer first.

Yes. You may continue to write to me as you see the need. I am always both willing and when the letters contain nice news, “happy” to read letters from my disciples, although I may not always be able to answer them promptly. Just please do not be disappointed if you do not get a prompt reply. I have so many letters and messages that I get every day. I must balance my work on replying along with my daily lectures, meetings and other responsibilities. Regarding your question about association, it seems that you might not be listening to my lecture tapes on this subject. I have given numerous lectures about “how” to associate with devotees. I specifically preach often on this theme since association with devotees is the primary foundation for spiritual advancement. If someone is unable to associate with devotees, then his life can become too easily influenced by the attitudes of nondevotees. We are by nature social beings. In His instructions to Srila Sanatana Goswami, Lord Caitanya quotes a verse from Katyayana-samhita:

“It is better to accept the miseries of being encaged within bars and surrounded by burning flames than to associate with those bereft of Krishna consciousness. Such association is a very great hardship. ”

In the neophyte stage of devotional service, it requires discipline to overlook the faults and to see the good in others. The neophyte mentality is such that the mind looks for the faults in others to avoid looking at the same fault within himself. Most often the fault seen in others that bothers the neophyte devotee the most is the fault that he has. He likes seeing that fault in others because it enables him to avoid confronting it within himself. This is largely due to pride. We want to think ourselves better than others. It gives us a sense of dignity if we can see ourselves above others. But rather than excelling in Krishna consciousness, under the sway of illusion, we try to excel by material consciousness, by putting everyone else down. How to put them down? Find faults in their character.

A sincere devotee will become disgusted with such a mentality, as you have yourself admitted. If there is disgust within oneself for possessing such a mentality, then there is hope. That, in itself, is a sign of sincerity. But if you actually want to become free from such pride, you must make an effort to discipline yourself when the mind harbors such thoughts. You can’t expect everything to happen automatically. You need to train the mind to see the good qualities.

It actually takes a lot of work. Therefore you must practice. When speaking with other devotees, glorify the good qualities in others.

Don’t let yourself speak only of the bad. This glorification of devotees’ good qualities will gradually neutralize the ill-effects of your fault-finding thoughts. Don’t be artificial. Sincerely look for good qualities. They must exist. If you can’t see them, then you must know it is due to your own envious quality. There are good qualities in all living entities because Krishna is in everyone’s heart.

In this regard I remember a particular lecture that I gave in Nizhny Novgorod about two years ago. It was a very long lecture on this subject. I am quite sure that my tape ministry has a copy of this lecture because I told Indriyesha dasa to include this lecture in my tape ministry. Also, just two days ago, on June 8, my whole Srimad-Bhagavatam class in Kishinev was on this topic. I expect that this lecture will soon appear in my tape ministry. You should request a copy of this lecture and listen to it carefully. I believe that it will help you.

Regarding the name of your son, Niranjana is a name for Krishna. It means “Never contaminated by material qualities”. So, if it is your desire, you can name your son Niranjana. I do not have a monopoly on that name. I am simply the servant of Niranjana. In fact, I have a godbrother who lives in India whose name is also Niranjana dasa.

He is the servant of Niranjana, too.

Regarding your desire to serve Sri Sri Gaura-Nitai, from the tone of your letter it sounds that you are your husband are not living according to very high standards. It is best to worship the Deities in the temple. If it is impossible for you to attend the temple programs in..., then it is permissible to worship Gaur Nitai Deities at home.

But you must also make an effort to come to the right standard. Begin with simple worship, but worship Them by also meditating upon Them as persons. Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda are very, very merciful. Whatever meditation you perform upon Them will have a cleansing effect. You should allow Them to clean the dirty desires in your heart so that you will gradually come up to a higher standard of devotional life. Regarding your husband, he must live as a responsible householder.

Householder life does not have to mean “fallen”. There are so many examples of ideal householders. If he is sincere in his efforts to live in an exemplary way, certainly he will get Sri Sri Gaura-Nitai’s mercy. Rather than arranging his life in a way that will get him recognition for a recommendation for initiation, he should arrange his life to get recognition from *guru* and Krishna. By pleasing *guru* and Krishna, recommendation automatically follows. Krishna is in the heart. He knows one’s sincerity of purpose. He will arrange everything if the right attitude is there. He must have faith in this, and everything else will come. I hope that this letter meets you and your family in good health.

## Letter #49 (NS—&gt;) Kiev, Ukraine. June 17, 1997

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

This is a reply to your letter dated May 7, 1997, which was given to me just before I left the United States.

First, there is a sentence in your letter, which confuses me, considering the text of your whole letter. You have written:

\*\*\*

“Recently you have said that we can write you letters and tell everything what we are doing and what we living with. But I can write you when anxiety and pain inside is too intolerable. ”

Did you mean to ask, “But can I write you when anxiety and pain inside is too intolerable? ” There is a big difference. For the purpose of trying to answer your letter, I will assume that you meant the second and not the first. It seems more consistent with the rest of your letter.

Second point, is that I am satisfied with your service. Even more than you would expect, because I already know that some-times you are experiencing anxiety and pain inside, although I don’t necessarily know what level of tolerance you are at when you’re experiencing that anxiety. However, continuing with my second point, I am especially pleased with your service to Lord Jagannatha.

I have on a few occasions expressed to devotees (and I was assuming that my words were passed on to you) that I have never seen Lord Jagannatha, Lord Balarama, and Subhadra dressed so nicely in since the days that was worshiping Them. Each time I take *darsana* of Them, They are a complete feast for the eyes.

In fact, just before I left..., I was in anxiety because Yamuna Jivana dasa was putting together a picture frame for me to travel with, and there was only one place for a large Deity photo. I was debating with myself back and forth for several days, whether or not I should put a photo of Radha Gopivallabha there or a recent photo of Lord Jagannatha. Radha Gopivallabha won, but I really wanted a photo of Lord Jagannatha, too. The problem is that I don’t have much room in my suitcase for more than one frame, and if I take a photo without a frame, it always gets destroyed. So I had to surrender the idea of taking of photo of Them this time. But the reason I'm mentioning all of this is because prior to last year when you started dressing Lord Jagannatha, I would have never even had such a debate in my mind. I would have just put a picture of Radha Gopivallabha in that place, without a second thought. Lord Jagannatha has captured my attention, and I'm convinced that it’s because of the nice service that you are rendering to Them.

Now, back to your anxiety about being unable to reveal your mind.

I would be dissatisfied if you were to do something foolish due to not revealing your mind to anyone. Therefore, every devotee must cultivate trust in someone to whom they can reveal their mind without the fear of being exploited for revealing such thoughts of weakness. However, you have written, “I can open my mind to very few people who will understand me. ” I am therefore assuming that there are devotees to whom you can reveal your mind. Then I would have to ask, “Are those devotees capable of dealing properly with what you’ve revealed to them? ” If so, then I am neither so worried nor dissatisfied. If not, however, then I would be disturbed.

You must have enough discrimination to determine the answer to this question yourself. There is a difference between revealing your mind to a friend, who is a friend on the basis of helping each other in Krishna consciousness, and to a “friend”, whose friendship has been established on the basis of sharing a similar illusion. The latter may not only be powerless to stop you from doing something foolish, but also capable of encouraging you to do it. Of course, there is no assurance that even the most advanced devotee could help. At times, I have also felt powerless to help my own disciples who were determined to deviate in one way or another. But at least a more advanced devotee would certainly try to help, which means that they would be more capable of invoking Krishna’s help as well.

Therefore, please make sure that you have the confidence of a friend of the first category. If you don’t have one now, then try to find someone. And if that’s not possible, then you should not be fearful of revealing your thoughts to me. If you’re afraid, then that means that you are afraid of the answer. Otherwise, why would you be afraid? You should not be afraid of such things. I’ve been signing my letters to you as your well-wisher for years. My intentions have never changed.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letters—1998

## Letter #50 (NS—&gt;) March 21, 1998

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Regarding your questions:

1\) How do you think where and how much should I serve?

Because you have a flat in..., it makes the most sense for you

to return to... It seems that you have been moving around a lot,

between and You really should settle down someplace,

and just become steady doing some responsible work, and keeping as much association with devotees with possible for the purpose of hearing and chanting about Krishna. I just recently answered

a letter to Bhaktin ...in..., where she was explaining how

she sells *prashadaam* with other devotees. My letter to her was as follows:

\*\*\*

Dear bhakta …

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for sending me this report. Staying in the association of devotees is always most important, but we have found that oftentimes economic pressures tend to come into conflict with that. If you can fulfill your economic needs through *prasadam,* in the association of devotees, then this is very good. But be careful to not base your association with devotees on the strength of cooking and selling *prasadam* together. To remain strong in Krishna consciousness association must always be based upon hearing and chanting about Krishna. If you remain dependent upon that, then even if economic constraints arise in the future, you will not abandon your “association” with devotees. Hoping this meets you in good health.

In other words, it is less important to keep association with devo-tees for economic purposes. It is more important to keep association with devotees for maintaining your Krishna consciousness. Even if you are “with” devotees for long periods of time “working together to maintain your bodies”, it is better even if you are with devotees for shorter periods of time, but for exclusively hearing and chanting about Krishna. In this regard, “quality” is more important than “quantity”. Of course, if you can do both, that is best.

But it seems that you are too disturbed due to lack of a steady means of support, and therefore neither association for maintenance nor association for hearing and chanting is happening for you.

If you live in..., you do not have to worry about a flat. As far as I know, the devotees are gathering regularly for classes.... Swami visits there regularly. Also Swami will be visiting there more often beginning this year.

That is my recommendation.

Just how you should support yourself? That is not a question to ask your spiritual master—especially since I never told you to leave the means of support that you had in at the time I agreed to initiate you. I still do not even know why you left it. Stop living like an artificial renunciant, please. But remain serious about becoming Krishna conscious and helping to become Krishna conscious.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #51 (NS—&gt;) March 23, 1998

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated September 3, 1997, and I have tried to note the contents to the best of my ability. The English was not very good, and I was therefore not so inspired to answer the letter. Nonetheless, having now read it a second time, I was able to understand enough of the letter to conclude that you must be suffering from the fact that you are becoming a victim to *maya. I* therefore could no longer neglect your need for some instruction.

The situation described (or which I think you have described) is not something unknown to me. *Maya* continues to strike anyone who becomes inattentive to the process of Krishna consciousness. In fact, nobody is an exception to this. That is the nature of the living entity. He is *tatastha-shakti,* which means that he always has the potential to fall under the influence of *maya.* Some fortunate souls, having once achieved the full protection of Krishna’s internal potency, do not fall under the influence of the three modes of material nature again... *mam ca yo vyabhicarena*... “One who engages in full devotional service, unfailing in all circumstances, at once transcends the modes of material nature and thus comes to the level of Brahman. ”

Under the circumstances, it appears that you have fallen victim to *maya*'s allurements. The more that you flirt with *maya,* the more susceptible you will become to her call for more sense gratification, and the more deeply entangled you become in sense gratification, the more difficult it becomes for you to break free from *maya*'s grip to get out. It all starts with a contemplation of sense gratification. The more contemplation there is, the weaker and weaker the determination to resist will become. There is protection from such spiraling downfalls into *maya*'s abyss, but you must want that protection. Naturally, one place to start is through the instructions of the spiritual master, and that you have done.

I can say, “Don’t do this again! ”, but can I expect you to immedi-ately follow? That instruction was already there and understood at the time of initiation. I need to give you a more positive alternative to *maya.*

The problem seems to stem from the fact that you have to spend a good portion of your time at home due to ill health. I therefore recommend that when you are at home, you increase the number of rounds that you chant every day. Chanting can help, if you chant in the proper mood of a full helplessness and dependence upon Krishna. Don’t take your chanting as some ritual. Call out to Krishna to help you follow the instructions of your spiritual master, which are meant to keep you out of *maya*'s clutches. If Krishna sees you make an increased effort to serve a higher instruction aimed to protect you from *maya,* He will notice the effort, and in this way, He can be invoked to give you more strength to resist.

I was just writing about spiritual strength to a devotee that I had known for many years, and who was coming back to Krishna consciousness. I will share the few words that I wrote to him on the subject.

\*\*\*

I completely agree with your desire to become “spiritually strong” again.

Lord Balarama, Ananta Shesha, is the source of all spiritual strength. Srila Vrindavana dasa Thakura writes that although Ananta Shesha does not rest upon anything, still, everything rests upon Him. It is by the power of His devotional service that He can support everything. Now that is spiritual strength! If we want spiritual strength we must get it from a spiritual source, from which hearing about “transcendental subject matter” is most important. Without regular doses of hearing, the influence of passion and ignorance will simply continue.

Therefore, please try to hear these words which are now coming from your spiritual master, and gain the strength that you need to resist all future temptations for *maya.*

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #52 (NS—&gt;Radha Kunda dd) June 1, 1998

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

It has come to my attention that some of my disciples have been independently publishing lectures of mine in one form or another, but according to reliable sources the translations are not accurate. As you know, since you and I have corresponded on this subject many times, I am very meticulous about the way that my lectures are put into print. On cassette, is one thing. I have no choice but to accept the quality of translation which is available at the particular time of my lecturing.

Fortunately, I believe that most of the translators who generally travel with me are reliable, but there is always a chance that there will be mistakes in a spontaneous translation. However, even those mistakes must be corrected before putting a lecture into print. When the message is published in either a book or pamphlet form, the impression given by the publication is that whatever is in print, is accurate. However, as mentioned above, this is not the case. It appears that these disciples who are independently publishing are unaware of how strict I am on this matter. Moreover, as you know, I also want all publications of lectures properly edited and proofread by qualified editors and proofreaders before being published as well.

Therefore, I am requesting you to please make it known to all parties concerned that I am hereby giving exclusive authorization to you for publishing my lectures under the name of “Gauranga Press”.

If anyone else wishes to help in this endeavor, they must coordinate whatever work they are doing with you, and upon completion of their work, they must submit everything to you to be used according to your discretion. It should be clearly known to all of my disciples that any other publication of my lectures in Russian is unauthorized, and should not be distributed.

Please give this matter your immediate attention.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #53 (NS—&gt;) Tula, Russia. August 3, 1998

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

We spoke only briefly in..., but I needed to impress upon you that I am not in favor of your decision to renounce your children in favor of “surrendering to Krishna”. I therefore tried to emphasize to you that you needed to regularize your life in Krishna consciousness by remembering those relevant instructions which I have given to living in Krishna consciousness. I realize that many of my instructions are related to living near to and associating with devotees, and your recent decisions were an attempt to align yourself with those instructions. I believe that there was a sincere intention behind those decisions, and I have not overlooked that. But, still, you must take the responsibility for raising your children, and providing them a Krishna conscious environment for them to live. It is your duty to do that as well.

Somehow he had some kind of romantic idea that he had to “surrender” and move to Moscow, but all this business of living artificially renounced is condemned by scripture. It is simply a disturbance to Society, and I’ve preached about these points again and again. Certainly family life is troublesome, and there are so many hardships, both economic and domestic. But these hardships are also part of the process for becoming Krishna conscious. As I mentioned to you and all the rest of the aspiring disciples in Voskresensk, the most important service you can render in your present condition is to hear the pastimes of the Lord from His devotees. This is the first and foremost instruction given by Narada Muni to Yudhishthira Maharaja when he asked for instructions relevant to householders. You should therefore both read Srila Prabhupada’s books, and you should listen to my lecture tapes.

You should have as many of my lecture tapes as possible to listen to, and you should listen to them regularly—every day. If it is not possible for you to acquire these tapes, then you should inform me when I pass through Moscow on the evening of August 11, and I will personally arrange with Indriyesha dasa to get you a large set of these tapes. Hear! Hear! Hear! There are so many instructions contained in those lectures, along with relevant pastimes of the Lord which invoke faith to the sincere listener. By regular doses of hearing, then gradually, the sincere listener can awaken from his/her dreamlike condition and can gradually become situated firmly in his/her devotional service. This hearing must be done simultaneously, while executing your duties as a mother and as a wife. I am tired of seeing women leave their husbands and children for a life of artificial renunciation. This business must stop. First become renounced internally, then you won’t have to ask me questions like “Where should I live? ”

I don’t have a hotel where I can pack in all women and sometimes even their children who foolishly leave the shelter of their husbands in the name of “surrendering to Krishna”. If you’re surrendering to Krishna then why do you ask me such questions as “where should I live? ” Is that what Arjuna asked Krishna when he surrendered to Him? He asked him about his duty. And I'm telling you what your duties are now, too. Now, please, adjust your life in an appropriate way and try your best to fulfill these duties. If you do this, everything else will be revealed to you in due course of time.

Hoping this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #54 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have your letter dated September 25, 1998, and have noted the contents.

1\. I understood that I associate with the Lord within my heart, although I don’t see His transcendental form yet. I also hear and understand His instructions within my heart. Guru Maharaja, can I develop these concrete intimate relations with the Lord and follow His instructions?

As I have many times explained in my lectures, the Lord dictates within our hearts in accordance with our desires. If we desire to approach Him exclusively, then He helps us to approach Him. The word Krishna uses in this connection is “*satatam*” which means constantly. But in the hearts of those who are still mixed in their motives, although one still is helped by the Lord, the Lord may also dictate in such a way so that we can fulfill our material desires because we are so persistent. Unless we are very advanced, it may not always be so easy to discriminate between the Lord’s desire and our own. I tried to explain this point about discrimination in my last lecture in Moscow, where I talked about the weeds that surround the devotional creeper, and how sometimes those weeds look exactly the same as the creeper.

Therefore, although you can develop concrete intimate relations with the Lord in your heart, such relations cannot be revealed until the heart is complete pure from all other motives. To prematurely declare that such relations exist, without a pure heart, is *sahajiya.* Therefore, the proper method of purification, is to “aspire” for such relations with Krishna, while simultaneously executing instructions given externally from those who represent Krishna’s will most in your life. As the process of purification continues, you will continue to get help both from within and from without, and you should always be careful to not abandon one for the other, until such a concrete connection with Krishna is confirmed both within and without.

\*\*\*

“I clearly understand that being in such conditioned state and not having transcendental vision it is practically impossible to develop my relations with Krishna. But still He wants me to reciprocate His love.

That’s why I hope for Your mercy so much. What should I do? ”

Listen carefully to the instructions which are being given to you. I hope that this letter meets you in good health and advancing on your path towards Krishna consciousness.

## Letters—1999

## Letter #55 (NS—&gt;) April 13, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am writing in response to your letter which was hand-delivered to me, I believe it was at the end of January. I am very sorry that it has been two and a half months. I have a tendency of putting letters written to me on paper into my briefcase, and since there is unlimited COM correspondence coming at me every day, the letters in the briefcase tend to just stay there because I'm never in one place long enough to pull them out. It is a habit, and it’s a difficult one for me to break.

I want to thank you for the realizations you’ve expressed in your letter.

They are “real”, and there is no need to correct you in your thoughts.

There is no doubt that the disciple gets help from the Paramatma, and therefore arranges for the disciple to receive relevant instruc-tions which he/she needs from lectures from the spiritual master. As long as we are sincere, the Lord as Paramatma will continue to help us. If possible, please get a copy of my Srimad-Bhagavatam lecture given in Kaunas on April 3. It was all about help from the Supersoul. Indriyesha dasa should have copies of all my recent lectures within the next week.

Regarding your motives, although your realization is that they were not pure, they were sincere and there is no need to feel too much despair about that. Please, please, please — I want you to listen to the lecture which I just gave last night here in Kiev. Many devotees are telling me how much this lecture has helped them gain the right understanding of how to deal with their own motives in devotional service. And although I cannot say what tonight’s class will bring, I am intending to continue the same theme. These lectures will also be brought to Moscow within in the next week, and they were from Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* given on April 12 and 13. I am trying very hard to deliver “relevant” lectures—lectures which are relevant to the immediate needs of the devotees based upon what I am seeing in their hearts.

I believe that last night’s lecture will be very relevant for you, and if you listen to it carefully, it will be the best way to address all that you have expressed in your letter.

On the other hand, I sense that you are struggling and I fear that although you are hanging on there in..., you are in need for a change—one that I do not see any immediate scope to provide for you. I am not very inspired by the possibilities which could await you if you were to go to the United States. I am afraid that you would run into much of the same problems and only become more frustrated. I believe that you need to live in a place other than an *ashrama,* and unless there is a husband who can provide such a place (which as you know, I have no control of this), you may have to take steps to live independently, supporting yourself independently in a place where you could maintain strong connections with devotees to whom you could still preach, using the skills you have thus learned, as well as with devotees you could look up to. That is not such an easy adjustment to make, and neither are such situations so readily accessible. I realize that you would need help to carry it out. Nonetheless, it is a reality, and rather than keeping it in the background, I thought that I should bring it out in the open—just in case you have already been thinking this way.

I am not suggesting any immediate changes. I just want you to know that I would support such thoughts, if they may have already arisen, because I “trust” (here is that important word) your motives and your sincerity, and I trust that you would not misuse this independence to your own detriment.

I know that you have close connections with devi dasi. She is definitely mature, and I “trust” that she is your well-wisher. You may want to discuss the contents of this letter with her, and then maturely look deeper into yourself and the possible opportunities which might be available for you to move into, and on my side, I will also look keep my eyes open for such opportunities. In principle, I believe that Kiev would be one of the best temples which could provide you with both a medium for preaching to devotees, as well as a temple filled with many senior devotees who have a lot of realizations they could share with you. They are really moving forward here in education, and are actually making substantial progress towards a Chowpatty model. The negative side is that the economy here is so bad that they cannot maintain devotees who need to live outside the *ashrama.*

This means that you would have to find someone to live with, and a means of support which the temple could possibly give a portion of, but it is unlikely that they would be able to provide all of your needs. devi dasi (with whom I just spoke this morning) is also pursuing something similar. She has good communication skills and can teach nicely. She has also become convinced that Kiev is the best place where she could teach, but she also cannot live in the *ashrama.* Although she is presently staying in the *ashrama,* she is hoping to acquire a means to live outside soon, and she is also looking for ways which she could possibly support herself with her language skills so that she could get as best as possible, the best of both worlds (being able to live outside, support herself, still have association with senior devotees, and to teach in the academy). I am just sharing some thoughts to illustrate that you are not alone in this endeavor, and that others are finding a means to overcome these obstacles.

Thank you very much for the information about devi dasi.

I was very happy to hear that she is doing better, and was especially happy to hear that she was visiting to preach. She also has a lot of realizations and it is good that she is feeling better about sharing them now.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #56 (NS—&gt;) April 14, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

“Sometime we have questions difficult to answer without someone’s else help.

Where is the border between *Krishna katha* and discussions of personal problems. So many times we begin to speak about Krishna and then unseeingly go to our own problems. How to avoid this? What is it *Krishna katha* at all?

Can we call *Krishna katha* the discussion on devotional service and related with it problems? Please help me to deliberate and understand the matter properly. ”

Srimad-Bhagavatam class is not for discussing politics, personal problems, local management, hyping devotees to make money, etc., etc., etc. Srimad Bhagavatam class is for deepening our attachment to hearing transcendental subject matters related to the pastimes and instructions of the Supreme Lord and His pure devotees. Such classes should inspire the proper service attitude towards the Supreme Personality of Godhead. If the speakers have an agenda that they want to communicate to the devotees, they should schedule regular *isthagosthis* for discussing such topics. Otherwise, each speaker should prepare nicely his/her class so that the devotees enthusiasm will increase to attend Srimad-Bhagavatam class.

I strongly suggest that you get a copy of the Srimad-Bhagavatam lecture which I just gave in Kaliningrad on March 30, 1999. These lectures will soon be delivered to Indriyesha dasa for the tape ministry. The subject of this class is a perfect answer to your question about what to do in Srimad Bhagavatam classes.

\*\*\*

“Guru Maharaja, as I know there are 4 disciples in...! Please visit us. We wait for You so much. ”

Unfortunately, since I was just in a few months ago, it is likely that I will not be visiting again until towards the end of the year.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #57 (—&gt;NS)

Please accept my respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet!

All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

First of all, please let me express my sincere gratitude to you for your attention to me during this time and especially in Mayapura. By your mercy and by the mercy of other senior devotees I feel inspiration in the service to Srila Prabhupada.

I want to ask you about an unusual help. In our temple Bhakta Rashid is going to leave his body. He serves in our temple during two years as a temple keeper.

He was not strong and successive devotee, even he violated some principles (attachment to smoking and alcohol) sometimes, but he sincerely served the devotees, and they respect him. Because he can’t follow 4 regulative principles, he is not initiated. Now he is in a hard situation: he eats and drinks nothing and quickly dies away.

Today he takes a decision to leave the body and stop struggling for recovering (doctors say it may happened soon). One of the devotees advised to ask spiritual master to protect Rashid at this moment, giving him initiation. I thought it was a good advice and ask your opinion. This devotee told that you gave an initiation once without seeing. If it can help Rashid please pour your mercy upon him and give him initiation, all of us beg you for this mercy and Rashid also. The devotees will protect him every time by chanting the holy names constantly, and he will also chant the maha-mantra as much as he can even in his mind if he can’t chant on beads.

I hope my request will not disturb you much.

## Letter #58 (NS—&gt;Vasushrestha d.; response to#57) April 15, 1999

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

In answer to your request I would like to relate the following: Just a few weeks ago, my disciple, Nirmala devi dasi, (31 yrs. old) left her body in the Boston temple. When it was found out that she had cancer last year, she tried her best to treat it, and many devotees helped her in her struggles, right up until the point when she left her body. The same evening that she left her body, I wrote the following, and the next day it was read to the general devotees:

\*\*\*

March 7, 1999; 1:00 AM

I am writing to those who were Nirmala devi dasi’s well- wishers, and especially to those who had given her so much personal care and attention since the time she discovered that she had this incurable disease.

Although I am sad to hear that Nirmala devi dasi has de-parted, I am also relieved to know that her future is bright due to the care, well-wishes, and blessings of so many Vaishnavas who bestowed inconceivable mercy upon her. Although we often hear much emphasis that the relationship between the spiritual master and disciple is required to obtain Krishna’s mercy, I am convinced, from carefully observing Nirmala’s case, that all of you have played a far more significant role for invoking this mercy than I.

Therefore, feeling bound by the duty expected of me, to bring those who are connected in a guru/disciple relationship closer to Krishna, I am both grateful and indebted to all of you for this mercy that you have kindly given to her.

By the good will of Lord Krishna, Nirmala has come into the association of Vaishnavas in this life. And also by the good will of Krishna, Nirmala has heard so much about Krishna, chanted His names, honored Krishna *prashadaam,* served His Deity form, associated with His devotees, visited His sacred places of pilgrimage, read Srila Prabhupada’s books, and from all of these as well, she has established her eternal connection with Krishna. The Lord as Supersoul will now help her keep this connection, and will go with her as her well-wishing friend to remind her of the wonderful association she had with Him practicing Krishna consciousness with all of us in this life.

Wherever she has gone now, I have no doubt that she will move forward to establish a firmer connection, and this is all due to the mercy of Krishna and Vaishnavas. I pray that Lord Krishna will bless all of us to stay in the association of Vaishnavas until our last breath as He has so kindly done for Nirmala today.

I would like to relate one other incident. Last year the devotees in Kaliningrad were driving back from the airport after seeing off Bhakti Caitanya Swami who was flying to Moscow. They got into a car accident because the driver was a little careless. Eight year old, Krishna Lila, the daughter of an aspiring disciple of mine was immediately killed from being thrown completely out of the vehicle. After that incident, I wrote the following to the girl’s mother (who was also injured in the accident):

\*\*\*

June 12, 1998 Lvov, Ukraine.

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Although I am sad to hear that Krishna Lila has departed from our vision in such an unexpected way, please try to understand that I am also relieved to know that her future is bright. Naturally we lament for the physical loss of someone who is dear to us. But we always have Lord Krishna and Lord Krishna’s teachings. That we can never lose, and times such as these are the best time to embrace what Krishna gives of Himself for our welfare, to help us adjust to the constant changes of this material world.

By the good will of Lord Krishna, Krishna Lila appeared as your child. And also by the good will of Krishna you brought her into the association of devotees, to hear about Krishna, chant Krishna’s names, taste Krishna *prashadaam,* associate with Kri-shna’s devotees, read Krishna’s books, and most important of all, to establish her eternal connection with Krishna, a connec-tion lost to most conditioned souls who have been wondering throughout this material universe since time immemorial. Once a connection to Krishna through devotional service has been established, it can never be lost. The Lord as Supersoul who has been traveling with Krishna Lila from each birth to the next will now help her to keep this connection, and will go with her as her well-wishing friend to remind her of the wonderful association she had with Him practicing Krishna consciousness with all of us in this life. Wherever she will go now, I have no doubt that she will move forward to establish a firmer connection with her worshipful Lord, and she now, by Krishna’s grace, and by your grace as well, has the best chance to become really happy— eternally—and to enter into the best association of all—Krishna Lila.

For whatever reason, unknown to us, Krishna has chosen to take her away from the association she had with all of us now. Though it is hard, we should try not to lament, and we should take shelter in the knowledge that Krishna has so mercifully given for those of us who are still here together. Certainly if we practice Krishna consciousness, increasing our connection with Krishna, and Krishna Lila goes forward in her Krishna consciousness, we can look forward to associate all together, with our eternal, beautiful, blue cowherd boy resident of Goloka Vrindavana in the midst of us all—eternally.

As you probably know, I am scheduled to visit Kaliningrad for two days at the end of next month. By Krishna’s grace, I hope to see you then and all the devotees of Kaliningrad *yatra.*

Hoping this meets you in improved health.

My point is this. The Lord is in the heart of all living entities, and He is directing their wonderings from one body to the next. You are all caring devotees, and as I referred to in the case of Nirmala devi dasi, the blessings of Vaishnavas are very powerful. All of you should do your best to create a Vrindavana atmosphere for Bhakta Rashid. There should be constant hearing and chanting, *kirtana,* Srila Prabhupada’s tapes played in the background, and every effort should be made to help him fix his consciousness on the pastimes, names and form of the Lord. If it will help him, and all of you, to create a Vrindavana atmosphere, then whenever referring to Rashid call him Radha Syamasundara dasa. I am not formally initiating him by offering this name. But I see no reason why such an appellation could not be given to him to help him fix his mind on the worshipful Lords of *Sri Vrindavana Dhama.*

I hope that this helps, and I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #59 (Vasushreshtha d. —&gt;NS)

Please accept my respectful obeisances.

All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

I am very thankful to you for your quick and detailed answer, and for your participation in the fate of Radha-Syamasundar dasa. Radha-Syamasundar das and all our devotees are very happy and inspired. All of us felt the mercy.

Thank you very much. From all Ekaterinburg devotees,

## Letter #60 (NS—&gt;) Hartford, USA. May 8, 1999; June 1, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Since I am not planning to visit until the earliest, sometime in August, and since I have heard that you are feeling a little depressed these days, I thought that I would write to tell you a little something about what I have been preaching a lot about these days.

Lord Krishna tells Uddhava in the 11th Canto of Srimad- Bhagavatam: “Having awakened faith in the narrations of My glories, being disgusted with all material activities, knowing that all sense gratification leads to misery, but still being unable to renounce all sense enjoyment, My devotee should remain happy and worship Me with great faith and conviction. Even though he is sometimes engaged in sense enjoyment, My devotee knows that all sense grat-ification leads to a miserable result, and he sincerely repents such activities. ”

These are the symptoms of a devotee. In other words, the characteristic of a devotee is that he has “faith” in the Lord’s pastimes, and his faith has been awakened by the association of devotees. The pastimes of the Lord are “real”. They are not simply stories, which have been made up by somebody to make us think that there is a God. These pastimes are transmitted into the material world via the medium of the Lord’s devotees who have personal experience of this transcendental realm of existence. By dint of their realization, they are able to awaken within us the faith that by associating with the Lord through the narrations of His pastimes, we also can enter into the spiritual realm of existence. But if we are always absorbed in this thought of this temporary material world, the Lord and the transcendental nature of His pastimes will remain hidden to us. Without faith in these pastimes, it will remain difficult for us to make progress. But if there is faith, and we go on hearing the Lord’s pastimes with great faith, then even if we sometimes engage in sense gratification, we should not become depressed thinking that we are too fallen.
