No! Krishna says that we should remain happy, and that we should go on worshipping Him with great faith and conviction. Gradually, by this faith, our attachments to sense gratification will be taken away as an extraneous fruit by the mercy of the Lord. We must simply know that sense gratification will not lead to happiness. It will only lead to misery, even though we cannot completely act on this understanding.

Therefore, please listen very attentively to the Lord’s unlimited pastimes. They are fully transcendental and when vibrated by the Lord’s devotees they contain all the necessary ingredients to awaken an increase of faith in the Lord. I was reading from a wonderful verse in the Srimad-Bhagavatam the other day, (SB 4. 30. 35). There the Prachetas had the following to say to the Lord:

“Whenever pure topics of the transcendental world are discussed, the members of the audience forget all kinds of material hankerings, at least for the time being. Not only that, but they are no longer envious of one another, nor do they suffer from anxiety or fear. ”

It is so obvious that the Lord’s pastimes are completely transcen-dental, because even by hearing them, our consciousness becomes transcendental, and for the time being, we forget all kinds of material hankerings.

Therefore, please do not let yourself become too depressed. There is no need for depression as long as there are devotees who are eager to recite the Lord’s glories present with us on this planet. There is no more auspicious place to be than that place where the Lord’s devotees are chanting His glories. Please. Enter this place with faith, and let Lord Caitanya’s pastimes flow into your heart. You will become happy.

Please try to acquire the latest lectures which I was giving during my last tour in Russia and Ukraine. These points were emphasized again and again with much greater detail, and I related so many different wonderful pastimes which have been given to us by the mercy of the Lord and His devotees.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #61 (NS—&gt;) June 25, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

“Sometimes I feel that close association with devotees is very intense. In my mind I often feel some insecurity that perhaps I am doing something wrong and maybe a devotee will correct me.

When other ladies correct me for doing something, which they thought was wrong, but which I felt was okay, I feel afterwards very insecure and I immediately take distance from that devotee. After some time I try humbly to explain to her the real situation and how I felt when she was speaking so strongly to me. After that I can relate to her again nicely and she is happy to understand the situation better. My question is that we hear in classes that we should be humble and not retaliate when rebuked. Is it that I have to tolerate my false ego and try to be humble otherwise I will not get the mercy? Because it seems that when I express my feelings to the other devotee the problem is solved and our relationship is improved, whereas if I just try to tolerate then the relationship becomes distant. ”

I have speaking a lot lately from the following verse in the 11th Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam:

“Having awakened faith in the narrations of My glories, being disgusted with all material activities, knowing that all sense gratification leads to misery, but still being unable to renounce all sense enjoyment, My devotee should remain happy and worship Me with great faith and conviction. Even though he is sometimes engaged in sense enjoyment, My devotee knows that all sense gratification leads to a miserable result, and he sincerely repents such activities. ”

The essence of this verse which is relevant to your inquiry is this: We should know what standard of behavior we should aspire for by having faith in the descriptions of the Lord’s pastimes in connection with His devotees. The standard of behavior of the Lord’s pure devotees is a standard which is achievable to us, but not by some mechanical or artificial means. It is a natural cultivation which comes primarily by hearing the Lord’s pastimes in the association of advanced Vaishnavas. This helps to increase one’s faith, and it is by this strong faith that we can gradually begin to act on a more realized platform (such as the type of humility which you are describing). Therefore even though you, from time to time, feel the need to explain yourself in order to relieve a stressed relationship, as long as you continue to hear of these standards of behavior (in connection with the Lord’s pastimes) with faith, you don’t get discouraged because you are not yet at this standard, and you sincerely repent if at times you might speak out of a lack of complete humility, you will continue to get mercy. Of course, there may be times when it is perfectly justifiable to say something about a way you were treated, and in these times, what you are doing is the best way to deal with such situations.

\*\*\*

“I have a question about accepting a spiritual master. Lately I hear devotees preaching to non initiated devotees, but not new devotees, that they already have a spiritual master who is Srila Prabhupada and they don’t need to be in such a hurry to choose a *guru.* But in the nectar of devotion it is mentioned that it is important to find a bona fide spiritual master and take initi-ation from him. I know that Srila Prabhupada is the *shiksha guru* for every ISKCON devotee, but I feel that we should encourage devotees, who are already practicing Krishna consciousness for some time, to take guidance from senior devotees with a view to getting initiation later, otherwise their may be more of a tendency towards independence instead of surrender. Could you comment on this? ”

The principle about accepting a bona fide spiritual master, executing devotional service under his instructions, and taking ini-tiation from him “must” be preached because it is the essence of the teachings coming from our previous *acharyas.* Srila Rupa Goswami has listed these as the first three items for executing devotional service. You should not feel intimidated to preach these principles, but your preaching should also include the criteria which one should look for in a bona fide spiritual master (also according to *guru, sadhu* and *shastra*) and the devotees themselves should decide when they are ready to come to that standard and when they have found such a *guru.* Certainly Srila Prabhupada’s *shiksha* should be taught to be the fundamental basis for all instruction given in our Society, but *shiksha* must also dispel doubts, and doubts are not always dispelled simply by reading Srila Prabhupada’s books and listening to his tapes. It is oftentimes found that a person’s doubts are dispelled by personal association with advanced Vaishnavas who help the candidate to better understand Srila Prabhupada’s *shiksha* both by their example and by their instruction. When such an experience is achieved, then it becomes more natural for the candidate to understand what it means to seek out the association of advanced Vaishnavas, to take instruction from them, and gradually to aspire to take initiation from them. Without such association, it is very difficult to legislate that one “must” surrender to a spiritual master and take initiation from them. We can preach the principle, but the bona fide *guru* must actually manifest himself into the heart of the candidate before the candidate can practically act on this instruction.

## Letter #62 (NS—&gt;) July 4, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

You have written:

\*\*\*

“Gurudeva, the sicknesses are changing one another.

I am ill again and it is very difficult for me to rise funds for FFL.

It is my strong desire to get required qualifications to become a good preacher in the educational programs for the devotees. ”

I am sorry to hear that your health is becoming worse again. Please take care of your health and do not neglect it. It causes me great pain when I see devotees neglecting their health unnecessarily.

We have to use these bodies for Krishna’s service, and taking care of the health is part of that service. If we make the effort, and despite our efforts, our health does not improve, then that is Krishna’s mercy. But if we don’t make the effort to take care of our health, then we cannot just say that it’s Krishna’s mercy. It is also our neglect. So you must try, and in the meantime continue to bathe your consciousness in the holy name and *Hari katha,* and all the qualifications to preach will automatically manifest.

Hoping this meets you in improved health.

## Letter #63 (NS—&gt;) July 5, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Before I begin answering your letter, I want to tell you that I have read your letter at least 20 times, trying to fully understand everything that you are saying. I'm saying this because I want you to know how serious I am taking your letter. I do not want to “miss” the points you are making to me. But at the same time, the reason why I have to read your letter so many times is because all the points are not so clear to me. This is a very common experience for me when I get letters from Russian-speaking translators who, for the most part, can convey the general thought of what they are translating, but oftentimes they either miss, or misrepresent important details, and I'm left to speculate, “Is this what she means, or did she mean something else? ” Moreover, oftentimes the original author of the letter has written something not clear to the translator, and the translator is also left to speculate in their translation. The result is that when the final product reaches me, I don’t have a completely clear picture of what is being said, and I therefore try to capture the “essence” of what is being said, and address the essence, rather than addressing each point. If the disciple’s faith is strong, and if I have accurately captured the essence, then usually this method of answering letters works—the author is satisfied, and either their doubts have been cleared, they have some meaningful instruction which they can act upon with strong faith, or both.

But there have been times when getting answers to the details were more important to the author than getting an answer addressing the essence, and I had missed the details for the above reasons. The result was that the author remained dissatisfied with my answer, and their faith was affected because they felt that I did not understand them. This is exactly the type of reaction that I am desperately trying to avoid in this exchange with you, and “that” is the reason why I have read your letter so many times.

I am therefore requesting, if it seems that I have not answered your points, please do not dismiss our exchange as unsuccessful because I did not satisfy your doubts. Be patient, write another letter, and try your best to work with the translator to make sure that what you have written in Russian has been, as clearly as possible, expressed in English. Do not add new points to your second letter. Stick with the same points which were written in the original letter, but elaborate upon them in such a way that will enable me to compare both letters and hopefully fully capture the point so that I can answer it. This way, it will become clear that I have understood, and I’ve been able to address your doubts. Or, it might become clear that I am not capable of dispelling your doubts. I sincerely hope that it does not come to this, but I have to accept that possibility.

I’ll give you an example of an uncertainty I have about what was written, quoting the following from your letter:

\*\*\*

“But You said no, and I did not know what to think. Should I feel offended towards Krishna or You. At that moment I thought that I will not be able to continue living any more. But Krishna has sent me …

Guru Maharaja, I always wanted to have personal association with a spiritual master. But I have got a feeling that You always try to send me to someone else. And when that person makes mistakes, I feel that I am a burden for You, and You try to get rid of me in this way. ”

I'm confused by these statements in the way they are written. You were devastated because I did not give you second initiation. You did not know what to think. But then “Krishna” sent you … If Krishna sent you...., and you were seeing it in just that way, “Krishna sent me...! ”, then why were you still in so much doubt after your discussion with....? Did Krishna send him to you or not? Krishna sends *guru* to clear up our doubts. But then you wrote that “I” always try to send you to somebody else. Which is it? Did Krishna send to clear up your doubts? Or, are you saying that I sent you to… because I did not want to deal with you because you are burden?

Then in the next statement you are saying that “that person” to whom I have sent you makes mistakes. Are you saying that made mistakes? If that is the case, then it’s now clear why you are still having doubts. But because your statement was written, “But Krishna ’has’ sent me... ”, the word “has” in the present tense implies, that you are still seeing it that Krishna “has” sent you …Then why did you say that “that person” makes mistakes?

If what you meant to write was that Krishna “had” sent you..., but later, you realized that made mistakes, and you were no longer seeing his appearance in your life in the same way, then you would have written, “Krishna ‘had’ sent me..., (in the past tense). And then you would have written, ‘But then I saw that he made mistakes. I then understood that Krishna did not send me..., but I was only thinking that way because You (Niranjana Swami) were trying to get rid of me because I was a burden. ’ ”

Can you understand why I'm confused about what was written? I'm not sure how you are presently thinking about If you still have faith in him, and if he clears up your doubts, which is one of the primary duties of *guru,* then as your well-wisher, I should encourage you to take his association. But if you went to him only because you were thinking that you had to go to him because you were a burden for me, I wanted to get rid of you, and did not clear up your doubts because your association with him was not really Krishna’s arrangement, but was only your reaction to feelings of neglect from me, then I must clear up your doubts, and somehow convince you that I am not trying to send you to somebody else. Actually, this is my duty, anyway, whether you take instruction from or not.

But I do not want to restrict you, in any way, from taking instruction from somebody in whom you have strong faith by insisting that I am the only person who can clear up your doubts. With Guru’s blessing, a disciple can also receive instruction from another Vaishnava, and on the strength of that blessing, as well as by the mercy of the Vaishnava, a disciple can overcome doubts and make progress. I do not want to withhold that blessing out of fear that you will think that I am trying to get rid of you. The fact is that I have never thought that I should get rid of you.

Even when I agreed that you should go home, during the en-counter you had with Prabhu, I was “never” thinking that by allowing you to go home I was getting rid of you. I have al-ways seen the sincerity in you, and I still do see the sincerity in you. It was because I trusted you that I agreed that you should go home. I trusted that you would not deviate; you would not leave Krishna consciousness, and that the experience at home might have been just the catalyst that you needed to help you make further progress. My realization is that oftentimes devotees can become more strong in Krishna consciousness living outside the *ashrama* than by living inside the *ashrama.* Many of my female disciples live outside an *ashrama,* they maintain themselves independently, and keep connections both with the temple and with myself through correspondence. I don’t agree for them to live outside the temple in order to get rid of them. On the contrary, sometimes their connections become stronger because by living outside the *ashrama* they become more dependent upon the instructions of *guru,* because that’s all that they have. In the *ashrama,* there was so much advice and instructions but it only confused them. Outside the *ashrama,* they naturally became more dependent upon the instructions of *guru.* Of course, I am not saying that it works this way in all cases. And neither do I recommend this in all cases. But I oftentimes will “agree” to allow disciples that I “trust” to live outside the *ashrama* for this very reason. In fact, there are times when I even recommend it.

Naturally, there are cases when I agree or recommend that a disciple should live outside the *ashrama* because they are too much of a disturbance inside the *ashrama.* But that was not the case with yourself. Please try to understand this point.

Moreover, I do not agree that it is *“maya”* to work to main-tain oneself living outside the *ashrama,* if the disciple is strong enough keep hearing about Krishna as one’s focus for maintaining the body. It is for this very reason that I have been giving so many lectures on the subject of maintaining one’s body in rela-tion to Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur’s instructions on the subject of enthusiastic devotional service. Especially this year I have given many lectures on this theme, and I have requested Indriyesha dasa several times to make these lectures available for my disciples. In the last few months, I must have lectured at least 15 times on this theme. Why? Because I want my disciples to know that I need each and every one of them very much. I don’t need my disciples to cook for me, to give me money, to give money to the temples, or any of these types of services. If they are offered out of love, I will accept. But I do not need this service. The spiritual master is not supposed to depend upon physical maintenance of any sort from his disciples. If he becomes dependent and loses his dependence upon Krishna, then he is not *guru.* He cannot give Krishna or pure *Krishna katha* if he is attached to service from his disciples.

But as I tried to explain this year in my *Vyasa-puja* lectures in Kiev, I need my disciples to be attached to hearing about Krishna’s and Lord Caitanya’s pastimes and instructions from me. I need and want you for that. Do you understand? In order for me to serve my *guru* and Lord Caitanya, I must preach Their glories to “someone”. My disciples are Their representatives, and they are giving me the opportunity to purify myself by serving my worshipful Lord and Masters, when they are eagerly sitting and hearing *Krishna katha* from me. If my disciples can understand this point, then they can live anywhere—inside the *ashrama,* outside the *ashrama, or* wherever. And because they are eager to hear, they will remember and will carry those instructions and pastimes in their hearts. At some point, when they have sufficiently heard, the results of this hearing will come out of their hearts. But if they are “hearing” then that is service. In fact, out of the nine processes of devotional service “hearing” is the most important. And when the disciple is hearing, this gives the spiritual master such great satisfaction because it is the purpose of his existence in this world. He wants to glorify the Lord, but to whom should he glorify? Please understand this point. And if you don’t understand, then please “try” to understand. I don’t want to get rid of you. That thought is not there in my heart. I want to be with you always, in the form of transcendental sound.

If you can understand then this you will see that Krishna “is” there, He is hearing your prayers, and He is answering your prayers.

So, in this letter, I have tried to capture the essence of your letter, and I have tried to answer accordingly. If there are still doubts that I have not understood what was written in your original letter, please refer to the instructions given in the beginning of this letter and act accordingly.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health and happy in Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #64 (NS—&gt;) July 19, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

The main point of concern is the high risk that you are taking under the present conditions that you are living. That you will have to expect. As you say, in your work you are primarily with nondevotees. But, according to Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur, working with nondevotees does not necessarily mean that you are associating with them. He states in *Sri Bhaktyaloka* “Activities like talking with worldly people in the course of one’s work is not considered association. When the same activities are done with affection for someone, then that is considered association with that person. ” That is the risk... if you should start feeling affection for them or especially “one” of them. To not allow that to happen you need internal strength. You really have to pray, and not allow too long of a period of time to lapse without associating with devotees, primarily for hearing and chanting. If within, you find yourself becoming attracted to nondevotional qualities, then I would have to say that, at that point, you would be in danger, and should seriously reconsider whether or not you want to run the risk of sacrificing your life for *“maya”.* But, “at that point” could also be too late. Due to the association, which developed from “affection”, you might already justify within yourself that, “Actually, why shouldn’t I appreciate nondevotional qualities? Maybe Krishna consciousness is too fanatic? ” etc. etc. Gradually, the power of discrimination will decrease, affection towards the material energy will increase, and you will no longer be able to identify yourself as a spirit soul, part and parcel of Krishna.

I'm being perfectly straightforward, saying all that I've said, not to sway you to give up your present pursuits, but to open up this scenario for you to see “now”, while you still have the power to see and to discriminate. It is my hope that it will keep your powers to discriminate sharp, and you will walk the razor’s edge with greater caution.

Also, although in principle, I understand your reasoning for not going to Vrindavana to take up the study, which you previously so much wanted more than anything else, I feel the need to point out that this is just what happens when your external obligations take control of your life. If you are not careful, it won’t stop there. They will only increase. Therefore, please be careful. If you are not careful, soon the rounds will be swept aside (no time), and other priorities, which are basic requirements for maintaining a healthy internal life, will gradually decline as well. Such is the power of the illusory energy. Please don’t fall into her trap.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

**Letter #65 (NS—\>) August 15, 1999**

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

For now, I can give a brief reply to your letter.

First of all, I want to thank you very much for the care that I am seeing you have towards my disciples. This quality that you are developing, as I mentioned in a previous letter, is a very important service and helps your spiritual master to go on carrying the weight of responsibility he has towards his disciples. I have so many disciples who are helping me in this way, but most of those who carry this weight are in leadership positions. Unlike them, you have been able to remain outside any kind of formal managerial role, but at the same time, you are watchful and sensitive to those who you feel are in need. I may not always be able to immediately respond to your suggestions, for reasons that I will not go into at this time. But please don’t ever let that discourage you from giving your observations to me, and from giving attention to my disciples who you think will respond to your help. I am still always remembering the help you gave to d. d. Can you tell me something about her? Is she still doing better?

Regarding your invitation for September 18, again, I must apol-ogize but my schedule will not allow it. I am planning to be in Kazakhstan at that time, and my invitation for a visa has already been sent. From Kazakhstan, I am scheduled to finish my travels in Ukraine, which I started in May but was unable to finish. I will then attend the festival in Divnomorsk, there is a slight possibility that I might be able to accept an invitation to stay in and rest for a few days before I fly back to the United States on October 12th from Kiev. But I cannot be sure of that yet. I will have a better idea once I see if I can finish my travels in Ukraine before Divnomorsk.

Regarding dasa, we spoke a little bit in I believe that he was indirectly asking about *brahminical* initiation, but it is good that he was not forceful in his approach. I will have to think about what you have said. In principle, the *brahminical* standards are being strictly enforced by the Russian leaders, and, as you know, *Bhakti-shastri* is mandatory. I have not discussed with anyone if there is any scope for exceptions, and if so, what the criteria is for an exception. These rules have been made under direction and with the full support of the GBC’s, because in principle, there is a strong need for such rules. For the most part, devotees are not being adequately trained in philosophy by their local temples, and the *Bhakti Sastri* course has been developed to compensate for that lack of training. So, because I believe in the need for strict rules, I am hesitant to transgress this rules without an exceptional cause to do so. I need to be more convinced that is such an exception, and I also need to hear others’ opinion about what criteria should be considered as an exception.

Regarding..., if she gets a recommendation from local au-thorities, I will certainly review her recommendation as I do all recommendations, and will seriously take into account your strong recommendation.

Regarding Bhakta..., when and if the opportunity arises I will certainly speak to him about his possible misunderstanding about his wife and child.

Regarding request, although even requests for pranama-mantra are supposed to be approved by the local authorities, I will, without reservation, make an exception in his case. I know him and trust his sincerity, and since the authority structure in has apparently changed since my last visit, I do not want to subject him to going through the formalities after coming to such a decision after all these years. Please tell him that he has my permission.

I wish you all success in the festival next month. If, for some reason, my plans do change, I will certainly try to organize things in a way which would enable me to attend. But, at this point in time, I see no change in my plans.

Hoping this meets you and your family in good health.

## Letter #66 (NS—&gt;) Hartford, CT USA. November 24, 1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

“Please accept my humble obeisances to your lotus feet. Our talk in was short and it resembled ‘negotiations a peace’. ”

I am not sure what this statement means. If you would like me to comment on it in detail, I think that you will have to elaborate on it a little bit more. I agree that I was liberal in my dealings with you—in that I did not pose many demands upon you that I would normally try to impose on a disciple who is under my training. But I don’t recall negotiating with you during that discussion. I was mainly trying to encourage you to remain on the spiritual path. Was there something which needed negotiation?

\*\*\*

“I could not give utterance to my feeling such suffi-ciently as I would. Fortunately you understand me in despite of my desperate defense of my materialistic position. Instead the complete surrendering to you I run away from your magic power. I surround myself of different things and I try to have a great amount of work and to be in contact with other people. I am Capricorn and I try to be a matter-of-fact person. But at times I get out of my depth. It is difficulty for me to accept a fact of approaching growing old and indigence. What method will help me to win my sadness and to balance both questions of spirit and matter? I am humbly uncovering for you my heart and I hope that you will not break it. I am asking about your answer. ”

First of all, in late April or early May of this year I gave three lectures in the same week in Moscow (Begovaya) on the subject of old age. I was told that many “older” devotees appreciated those lectures because they were balanced and gave them an opportunity to look at old age in a positive way. You may want to inquire from Indriyesha dasa (through Kalpataru d. d. ) about the availability of these lectures.

Regarding a “method” to help you overcome the sadness of old age, the method is to arm yourself with the weapon of knowledge of the self. One has to gain this knowledge not only by study and hearing, but by practical engagement in a way that corresponds to one’s status in life.

This balancing of spirit and matter is no easy endeavor, because the material situation is always changing too quickly and therefore makes it difficult for us to keep everything in balance. As soon as we think that we’ve found the right balance, something happens to change it and we’re looking for a way to adjust ourselves once again.

We therefore need help from outside our realm of individual endeavor, and by the mercy of the Lord and His devotees, we may be able to find the strength that we need to withstand the constant changes of this material world (i. e. the aging body, disease, etc. ) The best means for obtaining this mercy is through “hearing” and “chanting” the holy names and, no less important, through hearing transcendental subject matter. In every status of life, this is the primary “method” for “self-realization”, although the intensity of one’s hearing and chanting may vary according to one’s status in life.

I will share something with you which was written by Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur in his “Vaishnava Siddhanta-mala”. I will then comment on it afterwards:

“Mahaprabhu’s order is simply this-that everyone should incessantly chant *hari-nama.* Incessantly chant *hari-nama*— the meaning of this command is not that people should always chant the holy name while completely desisting from all bodily activities, household duties, and dealings with others. By ceasing all actions of bodily maintenance, the body will be destroyed in a short while. In the context of this command, then, how should one engage in *hari-nama?* Since the Lord gave humanity the command to incessantly take the holy name, then the true meaning is that everyone, whether *grihastha* or *sannyasi, vanaprastha* or *brahmacari, brahmana* or *kshatriya, vaisya* or *sudra,* low-born or *mleccha,* and so forth-all people should remain in their respective situations and chant *hari-nama.* Verily this is the only meaning.

It is necessary to nicely remain in one’s own natural situation because that position facilitates the proper perfor-mance of one’s activities for bodily maintenance, and thus the body will not expire untimely. Bodily maintenance re-quires dealing with others. It is essential that all such actions be observed in a pure and undisturbed fashion. Then all of these things will be conducted very nicely. ”

The point is that the ultimate goal in life is to become fully absorbed in thoughts of Krishna—24 hours a day. That is the goal, and we should work “towards” that goal. We cannot imitate the life of a *paramahamsa.* As long as we identify with the body, we must accept various duties in connection with the body with the “goal” of maintaining the body for “hearing” about Krishna. In other words we should not glorify God for the purpose of being able to live in this world (and in this body), but we should live in this body ultimately for coming to the platform of always glorifying God (whether in this body, in our next body, or in our spiritual body). This means that we favorably accept those duties in the connection with the body as a means of maintaining the body, because the body is the most important instrument required for “cultivating” devotion to the Lord. Without the body, how can we cultivate devotional service? When we look at our duties towards the body in this way, then the bodily duties become a part of devotional service. But without the goal (hearing and chanting) what is the value of simply maintaining a material body? That is not the goal of life.

Now one’s duties towards the body change according to one’s status in life. If one is practiced in hearing and chanting, according to one’s means, throughout his different stages in life, then as he grows older, he is more prepared to minimize the duties towards the body in preparation for increasing his hearing and chanting. The minimization is natural because he has acquired some realization of the importance of transcendental topics, as well as some taste for these topics. Transcendental topics are “higher” and spiritually more pleasurable than talks connected to this body—either gross or subtle. However, if one lacks the realization, or for that matter, if he lacks the taste as well, then it is more difficult to forcefully minimize. Still—as soon as one realizes the urgency, he can begin to increase his attachment to transcendental subjects. Srila Prabhupada states in the *Bhagavad-gita* purport (BG 3. 41):

“When love of God deteriorates into lust, it is very difficult to return to the normal condition. Nonetheless, Krishna consciousness is so powerful that even a late beginner can become a lover of God by following the regulative principles of devotional service. So, from any stage of life, or from the time of understanding its urgency, one can begin regulating the senses in Krishna consciousness, devotional service of the Lord, and turn the lust into love of Godhead— the highest perfectional stage of human life. ”

And in Srimad-Bhagavatam 2. 1. 15, he also states:

“Therefore, one has only to change the aspiration during the course of this present body, and for this there is need of training in the current duration of human life. This training can be begun at any stage of life, or even a few seconds before death, but the usual procedure is for one to get the training from very early life, from the stage of *brahmacharya,* and gradually progress to the *grihastha, vanaprastha* and *sannyasa* orders of life. ”

And in the SB 2. 1. 16 he also states:

“In order to be saved from the danger of spoiling the human form of life and being attached to unreal things, one must take warning of death at the age of fifty, if not earlier. The principle is that one should take it for granted that the death warning is already there, even prior to the attainment of fifty years of age, and thus at any stage of life one should prepare himself for a better next life. The system of the *sanatana-dharma* institution is so made that the follower is trained for the better next life without any chance that the human life will be spoiled. ”

My point is simply this. Now that you have “realized” that you are getting older, you should realize the need for maintaining discussions, which have nothing to do with this material world—this planet or the heavenly planets. You must seek out the association of those who will talk to you about the Lord’s transcendental pastimes as they are revealed from the lips of His pure devotees. And in the absence of such association, you should “read” about such subject matters. This endeavor, in of itself, is the primary method for obtaining the Lord’s mercy—at any stage of life—and this mercy will enable you to be armed with the knowledge required to overcome your sadness of old age.

Lord Brahma states in the SB 10. 14. 3:

*jnane prayasam udapasya namanta eva*  
*jivanti san-mukharitam bhavadiya-vartam*  
*sthane sthitah sruti-gatam tanu-van-manobhir*  
*ye prayaso ’jita jito ’py asi tais tri-lokyam*

“Those who, even while remaining situated in their estab-lished social positions, throw away the process of speculative knowledge and with their body, words and mind offer all respects to descriptions of Your personality and activities, dedicating their lives to these narrations, which are vibrated by You personally and by Your pure devotees, certainly conquer Your Lordship, although You are otherwise uncon-querable by anyone within the three worlds. ”

And in the Srimad-Bhagavatam, 10. 31. 9, the *gopis* pray to Lord Krishna:

*tava kathamrtam tapta-jivanam*  
*kavibhir iditam kalmasapaham*  
*sravana-mangalam srimad atatam*  
*bhuvi grnanti ye bhuri-da janah*

“The nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your ac-tivities are the life and soul of those suffering in this material world. These narrations, transmitted by learned sages, erad-icate one’s sinful reactions and bestow good fortune upon whoever hears them. These narrations are broadcast all over the world and are filled with spiritual power. Certainly those who spread the message of Godhead are most munificent. ”

I hope that this explanation helps.

I again ask your understanding for my inability to answer your letter before this. It is too difficult for me to give the necessary time for such letters during the course of intense travels. I need time, without the constant expectation for my personal appearance at a lecture or meeting, to be able to thoughtfully answer such questions. I sincerely hope that this answer has not “broken” your heart. Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #67 (NS—&gt;godbrother) 1999

Regarding the preaching in my zone, well, there’s always plenty of it. I'm inspired when I'm preaching Krishna consciousness, asserting Krishna conscious solutions for the host of problems I'm confronted with on a day to day basis. I'm not inspired, when I get pulled down into the day to day problems and I forget that Krishna consciousness is the only solution. How much time I spend in each place (Krishna consciousness vs. society consciousness)—I cannot say. But to be honest, although it can sometimes be overly demanding, lecturing daily when I'm traveling is what saves me. And I am practically seeing that devotees are feeling helped by it, too. Moreover, I am also seeing that new people are still coming to Krishna consciousness. My concern, and it seems that it’s the concern of our Society as a whole, is that those who have already come, must remain inspired to seek Krishna consciousness as their only solution as well. It’s not an easy assignment that Lord Caitanya has given to us. We can make so many plans for social organization, to keep devotees within the net of our institution, but if the devotees are not getting the essence, Krishna consciousness, then what is the goal of all of our endeavors?

Krishna was very kind to me. When it was my turn to lecture in Mayapuraa this year, He gave me the following verse and purport from Fifth Canto SB to speak from:

“TRANSLATION. Who are the pure devotees men-tioned here? In an assembly of pure devotees, there is no question of discussing material subjects like politics and so-ciology. In an assembly of pure devotees, there is discussion only of the qualities, forms and pastimes of the Supreme Per-sonality of Godhead. He is praised and worshiped with full attention. In the association of pure devotees, by constantly hearing such topics respectfully, even a person who wants to merge into the existence of the Absolute Truth abandons this idea and gradually becomes attached to the service of Vasudeva.

PURPORT. The symptoms of pure devotees are described in this verse. The pure devotee is never interested in material topics. Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu has strictly prohibited His devotees to talk about worldly matters. *Gramya-varta na kahibe:* one should not indulge in talking unnecessarily about news of the material world. One should not waste time in this way. This is a very important feature in the life of a devotee. A devotee has no other ambition than to serve Krishna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. This Krishna consciousness movement was started to engage people twenty-four hours daily in the service of the Lord and in His glorification. The students in this institution engage in the cultivation of Krishna consciousness from five in the morning to ten at night. They actually have no opportunity to waste their time unnecessarily by discussing politics, sociology and current events. These will go their own way. A devotee is concerned only with serving Krishna positively and seriously. ”

I really took the opportunity to emphasize the need to hear and to take shelter of Lord Caitanya’s pastimes, over and above the need to talk about social organization—especially when such a large gathering of devotees are assembled in the *Holy Dhama. I* used several verses as evidence, but one of my favorites, is the verse spoken by the *gopis* to Lord Krishna, *tava kathamrtam tapta jivanam...* “The nectar of your words and the descriptions of your activities are the life and soul of those suffering in the material world... ” Just actually hearing the Lord’s words and the descriptions of His pastimes gives relief, and I used the example of how Lord Caitanya does not like to see His devotees in distress. His words always relieve the distress of His devotee, and I gave the example of the pastime in the house of Shrivasa Pandita, when Shrivasa’s son left his body for the spiritual world. Everyone (except Shrivasa) was distressed, but they were told by him to hide their distress. Still, the Lord, knowing the distress that was in the hearts of His devotees, later came to external consciousness and both Himself spoke and He also arranged for Shrivasa’s son to speak which immediately relieved everyone’s distress.

Anyway, these are my thoughts about preaching in my zone, and this is what I always try to emphasize the most. Other discussions, as Srila Prabhupada stated in his purport, “will go their own way.”

By saying all this, I do not mean to diminish the importance of discussions on social organization.

Please don’t get the wrong impression. They must go on, and many such discussions can be perfectly Krishna conscious and right on the mark. But I'm convinced that we should not risk losing the essence when too much emphasis by the leaders as a whole is on social development.

## Letters—2000

## Letter #68 (NS—&gt;) Kiev, Ukraine. January 31, 2000

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

“Last night I had a dream with you in it so I felt that probably I should write at least little something. ”

Thank you for thinking to do this.

\*\*\*

“I am still working on Gaudiya (the project you’ve sent). I put the whole Index portion of it together and tried to make it look more organized. I am currently working on the main body of the text. I got so far to the letter M. The text itself also needed some formatting. Every after 5—6 lines the next line would skip down and get split for some reason, so I am putting it back together. The book is incomplete, for example, letter C is not there at all; H—names from 55 to 64 are also missing. That’s what I found so far. I am keeping a note of all these things. ”

This is very nice. Having this book nicely laid out and indexed will be a very valuable asset to have in my reference materials. I am aware that some things are missing. Unfortunately, I do not know where to find the parts which are missing, but at least I will have most of it. Please let me know once the project has been completed.

\*\*\*

“I’ve got a job at last. I just started 3 days ago and I’m working for the school I went to study Travel and Tourism at. They have a very slow running agency there and besides they ask me to do different things for the school in my free time (which is most of the time). Actually I am happy that Krishna arranged it like that. I was not scared at all of this job because it’s rather easy and now the push is there to regulate my daily activities and wake up early. Before I had time for everything so I would space out a lot. I’ve increased my Deity worship standards a bit. I never used to do *arati* before but would just offer an incense. Now I am doing a small *arati* every morning but just with incense, flower, *chamara* and blowing conch-shell. I am happy to do it. Hopefully one day I can get to the standard of full *arati. ”*

Having home Deity worship is important. It is an essential part of *sadhana* for a householder and you should try as much as possible to make it an essential part of your daily meditation. Do not diminish the importance of carrying thoughts of service to the Lord in your mind. Your day is made up of a cumulative series of thoughts, from one action to the next. Somehow, you must try your best to force in those thoughts which are purely connected to simple activities aimed at pleasing the Lord. The more you practice, the more these thoughts will grow, and they gradually begin to envelope more and more of your daily actions.

\*\*\*

“We had a small home program 2 weeks ago. About 11 devotees came and we had *kirtana, prashadaam,* discussion and CC reading and more discussion. Now that we have a suitable place we want to do that from time to time. ”

This is also very nice. Please continue this as much as possible.

\*\*\*

“I am not doing much temple service anymore. Only on Sat. do I cook the last offering and is doing his last *arati* at the same time. ”

That your service to the temple is reduced is understandable, but the service perform should be “quality” service. Again, a form of meditation on doing something very nice and pleasing for Their Lordships Sri Sri... In fact, just earlier today I wrote the following to another disciple of mine in Since it is relevant to the above, I will share it with you:

\*\*\*

Dear dd

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Regarding your first question about what you can do for me, you can pray to the Deities, Sri Sri Gaura Nitai, every day to bless you with pure devotional service. If you can achieve such a blessing in this life then this would make me very happy.

Regarding the question about arranging the temple’s docu-ments, generally I encourage all my disciples to perform a service for their local temple without considering if it is a means of livelihood for them or not. In other words, it is the duty of each one of my disciples to determine for themselves upon whom they are depending for their means of livelihood. I don’t think about it either way, and I support their decision either way (to take their livelihood from a temple or from elsewhere).

Because somebody appears to do a “lot” of service, while taking a “lot” of financial support from the temple, it by no means puts them in a spiritually superior position than someone who can only offer a small service a few hours a week, while taking either very little or nothing at all from the temple. I see this again and again as a strong misconception held by many who do not properly understand the criteria for pleasing the Lord.

It is my experience that most temples in the former Soviet Union cannot provide a reliably stable means of support for anyone other than a simple, young *bramachari* who needs no more than a couple of meals a day and enough possessions to fit inside a small bag. Therefore you will have to determine for yourself in what capacity you want to serve the temple. But when you decide, make your decision based upon what will be best for increasing your attachment for hearing and chanting the glories of the Lord.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

\*\*\*

“Please shower your blessings on me so that I may remember Krishna more.

That is my latest discovery how little or not even at all do I remember Him. Of course I understand that it is not so cheap to remember Him especially if your day and every activity is not dedicated to serve Him. ”

I hope the above instructions will help you in this regard. Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #69 (NS—&gt;) May 14, 2000

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Thank you very much for your letter dated May 7, 2000. I have carefully read it and have noted the contents.

Your letter was not too long for me to read. In fact, it was very easy for me to read compared to most other letters I receive. It was well articulated and well-translated and was therefore more of a pleasure to read.

Regarding your question, you are asking me to judge by the history of the movement, so it is on that basis which I am answering your question.

Overall, the greater cause of problems in the spiritual life of ISKCON’s members, in my opinion, when measured by the two standards which you have given, would be fanaticism. Devotees need to extract the “essence” of the details, and in a devotional spirit, they must be ready and flexible enough to adjust their own lives, as well as the standards which they impose upon others, to the constant changes which inevitably appear in day-to-day devotional and practical life. It is acceptable, or more accurately, preferable, for devotees to be hard on themselves, if such hardness coexists with a soft attitude towards others. But when devotees are hard on themselves to follow all details, and the heart does not become soft, they tend to mistreat others for not following the same standards as they follow. They tend to think that their commitment to that standard, although more external than internal, is an indication of their elevated position, and they mistreat those who, for whatever reason, are incapable of following the same standard. It is this mistreatment of others, which, I believe, is counterproductive and spiritually disruptive to a devotee’s own natural growth in spiritual life and to those whom he has mistreated as well.

Of course, this explanation does not obviate the need to be attentive to details. There are many details which simply cannot be overlooked—many which if I had the time, I would outline in this letter. Moreover, certainly inattentiveness towards these essential details is also disruptive towards one’s own spiritual life and the spiritual lives of others as well. But, for practical purposes, since I am basically responding to your question in the manner which it has been presented to me, I will simply answer your question by stating that it is my opinion that historically, fanaticism has done more to harm the spiritual growth of the devotees than inattentiveness to details.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #70 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your report, dated July 26, 2000. I have read it and carefully noted the contents.

Regarding your desire to be married, in principle, I am not opposed to this. Previously, I had taken a position that marriage should not be an option for uninitiated broaches living in the *ashrama,* but there can be exceptions.

If you can approach this matter maturely, and in close consulta-tion with your counselors, then it would have my support.

In principle, there should be a serious discussion how you would make the transition, with a long-range plan for meeting both your needs as a husband and your needs as a devotee—to have the association and *sadhana* required to nurture your devotional creeper. Most devotees are unprepared for the circumstances that befall them upon entering the *grihastha ashrama.*

To be independent is “not” the right reason for becoming a *grihastha.* Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakur writes:

“As far as maintenance of one’s livelihood is concerned, everyone is dependent upon other people or things. Even those who call themselves ‘nirapeksha’, devoid of all needs, are also dependent. All embodied beings have needs. They are dependent upon medicine when they become ill, upon the acquisition of foodstuffs when they become hungry, upon clothing to dispel the cold, and upon a house for protection from excessive heat or rain. The real meaning of ‘nirapeksha’ is to reduce one’s necessities as far as possible. In reality, no one can be ‘nirapeksha’, absolutely independent, as long as the body remains. Yet the more one can be free from material dependency, the better it is, and the more conducive it is to advancement in *bhakti.*

Only when one links all the previously mentioned activities with Krishna do those activities become free from defect. For example, one should not enter marriage with a desire to beget children or to worship the forefathers. If one thinks, ‘I am only accepting this maidservant of Krishna so that we can assist each other in the service of Krishna, and with her I will establish a Krishna-centered family life’, that determination is favorable to *bhakti*... ”

I hope this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #71 (—&gt;NS)

Please accept my humble obeisances at your feet! All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

I want to thank you, Maharaja, that you kindly gave me your permission to write you. I am grateful to you for your lectures, full of pure devotion to Krishna and your nectar *kirtanas.* Each your visit gives me so much faith in Krishna, Srila Prabhupada and devotional service process. Though I am not your disciple your personal example inspires me so much.

Unfortunately I have no frequent opportunity to meet my spir-itual master, Srila Swami. He does not come to..., on the other hand I am not able to go to the places he visits. I haven’t already seen him for around three years and received no mail replies for so long. Probably Guru Maharaja is very busy preaching. I understand that *“vani*”—instructions of spiritual master are more important than his physical presence—*“vapuh”,* but for my level personal association is also essential.

One devotee noticed to me that it is very bad to be independent, and in our life there must be some spiritual guidance to follow. I begin to realize more and more how it is bad to be on one’s own.

Maharaja, I have to confess, I’ve lost the desire to distribute books.

Before I could distribute books for the sake of *guru* and Krishna, for the sake of Srila Prabhupada’s mission, but now I’ve so degraded that when I go out on *Sankirtanaa* I do it just for my maintenance. I understand that it is bad to distribute books with material motives, but I can do nothing about it. I do not know why is it like this? The more we chant mantra the more we should get purified. Maybe I commit some offences, or I do something wrong? It is difficult for me to figure it out.

I would be so grateful to you if you give me some instructions, because I still want to proceed with my spiritual life. Though I have a lot of material desires I pray that I could attain pure devotion.

If you can, please, write to me back.

## Letter #72 (NS—&gt;; response to #71) Almaty. December 21, 2000

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am sorry I could not answer your letter sooner. I hope that you were informed when your letter was sent (by the automatic response which my email account was generating) that I was offline during my stay in India. I was therefore unable to answer any email during that time.

Regarding your loss of desire to distribute books for the pleasure of *guru* and Krishna, although this is certainly a cause of concern, you should not allow yourself to become too morose by this. The important thing that you should be most concerned about, is your desire to rekindle your desire to please *guru* and Krishna. I can see that you have this desire. Whether you are distributing books, hearing Srimad-Bhagavatam, reading books, worshiping the Deity, or chanting the holy names, by any of these activities, you can please *guru* and Krishna—not “just” by distributing books. The stimulus to please Them, however, will come from your appreciation of what They are giving to you. This is natural.

Within each and every one of us, there is natural desire to reciprocate with those who are giving something wonderful to us. Krishna consciousness is a current which flows through the words and activities of Krishna’s pure devotees. Wherever we can find this flow of nectar, if it deepens our attachment to Krishna, if it deepens our attachment to Srila Prabhupada and the wonderful gift of Krishna consciousness that he’s giving to the world, and if it deepens our attachment to any one or all nine of nine processes of devotional service, beginning with hearing and chanting, then we should drink it without hesitation. As we get a taste of this wonderful nectar, it will gradually soften our hearts, and it will instill within us the desire to reciprocate with those who are distributing this nectar. This is how Srila Prabhupada inspired us, his followers, to distribute both his books and Krishna consciousness in general.

In the fourth Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam, Prithu Maharaja states:

*sa uttamasloka mahan-mukha-cyuto*  
*bhavat-padambhoja-sudha kananilah*  
*smrtim punar vismrta-tattva-vartmanam*  
*kuyoginam no vitaraty alam varaih*

“My dear Lord, You are glorified by the selected verses uttered by great personalities. Such glorification of Your lotus feet is just like saffron particles. When the transcendental vibration from the mouths of great devotees carries the aroma of the saffron dust of Your lotus feet, the forgetful living entity gradually remembers his eternal relationship with You. Devotees thus gradually come to the right conclusion about the value of life. My dear Lord, I therefore do not need any other benediction but the opportunity to hear from the mouth of Your pure devotee. ” (SB 4. 20. 25)

We all want to come in contact with Krishna, to be captivated by His beauty, His personality, His qualities, His pastimes, and to establish our eternal connection with Him through loving devotional service. That is our innate desire. The merciful Vaishnavas, by their causeless mercy, are forcing their way into our lives by their efforts to give us these saffron particles from Krishna’s lotus feet. To the degree that our forgetfulness of Krishna is dispelled and our remembrance of Him is awakened, we will once again feel the desire to please His devotees, Srila Prabhupada, and ultimately Krishna, Himself, grow within our hearts. Of this, there is no doubt.

This nectar is available, but you must be willing to pay the price—the intense greed to have it. Wherever it is available, you must purchase it without delay. Everything else that you do should be subsidiary to hearing about Krishna.

Although you are finding your desire to distribute Srila Prabhu-pada’s books for the pleasure of guru and Krishna decreasing, you should not allow your desire to hear about Krishna decrease. This desire—to hear about Krishna—is not only pleasing to Krishna’s devotees, but is pleasing to Krishna, Himself.

Therefore, for you, it is now better that you focus more on hearing as your method for pleasing guru and Krishna. By emphasizing this point, I am not saying that you should stop distributing books. But, without hearing, your book distribution will simply be fruitive, and it will neither satisfy you nor will it satisfy guru and Krishna.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter—2001

## Letter #73 (NS—&gt;) January 27, 2001

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am satisfied with my disciples when they are steady in executing both their material and spiritual duties. This means that each disciple, according to their position, must fulfill their duties as either *bramachari or grihastha,* and at the same time, they must remain dedicated to chanting their prescribed number of rounds, reading, and associating with devotees for hearing and chanting about Krishna. Beyond that, they should be cultivating their internal life based upon remembrance of the Lord’s name, form, qualities and pastimes. The service of spreading the glories of the Holy Name comes as a natural outgrowth from this steadiness, and when it is performed on that platform it is a source of great satisfaction to me. Then there is real substance to preaching. Of course, I am also pleased when a sincere disciple tries to preach, even though they are having difficulty remaining steady, but usually such preaching will not last, as they begin facing so many obstacles, both externally and internally.

In this respect, and I am pleased that you are dutiful to your spiritual duties and that you are trying and have been trying to remain a dutiful wife, committed to overcoming the obstacles that have existed in your relationship with your husband. I would like to see you remain committed to this endeavor, but I cannot force you. It is a decision which you must make yourself, although you should know with confidence that I will support whatever decision you make in this regard. I do not reject my disciples if they leave their spouses, although it is always my preference that they remain with their spouses and learn how to be Krishna conscious in the situation which they already committed themselves to when they decided to marry. That is naturally my preference because it is more on the platform of morality, than to leave one’s family situation without adequate purification of heart to live the rest of one’s life in a renounced way. But if my disciple is internally convinced that there is no way that one can go on living in that relationship without completely sacrificing their Krishna consciousness, and they are prepared to accept the consequences of that decision, then I cannot reject them. I will continue to support them in their Krishna consciousness, since it is a commitment which I’ve made as a spiritual master. Therefore, ultimately it is your decision—it is not a decision I can make for you, and I never make this decision for any disciple.

## Letters—Undated

## Letter #74 (NS—&gt;) Between 1996&amp;1999

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Recently we were speaking about one maidservant of Shrivasa Pandit whose name had formerly been Dukhi (sad). Everyday she was going to the Ganges, filling up buckets, one by one, to bring for Lord Caitanya to take His bath after long *kirtanas.* One day, Lord Caitanya simply noticed the buckets lined up, one after another, outside the house of Shrivasa Pandit. He asked, “Who is bringing these buckets of water every day? ” The devotees replied that these buckets were being brought by Shrivasa Pandit’s maidservant named *“Dukhi”.* Lord Caitanya replied that, “She may be named *Dukhi,* but within my heart she is always ‘*Sukhi'* ” (happy). Lord Caitanya thus blessed her to be eternally happy in devotional service. Vrindavana dasa Thakur thus went on to describe that simply by shaving one’s head, there is no guarantee that one will escape the punishment of Yamaraja. The example of *“Sukhi”* serves to show us that our steady service rendered for the Lord’s pleasure will capture His attention, and all blessings for eternal devotional service will come.

Similarly, by your steady efforts to enlighten the people of St. Petersburg with transcendental knowledge, Lord Caitanya will certainly take notice. Dukhi was not standing out in front, trying to capture the Lord’s attention with some big display of service. She was simply meditating on the her service to Him, and was dutifully performing it day after day. The few buckets of water in the same place every day is what captured the Lord’s attention. Similarly, do not think that “big book scores” are the necessary requirements for satisfying the Lord. Just go out, and make your effort to turn others’ attention towards Krishna. Even if only a few books go out, because the effort is there every day, Lord Caitanya will certainly see that more than anything else.

Thank you very much for your sincere service. I am always indebted to my disciples who are eager to accept so much personal sacrifice to serve Srila Prabhupada’s mission.

## Letter #75 (—&gt;NS)

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhu-pada!

I am sorry that I waste Your time; first I want to say that I don’t wait for the answer, but deep in my heart I hope to receive Your personal instructions. In no case I insist—it is unlimited mercy, that I am respond for tape collection of the lectures that was given by You (we have about 200 lectures). And therefore I have no rights to require the additional instructions, understanding You are extreme busy.

First I would like to do is to correct the lacks of the vows that, I have gave at the initiation into disciple. I didn’t mention about chanting on *ekadasi.* So, taking the account to myself of the fact, that Krishna is looking after me from my heart, and You are reading these lines, I promise to chant 25 rounds of Maha-mantra on *ekadasi* days.

Also I want to specify the vow “No illicit sex”—it means no sex out of marriage, and in marriage—only for the conception of children, which I must to bring up in Krishna consciousness.

But on the spot I want to say that I hardly imagine myself in the role of *grihastha.* Maybe the cause is my youth (I am 20 years old), and maybe I fear to take responsibility. I didn’t experience the taste of friendship with *matajis. I* never had aspired to make a friendship with the boys either. I live inside of myself. This is my nature. You may use it as You want in the service to Krishna.

I would like to write what I am feeling being engaged in the service to Krishna. I hope it will help You to reveal the *anarthas* in my heart, and You could help me to destroy it for become more effective and useful in service to Krishna. I’ll try to be maximum opened.

I should admit I don’t burn by the desire to satisfy You and Krishna. The main motive of my activity is to get situation when there are no enemies around me. I want to have a security for my false ego. I think it was the original cause of my accepting Krishna consciousness (as in BG 7. 16). I see a big problem in it.

And moreover—the motive is not ganging above the time. I have thought that I loved Krishna, but after the sometime I had sight that such a sensation appears only in account of reading Srila Prabhu-pada’s books. Being a selfish person, I have the inclination to exploit Krishna’s mercy. I started to conscious it more and more, since I stopped to value every chance to hear, read about Krishna, glorify Him. It became daily routine and easy to access. I have exchanged Krishna for my own security—I realized this writing this letter.

Also I should admit that I have no firm faith in Srila Prabhupada and Lord Caitanya. But so long as I don’t find firm base anywhere else, I am positively fully resolved to devote my life to the process that was given by Them. If this is not the solution, I don’t see any reason to continue this foolish life. I just can’t understand, what so specific people know, that it make them so sure in themselves and in the ways they are living? When I propose them the books, I crash against their faith. I cannot push up people to take philosophy of Krishna Consciousness because I feel weakness against their deep *maya.*

I understand, that the cause of my weakness is in my attachments, first of all to the security. I don’t see any excuses to myself that it goes on. I feel myself like ended rascal. I know that the process of purification is gradual. But by Your mercy it can be much more quickly. I keep on hope for Your personal instructions, although You may act as You wish. I don’t want to become an obstacle for the expansion of Lord Caitanya’s mission by taking Your time.

The main purpose of this letter is to establish personal relation-ships with You. And the fact, that You have known about me, is completely sufficient. But, nevertheless, I feel necessity of Your personal care. You can understand what is this—whim of my mind or need.

Searching for the reliable shelter, but not finding it anywhere else, accept Your lotus feet.

## Letter #76 (NS—&gt;; response to #75)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have taken note of the contents of your letter that was sent to me by E-mail. It is good that you are open and honest. If you maintain this honesty, both with yourself and myself, then it will help you to make progress.

There can be so many motives mixed in with devotional ser-vice. Just yesterday, we were discussing in Srimad-Bhagavatam class exactly this point. We need to hear the glories of pure devotional service so that we know what standard of devotional service we must ultimately come to. By hearing the glories of pure devotional service, we can also recognize what motives are within ourselves. We should not become discouraged, though, by seeing our other motives. If we stick to devotional service as given to us by the Lord’s devotees, the adherence to that order will gradually wash away all other motives. If it seems that you are not changing, and that your impure motives are dominating above all other motives, this is due to attachment. It is fact, that these attachments prevent us from making advancement, but Prahlada Maharaja explains that the only way to become free from attachment is by taking the dust from the feet of the Lord’s devotees upon one’s body. This “taking the dust” means that we must always keep ourselves in the association of devotees who are always speaking to us about Krishna.

It’s not what materialistic people “know” that makes them so sure about the way that they live. It’s what they don’t know. They do not “know” that they will have to suffer repeated birth and death. They don’t know that they will have to pay for the sense gratification by so much suffering.

They don’t know that they are not their bodies. They don’t know that Lord Caitanya is the most munificent Lord, and that our eternal relationship with Him is the most important relationship. They don’t know anything about the spiritual world. This is called ignorance. Just as a hog lives in ignorance thinking that his hog’s life is bliss, in the same way, materialists live in sense gratification thinking that it is the highest pleasure.

But a devotee, having even once experienced the higher pleasure of devotional service, “knows” that all such material pleasure is temporary, and brings only suffering.

Therefore a Vaishnava is merciful and he tells others about how much suffering there is without Krishna consciousness, and how much pleasure there is “in” Krishna consciousness.

Even if the materialists cannot immediately understand, still they can benefit from hearing Lord Caitanya’s message—especially when all their sense gratification is gradually taken away from them.

So, do not be afraid to tell anyone who is willing to hear even a little bit about Krishna consciousness. Your efforts will never go in vain. They will benefit, and ultimately, you will benefit by rendering this confidential service to Lord Caitanya.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #77 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your last letter. I am very happy to read that you are feeling some progress... so much so that I read your letter several times.

The other day we were giving a class about spiritual strength, ex-plaining how the source of all spiritual strength is Lord Balarama. By the power of His devotional service, Lord Ananta Shesha (Balarama) holds up countless universes. He is not even aware of the universes, which rest like a single drop on one of His thousand hoods. Although He does not rest upon anything, everything rests upon Him.

This is inconceivable, isn’t it? How can someone not rest upon anything but at the same time unlimited universes rest upon Him? He must be the source of all strength. It is the strength of Lord Ananta’s devotional service which gives Him that strength.

Devotional service is the most powerful, because by the strength of devotional service, one can invoke the Supreme power from the Supreme Lord.

Similarly, in *Bhagavad-gita,* (3. 43), Lord Krishna states,

“Thus knowing oneself to be transcendental to the ma-terial senses, mind and intelligence, O mighty-armed Ar-juna, one should steady the mind by deliberate spiritual intelligence \[Krishna consciousness\] and thus—by spiritual strength—conquer this insatiable enemy known as lust. ”

If we want the strength to overcome the fight against *maya,* \[lust\] then we must seek that strength from the source. There is no other way that one can be successful in the fight against the illusory energy.

There may be so many diversions, distractions, and seemingly failures in the effort to fight against the illusory energy, but most important of all, is that one should never give up fighting. If we surrender to the enemy \[lust\], then, just as in war, when one side sur-renders themselves to the other, they are immediately taken prisoner, their independence is lost, and they are forced to work under the jurisdiction of the enemy. Similarly, if we give up the fight against *maya,* then she will take us captive, and we will be forced to serve the illusory masters of lust, anger and greed with no hope for release.

But as long as one can fight, one must fight. Don’t allow yourself to become so weak that you can no longer fight. The more sense gratification you engage in, the more difficult it will be to fight. But the more struggle you take for devotional service, the more you will get strength from Lord Balarama to go on fighting.

Do not become disappointed that your devotional service is not always unbroken. You will have to pass through many more tests be-fore devotional service will come to the unbroken stage. But, as you are now experiencing, your “desire to solve and to stop this problem has become a lot stronger”. This means that you are getting help from the source. Please always remember this, and you will be successful.

## Letter #78 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your thoughtful letter.

It’s clear that you are taking the idea about entering householder life seriously, and I approve of this approach.

As soon as the thought of becoming a *grihastha* starts to linger within the mind of a serious *bramachari,* then that in itself is a pretty good indication that householder life is most likely the direction that he will need to move in. This does not make the *bramachari* any less serious because he is contemplating householder life. I used the word “serious” only because I do not necessarily believe that householder life is the solution for somebody who was never even a very serious *bramachari.* In your case, I have therefore used the word serious.

Now please continue to remain serious, and become a responsible *grihastha.* Do not just jump into householder life without “seriously” considering how you will be able to live as a serious householder.

Consult with your temple president, and consult with senior, and exemplary *grihasthas* as well. Remember Narada’s instructions to Yudhishthira Maharaja on ideal family life. “One should desire as much wealth necessary to maintain body and soul together. Those who desire more than that are to be considered as thieves and therefore deserve to be punished by the laws of nature. ” Accept a wife who is also serious about sharing your own serious views on how you must live as a householder. There will be many tests, but if you know what your duties are as a householder, and do not neglect your spiritual duties of chanting, hearing and associating with Vaishnavas for the pursuit of Krishna consciousness, then you will pass through all tests under Krishna’s protection.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #79 (NS—&gt;)

PAMHO. AGTSP.

Thank you very much for your report. It definitely gives me the impression that the program is going steadily.

As far as advice towards improving the *nama-hatta* program is concerned, my main concern for *nama-hatta,* as well as for all temple preaching is that the devotees who are being cared for are being responsibly trained in Krishna conscious philosophy. “Training” is the keyword in practically every discussion I have concerning the preaching in the FSU. And I am expecting more and more that the senior devotees take up this responsibility of training. Naturally I see it as my duty to train also, and whenever I see someone who has potential to be trained, I try to give them whatever I have learned in philosophy and practical management. For instance, I am always preaching to and reminding dasa that his real duty as Temple President is to take care of the spiritual needs of the devotees. Without strong local spiritual leadership, the devotees become weak, they lack philosophical discretion, and they lose their enthusiasm for accepting austerities in service to *guru* and Krishna.

The devotees need to feel cared for, and it’s not just the duty of their spiritual master to give them this care. Therefore, please always emphasize this point in your own service.

Systematically train the devotees who attend the *nama-hatta* programs in philosophy. Instill within them the urge to learn the philosophy. I strongly recommend that you acquire a course outline of the 3-month *bhakta* training course, and systematically teach the whole course throughout your *nama-hatta* region. When that is finished, you can simultaneously begin a new course for newcomers, along with beginning a more developed course for those who have already gone through the 3-month training course. Then *nama-hatta* can become something very dynamic, as you systematically begin to train devotees as preachers, who, in time, will also expand *Nama Hatta* preaching.

We should always be careful to not always be thinking that expansion means to increase the numbers. Expansion also means increasing the Krishna consciousness of those to whom we are preaching. Then our preaching will expand naturally.

Regarding the recommendations for the two *bhaktas* on your program, you can do both. You can test them, and give their recommendations.

I look forward to your continued reporting of *nama-hatta* devel-opments.

## Letter #80 (—&gt;NS)

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of Your lotus feet.

All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

For disciple it’s natural to approach to spiritual master in difficult situations. You are very important person in my life, you are my spiritual master. And now I need Your instructions very much.

It is so that this Prabhupada’s Marathon and this year in general is the most difficult time in my devotional life. I feel physical, mental and spiritual weakness. I know very well where it comes from. Srila Prabhupada said that purity is the force. But my heart is so dirty and there are so many *anarthas,* that it’s almost impossible for me to overcome it. I am in despair.

In one of Your letter to Your disciple You write that Krishna will always help us we remain sincere. What does it mean? What does it mean to be sincere? Forgive me for my stupidity. These very basic things are most difficult to realize. The answers on these questions are very important to me now. I simply want to be able to surrender and dedicate myself to Your vision. I understand that difficult times are blissful, but I just can’t help myself.

I know that You wouldn’t like to read such sentimental letters all the time. But I don’t know to whom can I approach for help.

Hoping to become an object of Your compassion
