## Letter #81 (NS—&gt;, response to#80)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I received your message several days ago, and noted the contents. A sincere person is someone who is honest about their own shortcomings in Krishna consciousness. They do not try to put on a show for some external recognition. Yet they are always trying to put Krishna’s interest ahead of their own, even if their standard of surrender does not come up to the same level as that which they may see in others. Still, the struggle to keep Krishna’s interest above our own, is the sign of sincerity which Krishna takes note of more than anything else.

In Srila Prabhupada’s letters, he would often use the word sincerity. I will give you a couple of examples:

“... in everything we do devotion and sincerity are the real things. There is a word in Sanskrit; *bhava grahi janardana:* This means the Lord accepts service in devotional emotion.

If we are sincere in offering something to the Lord in devotional love, He will accept it. The procedure may not be very right, but the desire being sincere, He accepts our offering. This is also confirmed in the *Bhagavad-gita* that He accepts foodstuffs from devotees because they are offered to Him in complete love and affection. That is required.

Although you have been associating with us for a short time, I can understand that Krishna is giving you good opportunity to serve Him nicely. It is very kind of you to be donating all of your earnings to our Seattle temple, and surely Krishna will bless you for your sincerity. It is this willingness to serve which Krishna wants to see in us. We have nothing to offer Krishna because He is the Proprietor of everything that be. But when He sees that a living entity is trying to serve Him, that is appreciated. In *Bhagavad-gita* you have seen that Krishna says to offer Him a leaf, a fruit, a flower or even some water. Actually, the Supreme Lord has no necessity to request us to offer Him these items or any other items; but He gives us the opportunity of serving Him so that we may become qualified to enter back to Home, back to Godhead.

You mentioned that you are not yet a very bold preacher, but you will become bold, if you have got sincerity. In the beginning also I could not speak. But Krishna is within you, and when you are serving Him sincerely He will give you courage, boldness, everything. We are not going to bluff anyone or cheat others, and we are delivering the message on behalf of the Supreme Lord, so we haven’t got anything to fear and we should be always mindful of our topmost position of occupation of life. To be the messenger of the king is practically as good as to be the king, so the king is the most exalted position. I act like a king because no one can defeat me, and similarly, you should take your responsibility very, very seriously as being the representative of Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu, Who is God Himself. ”

So please take all these encouraging words into consideration and please note that by the mere fact that you are asking this question, that is also a clear indication of your sincerity as well. I know you to be a sincere girl.

Please do not be neglectful of your health. I was very concerned when I heard from... d. d., that your health was in such a condition. Do not think that you have to sacrifice your health to be on a high level of sincerity.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #82 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

It is naturally disturbing to hear that you are unable to follow the regulative principles and to chant your rounds.

But it is not so easy for the spiritual master to reject his disciple. Your spiritual master also feels responsible for your present condi-tion, and he suffers in more ways than you can know, including the anxiety he feels for the suffering his disciple will have to experience as a result of his committing sinful activities. There is no way that you can accept “all” the suffering in the way you have requested, as long as the spiritual master feels compassion for the sufferings of others.

On this basis, how can I therefore reject you? I must pray to Krishna for the spiritual strength to help you someday realize that you cannot be happy be sinful activities.

Meanwhile, that you are not blaming others for your present condition is good. Please keep the association with devotees as much as possible. They will protect you from bad association.

As much as possible you should also serve the devotees. That service will also help you from becoming too entangled in serving nondevotees.

I hope to visit later this year. Please, when I visit, do not be embarrassed to come and see me. I will be happy to see you.

## Letter #83 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

There are different ways to preach in colleges and institutes in the West. One is to get formal classroom lectures by getting invited to lecture on various topics by the professor. Generally we are invited by professors to speak on a very wide area of topics, such as philosophy, religion, comparative religion, sociology, psychology, health education, etc., etc. We generally find in the West that once a particular professor invites a devotee to speak to their class, they will invite devotees back every semester. The important thing is that the presentation must be authoritative, and must be presented in a way that helps the students gain a better understanding of the sociological, religious, and philosophical aspects of our movement, without preaching too heavily. Generally, the students will very much appreciate such a talk and many of them will come up after the presentation to ask further questions and even to purchase some books.

Another way of preaching in the institutes is by forming clubs, such as vegetarian clubs, or philosophy clubs. But these clubs must be formed by the students themselves. In institutions in the West, the students have the right to form a club of their own interest, they are given a room to meet in, and are allowed to have their own expense account with funds that they collect amongst themselves. We, as preachers of Krishna consciousness do not have the right to start a club in a school, but if we can find enough students who are interested, then they can gather other interested students. If there are enough interested students, then they can start the club themselves and then they can invite us to either come and speak, or to teach vegetarian cooking. Generally, most clubs in the West are started with signatures of approximately 10 interested students. If there are students coming to the temple, you can ask them to inquire at their schools just what kind of facilities the school allows for this kind of extracurricular activity.

Another nice way to preach in schools is simply by going outside the school with magazines, invitations, and some simple *prasadam,* and just hand out invitations to the students, speaking with any interested students you meet. There is a devotee I know in the West, a disciple of Hrdayananda Maharaja. His name is Sarvatma dasa, who is very expert at this. He just makes friends with the students, and then invites them back to the flat that he rents as a small preaching center one by one. He gives them nice *prashadaam,* and just makes friends with them. Then he organizes a program, invites all the students he’s met over the past month, and invites a guest speaker (generally a *sannyasi*) to come and speak. He cooks a very nice feast, and sometimes he’ll get as many as 60—70 people to attend the program—just from the people he meets on campus. He’s very expert.

These are just some ideas for you to think about. There are many ways we can preach to college students, if we are simply convinced that college preaching is very important and we have a strong desire.

## Letter #84 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

When I was in in June, I had inquired about your where-abouts then. I was told that you had emigrated to Israel, and that’s all that anyone knew. I'm grateful that you have now written to me. Thank you.

Please take it as a serious mission to associate with and assist the devotees efforts in Israel. Otherwise, what good can come out of emigration if you do not? Most important is that you keep association with devotees to gain strength in Krishna consciousness through hearing. If you neglect hearing, then everything else degrades from that point. From hearing will come chanting, and the strength as well to go on chanting, expanding the Lord’s glories. Just two days ago, on Lord Balarama’s Appearance Day here in Kiev, we were speaking in relation to this point. Lord Balarama is nondifferent than Ananta Shesha, from whose thousands of mouths are continuously coming the glories of the Lord. We were quoting Srila Vrindavana das Thakur, who offers his obeisances to Lord Balarama, whose thousand mouths are the home of Lord Krishna’s glories. “As a great jewel is kept in a favorite place, so the jewel of Lord Krishna’s glories is kept in the jewelry case of Lord Ananta’s mouth. A person who offers prayers to Lord Balarama will find the glories of Lord Caitanya appear in His mouth. ” (CB Adi 11—12). We must pray to Lord Balarama, the keeper of the jewels of Lord Caitanya’s glories. If we pray to Him, and hear His glories, we will get the strength to expand the Lord’s glories, the service which is most dear to the Lord.

I hope that this meets you in good health, and resolute in Krishna consciousness.

## Letter #85 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for this report, both on the affairs concerning the invitations for the Western devotees, as well as on your own de-votional service. I am happy to hear that you are inspired by the service of giving knowledge to others, and if, as you say, you work hard to get rid of all selfish motivations, then Lord Caitanya will make more and more of an effective instrument to do this. The real effect of our knowledge is that it must bring us closer to Krishna. If any knowledge does not achieve this end, then it is *mayayapahrta- jnana,* or one’s whose knowledge has been stolen by the illusory energy.

Regarding your question about friendly dealings with the spir-itual master, just this morning I was listening to one lecture from Srila Prabhupada where he stated the following: “Just like Cai-tanya Mahaprabhu says: *ramya kacid upasana vraja-vadhu-vargena ya kalpita.* ‘Oh, there is no better mode of worship than what was conceived by the vraja-vadhus, the damsels of Vrindavana. ’ Cai-tanya Mahaprabhu was very strict about women. In His family life also, He never played any joke with women. He was very joking. But all, all with men. He never played any joke with women. No. Per-haps only once He played joke with His wife, Vishnu-priya. When Sachimata was searching after something, she, He simply played a joking word: ‘Maybe your daughter-in-law has taken it. ’ That is the only joking we find in His whole life. Otherwise, He was very strict. No woman could come, when He was *sannyasi,* could come hear Him to offer obeisances. They would offer obeisances from a distant place. ”

Of course, there are so many references about dealings between a *sannyasi* and women, but since this reference was so fresh in my mind, I chose to use that. But as you say, this is the negative aspect of friendly association with a *sannyasi.* Moreover, as far as external dealings are concerned, there are fundamentally NO outlets for “friendly” exchanges between a *sannyasi* and a woman. Perhaps, this is, also fundamentally, why many women become frustrated when trying to associate with their *sannyasi* spiritual master. They expect that there should be friendly exchanges. But what is the basis of friendship? Srila Rupa Goswami has given the basis as exchange between one devotee and another—just as in the exchange of gifts. This is the actual basis of friendship. Not that one side just takes while the other side gives.

No. When their is exchange, then that is the basis. Therefore the spiritual master must always provide a forum for this exchange, while always strictly keeping within the formalities required between a *sannyasi* and a woman.

The spiritual master gives knowledge. That is his gift to his disciple. And the disciple in exchange renders service to the spiritual master according to his wishes. This means that the service must be rendered in respect of the spiritual master’s position as a *sannyasi.* A woman must respect the *sannyasa* order, and not try transgress the formal exchange which must be maintained to protect the integrity of the *sannyasa ashrama.* When such a healthy respect is maintained, “that” is reciprocal service which is pleasing to the spiritual master, and “that” is the positive basis of friendship. If more and more women could understand this principle, and act upon it, then they would actually grow in their relationship with their spiritual master, having pleased their spiritual master by this service attitude. So you should teach this principle to others. Just see how Krishna is now putting you in a position of teaching. If you follow this example nicely, and teach it to others, then this will be very pleasing to me. Thank you very much for asking this question.

I hope this meets you in good health and happily engaged in your service.

## Letter #86 (—&gt;NS)

Please, accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

I have one very serious question. Please, reply.

If I will remain a lifelong *brahmacari,* can I deliver from material desire?

## Letter #87 (NS—&gt; response to #86)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Remaining a lifelong *bramachari* does not mean that you simply wear saffron for the rest of your life. There is a “consciousness” that must accompany life as a *bramachari* as well. It is foolish to think that constant meditation upon material desires will result in the deliverance of material desires, isn’t it? Meditation upon devotional service, the instructions of the spiritual master, the holy name, the Lord’s pastimes, will deliver one from material desire. Therefore remaining a *bramachari* in “this” consciousness will certainly lead towards relief from material hankerings, even though one may from time to time disturbed by material desires. A *bramachari* will not allow himself to remain disturbed by such thoughts. By his determination for Krishna consciousness, he will push them out with a superior meditation. This determination is *bramacharya.*

This type of *bramachari* will make it in the *bramacharya ashrama* throughout his life. A saffron-clad “*bramachari*” who hankers for the association with a woman, will simply fall into the object of his meditation.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #88 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your report. I am happy to hear that you are preaching successfully to the congregation in... Congregational preaching requires a deep vision. The congregation must be educated to not only accept a spiritual master, but they must know just what kind of a commitment is required to accept a spiritual master. The congregational preacher should not think that his business is finished simply by bringing congregational devotees to the point of seeking initiation, or even to the point of receiving initiation, and then he leaves all the rest of the responsibility for elevating the congregation in the hands of their spiritual masters. The congregational preacher must become a spiritual leader himself, and he should feel the weight of the responsibility to continue to encourage initiated congregational members so that they do not go astray after taking initiation. If he does not feel confident about their integrity, then he should not recommend them for initiation. Not all congregational members will be initiated. Many should remain just congregational members until they are able to make the commitment to become an initiated disciple. And if the congregational preacher is inspired and responsible, he will provide the support that the congregation requires to remain strong and dedicated congregational members, whether they are initiated or not. To do this, one must become fixed up in the philosophy contained in Srila Prabhupada’s books, and he should teach it to others.

Please take this preaching mission seriously, and wherever you are asked to preach, (whether it be..., ..., or wherever) become a serious preacher willing to take responsibility for the spiritual upliftment of others. If you take this preaching mission seriously, Lord Caitanya will bestow all blessings upon you.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #89 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

It seems that during my visit I never answered your question regarding your singing in the chorus. As I have understood it, everything seems all right with me. Most important is that you keep yourself committed to chanting your rounds and daily absorbing Krishna consciousness philosophy. As you hopefully saw in my meeting with disciples, this, more than anything else is the primary concern for my disciples.

Unfortunately, we have witnessed that from time to time, dis-ciples “forget” that the material energy is very powerful, and that their consciousness and certain convictions that they previously held, have shifted in emphasis. As soon as we shift the empha-sis from Krishna’s pleasure to the bodily pleasures, we are subject to go deeper and deeper into forgetfulness of the most essential goal of our existence. Therefore, on our parts, there must be a certain level of commitment to the practices which help us remember.

If we lose that sense of commitment, and illusorily think that we can just go on managing our daily affairs without having to worry about changing our consciousness to Krishna consciousness, then we will once again become covered, thinking that, “I am OK? ” “This material world is not so bad, after all. ” “Oh, all those warnings about *maya* are just aimed to scare us so that we can be manipulated by our authorities. There is really no need to be scared about *maya”.*

When we start thinking this way, we should know that this is the symptom that we have neglected our hearing. Therefore, I try to relentlessly hammer these points, hoping that in some of the hearts of my disciples the message will stick, and knowing that there will always be some casualties, and therefore in the hearts of some, they will forget.

Please, do not forget. If you don’t forget this point, then you can assume any type of work or study for Krishna, without falling into the trap of *maya.* Krishna will protect you.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #90 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Yes. I am planning to fly to India on October 1st. I hope to stay in Vrindavana for one month, and then I will return to the United States for one more visit this year.

I just now returned from a wonderful program here in Voronezh, Russia. There is no temple here. Just a small *nama-hatta.* It is only the second time I visited this city. When we went to the rented hall to give the lecture, it turned out that the hall was in the same building as a discotheque, and the music was blaring much too loudly. So the devotees found a small soccer field and stadium nearby. We sat down right in the corner of the field in front of the stadium, had a *kirtana,* and I gave a BG lecture to about 50 people.

By the end of the lecture, another 20 people had gathered, listened to the end of the lecture, and then stayed to watch and even some took part in another *kirtana.* The devotees liked the lecture so much that they all started applauding after it was over. After that, everybody took *prashadaam* in great ecstasy. We finished at about 10: 00 PM.

(It’s still light here at 10: 00 PM). Tomorrow morning they will all come to the flat where I'm staying for Srimad-Bhagavatam class, and then we’ll move on the Kursk, another city with no temple but a *nama-hatta.* I'm presently moving from one city to the next, one day at a time in the Moscow region. The pace is quite exhausting, but the preaching is definitely making it all worthwhile.

Mahamantra Prabhu is traveling with me right now, and he is also relishing all that is going on here. Although we don’t talk about it in our lectures, nonetheless, we are still conscious of the fact that we must be prepared for whatever tests *maya* throws our way. It’s hard to imagine, but within a few days, it is possible that they could pass a law that would make our public preaching illegal again, and which could also restrict my entry into this country. I'm feeling the need to really impact Krishna consciousness into these people’s hearts so that they can go on, under whatever circumstances will come their way. It’s a great challenge, but by Lord Caitanya’s mercy, anything is possible.

## Letter #91 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

You have written:

\*\*\*

“Dear Guru Maharaj I need your blessings to surpass this wave of difficulties in my life and stay in devotional service. ”

You have my full blessings to always stay in devotional service because devotional service automatically enables any sincere soul to overcome all difficulties in life. That is the power of devotional service. And there are no material impediments to devotional service, which means that devotional service can be rendered in any condition of life. We cannot measure one’s degree of engagement in devotional service by the quantity of work performed with the physical body for Krishna. That is not how Krishna measures devotional service.

I was just recently reading Caitanya Mangala, and in there Lord Caitanya was quoted, Himself quoting the two following verses from *shastra*: “Who was the hunter who was full of bad habits, and how old was Dhruva? What was Vidura’s family background, and what was Ugrasena’s valor? How beautiful was Kubja? And was Sudama *brahmana* wealthy? From these examples we can understand that Lord Madhava cares more for devotion than for innumerable personal qualities. ”

The essence of the verse is that the Lord does not look exter-nally for measuring the level of one’s devotion to Him, and most importantly for reciprocating with the devotee. Similarly, even though you may be physically incapacitated and physically unable to render full-time devotional service, still, full-time devotional service is always possible for one who has firm faith in Krishna, and who knows that devotional service is performed by the spirit soul and not by the physical body.

The reason why I am always stressing this point, that one’s mind and words must also be engaged in Krishna’s service along with the body is because each and every embodied soul must at some point in their embodied life come realize that they are not going to stay with the body forever. Most unfortunate souls do not learn about this during their lives, and therefore they are completely unequipped to deal with becoming disembodied. But a devotee, by dint of the spiritual activity performed in Krishna consciousness, can be equipped for the inevitable, no matter which stage the test will come. Thinking of Krishna’s service, instruc-tions, pastimes, and chanting His holy names, is purely spiritual activity, and the devotee who adjusts himself to these activities, no matter what external changes come about, is one who is best prepared for full-time devotional service. It is not necessarily the one who is always physically active, because the physically active soul may also be constantly thinking of himself or sense gratifica-tion.

Of course, when one is under physical distress, it is more difficult to remember Krishna. One’s mind is always thinking of ways to relieve the distress, hoping that some material adjustment will help one gain the relief. That is where our training in “Krishna consciousness” comes in.

Now is the time for you to put into effect all the training that you’ve undergone during the last 7 years of devotional service. Please now apply yourself to the process with faith, and by your devotion invoke Krishna’s decision as to how you can best serve Him now and in the future.

This the best blessing I can offer to you at this difficult time in your life.

Other than that, still, you must be dutiful, and because that body has been given to you by Krishna for His service, you must make an effort to treat it according to the means which is available to you. Please take some treatment, but do not waste time in executing your primary duty in Krishna consciousness, which is to fix the mind upon Krishna.

I do not know what methods of treatment are available to you in Murmansk. d. d. in St. -Petersburg, to my knowledge, is also taking treatment in a local hospital, as I have instructed her to do so. Similarly, you may also need to take help from local physicians. Do not leave your physical problems unattended.

I hope that this letter meets you in improved health. Please keep me informed if there are any serious changes, both in terms of improvement or depreciation.

Hoping this meets you in improved health.

## Letter #92 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Regarding your last letter, I am certainly concerned about your confusion. It seems that the Sukharevo temple is not providing the education and training that devotees require if they are expected to make sacrifices for their temple. I do expect more in the way of training, but I am also painfully aware of the financial struggles so many of the temples (like Sukharevo) are facing. In some cases, therefore, I am recommending devotees to live outside the temple, maintain themselves independently, but they must adjust their schedules and priorities so that they can take part in the temple’s devotional activities and training programs being offered at certain temples. Maybe someday Sukharevo will offer such training, but it is clear that it is not offering it now. Moreover, although Begovaya temple is offering training, the temple cannot provide living and maintenance for devotees attending their training programs. It is like this in many temples. At this moment, I cannot even think of a temple where I could recommend you to go.

Therefore, I strongly recommend that you complete your uni-versity education, but do it without sacrificing your Krishna con-sciousness.

Live with other devotees, attend temple programs, and or-ganize your schedule so that you can attend the next *Bhaktin* program in Begovaya, which should begin in approximately three months.

You should not consider it a step backward, but in fact, you should see that whatever Krishna consciousness you’ve gained by your surrender should now be your foundation for moving further forward in Krishna consciousness. Lord Caitanya wants us to preach Krishna consciousness to everyone we meet. This is what protects our Krishna consciousness, no matter where we are, what we are doing, who we are surrounded by, etc., etc. You can tell others about Krishna in school. That will give you all the strength and realizations that you need to make progress in Krishna consciousness. I have no doubt that if you can master the art of being Krishna conscious while continuing your education, then Krishna will help you to find your “place” in ISKCON. ISKCON reaches much farther than life in a temple. ISKCON reaches out to everyone who takes up Srila Prabhupada’s and Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s order to preach the glories to Sri Krishna to the forgetful conditioned souls. There is more to ISKCON than simply preaching about ISKCON. We must tell others about Krishna.

Just last week in a class that I gave in Smolensk, I was preach-ing very strongly that devotees do not need to package Krishna consciousness to make Krishna attractive. Krishna is already all-at-tractive. We may try to package Him by presenting something other than Krishna consciousness, but what is the value if people open the fancy package and cannot find Krishna? We have to depend upon Krishna, and present Him as He is. He is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He is the Lord of all living beings. He greatest opulence is the love He displays towards His unalloyed devotees. The Lord wants to share this love with each and every one of His parts and parcels, but the forgetful living entities are turning away from Him in pursuit of worldly pleasures.

We must try to turn other people’s attention to Krishna. When Krishna sees us making this effort, no matter where we are, no matter what our external duties are, He is very, very pleased, and He blesses us with a mission and not only a place within ISKCON, but a place in His eternal abode. Please remember this always, and take shelter of these instructions. If you act with this faith, Krishna will always give you protection from the dangers of this material world.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #93 (—&gt;NS)

PAMHO! AGTSP!

Please excuse me for disturbing you but we’ve got a problem. In our *yatra* there are some devotees, who possess super sensual perception, I mean, that they are perceive some subtle energies (they can for example to cure someone from disease; can see future; can directly perceive some special instruction from the spiritual master, can understand his confidential desire; Srila Prabhupada also give them some sign, speaks to them and so on). I'm T. P. and my problem is that the some other devotees hear such talks and take all kinds of such nonsensical instruction as their guideline in their lives and also propagate this ideas. Please kindly say what do you think about such things and give your answer for this reason so that I can have your authoritative opinion and will be able to preach to the devotees in this way. ## Letter #94 (NS—&gt;, response to #93)

Hare Krishna. I meet these extra sense people all the time in the course of my travels in the former Soviet Union. I am 100% convinced that 99. 99% are complete quacks and even if they have some potency for extra sensory perception it is absolutely useless for Krishna consciousness. They are simply motivated for name, fame and a following. That is why they advertise themselves. If they were actually Krishna conscious, they would not advertise themselves nor their abilities.

Moreover, In the 11th Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam, in Krishna’s instructions to Uddhava, Lord Krishna states:

“Learned experts in devotional service state that the mystic perfections of yoga that I have mentioned are actually impediments and are a waste of time for one who is practicing the supreme yoga, by which one achieves all perfection in life directly from Me. ”

And if you really want to expose these people then just read to those who are being misled by these people the following crystal clear statements made directly by Srila Prabhupada from a lecture in 1968:

“Yoga practice is, therefore... It is more or less material activity.

Because when they are powerful to show some miracles and people become captivated, Oh, he is performing such miracle thing. In Benares in India there was a yogi. His business was anyone who will go there, he immediately produced two or four *rasagullas* and offer him. And many hundreds and thousands of educated men became his disciple simply for the matter, *rasagulla,* which is only four annas worth. So people want to see this jugglery. And those who want following some or some material achievement, they want to show... Actually it is a fact.

Suppose if I could manufacture *rasagullas* by some mantra, Hare Krishna mantra, oh, thousands of people will come immediately.

You see? People want to see me, and those persons who want to have a cheap following, they want to show such jugglery. But a devotee sees... (break)... is not of that mentality. They will simply, humble servant. They are satisfied by serving the Lord. That is devotees position.

So your statement, that Why one should bother with these things? That’s a very nice proposal. Why?

There is no necessity. Suppose if I can manufacture some *rasagulla*, what is the worth of this *rasagulla*? Oh, we can, if we spend ten cents, we can make it. So why shall I waste my energy for manufacturing *rasagulla* in the yoga system?

Actually, therefore, Krishna says that the perfection of, real perfection of yoga, the first-class yogi is he who is always thinking of Krishna.

That is first-class. ”

## Letter #95 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

In answering your questions I will preface my answer by stating that there is no absolute law about how to conduct the *Bhakta program.* As I already mentioned inspiration based upon preaching is the most important. Moreover, just what you will do daily, and how you will deal with the individuals under your care will vary according to the individuals themselves. There will be *bhaktas* and there will be guests. And their level of commitment will also be quite different from one another. You always have to be far enough “above” the situation so that you can see how to bring out the best in each individual. Don’t be so attached to the structure that you miss out on the goal. On the other hand, there must be some structure, otherwise both you and those dependent upon your direction will flounder with no clear definition of what needs to be done. Therefore, as much as possible, your assistant should maintain the structure, which you should definitely take part in as well. But, at the same time, you should always be able to step out of it, so that if need be, it can go on without you while you either preach to a guest, counsel a *bhakta,* go out on a preaching engagement, meet with temple authorities, study, or whatever.

Obviously, however, you must also make sure that your assistant gets his time away from the *bhaktas* as well, and gets time for himself. Otherwise, he probably won’t last very long. Your ability to inspire your assistant to be committed will depend upon your own example. You must show him that you are committed as well, and that your time spent on other affairs is always directly related to the well-being of the program.

I must tell you that my concept of what a *bhakta* program is today, and what it was almost 20 years ago when I first started it in Boston is quite different.

Although I appreciate the need for a well-structured *Bhakta program,* as described in my previous message, I also expect that the *bhakta* leader should also develop the depth in his preaching to be able to preach to, inspire, and to be exceptionally careful to not demean independently-minded and intelligent people who are not necessarily *Bhakta program* material.

Whether or not the preaching in is so departmentalized to the degree that it will be somebody else’s responsibility to deal with such people, I cannot say. I suspect not. But, in my humble opinion, the *bhakta* leader should become well-rounded enough in his preaching that he is capable of inspiring and in some cases, “empowering” capable people to engage in devotional service even though they are not ready to conform to the rigidity of temple life. I suppose that to a large degree, I was not capable of doing that 20 years ago, and that’s why I was not encouraged to do it.

But I would like to see you come up to that standard.

Now, to your questions.

1\) Best if the *bhakta* leader has separate facilities, and the assistant stays with the *bhaktas.*

2)Harinama as much as possible.

3\) Enough so that they had some clearly defined responsibility each day, but they were never “possessed” by any other temple authority.

Again, it depends on the maturity of each *bhakta.*

## Letter #96 (—&gt;NS)

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhupada! All glories to Sri Sri Guru & Gauranga! All glories to Your service for Srila Prabhupada and Sri Sri Nitai-Gauranga!

I think about choice of *guru,* and I have two questions:

1\) Tell me please, what should I be guided at choice of *guru?*

2\) How can I know, that this choice is good for me personally?

## Letter #97 (NS—&gt;, response to#96)

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am not sure why you have directed your questions to me. But maybe you’ve sent them to everyone on COM, and I am just one of many recipients to your inquiry.

Whatever... The simple answer to your question is that you should constantly pray to the Lord in your heart, begging Him to allow you to become His servant by directing you to the lotus feet of a qualified *guru.* If you are genuinely sincere in this prayer, and you really want a bona fide *guru,* the Lord will help you and will reveal to whom you should surrender, and in whom He will manifest Himself to guide you on your path back home, back to Godhead. If you have no such constant prayer as the foundation for your search for a *guru,* you will have no qualification to recognize one.

## Letter #98 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

"I'd like to ask you to allow me to write to you more often. ”

Yes. You may write to me more often. At least you could write to me more often than you have been writing to me in the past. But please understand that I am already getting a lot of mail, and when I travel as much as I usually do, it will often take me time before I can answer your letter. Also, knowing my own writing habits, you will likely get a reply faster if your letter does not have too many questions. If letters are long, and complicated with a lot of questions, I am usually forced to wait for an appropriate time when I can give adequate thought, and that may take as long as three months before I can get such an opportunity.

\*\*\*

“Why a person, who previously sincerely chanted the holy names, distributed books and served the Deities, now is starting to act as an enemy towards devotees? What is the cause? Please, explain me. ”

It is due to attachments and previous offenses. In the Caitanya Bhagavata it is stated that when one someone is envious of Krishna it is due to previous offenses.

*kamsadiha atma krsne tabe himse kene?*  
*purva-aparadha ache tahara karane*  
*(CB. Adi 7. 58)*

“One may ask: Why do Kamsa and the other demons try to kill Krishna? The answer is: Previous offenses is the cause of those actions. ”

*sahaje sarkara mista, —sarve-jane jane*  
*keha tikta vase, jihva-dosera karane*  
*(CB. Adi 7. 59-60)*

“Sugar is sweet. That everyone knows. If someone tastes it as bitter, the reason is that his tongue is at fault. The tongue is at fault. The sugar is not at fault. The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Caitanya, is the sweetest of all. ”

Envy of Krishna, or of Krishna’s devotees, who are fully dedicated to the Lord’s service, can only be due to previous offenses. Because at some time your husband offended the Lord, the devotees, or both, he is now in a position of acting as an enemy towards the devotees. There are many examples in *shastra* of even devotees who acted as an enemy towards another devotee due to attachment. For instance Prajapati Daksa acted as an enemy towards Lord Siva, and he even cursed Narada Muni. Why? Because of pride and attachment. He felt his attachments were threatened by the purity of these devotees.

Of course, that is one explanation. But another explanation, when seen in relation towards ourselves, is that if someone behaves as an enemy towards me, then I should try to see it as due to my own past misdeeds. Such a mentality for a devotee, towards himself, is purifying, and gradually captures the Lord’s attention. As Srila Prabhupada writes in the *Bhagavad-gita* purport:

“A pure devotee is never disturbed in any circumstances. Nor is he envious of anyone. Nor does a devotee become his enemy’s enemy; he thinks, ‘This person is acting as my enemy due to my own past misdeeds. So it is better to suffer than to protest. ’ In the Srimad-Bhagavatam (10. 14. 8) it is stated: *tat te ’nukampam susamiksamano bhunjana evatma- krtam vipakam.* Whenever a devotee is in distress or has fallen into difficulty, he thinks that it is the Lord’s mercy upon him. He thinks, ‘Thanks to my past misdeeds I should suffer far, far greater than I am suffering now. So it is by the mercy of the Supreme Lord that I am not getting all the punishment I am due. I am just getting a little, by the mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. ’ Therefore he is always calm, quiet and patient, despite many distressful conditions. A devotee is also always kind to everyone, even to his enemy. ” Bg 12. 13, Bg 12. 14.

Of course, this is a very advanced position, which cannot be imitated. But at least we should know that that is the ideal, and we should always try to see things for our own purification, thus making us more and more dependent upon the Lord.

\*\*\*

“Or am I just foolish and naive and now no one is to be trusted? ”

It is always possible to confide in someone. But there must be trust. It is not foolish to trust, but one should not foolishly trust. There should be clear evidence of why you should trust based upon a devotees ability to be able to strictly follow. Trust is not cheap and it takes time to cultivate. But once you have it, it is a very valuable gift to be treasured.

\*\*\*

“I do not know how to improve my position. What should I do in this situation? ”

Devotional service is not something external. Even if you change your external situation and move near a temple, there is no guarantee that your situation would improve. I'm not saying this to discourage you from trying to make this change. But, the internal change is more important, and that can take place anywhere. Krishna will put His devotees in difficult situations to help them increase their attachment to Him. Attachment arises from the degree that we actually take shelter of hearing about His wonderful pastimes with His devotees. These pastimes are a transcendental reality which give solace to a devotee, no matter what condition he is in. For a devotee, there is no worse suffering than to be away from the association of devotees. Why? Because devotees are always speaking about Krishna. That’s why we always want to be in their association. Because we have some *lobha,* some greed for hearing. If we see that suffering properly (the absence of devotee association), then we will immediately prepare ourselves for devotee association through prayer, and remembrance of the Lord by reading. Force the mind to think of Krishna, and within your heart, pray to Krishna, every day, not to relieve the suffering, but to give you the association of those devotees who will help you to remember Him. That type of cultivation takes determination, and I will try in this letter, to give you the necessary strength to sincerely pray in this way.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #99 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Thank you very much for your nice, sincere letter.

\*\*\*

“Guru Maharaj, I will be very obliged if You kindly give me Your advise on this so I can act accordingly from now on.

My heart really is in living with an ISKCON *sadhu* as servant for some months or years and being nicely trained. ”

To become a personal servant and to be sheltered by a senior Vaishnava is great mercy and if it is available, then by all means, it is the best position for any *bramachari* to be in. I would always give my blessings to you for adjusting your life in order to get this, but in this particular case, you have been given an order by me to serve there in..., and to do something (no matter what the order was) contrary to a direct order, which I personally gave you, I would expect you to come back to me first before making any changes.

There are many times when my disciples are given services by their local authorities, and there are also many times when I might have given some approval for a disciple to fulfill a service which they might have proposed either to me or to a local authority. In both of those cases, I would be “less” concerned (though still concerned) about bringing the matter back to me before changing. But in your case, and in any case with any disciple, when a clear instruction was given, then I would expect to be consulted—in all cases—before making any changes.

Moreover, as I hope you would remember, the reason why you were not sent to Prabhu in the first place was because he was not able to give you the time to oversee your activities in He clearly told me that he could not do it, but if you were to go to ..., whenever he was there, he could check up on you. There is big difference to “being checked up on” every once in awhile, and getting shelter and personal training from a senior Vaishnava— the second being what you have proposed. I am not very inspired to see my *bramachari* disciples living independently in..., or anywhere else for that matter, under no authority, and if that would be the result of your leaving..., then I would not be very happy about it.

However, I would release you from your present obligations in ..., and would be happy to allow you to remain in..., only if you were to become a personal servant and to be sheltered by a senior elsewhere in If you remember, I had told you that if you decided to leave after some short time, then I would insist that you return to... and resume duties there. This is still my inclination unless Prabhu or somebody else who also can be trusted, is willing to take you. You will have to do the research yourself and let me know the results of your findings.

I do not want you to think that I do not trust you. My main concern is that you have a clear understanding what my expectations are from you, and that you understand why I have these expectations. Contrary to the points in your letter, I have not felt that you have been burdening me with small things. In fact, you have had so much freedom and license to use your independence and intelligence in so many different service capacities without me, or anyone else, standing over you, because I do trust you.

I will wait to hear the results of your research and your decision based upon those results.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #100 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your personal report and a report of those whom you are counseling. I am very happy to hear that you are preaching enthusiastically and that you are finding satisfaction in this most important service. Actually, just this morning, I was preaching to a few leaders in the local temple which I just visited, explaining to them that unless our leaders are preaching, feeling some satisfaction in preaching, and are strictly following the simple process of *sravanam kirtanaam* in the association of their dependents, then no matter what management formulas they put together for spreading Krishna consciousness, they will not work. *Hari katha* is the life breath of Lord Caitanya’s movement, and without the breath of life, there is no meaning to the body. Similarly, without *Hari katha,* there is no meaning to management. Please, therefore, always remember this in your preaching, and you will certainly realize the meaning of the word success in Krishna consciousness.

Regarding my travel plans, unfortunately, I do not have any plans for traveling to any of the regions in Russia this year, other than those to which I have already traveled. I had to spend an extraordinary amount of time this year taking treatment for my health, and unfortunately, this cut into some of the time which I would have used for these travels. However, certainly the devotees can expect a visit from me next year, although I cannot say yet when that will be. I should say, however, that Vishnu Tattva dasa begged me to visit Barnaul the next time I visit Siberia, instead of Novosobirsk, if I have only enough time to visit one of the two cities. I'm wondering what your opinion is of this. Would my disciples in Novosobirsk be able to attend lectures in Barnaul?

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #101 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

“Writes to you of das. I have received dedication from you in Divnomorsk. Yours of divine grace, I wanted to ask you to solve to establish houses *murti* Gaura-Nutai. I very much need in service to home deities. ”

If there is a valid reason why you are not worshiping the Deities in the temple and regularly attending the temple programs, then I can permit you to worship Gaura Nitai deities at home.

I would consider the following as valid reasons:

1\) your body is too unhealthy, you cannot live in the *ashrama,* and you are living too far from the temple;

2\) you are preparing for household life, and you are working independently from the temple in order to be able to support a family in the future. And you are also living too far from the temple;

3\) you are already married, working independently, and cannot attend the temple programs daily;

4\) in consultation with the temple authorities, it has been decided that you should live as a congregational member rather than as *bramachari,* and you are living too far from the temple to attend the temple programs daily.

If you are living close to the temple and you can attend the morning programs, then I would definitely recommend Temple worship above home Deity worship. Or, at least, if you feel the need to have Deities in your home, and you are close enough to the temple, you should not use your home Deities as an excuse for not worshiping the Temple Deities and attending the Temple programs.

Also, you should never live with a woman to whom you are not married. I cannot encourage such activity, and I would not encourage it by giving permission to worship Deities together under those conditions.

Not knowing the reasons why you are asking me to worship Deities at home, I have therefore given the above explanation for you to determine yourself whether or not I would approve of your worshiping Deities at home.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #102 (Premananda d. —&gt;NS)

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you and to Srila Prabhupada.

Last month, I wrote you with a request for a possible lecture at Harvard as part of Nitai Charran Prabhu’s monthly Chaitanya Vaishnava Society speaker/film series. You said at the time that we shouldn’t count on your having the time, but I'd like to pass along the request again.

In my heart for some time I have cherished the notion of having you speak at an area university about your experiences preaching in the former Soviet Union. I think it would make a dynamic presentation of great interest to students of religion or Russian history and culture. I envision the title of the talk being something like: “The Rise of Religious Diversity in Russia: Karl Marx Meets the Flare Krishnas. ”

This perhaps wouldn’t be so much a philosophical presentation, but a sociological or historical one, tracing the history of ISKCON in Russia and the CIS from the Communist days to the present, discussing the changes and challenges at each stage. Perhaps some of your videos from Russia would be useful as part of the presentation. Discussion of the current state of religious freedom in Russia would be a natural (the registration laws and some of the groups that have been refused registration —I heard the Scientologists were recently rejected).

As for scheduling, we were thinking about Thursday, November 18 at 8 p. m.

Do you think this will work?

## Letter #103 (NS—&gt;Premananda d.; response to #102)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I'm sorry but I still do not know when I will be in Boston. It is highly unlikely that I will be there by November 18th. Please do not schedule this lecture.

I think it is important that you understand my primary purpose for visiting North America. It is no longer possible for me to even think of my preaching in North America as a means for reaching out to the nondevotees. Due to the demands on me over here, I stopped thinking that way several years ago. I must leave the outreach up to those who are there preaching on a regular basis, both senior and junior devotees alike. It is for this reason that I have been trying to stop accepting disciples outside of the former Soviet Union. I simply cannot give any more attention than what I am already giving—and even that is not enough.

Now when I go to North America it is primarily to associate with devotees, both those living in the temples and those living outside the temples. It is for this reason that I go to North Carolina, Canada, and even Hartford. I also try to do that in Boston, by simply maintaining those connections which I’ve had for so many years. But, to be honest, I do not even know the names of more than half of the people who now visit the Boston temple. Many have begun coming during the 90’s and are therefore new faces to me. It is my hope that the regular preachers in North America will establish connections with these people (including those preachers who both live in Boston and visit to Boston) and will guide each of them according to their needs.

Beyond that, when I go to North America it is to rest and recover from the hectic pace which inevitably comes when I’m here. As you know, sometimes when I land in Hartford, I literally collapse and can barely move for days.

Traveling is taking its toll on this body, and I’ve come to learn that I cannot push it in the same way that I’ve been doing for the past 15 years.

If I don’t start pacing my activities now, I can’t imagine the kind of condition my body will be in the next 5—10 years.

Therefore I hope to be able to at least continue, without inter-ruption, with my tiny efforts connected with the Boston temple, by giving Srimad Bhagavatam classes, and whenever possible, other lectures in the temple—and by maintaining my connections with the devotees as well as the service of Sri Sri Radha Gopivallabha. Beyond that, at least in my mind right now, anything else is sec-ondary and subject to refusal (such as this request to lecture in the university).

Presently you have Bhakti Vaibhava Maharaja in Boston. He has no managerial responsibilities towards any project in North America. He is an excellent preacher. He loves to preach in the universities, and I am assuming that he has time for such engagements while he’s there. In fact, he gave a week-long seminar in the Odessa University last year which was overwhelmingly appreciated both by the students and faculty. And last, but not least, he also has been preaching in the former Soviet Union for almost as long as I have. Why don’t you ask him to give this lecture? That’s at least one suggestion.

If there is a particular reason why it must be me (which I cannot imagine), then I would not even consider the proposal until I am actually in North America and I know what my schedule will be for that visit. I rarely work out my schedule for N. A. until I arrive. It is for this reason that I do not like making any commitments for any event on any particular date in North America. I deal this way not only with you but with everyone else who tries to pin me down to a date. So please do not think that you are being singled out by me.

I hope that this small explanation helps you to better understand the way that I'm thinking in relation to the preaching in Boston, and in North America in general.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #104 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

“I haven’t heard from you for such a long time and it give me the intuition that you must be very very busy and mobile. I hope that you were satisfied with your *Vyasa-puja.* I remember how the last year ceremony pleased you. ”

The *Vyasa-puja* ceremony and the other three days of lectures, *kirtanas* and dramas, all went very nicely this year. Many devotees, those who were not my disciples, expressed gratitude for the oppor-tunity to participate in such a nice, simple festival. There was a very nice family atmosphere generated and Mahamantra Prabhu’s presence and inspiring lectures did much to greatly enhance the whole event.

What may appear to be materially-motivated decisions are not necessarily so when they are made with an ultimate objective of pleasing Krishna in mind. In householder life, when all one’s material activities are related to Krishna, then such activities become sinless and a part of devotional practice in relation to Krishna. Bhaktivinoda Thakur talks about these points in Sri Bhaktyaloka. For instance, one should not speak about others. This is a spiritual principle. But there are different spiritual principles for those who are renunciants and for those who are householders. Householders have to speak about others because they are engaged in earning, saving, protecting, and maintaining the family. One might say that such activities are material. But they become sinless, when the goal of those activities are aimed at producing a favorable result for hearing and chanting about Krishna. Similarly, it appears you must make some adjustments for the above-mentioned reasons. Then make them for producing a result which is favorable for Krishna’s service, and then they will be spiritual.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #105 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I give my blessings to all of my disciples if they want to be helped by other Vaishnavas. Those blessings are automatically there. According to *shastra,* all Vaishnavas are to be considered *guru.* Of course, there is a special relationship with one’s initiating *guru,* and one must always seek the blessings and guidance from one’s own initiating spiritual master, even if one wants a “special” connection with another Vaishnava.

However, just because somebody formerly accepts disciples does not mean that they are automatically more special than other Vaishnavas who do not accept disciples, and that some kind of “special” connection has to be established with them because they have offered to help you. It may develop into something special, but that would depend upon the kind of instruction they would be giving to you. Not just attention, or an offer, for example, to help you find a husband. If any of my disciples can explain to me the superior instructions they have received from another Vaishnava, and by so doing, also request me to have a special connection with that Vaishnava, then I would likely give my blessings for such a special connection.

In the meantime, as I have explained above, you may certainly take help from as you may from any other Vaishnava.

Certainly, you also have my blessings to keep the proper mental-ity from you visit to the *Holy Dhama,* but that will also depend upon your own determination as well. A visit to the *Holy Dhama* should be made for an intense search for Krishna. All service performed should be for increasing attachment for Krishna. It is Krishna Who can fulfill all of one’s desires. If you pray to Krishna in the *Holy Dhama,* “Please Lord, you know my desires, you know what’s inside my heart, you know best how to bring me to the shelter of Your lotus feet, please, therefore, make me Your servant and do with me as you like. ” If you pray like this in the *Holy Dhama,* the Lord, understanding your heart, will help you and will arrange all that’s necessary for your further progress in Krishna consciousness. He gives His assurance in the *Bhagavad-gita* of this.

If you pray like this, and associate with Vaishnavas in this mentality, then certainly your sister will be benefited by her actions as well.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #106 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have not refused any of Harikesha Maharaja’s former disciples who have approached me for a *shiksha* relationship. And certainly I will not refuse you. The most important aspect of the guru/disciple relationship is *shiksha,* for without instruction what is the meaning of becoming a disciple?

Therefore please try to read the published books of my lectures and instructions, along with Srila Prabhupada’s books, and hopefully these books will help you to make your connection firmer with Srila Prabhupada’s instructions. Although certainly the primary duty of the spiritual master is to help establish his dependent’s connection with the Supreme Lord, still for those of us who are serving under Srila Prabhupada’s shelter, we must always see Srila Prabhupada’s instructions as the guiding light of our lives, always following those instructions with full faith. I am convinced that those of you who have remained faithful to Srila Prabhupada’s mission could have done so based only on your faith in Srila Prabhupada. It is my duty to help reinforce that faith so the connection will remain lastingly firm.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #107 (—&gt;NS)

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhu-pada!

I am approaching you by the advice of my councelor in order to find out your opinion about this question important for me. Recently I’ve got a desire to ask you about an initiation, but I am not sure about my motives. I understand that I should be honest with you and therefore I am writing about my doubts and asking to validate my mentality and to direct me in a right way.

Firstly I will write a little bit about myself. I am 26. I am chanting 16 rounds of Hare Krishna Maha-mantra for 4. 5 years. For the last 4 years I was staying at the *brahmacarini ashrama.* In 1995 I joined the ashram in to stay in. And in November, 1996, by your permission and by your mercy together with the other ladies from *ashram I* moved to the temple in Since that time I am serving and staying at this temple.

Mainly I’m taking care about the flowers for Sri Sri Gaura— Nitai, but lately I’ve started taking part in prabhu’s preaching program and also together with either devotees in we try to start *Bhakti-vriksha* program here.

I chose you to be my spiritual master in August, 1998 and have received your *pranama-mantra* on *Vyasa-puja-98* in Kiev.

Now I’ll try to write briefly about what’s disturbing me.

I may clearly see that my attachment to you as my spiritual master is very weak due to my contamination. One may say it’s practically absent. I realize the mercy of the Lord that He let me to see you as a *guru,* but it comes from my intelligence more than from my heart. When I am getting association of the devotees who sincerely dedicate themselves to your service I may feel that their example inspires me and lets me to understand what I should aspire for. As it happened on your last *Vyasa-puja* in Minsk.

But usually in my service I simply execute my duties before other devotees with whom I stay together. In this way my spiritual life fully depends on my relations with them. Several times I used to have an experience of my spiritual life getting nullified when I leave for some time to my parents place and do not have any connection with devotees. Then I start to read *karmic* books, to watch TV, stop chanting *japa* and even may eat *bhoga.* I know that I shouldn’t let such things happen. But when my desires are contrary to my intelligence I more often do what I wish to do although I understand that I’ll have to suffer in the future.

At the time of initiation a disciple gives promises to the spiritual master and even now I do not have full faith that I’ll be able to follow my promises if my life changes and I do not get a support and protection from devotees inside the temple anymore.

While I am at the temple I get enough inspiration for spiritual life, but from another side it appears to be very difficult for my mind and body to follow all standards and regulations of temple life. For example because of the problem with my health I attended *mangala- arati* very seldom for the last year. Ideally, as I see, it would be better for me to stay out of the temple. I would be able to continue my spiritual life associating with devotees either under the protection of a husband or by earning money to support myself. But at the present moment I do not have such an opportunity for although I have a higher technical education I can’t work in for I am Russian citizen and at my parents place, in I can’t survive spiritually.

I am writing about all these because I understand that I don’t change ashram during the next one—two years I still have to leave from the temple. And I therefore afraid that if the circumstances of my life are tough I may fall in *maya.*

Guru Maharaj, I'd like to tell you that I also think the following way: “While I am at the temple it is easier for me to get initiation

(I've already approached Prabhu and he is not against this) and because I am going to leave from the temple in a not far future it’s better to use this opportunity now. Although it seems to me that I am not ready for initiation from Guru Maharaj, still I should not loose this chance even if it will be just a formality. ”

Generally such ideas pushed me to start thinking about getting initiation.

Dear Guru Maharaj, please let me know your opinion about my situation.

Do you think it is possible for me to be initiated by you? What should I change in my mentality? And how should I further develop my relations with you?

Trying to become your servant,

## Letter #108 (NS—&gt;; response to #107)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your honest and frank letter. Such letters deserve attention and I am grateful that your well-wisher, devi dasi, requested me to consider it serious.

\*\*\*

“Dear Guru Maharaj, please let me know your opin-ion about my situation.

Do you think it is possible for me to be initiated by you?

What should I change in my mentality? And how should I further develop my relations with you?”

Yes. It is possible to be initiated, but just as you have nicely presented, if it is just a formality, will it actually help you later, when you are living away from devotees? That’s really the question you’re asking me. And my answer is that it really should be more than just a formality.

I have oftentimes used the example that an insulated electrical wire connected on the outside may not necessarily be connected inside, where it really counts. Similarly, the connection of some formal initiation may be there, but if the more important connection (attachment to and remembrance of the instructions of the spiritual master) is not there, then progress will be slow and stunted.

It is therefore better to cultivate a stronger connection first, which as you have admitted, is not really there. In general, you are driven by and are influenced by those who surround you. In one sense that is good, because you are feeling at least some pressure to chant your rounds, attend programs and to behave as a devotee should behave.

But mere social obligations to those around you will not carry you through difficult tests which will inevitably come. Such type of association and social obligation helps in the beginning, but as one progresses one must develop a deeper reason for making sacrifices. That’s where the importance of the relationship one has with the spiritual master comes in.

The spiritual master’s instructions are meant to connect our sacrifices to Krishna. Without that connection, how can there be an ultimate goal for our sacrifices? All our sacrifices will ultimately end in disillusionment and frustration, such as sacrifice to “society”, “friends”, “community”, and “family”. These are all words we read a lot in Srila Prabhupada’s books, and we tend to think that it only applies to nondevotee “societies”, “friends”, “communities”, and “families”. But that’s not always a fact. If we are not careful we may fall into the same trap of making sacrifices, but neglecting to put Krishna in the center of our sacrifice.

Therefore we all need some link to connect us, and that link must be felt through instruction. By remembering the instructions of our *shiksha* and *diksha gurus,* it enables us to serve society, friends, community and family in the right consciousness, and it will give us the strength to overcome the temptations of illusory dictations which, if one is not careful, can pull us away from the path back to Krishna.

My instructions are there for you, and you have the power to remember them and to feel my presence in them. Those instructions are more than to just to take care of Sri Sri Gaura Nitai’s flowers, to take part in public programs, and to take part in *Bhakti-vriksa* programs. There is the danger that although all of these services are very nice and important, they can also be performed very mechanically, primarily from the pressure and expectations of those who are depending upon your services. The instructions I give are meant to help you get off the mechanical platform and to perform these same activities in the right consciousness.

*smartavyah satatam visnur*  
*vismartavyo na jatucit*  
*sarve vidhi-nisedhah syur*  
*etayor eva kinkarah*

“Krishna is the origin of Lord Vishnu. He should always be remembered and never forgotten at any time. All the rules and prohibitions mentioned in the *shastras* should be the servants of these two principles. ”

In other words all the duties and obligations you perform are meant to be executed with Krishna’s pleasure in mind. If you remember this, then you should also remember who has instructed you to remember this. Then the connection is there. You will feel some reciprocation from Krishna through that connection, and that experience will help you to remain connected, no matter where you are, and what duties you have to perform, even if some day you are living outside the temple. That is the training which you should be undergoing now while you “are” living in the temple.

Therefore, please, along with your duties, listen to my lectures, read the books which have been published of my lectures and instructions, and always associate with those devotees who help you to remember Krishna because they are helping you to follow my instructions. So now you have a direct instruction to do this, and if you simply try to remember these instructions then initiation will automatically follow.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #109 (—&gt;NS)

Please accept my humble obeisances.

All glories to Srila Prabhupada!

Dear Guru Maharaj, I must admit that I’ve been not practicing *bhakti-yoga* for already a year. I don’t follow *sadhana-bhakti,* don’t offer my food to the Lord and don’t chant Hare Krishna *Maha-mantra. I* joined Hare Krishna being a weak and depraved person and now my low inclinations got stronger.

Whatever happens to me you will forever stay the most dear person to me.

Bowing down to your lotus feet,

## Letter #110 (NS—&gt; response to #109)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have received your latest letter and have noted the contents.

First I want to thank you for writing to me and for being honest and straightforward. I always appreciate honest and straightforward dealings from my disciples, even if it also brings pain to me, as this letter of yours has done. It is naturally painful to hear that you have not been able to continue with the struggle against *maya,* and have succumbed to her dictations.

But there is never any loss in the performance of *bhakti.* Because you are grateful for all that I've tried to do to help you in *bhakti- yoga,* this means that the seed of *bhakti* is still there in your heart. And even though it may temporarily be stunted for some time, it has not been uprooted. In due course of time, it will grow once again, and you will find yourself anxious to practice *bhakti-yoga* in the association of Vaishnavas again. I have no doubt about this.

In the meantime, please be assured that I will always be happy to see you attending *kirtanas,* classes, or festivals, whenever I am nearby, and you should never feel that you cannot be seen by me, even if you are not following the principles of *bhakti. I* can see more than you might think I can see, and I know that the sincerity with which you have tried to practice will bear fruit.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #111(NS—&gt;)

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for sending me this report about Bhakta Slava. I am happy that you and he have good relations, and I hope that they can continue nicely.

Regarding ways to improve your service as counselor, best is to simply care about others’ progress in Krishna consciousness. When genuine feelings of concern are there, then the whole process works nicely, and devotees feel protected. Of course, it’s not that we should pamper the whims of devotees if they should need good instruction, but without feeling that the instruction is being given out of a genuine concern, devotees will be much less responsive to instruction. In the earlier stages of the counselor relationship, this may not be as noticeable. But as time passes, it will become far more relevant for maintaining proper relationships with the devotees you’re caring for.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #112(NS—&gt;)

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your detailed letter about yourself and about your progress in Krishna consciousness. I am always happy to hear people’s story about how they came in contact with Krishna in this life.

Regarding the guidance you’ve asked of me, I am always willing to guide and help people mold their lives around Krishna, but in the beginning stages of the relationship I am dependent upon my senior disciples to help me in this endeavor. My disciples are also working hard, publishing books of my lectures and instructions for the benefit of others.

I therefore request those desiring direction from me to first seek answers to their questions from Srila Prabhupada’s books first and foremost, from my lectures or recorded lectures, from these books which my disciples are producing as well as from my senior disciples.

Under the circumstances, it is not possible to “guide” otherwise, since I have decided to become less accessible by email due to the excessive amount of mail I get, including so much mail from my already initiated disciples. I have been forced to limit my email to zonal leaders and initiated disciples, although I am making this letter to you an exception, since I felt it was so sincere that it warranted a response.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #113(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter which I just received last night. I am very grateful to have such caring disciples as all of you and it gives me great strength.

I have been following a treatment since the middle of February, and I will continue to do so until the beginning of April, at which time I hope to visit Russia. By Krishna’s grace, my health is improving greatly. When the doctors saw me last week they were very surprised to see how low my blood pressure was, and even more surprised to hear that it has been like that for almost two weeks. These doctors are so used to prescribing all kinds of medicines for my problem, that they were surprised to see that by rejecting their medicines I was able to accomplish by diet, rest, and exercise, what they thought only their medicines could accomplish. So that’s the situation right now. The doctor said, “I don’t know what you’re doing, but whatever it is, just keep on doing it! ” So I will continue like this for approximately one more month.

In the meantime, you ask what you can do for me. All of you should serve as a good examples of Krishna consciousness. Become steady in Krishna consciousness and don’t allow external changes, which will always come, to adversely affect your deeper commitment—to glorify and hear about the Lord, each according to your individual means. Use the knowledge gained from your experience and from what you have learned in your studies for helping others to remain strong in Krishna consciousness. This takes time according to each of your capacities, and I do not expect any immediate results. But when you set a good example, others will gradually notice, and they will want to learn what you’ve learned to come up to that standard. Example is also preaching, and it should come “before” you preach. Not after.

But don’t try to flaunt your example. Then it will not have any affect because it will be too motivated. Don’t think, “Oh my Guru Maharaja wants me to be a good example, so I have to get other devotees to notice me and what I'm doing. Otherwise, how can I be good example? ” No. This is not the type of thinking you should have. Just be a good example. Everything else will follow. When you are Krishna conscious you don’t have to prove it to others. It is self-evident. Automatically devotees will notice—because you’re not looking for attention. You are simply looking to please *guru* and Krishna.

I want to bring up disciples as good examples. Then their preaching and the help they can do for me will naturally grow from that.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #114(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your letter delivered to me earlier this month. It is very satisfying to hear how much benefit you derived from your attendance at the *Bhakti sastri* course in Sukharevo. Do not worry. You will never lose the matchless treasure derived from hearing about Krishna. It is eternal gain.

You asked what you can do for me. I have many times cited ex-amples of what devotees ask from others. What did Lord Nityananda and Haridas Thakur ask in charity from those whom they accepted alms? What did Narada Muni ask from Mrgrari the hunter? These and so many other examples help us to understand, at least, help ME to understand, what I should be asking from you. Whatever else I may ask of you should only serve as a basis for this end.

Other than that, you should serve as a good example of a devotee who lives outside the temple, while always keeping herself connected to the temple. Use the knowledge gained from your experience and from what you have learned in your studies for helping others to remain strong in Krishna consciousness. This takes time, and I do not expect any immediate results. But when you set a good example, others will gradually notice, and they will want to learn what you’ve learned to come up to that standard. Example is also preaching.

But don’t try to flaunt your example. Then it will not have any affect because it will be too motivated. Otherwise, how can I be good example? ” No. This is not the type of thinking you should have. Just be a good example. Everything else will follow. ## Letter #115(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

\*\*\*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet!

I need your mercy very much! ”

Ys... d. d.

Just this evening we were speaking from Srila Bhaktivinoda’s *bhajan* “Doyal Nitai Caitanya”, the Most Merciful Nityananda and Caitanya.
