Bhaktivinoda Thakur writes that at the end of life we will see the beautiful forms of Radhe Syama in Vrindavana by only if we first get the mercy of Lord Gaura. And then we spoke from Caitanya Caritamrta, where Krishnadas Kaviraja Goswami explains, “If one chants the exalted holy name of the Lord again and again and yet his love for the Supreme Lord does not develop and tears do not appear in his eyes, it is evident that because of his offenses in chanting, the seed of the holy name of Krishna does not sprout. But if one only chants, with some slight faith, the holy name of Lord Caitanya and Nityananda, very quickly, he is cleansed of offenses. Thus as soon as he chants the Hare Krishna *Maha-mantra* he feels the ecstasy of love for God. ” (CC. Adi 8. 30-31)

We then spoke about a few different subjects but we spoke how Srila Prabhupada explained that the main qualification for Lord Caitanya’s mercy is our desire to have it. Lord Caitanya is very eager to distribute it. All we must do is desire it. We have to learn to cry for it. We must always think ourselves exclusively dependent upon the Lord’s mercy, and that is our qualification for receiving it. We then spoke how the Lord sometimes mercifully puts his devotee in such an exclusively dependent position in order to increase the devotee’s attachment for Him. It is a very wonderful position to be in. It is mercy.

I pray that you will become an object of that mercy.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #116 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for sending me this report. You have a very important service, to see that people who come to us for spiritual guidance are properly taken care of. It is not an easy service and must always be performed with a selfless attitude. We cannot be attached to an idea or a project and manipulate people into our own ideas.

We must always be open, and must look for ways to inspire people to direct what they have, and want, to offer in Krishna’s service; even if it means encouraging them to take initiative to do something that we may not necessarily be in charge of. Of course, there are limits, and we cannot simply encourage everyone to represent our Society to the public if they are not yet mature enough, and have a tendency to misrepresent. But if we are sincere, and if we genuinely care for and extend trust to others, they will have the best chance to become qualified for the service.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #117 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Regarding Sri Chinmoy, since I do not know anything about him, I am unable to make any comments about him.

If I am to judge from the poem you sent me, it was not very deep poetry and left me very unimpressed. Especially when I read the poetry of Narottama dasa Thakur and Bhaktivinoda Thakur, and compare it to that which you’ve sent (although I'm sure that there must be something which he’s written which is a little more impressive), there is simply no comparison between the realizations of Gaudiya Vaishnavas and those who have no conception of the *rasas* which exist in the realm of Goloka Vrindavana. In the past, I was a seeker of sorts, and read many different conceptions of Absolute Truth, but I have never come across any teacher or religious system which gives the highest conception of self-realization as that which is given by Gaudiya Vaishnava *acharyas.*

Yes. Meditation within can bring one to a platform of internal peace and tranquility. I do not deny that such meditational practices exist. But what is the goal? Is our goal simply to enjoy peace? Or is it to reestablish our eternal relationship with Krishna, the reservoir of all *rasas?* Having associated with Srila Prabhupada, and having read the works of Gaudiya Vaishnavas, I do not have the slightest grain of doubt that the realization and experience of a personal relationship with God in one of the five primary *rasas* is the highest achievement available to the living entities tormented by suffering in the material world. Having achieved their association, there cannot be any other hankering in my heart for anything other than perfecting this eternal relationship with Krishna, and hearing and chanting about the Lord’s eternal relationships is the topmost means for obtaining that eternal association with the Lord. Meditation must be there, but there must be an object of that meditation. Krishna, the reservoir of all *rasa* is that object, and His pastimes must be revealed and remembered through service to Him in that way.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #118 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I just arrived in Kiev from the United States last week. My health has improved, at least to the point where I can resume traveling. However, it seems that I will have to reduce my traveling if I am to maintain my blood pressure at the proper level. My plan, at least for this year, is to stay longer each city I visit, and to visit much less cities than I normally visit.

Unfortunately, due to these circumstances, I do not have any plans to visit for the immediate future, since such a visit would obviously increase my travel—and not decrease it. I apologize if this causes you, devi dasi or also devi dasi any problem, but it is an event which I did need to warn you about many years ago before you took initiation. I am very concerned that I am not negligent towards my disciples, but having disciples spread out in areas where I cannot visit, means that I must somehow produce instructions which can be disseminated to them through either magazines, books, tapes or some other media. My disciples in Russia are working very hard to produce these materials of my lectures and other relevant instructions, and for now, I know of no better way to communicate to you with “relevant” information than by this method.

Email is a very, very, very difficult medium for me. I often feel like a prisoner, incarcerated in front of a computer, having to read daily dozens of letters. I feel as though I can better serve my disciples and others if I withdraw from email and devote more time to writing, along with limited traveling, and I am seriously considering moving in this direction—although it will not be immediately.

In fact, just yesterday, I wrote a letter to somebody in the United States, asking them about opening up a post office box for me, so that I can gradually switch from email to paper mail, which means there might come a time soon, when I will only be available by posted letters. As I mentioned, I am not going to do this immediately, because I can see that not everyone is ready for that yet. But it is a direction which I plan to move in.

I do think of yourself and ...—of this you can be assured. I often feel pained that I cannot provide you the association which you are anxious to have. But, still, as I mentioned above, I know of no better way to give you the association you need than through my lectures.

From time to time, I can write a letter—such as this one—and try to give you instructions which are relevant according to time, place and circumstance. I believe that I have seriously tried to do that many times in the past. But as you say, you have difficulty understanding my English, and therefore, for whatever reason, my message does not have the impact on you which I had hoped it would have. I am always eager to deliver something to you that you can take firmly, hold onto, embrace, so that it can become a basis for your spiritual life and a source of spiritual strength. This I have tried to do in previous letters. Last November, I remember writing you a letter which took me several hours to write. I tried pouring out as much “relevant” instruction in the most sensitive way I could possibly give to you, in order to offer that which was in my heart for your welfare. To write such letters of that nature takes me a very long time. I try not to be whimsical in my instruction. I do not regret taking so long to write these letters. I am happy to do it. But I cannot devote a major percentage of my life corresponding in this way. Life is too short. My life is too short.

Your life is too short. We must also develop our internal life along with these other forms of communication and responsibilities on the external plane of existence.

Without *bhajan* (*sadhana)* we can never become peaceful. I therefore emphasized also in that letter, methods for performing *bhajan.*

Having home worship is very important for those who live alone at home. It is an essential part of *sadhana* because it is an integral part of one’s daily meditation. Do not diminish the importance of carrying thoughts of service to the Lord in your mind. Your day is made up of a cumulative series of thoughts, from one action to the next. Somehow, you must try your best to force in those thoughts which are purely connected to simple activities aimed at pleasing the Lord—whether it be cleansing the altar, making a garland, bathing the *archa vigraha,* preparing an offering. The more you practice, the more these thoughts will grow, and they gradually begin to envelope more and more of your daily actions. This is part of internal life, and at your stage of life, it is most important to cultivate this internal life.

It is now already very late for me. My lecture ended at 9: 00 this evening, and I intended to take rest just after my lecture. But instead I started to write to you, because you always write urgent as the subject of your message. So I am taking your message as urgent, but, please try to understand that if every message you write to me is “urgent”, gradually the urgency of your message will be diminished.

I hope that you will be able to understand my language in this letter. My feelings are genuine, and I hope that you will appreciate that they are caring. I want you to be happy in Krishna consciousness and to be prepared for the future tests, which are awaiting you as you grow older and hopefully more and more dependent upon the Lord as your protector.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #119(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I'm sorry for not writing you sooner. Email has been extremely difficult to keep up with.

I re-read your lengthy letter describing your pilgrimage to India and have been thinking about your questions.

You wrote:

\*\*\*

“But I have some questions. May be, Guru Maharaj, could you give me the clear answer and dispel my doubts. Should we fight for mercy or simply pray and do nothing? What is the goal of this fight? ”

One who is fortunate receives the mercy of Krishna in the form of the spiritual master. The spiritual master is very merciful upon a deserving disciple. The *guru* trains his disciple in devotional service.

When the disciple is faithful and satisfies the *guru* he receives the *bhakti lata bija or* the seed of devotional service. This is the best thing that a *guru* can offer his disciple. After receiving *guru kripa,* one must repeat his instructions, *sravanam kirtanam.* To sit and only chant is for elevated souls such as Haridas Thakur. One should not imitate great souls such as he. Srila Prabhupada has given us much work to do. Sri Krishna is very merciful to a devotee who is engaged in devotional service and surrenders unto Him. I’ve never read that a devotee should just pray and do nothing.

Therefore, if one wants to be fully engaged, one must fight, but one must always fight against the influence of *maya. Maya* will be there, every step of the way, testing us. Mercy will come, if we fight in this way. The goal, of all of our efforts is to love Krishna—with no other motive than to love Him.

Just this morning I was explaining to one devotee that the residents of Goloka Vrindavana love Krishna without any desire for personal gain. They are not even interested in knowing of Krishna’s opulences. All they know is their relationship with Krishna, and they even think Krishna to be equal to them. This is all by the wonderful influence of Yogamaya. Krishna wants to be loved because He is who He is—not because of His opulences. Just like a very wealthy man, or a very famous man, they often do not have any real friends and are very lonely. They can’t trust anyone, because their so-called friends and lovers are more attracted to their opulences than they are of them. Krishna is never lonely, even though Krishna has all opulences. He is the most wealthy, attractive, intelligent, etc. That cannot be changed because He is the Supreme Being. But He likes to be controlled by those who love Him because He’s Krishna. Such are the affairs of the spiritual world, where the all-attractive person resides eternally with His intimate cowherd damsels and boy friends. Each and every one of us should make that place our topmost goal in life by striving to love Krishna, freed from all other motives except by the exclusive desire to love Him.

\*\*\*

“Why do we have the sense of property during the service? One Mataja and I were making the garlands for the B. B. Govinda Swami’s Deities and I thought that except me nobody would able to do it. And Krishna must be offered only very beautiful garlands. Perhaps I have a lot of proud and selfishness. Guru Maharaj, how many lives should I have to get rid of these qualities. ”

To want to offer Krishna nothing but the best is the desire of a pure devotee.

To think that no one else but myself can offer the best is called pride.

Pride is a stumbling block. It interferes with the surrendering process. By cultivating knowledge one can stop being deluded by pride. Srila Prabhupada once said that “I am cooking the offerings, I am doing the arotikas, I am preaching and distributing so many books, now kindly assist me. ”

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura has sung:

*Sri vigraharadhana nitya nana,*  
*sringara tan mandira marjanadau,*  
*yuktasya bhaktams ca niyunjato pi*  
*vande guroh sri caranaravindam*

“The spiritual master is always engaged in the temple worship of Sri Sri Radha and Krishna. He also engages his disciples in such worship. They dress the deities in beautiful clothes and ornaments, clean Their temple, and perform other similar worship of the Lord, I offer my respectful obeisances unto such a spiritual master. ”

Srila Prabhupada has supplied us with unlimited service to him. We are assisting in his mission, who is assisting the six Goswamis in Lord Caitanya’s mission. When you keep this in mind of being dasanudasa and you are fully absorbed in that mission you won’t have time to be prideful.

Hope this meets you well.

## Letter #120 (NS—&gt; )

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Thank you for writing and explaining your situation.

Wherever you will stay, a primary concern should be, “From where can I get nourishment to strengthen my devotional creeper? ” For instance, there was so much nourishment to be gained in Odessa, but due to circumstances, you were unable to attend. This nourishment should be the goal of all of your work, whether that work is performed in America, Australia, Moscow, Kharkov, or wherever. If you make this your goal, then whether you are in America, Australia, Moscow, Kharkov, or wherever is of less importance to me. I will be satisfied to know that you are not neglecting the goal.

Krishna has given you a talent for painting. Certainly it would be ideal if you could couple this talent with a means of support, for your wife and yourself, which would enable you to paint pictures glorifying the Lord’s pastimes. Your pursuit in this endeavor has my full blessings.

In America there may be scope for that, but then there is a “down-side” to living in America as well. I will here quote from one letter written earlier this year to an aspiring disciple:

\*\*\*

All I do know, is that practically every devotee who goes to America from the CIS goes with the wrong expectations. They foolishly think that they will do a little service for the temple and because it’s an “American” temple, the temple will maintain them. In most cases, this is a completely false expectation because most devotees lose their interest in serving the temple and become more interested living like an American than living like a devotee. Gradually they become absorbed in making money, and once that happens, making money very quickly becomes their primary focus in life.

There is nothing wrong with making money, especially for a householder, but unfortunately, also what happens very quickly to these devotees is that chanting Hare Krishna, hearing about Krishna, and associating with devotees is no longer as important to them as it was to them in the CIS. I pray that this is temporary, and that someday these devotees will again appreciate the importance of these activities. But whether or not that will happen, remains to be seen.

Of course, this phenomena is not only manifest to those who are in America. It can happen anywhere. But I am presenting it to you as a warning. If you take heed of what I’ve written in the first paragraph above, this obstacle can be overcome. One plus in America is that I visit America twice a year. Although I generally do not travel too extensively, still, it would not be too difficult for you to go to where I am visiting (if you maintain that as one goal of your work).

Please keep me informed of the results of your efforts to find a place where you can fulfill all of these objectives.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #121 (NS—&gt;)

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Regarding your letter dated August 20, 2000, all that I can say is that what you were experiencing at the time you had written that letter is common to everyone who has unexpectedly realized that they are eternal servants of Krishna and not meant to remain in this material world. A transformation of values takes place, and a gradual indifference towards mundane pursuits is awakened.

But I strongly recommend that you do not act impulsively with this experience. A life of complete renunciation of all worldliness is usually adopted only after a long period of cleansing the heart.

The cleansing can continue without having to make abrupt external changes. Chanting the holy name is the key, and hearing about Krishna is another. Your life should be molded in such a way that you can maintain both. This is also service, and it should not be neglected.

These things can be and should be discussed with those who are closer with your day to day experiences both in Krishna con-sciousness and in the secular world. Experienced devotees can help you find the right balance so that you do not have to sacrifice your “cleansing” while working to maintain your body. The human body is a suitable vehicle for carrying oneself across the ocean of material existence. If it is maintained in this spirit, then work does not have to be an impediment.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #122 (NS—&gt;)

Hare Krishna. Thank you for your letter dated May 18, 2000. As I mentioned to you in August, I received it through my secretary, dasi, who told me that she informed you of the possible delay and the reasons behind it. I'm sure that you didn’t expect the delay to be this long, but unfortunately, it’s often this way. I simply do not have the time required to answer all the letters I get.

Regarding your questions:

\*\*\*

‘“The woman, created by the Lord, is the repre-sentation of *maya,* and one who associates with such *maya* be accepting services must certainly know that this is the way of death, just like a blind well covered with grass. (SB 3. 31. 40)

Does ‘association’ with the woman refer to materialistic women, or all women? ”

Actually, elsewhere Srila Prabhupada has explained that for men, women are *maya,* and for women, men are *maya.* The point is that it is not so much “women” or “men” as it is the spirit of enjoying the opposite sex. That enjoying spirit is *maya,* and it exists in both the woman and the man. Therefore, in dealings between men and women, there are generally guidelines which need to be followed when associating. There is a license for “enjoyment” between a man and a woman, and that license is called marriage. Association without the enjoying spirit is allowed, but that means that there must be a Krishna conscious objective, and once that’s fulfilled, there is no longer a reason to associate.

There is a common phenomena which is experienced by those coming to Krishna consciousness. When they are new, these guidelines (referred to above) are generally undefined. Relationships may be developed with devotees (including the opposite sex) in a more casual manner, and one grows accustomed to associating on that basis. As time passes, and one develops an interest to become more serious about Krishna consciousness, the relations with devotees of the opposite sex can often become more cautious and less responsive. One begins to wonder what happened. Was it something he/she did wrong? Or is this what it means to become more serious about Krishna consciousness—your KC friends are less responsive to you?

I don’t know if you’ve experienced this phenomena or not, but I'm bringing it up only because there are many who do experience it and at some point, they become bewildered by it.

The main point is that, in principle, in a “cultured” society based upon Vedic principles, men and women do not freely associate with one another because the subtle spirit of “enjoyment” creeps in and thus Krishna consciousness goes far away.

With reference to the above example, the explanation is that this usually happens because the more experienced devotee at some point realizes that his/her primary objective (to initially help someone integrate themselves with devotees) has been fulfilled, and it’s time for them to associate on higher principles (based upon the guidelines referred to above).

\*\*\*

“Does this mean that any man should not associate with any woman, or is there a difference when two are both engaged in service to Krishna? ”

This has already been somewhat answered in the explanation above. For Krishna’s service it is possible. But there are still restrictions—even in marriage, what to speak of for those who are unmarried.

\*\*\*

“And what does ‘accepting services’ refer to? ”

The point is that nobody can accept service without giving something in return. If one accepts service in the mood of enjoyment, then one is “bound” to offer service in return. If the enjoying spirit is there when accepting the service, then the service rendered in return will be motivated by the desire to continue to enjoy the same service. It not rendered for Krishna’s pleasure, and neither is it for the ultimate benefit of one’s partner. Thus one paves one’s path to hell by becoming increasingly entangled into a network of enjoyment and services that have no connection with Krishna.

Of course, in KC married life, it is the duty of the wife to serve the husband, and it is the duty of the husband to accept service from his wife. But the spirit of the husband should be that “I am only accepting this maidservant of Krishna so that we can assist each other in the service of Krishna, and with her I will establish a Krishna-centered family life”. This attitude in marriage is favorable to *bhakti.* Any other spirit of enjoyment leads to suffering and ultimately repeated birth and death.

I hope that this answers your questions and this letter finds you in good health.

## Letter #123 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I do not refuse any of Harikesha Swami’s former disciples who would like to establish a *shiksha* relationship with me. *Shiksha* is most important, for without *shiksha* there is really no meaning to *diksha.* Therefore, my recommendation to you for now is to hear my lectures as much as possible, read from Srila Prabhupada’s books daily, and devote a fixed time daily for chanting the holy names without distractions from external influences. This will depend largely upon your own ability to withdraw the senses and mind from external thoughts related to the maintenance of the body and mind. For this, you must take help from *guru* and Krishna. The mind is very powerful, and the tendency of the mind is to accept and reject thoughts on the basis of sense gratification. When chanting, you must very deliberately reject all such thoughts and as much as possible, bathe your thoughts with the sound vibration of the Lord’s holy names, as well as remembrances of the Lord’s pastimes in Goloka Vrindavana. We are not residents of this temporary material world, but if we want to change our place of residence to the transcendental domain, we must engage our thoughts in the activities of that domain. We cannot jump into a relationship with the Lord or His eternal associates by such meditation alone. But by remembering the Lord’s pastimes with His eternal devotees, we can develop an attraction for our relationship with Him, and thus qualify ourselves to enter into that relationship. Otherwise it is impossible to fully give up our relationship with this material world with all of it’s attachments and sufferings.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #124 (NS—&gt;)

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for your letter. It is very thoughtful and kind of you to write to me on d. d. ’s behalf. Thank you for caring for her.

Regarding my “whereabouts”, I am “out of circulation” (so to speak) for the next week. I will resurface in Prabhupada Village, North Carolina from September 16—19. From there, I will head north making a couple of brief stops at the homes of some devotees, and will arrive in Ottawa, probably on the 22nd. I will go to Boston on the 25th, to catch a flight to California on the 26th, returning to Boston on approximately October 3rd. Since I was already in Boston for 8 days, and since I'm expected in Russia by the 7th of October, I will stay in Boston, probably for 4 days.

Regarding d. d. ’s potential “outpouring”, I believe that her reservations are also based upon her fears that she knows what I will say. She’s approached me before about her dissatisfaction. I tried to stimulate her to make the adjustments she needs internally, since “externally” she is bound into a situation (being single, sponsored, and in ...) and it is not within my power to unbind her from the obligations that come with all of that. She knows that, but probably hopes that I can still rescue her. In that respect, nothing has changed. It is still not within my power to unbind her from these obligations, unless she can realize that she can only be unbound by an elevation of consciousness. In that respect, I will share with her a letter I wrote two days ago to one disciple who is in situation similar to that which d. d. is in:

\*\*\*

Today is Radhastami. I am staying at the home of a devotee family for a little while, and this evening we had a program attended by everyone in the family. We chanted Radhe Jaya Jaya, and then we spoke from Teachings of Lord Caitanya, about the characteristics of Radharani’s love for Krishna. We also spoke about Radharani’s mood as a left-wing gopi. The characteristics of left-wing *gopis* are that they are eager to be jealously angered, they are enthusiastic for that position, they immediately become angry when defeated, they are never controlled by their hero, and they are always opposed to them. The right-wing *gopis,* such as Chandravali, cannot tolerate womanly jealousy, they speak suitable words to their heroes, and they are satisfied by sweet words from them. Krishna is the hero and Radharani is the heroine. Their enjoyment with one another is expanded by unlimited *sakhis.*

There are five different types of *sakhis* who serve Radha and *Krishna—sakhis* (friends), *nitya-sakhis* (eternal friends), *prana-sakhis* (friends as dear as life), *priya-sakhis* (dear friends) and *parama-prestha-sakhis* (most dear friends). There are many different ways that they assist Radha and Krishna. For instance, they always glorify Radha and Krishna, they create situations in which Radha is attracted to Krishna and vice versa, they induce both of Them to approach each other, they dress and decorate Them for each other’s enjoyment, they create a jovial atmosphere, etc.

In this way they derive unlimited pleasure by increasing the pleasure of Radha and Krishna’s meeting. And Radharani derives a pleasure 10 million times greater than the pleasure of her own meeting with Krishna, by sending the *gopis* to asso-ciate with Krishna directly. Such are the affairs of Vrindavana, the transcendental realm, where the eternal residents know nothing other than their relationship Krishna, the beautiful, blue cowherd boy, Who, although He is the Lord of every-one, He is very eager to subordinate Himself to the love of his unalloyed devotees. Krishna is so much bound by that love, that He even becomes bewildered by that love, even though He is fully omniscient. Both bewilderment and om-niscience exist simultaneously in Krishna in His pastimes in Goloka.

Just tonight we were reading how Krishna became bewildered when Radharani became angry at Krishna, because She had heard that Krishna was with Candravali when He was supposed to be with Her. Krishna later returned to Radharani’s kunja, and begged to be forgiven. But He was not allowed in to see His beloved consort. Both Lalita and Visakha, prevented Krishna from entering. Krishna did not know what to do. He was feeling such separation from His beloved Radharani. Even though it seems impossible for Krishna’s omniscience to be covered over. Who or what can possibly cover over Krishna’s eternally perfect omniscience? For the sake of increasing Krishna’s ecstasy, *prema* makes Krishna forget His actual identity. Radharani is the highest embodiment of such *prema.* She made Krishna cry for her love. Such is the power of her pure love.

These are the eternal loving affairs in which every devotee can take part eternally. In these loving affairs, there is no impurity. There are no selfish thoughts. There is complete and unlimited absorption in ecstatic thoughts of the Lord and His eternal associates. There is no better way to become free from the entanglements of illusion than to submit our minds and our words to these affairs in the association of devotees who genuinely relish such discussion. The more we hear about

Krishna, the more the desire in our hearts become open to the reality of real eternal service.

I have no other answer to offer regarding your situation, at least not right at this moment. My prayers are with you, praying that Srimati Radharani will take you under Her protection, and that She will give you the opportunity for the eternal service you are praying for.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

I realize that’s it’s possible that the external circumstances that d. d. is presently in are (being alone much of the time) is not as favorable as possibly some other place where she could get more opportunities to hear and thus change her consciousness. But there is no perfect external situation anywhere within this material world. It’s also quite possible that she could have the best possible situation for hearing, and her consciousness would be absorbed in making external arrangements, therefore rendering her ability to hear to practically nil.

If you (or she) were to suggest that any situation would be better than the one she has now, I would agree that devotees need to be situated externally in the most advantageous position so that they can hear. Instead of pouring out their problems to their spiritual master, they should be eager receptacles to take in what is “most” needed from him—Krishna consciousness. I don’t think that any serious devotee would disagree with this.

But for arranging these situations, besides depending upon Kri-shna, I am very much dependent upon the greater community of devotees to provide her the support that she (and so many others in situations similar to her) needs. I am not in the position to move my disciples from one place to another, taking responsibility for their physical maintenance for each place that they go. Although, in the past, in some emergency cases, I tried to get involved (as I did with when she was so distressed in... ), but I have since completely stopped doing this. I believe that d. d. was the last disciple that I got involved with in this way, by making inquiries on her behalf.

However, my experiences in this regard (both prior to and including... ) have led me to a very deep conviction, that devotees need to take responsibility for these matters themselves, taking whatever help they can find from those whom they have some trust.

… d. d. is not bound there in by any instruction from me. As far as I am personally concerned, she is free to go wherever she wants. I would not interfere. I have already told her this.

If... d. d. is capable of understanding all that I’ve said above, (if necessary, with a little help from her friends), then from that point, I am perfectly willing to allow her to open herself up to me without her having to think that her outpourings would be too burdensome for me. Disciples who want to advance in Krishna consciousness are not a burden. It is my duty to help them, just as much as it is my duty to inspire them to want to advance in Krishna consciousness, which is the main thrust of this letter.

Again, thank you very much for your sincere efforts to help… d. d. by writing to me. Please be assured that I took no offense, whatsoever, from your letter.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #125 (NS—&gt;)

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Sorry for the delay. It’s hard to keep up with email while traveling.

In reference to some other more favorable circumstances for being able to hear and change her consciousness, you said that “there is no perfect external situation anywhere within this material world. ” From this statement, I, perhaps wrongly, inferred your meaning to be that even for spiritual advancement there is no perfect external situation. My understanding is that material happiness is not possible, therefore there is no perfect external situation that can render such happiness. However, at any given moment, those external circumstances in which we find ourselves, and which are imperfect to give us material happiness, do give perfect opportunities for our spiritual advancement because those perfect circumstances are arranged by perfect Krishna. So, it seems to me that we are always in perfect external circumstances for our spiritual advancement. Would you mind resolving this apparent conflict for me so that I can know whether my understanding is correct?

If Krishna is involved, then it’s not external. Krishna’s only contact with the external energy is through the glance of Mahavisnu. Otherwise, whatever arrangements made for His devotee is made through the Lord’s internal energy. The external energy may certainly act in a favorable way for a devotee, but under thoughtful analysis, one will see that it is actually the “internal” energy.

If you read through the following verse and purport from SB, Canto 3, Chapter 1, verse 16, you’ll find Srila Prabhupada’s complete explanation on this subject:

TRANSLATION. Thus being pierced by arrows through his ears and afflicted to the core of his heart, Vidura placed his bow on the door and quit his brother’s palace. He was not sorry, for he considered the acts of the external energy to be supreme.

PURPORT. A pure devotee of the Lord is never per-turbed by an awkward position created by the external energy of the Lord. In *Bhagavad-gita* (3. 27) it is stated:

*prakrteh kriyamanani*  
*gunaih karmani sarvasah*  
*ahankara-vimudhatma*  
*kartaham iti manyate*

A conditioned soul is absorbed in material existence un-der the influence of different modes of external energy. Ab-sorbed in the false ego, he thinks that he is doing everything by himself. The external energy of the Lord, the material nature, is fully under the control of the Supreme Lord, and the conditioned soul is fully under the grip of the external energy. Therefore, the conditioned soul is fully under the control of the law of the Lord. But, due to illusion only, he thinks himself independent in his activities. Duryodhana was acting under such influence of the external nature, by which he would be vanquished at the ultimate end. He could not accept the sound advice of Vidura, but on the contrary he insulted that great soul, who was the well-wisher of his whole family. Vidura could understand this because he was a pure devotee of the Lord. In spite of being very strongly insulted by Duryodhana’s words, Vidura could see that Dury-odhana, under the influence of *maya,* the external energy, was making progress on the path toward his own ruination. He therefore considered the acts of the external energy to be supreme, yet he also saw how the internal energy of the Lord helped him in that particular situation. A devotee is always in a renounced temperament because the worldly attractions can never satisfy him. Vidura was never attracted by the royal palace of his brother. He was always ready to leave the place and devote himself completely to the transcendental loving service of the Lord. Now he got the opportunity by the grace of Duryodhana, and instead of being sorry at the strong words of insult, he thanked him from within because it gave him the chance to live alone in a holy place and fully engage in the devotional service of the Lord. The word *gata-vyathah* (without being sorry) is significant here because Vidura was relieved from the tribulations which trouble every man en-tangled in material activities. He therefore thought that there was no need to defend his brother with his bow because his brother was meant for ruination. Thus he left the palace before Duryodhana could act. *maya,* the supreme energy of the Lord, acted here both internally and externally.

\*\*\*

“I'm very curious to know why it is such a bad idea for a spiritual master to get involved in arranging material adjustments for his disciples. ”

It’s not that the spiritual master cannot help his disciple by making some adjustment for him/her. But the disciple should not expect this from the spiritual master. The disciple’s faith in the spiritual master should not be based upon his ability to make material arrangements for his disciples. Otherwise, there is really no meaning to this kind of relationship. The disciple’s faith must be based upon the spiritual master’s ability to dispel the influence of illusion. When faith in the spiritual master is based upon this type of relationship, then there is no material obstacle, which cannot be overcome by the disciple.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #126 (NS-&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Thank you for writing. Although it may not seem so important to relate that which you had written about to me, I take it as important. So do not think it is a botheration for me to hear about your work and other activities. Of course, these things are temporary, and the real substance of our lives lies in those activities which are eternal. But, if we try to keep connection with those who remind us of the eternal, no matter how we keep the connection, we will gain some positive fruit.

Krishna is eternal. His cowherd boy friends and the *gopis* are eternal. His abode is eternal. His pastimes are eternal. His transcendental body is eternal, and our relationship with Him is eternal. Krishna wants us to enjoy an eternal relationship with Him in Goloka Vrindavana, and Lord Caitanya has appeared to make us dance in ecstasy, realizing one of the four mellows of loving devotional service found in Goloka Vrindavana.

Just the other day, I was giving the example that the residents of Goloka Vrindavana love Krishna without any desire for personal gain. They are not even interested in knowing of Krishna’s opu-lences. All they know is their relationship with Krishna, and they even think Krishna to be equal to them. This is all by the wonderful influence of Yogamaya. Krishna wants to be loved because He is who He is—not because of His opulences. Just like a very wealthy man, or a very famous man, they often do not have any real friends and are very lonely. They can’t trust anyone, because their so-called friends and lovers are more attracted to their opulences than they are of them. Krishna is never lonely, even though Krishna possesses all opulences. He is the most wealthy, attractive, intelligent, etc. That cannot be changed because He is the Supreme Being. But He likes to be controlled by those who love Him because He’s Krishna. Such are the affairs of the spiritual world, where the all-attractive person resides eternally with His intimate cowherd damsels and boy friends. Each and every one of us should make that place our topmost goal in life by striving to love Krishna, freed from all other motives except by the exclusive desire to love Him.

… dasi wrote and told me that you are holding a copy of Bhaktiratnakara for me. I would be very eager to get this from you when I visit As it looks right now, I am planning to go to... only, arriving on either September 22nd or 23rd, and I will stay until September 25th. If you could visit then, it would be very nice to see you. If you don’t hear from me again, you can assume that I will be keeping these dates. Could you please also notify dasa for me? He wrote to me in July, and asked what my travel plans were. I told him that I would ask to contact him, but it’s probably easier if you just notify him.

Hoping this meets you and dasa in good health.

## Letter #127 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Just this morning I was having a brief discussion about this with Radhanath Maharaja. Personally, I am against chanting Damodarastakam “during” *mangala arotik.* I never did it until I started attending *mangala arotiks* in the former SU. I’ve always chanted it “after” the prayers to Nrisimhadeva in the morning, after the prayers to Nrisimhadeva at the end of *Sundara arotik.*

Once I asked Swami why it was a policy to chant it during *mangala-arati* in his areas of influence. His answer was that he was saw it done that way in Bombay when Srila Prabhupada was present, and Srila Prabhupada didn’t say anything against it. I was not very convinced, but since the tradition was already established here, I just accepted it.

However, both Radhanath Maharaja and myself are of the opin-ion that *mangala arotik* is for *Gurvastakam* / *Prabhupada pranama* / *Jaya Sri Krishna Caitanya* and Hare Krishna. Damodarastakam should be chanted at a time when most devotees can attend. In Vrindavana, they do it only in the evening “after” *Sundar arotik.* In Chowpatty, they chant it after Srila Prabhupada’s *Guru Puja* instead of the traditional *kirtana.*

So, in my opinion, it can be done either once a day or twice a day, at a time when the most devotees can attend, but, not during *mangala arotik.*

## Letter #128 (—&gt;NS)

Here I’ve learned that many of our devotees are fond of so called system “Reiki”. Some of them completed the first and even the second course in Reiki. Thus they can come into subconsciousness. I am also suggested to go through these courses. I'd like to know your opinion of it. Can these courses help me to make progress in Krishna Consciousness? (They say it helps to open *chakras. )*

And can’t I lose all my realizations going through these courses?

During these classes some of devotees saw the image of Krishna. Can it be true or it’s simply hypnosis? Not only devotees, but also many *karmis* are fond of Reiki. Can’t they negatively influence people’s subconsciousness?

Again and again I bow down to your lotus feet and ask for your mercy.

## Letter #129 (NS—&gt;; response to #128)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

If Srila Prabhupada were present with us today, and he heard that so many devotees were taking shelter of this Reiki, I am 100% certain that he would be furious! Please do not get involved in this and disassociate yourself from anyone else who claims that Reiki is a means for advancement in Krishna consciousness. This philosophy is completely bogus and those who ascribe to will never be able to qualify for pure devotional service until it is given up.

According to Srila Rupa Goswami, advancement in devotional service is dependent exclusively upon the 9 processes of devotional service, beginning with hearing and chanting. If indulgence in anything else, overshadows our attention from these devotional activities, it will cover our devotional attitude.

Visions of Krishna can never be obtained by such mechanical methods as those which are induced by Reiki. Krishna consciousness is not so cheap. Devotee are not reading Srila Prabhupada’s books. That is the problem! If they were, they would read of so many examples given describing the means by which one can get the *darshan* of the Lord.

TRANSLATION. After that, under the shadow of a banyan tree in an uninhabited forest I began to meditate upon the Supersoul situated within, using my intelligence, as I had learned from liberated souls.

PURPORT. One should not meditate according to one’s personal whims. One should know perfectly well from the authoritative sources of scriptures through the transparent medium of a bona fide spiritual master and by proper use of one’s trained intelligence for meditating upon the Supersoul dwelling within every living being. This consciousness is firmly developed by a devotee who has rendered loving service unto the Lord by carrying out the orders of the spiritual master. Sri Naradaji contacted bona fide spiritual masters, served them sincerely and got enlightenment rightly. Thus he began to meditate. (Ref. VedaBase —\> SB 1. 6. 15)

Here in this verse, it is perfectly clear that one must learn such a process from fully liberated souls. It is only by the blessings of such liberated souls that one can get the *darshan* of the Lord. Liberated souls engage in liberated activities. They do not gain followers and prestige by inducing others to indulge in such mechanical processes for altering one’s consciousness.

If it is possible to “wake up” those who have fallen into this illusion, then please try your best. Otherwise, disassociate yourself and go on with your devotional practices of hearing, chanting, and worshiping the Lord in Krishna consciousness, and be assured that you will obtain the perfect result from these practices without being diverted.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #130 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for writing. I am always happy to hear from disciples— especially those who I have not been able to see for some time.

\*\*\*

“So many times I wanted to write you but I haven’t got any progress in my spiritual life and I can’t satisfy you as a disciple should do it. It’s my greatest pain. Now I try to bring my son and daughter, and to serve my husband. Every Sunday we visit our temple. Sometimes we take part in theatrical performance for Krishna. It’s a pity I have practically no time to read books. My husband supports us materially more or less, he’s not so enthusiastic in practicing *bhakti-yoga,* but when he’s got inspiration he chants and reads Srila Prabhupada’s books. The most difficult thing to me now is to live together with my relatives in one flat and to be cold-minded. My mother’s become fanatic Christian and I'm heretic for her and all others. ”

Even if you don’t feel spiritual progress, you can still write. Sometimes just writing to someone who you trust and you know they will listen, helps tremendously—even if there is no response.

I can appreciate that living in the same flat as a fanatic Christian who is your mother must be very difficult for you. I have many disciples in similar situations. You must be strong internally to tolerate the external conditions. Most important is your dedication to chanting. Krishna’s name is no less powerful than Krishna Himself. And He bestows all spiritual benedictions upon those who chant His names with great faith. Don’t ever think that chanting is not important. Chanting will protect you even in the situation you are living in.

\*\*\*

“I try to often associate with devotees, especially with d. d. When we told the devotees about your attitude to reiki, they replied that her questions to you were wrong and that they use reiki only for their health and as a job. It was prohibited to her to read or say something from your letter at Sunday lecture. ”

Someone in Kiev had seen my letter to d. d. and mentioned

something to me about it. I wrote the following response, which may shed further light on my letter to... d. d.:

\*\*\*

I'm not sure if you were aware of the context in which I had written that letter to devi dasi. She informed me that the devotees she was in contact with were preaching to her that Reiki is means for obtaining the *saksat darshan* of Krishna. It was in that context that I became so furious about the way devotees were twisting Reiki’s use, justifying it as a means for spiritual awakening, and therefore becoming more dependent upon Reiki than on the processes of devotional service. Whether or not Reiki is a viable way to treat the physical body, in the same way as Ayurveda, Homeopathy, Naturopathy, or Allopathy are also ways to treat the body, I cannot say. Personally I have no faith in it, whatsoever. But I do not denigrate others who may have a different experience than I have. If Reiki is seen only in that way, and is not misconstrued to be a method of spiritual enlightenment, then I would be much more tolerant of it’s use. Nonetheless, experience has shown that neophyte devotees often have a difficult time making these distinctions. To them, Reiki may appear to them to be an easier approach to enlightenment than chanting the holy names and voluntarily accepting austerities for the pleasure of the Lord.

Because of this reason, I believe that our leaders who do use Reiki as a method of treatment should be extremely careful to enlighten those who may look to their example for their own justification.

Additionally, I must also add that although I am aware of the economic hardships the devotees must go through, still, the devotees should also be careful not to promote Reiki as something spiritual for the sake of economic gain as well. Although I’ve received no indication that this is a fact, I just thought that I should add that one last point.

\*\*\*

“The economical situation isn’t good in our country and sometimes it seems to me that you’re the more respected the more money you can give for the temple. ”

Naturally, we are always preaching against such a mentality. Our leaders should always be careful to not fall into that kind of trap. Spiritual leaders see the potential for devotional service in others and do not judge that potential based upon a material asset. Desire is the most important asset, and it is the duty of the leaders to fan that desire wherever it exists—even in the heart of a pauper. Of course, on the other hand, sometimes devotees are subjective in their assessment of a leader’s attitude towards others, and they may see something that does not really exist. You should be careful to not allow your own subjective opinions to affect your perceptions of the temple.

\*\*\*

“May be for that same reason our devotees sell so many no-Prabhupada’s books. I think we were so lucky to get pure knowledge of pure devotion. And now we can see that people want to know a lot besides Srila Prabhupada’s books. (What is UFO? or Where it is said in Vedas about dinosaurs? ) May be it is normally nowadays. ”

Whatever the reason, ultimately, it is due to lack of faith in the Holy Name, *guru* and Krishna. We must try to inspire faith by our own example and by our own attraction to only those things which are in relationship with Krishna. It is described in *Harinama Cintamani* that our ability to fully taste the Holy Name is eclipsed by the fog of ignorance and the clouds of *anarthas,* just as the ability to fully appreciate the self-effulgent sun is affected by the clouds and fog. The first type of *anartha* is called *hrdaya daurbalya,* or the defects of the heart, of which there are four types. The first is to be attached to those things, which have no relationship to Krishna. The second is to find fault with others. The third is envy. And the fourth is the attachment to recognition. These four defects of the heart prevent us from appreciating the full glory of the Holy Name and thus, if we are not careful, we will become more absorbed in mundane interests.

\*\*\*

“Dear *gurudev,* please excuse me for taking your time.

Hoping it meets you in good health. ”

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #131(—&gt;NS)

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Srila Prabhu-pada. All glories to all vaisnava devotees.

Guru Maharaj I am writing the letter to you because I need your help.

I had a premonition that I will meet different testing or difficul-ties after the initiation.

There is a problem in my family. The problem is concerned with my oldest son One year ago he started to use drugs. It is a kind of a fashion among young people in our town. My husband and I have tried several times to help him, but some time after our attempts he started using them again.

Generally speaking, everything is like a hell for me. I pray Krishna to drag my son out of this swamp. And I don’t lose a hope.

I have been crying a lot. To write honestly, I pray Krishna to take me from this world. I am very tired. Now I sit at my home like a guard dog. Though my chanting, reading and worshiping of the Deities are going on as it was before, but in my heart I feel a pain because of my son. I want to help him very much. But as I see, I need a help myself.

I don’t know what to do. How to overcome this problem? How to help my son? How to remain steady myself?

Again and again I offer my obeisances to your lotus feet

## Letter #132 (NS—&gt;; response to #131)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

… dasa approached me the other day asking if I had received your letter. It seems that he received a letter from someone in and in the text of that there was an inquiry on your behalf. I told him that I did receive your letter, but since I was offline for six weeks at the end of last year, I have not been able to follow up on the correspondence which accumulated during that time.

It is natural for a mother to feel attachment to her son, and to want him to get the best out of his life as possible. The pain which you are feeling is the result of that attachment. But at some point, you have to let go and leave such matters in Krishna’s hands. This does not mean that you should neglect your son. But you must realize that he is an individual *jiva* soul, with a very long history prior to his appearance from your womb. Srila Prabhupada gives the example that family members and friends are just like tourists coming together in a hotel for a short time. Eventually their vacations end, and they return to their respective residences. Similarly a living entity with a long history of conditioning throughout various species of lives has taken birth as your son, and although it is your responsibility to help him become Krishna conscious, you must exercise that duty with a sense of detachment. Externally you must show affection to your son, by showing how much you care, but if you are too attached, it will neither help him nor will it help you. Internally, you must meditate on Krishna’s instructions. Krishna is in everyone’s heart, including your son’s heart. Krishna can change the situation, but don’t think that you have the power to do anything without His help. Ultimately everything is dependent upon Krishna’s decision. All you can do is perform your duty without attachment. As I said in the beginning, your attachment is natural, as a mother, but still, we have all been taught by Queen Kunti’s example, that even a mother under certain circumstances must cultivate detachment. Kuntidevi prayed to sever her ties of affection to her family members. Prabhupada explains in the purport:

“Srimati Kuntidevi was conscious of this fact, and being weak she prayed to be free from such bondage of family affection. The Lord is addressed as the Lord of the uni-verse, or the Lord of the universal mind, indicating His all-powerful ability to cut the hard knot of family affection. Therefore, it is sometimes experienced that the Lord, out of His special affinity towards a weak devotee, breaks the family affection by force of circumstances arranged by His all-powerful energy. By doing so He causes the devotee to become completely dependent on Him and thus clears the path for his going back to Godhead. ” (SB 1. 8. 41)

I am sure that you have already considered these points even before my writing them, but I am nonetheless still writing them to confirm that this is the best approach for you to take in this circumstance. Go on trying to help him overcome his problem, if after making so much endeavor, he still cannot give up this problem, you must simply pray to Krishna that someday your son will remember all the efforts you made to help him, and then simply wait, going on with your duties in Krishna consciousness, chanting, reading, worshiping the Deities. Ultimately your example of Krishna consciousness could save your son. But if you lose your Krishna consciousness, you will be of no help to anyone, including yourself.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #133 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Thank you very much for sending the *Sankirtana* scores from.... I am very happy to hear that the mood for preaching and distributing books has improved over last year’s mood. Preaching requires strength. Preaching also requires faith. Without faith, and subsequently the strength that one gets from surrendering, it is very difficult to go on preaching. As we mature in Krishna consciousness it becomes more and more clear that preaching cannot continue on the external platform. Preaching is not an external engagement, simply accumulating scores and looking at results from an external point of view. If we want to be successful preachers, there must be an internal life as well. This will give us the conviction and strength required to distribute Krishna consciousness because we, ourselves, know the real value of Krishna consciousness.

Just last night in my talk here in Boston I was telling the story of the disciple who, after receiving instructions from his spiritual master, further asked his spiritual master for the “most secret” instruction. His spiritual master told him to chant the Hare Krishna *Maha-mantra.* Taking this instruction to heart the disciple left the presence of his spiritual master and while walking he first noticed a group of washerwomen washing clothes and chanting Hare Krishna while washing. He then noticed some fisherman hauling in their nets, and while so doing they were chanting Hare Krishna. Further on he noticed some young boys playing and chanting Hare Krishna. He began to wonder why it was that his spiritual master told him that chanting Hare Krishna was the “most secret” instruction if washerwomen, fishermen, and young boys were all chanting the mantra. He returned to his spiritual master and inquired from him about this. Instead of giving the disciple a long answer he gave his disciple a precious jewel and told his disciple to go back and ask these people if they could recognize the value of the gem. He first approached the washerwomen and being simple washerwomen they knew nothing about precious gems. They said that they couldn’t offer anything of value for the gem, but if he wanted to give it to them they would use it for beating the clothes. The disciple told them that better for beating clothes they use a rock or a piece of wood. He would not give them the precious gem. When he approached the fishermen, they also could not recognize it’s value. They suggested that they take the jewel as use it as a weight to hold down their nets. And when the disciple approached the young boys, they also did not recognize it’s value and said that they would take it and use it as a ball to play with. The disciple returned to the *guru* and the *guru* asked him, “What happened? ” The disciple said that nobody could appreciate the value of the gem and would therefore not pay the required price. The spiritual master then said that similarly people cannot appreciate the value of the holy name and although they may chant, they are indifferent to the chanting. The Hare Krishna *maha-mantra* can only be revealed to those who are qualified to recognize it’s value.

Similarly, we must genuinely recognize the value of Krishna consciousness through our own internal experience if we want to distribute Krishna consciousness. Of course, by distributing Krishna consciousness and preaching, we get realization and conviction. This is the method, which Srila Prabhupada taught us for becoming Krishna conscious. But we should never neglect our *sadhana* and the cultivation of our internal life in the name of preaching. When our lives are balanced nicely in this way, we will have the strength to preach and this nice mood for preaching, as you described, will grow more and more.

Hoping this meets you and your wife in good health.

## Letter #134 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I’ve received your letter dated January 30 and have noted the contents.

First, thank you for writing. I’ve been inquiring about Bhakta but have been unable to get any kind of substantial report. I am happy to hear that he is still following Krishna consciousness.

Although you asked only for my prayers, I decided to write as well. Your fears are appropriate. If we are proud of our position, then we cannot be effective instruments for the Lord. What then can be gained by maintaining a position of false pride?

The best method for curbing pride is to always remain conscious of the fact that you are simply an instrument to the superior order. It is the superior order, which carries the potency for success in spiritual life. Our endeavors alone can produce nothing except further entanglement in illusion. Give credit to your superiors (for their instructions and guidance) when there are auspicious results. Take responsibility yourself and be careful to not blame others, when there are reversals. Do not judge your own spiritual qualifications based upon some material skill such as the ability to communicate or write. Do not misjudge others lack of spiritual qualification for not having material skills. Pride can do this and so much more. You can gauge your spiritual qualifications, but not without seeing the above qualities as prominent characteristics in your day-to-day life. In other words, if you forget that you are a servant of Krishna and act as a controller, that is a disqualification.

Of course, we must be active for service to *guru* and Krishna, and we should not withdraw from service just because we cannot follow the higher standards. But at least you should increase your own prayer for protection from pride, and should not simply depend upon my prayers alone.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #135 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Regarding these other books that you are reading, I'm sure that you must be aware that even though you feel some benefits from reading these books, there is also a potential danger in reading them. Though very subtle, most books of this nature are laced with impersonal philosophy. Although they may talk about so many so-called “spiritual” subjects, most philosophers’ conceptions of “liberation” or “salvation” are impersonal. They do not know the ultimate goal—“devotional service”. They do not know that we are eternal individuals and that life’s perfection is to enter into our eternal relationship with Krishna. Many even speak against the principles of devotional service, although it may be very subtle. If the writer is not a devotee, and has not surrendered himself to the feet of a Vaishnava, he cannot possibly lead his readers into the devotional conception as taught by our great *acharya'*s. Therefore, it is the greatest mistake to take their statements as absolute in any way, shape or form. Although we may see some “truth” in their statements, if we take all of their statements as truth, we will be misled. How can all of their statements be true if, all of them taken together do not culminate in devotional service to Krishna? It is simply not possible. If you understand what I'm saying, then you should be able to discriminate what knowledge they can offer to you for devotional service, and what must be rejected. If you don’t reject that which must be rejected, then you really should not read these books. That is my humble opinion on the matter.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #136 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Since our previous discussion I have heard that there be some

changes with the situation in..., and I actually see that it was

Krishna’s arrangement that we came upon the decision for you to not go there.

As I told you before, whatever duties you perform, whether in the temple, the BBT, or even maintaining yourself independently is secondary to the primary duties I want you to do which are most beneficial for your Krishna consciousness. When you are chanting your rounds, attending temple programs, telling others about Krishna, and especially when you are surrendering your mind to Krishna, His holy name, and to my instructions when there is turbulence, these efforts to remain steady in Krishna consciousness are far more important to me than your secondary duties. I am keenly aware of your struggles, and the fact that you are dutifully remaining faithful to the process of Krishna consciousness is, in itself, service to me. You are serving me by helping me to serve you, and as you gain strength from that service, your example will become a valuable service to me as well, because it will serve as a good example of a disciple who is faithful to the order of *guru* and Krishna. What better service can a disciple render to her spiritual master? Please try to see your service in this way, and whatever secondary duties you will perform will automatically become easier for you to execute.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #137(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

You have my permission to take responsibility as a counselor for aspiring disciples. Please bear in mind, however, that I do expect to receive reports about them, although they do not need to be frequent (at least twice a year), and I also expect that whatever problems they might have will, for the most part, be dealt with by you, with an explanation in your report of how you have dealt with their problem. Only in exceptional cases, should a problem of theirs be brought to me, and even then, their problem must be represented to me by you and not by them.

I am very anxious to get this counselor system established firmly, and I am also anxious to see that these reports are submitted to me in a responsible way. Please bear in mind, that unless there is clear evidence that this counselor system is effectively giving both disciples and aspiring disciples guidance, shelter, and the support that they need to progress in Krishna consciousness, without always having to bring disturbing matters directly to me— especially through COM, then I cannot go on accepting disciples either without further restrictions, or possibly even at all. On the other hand, I also want to be convinced that disciples and aspiring disciples are not withholding disturbing information from me, only out of fear of disturbing me, only to later find out that the matters concerning them were either not dealt with in a Krishna conscious way or worse, not dealt with at all. In other words, there must be an increase in Krishna consciousness and preaching Krishna consciousness. If by continuing to accept disciples, Krishna consciousness increases, then that is the only justification I can find for increasing the number of disciples. I am depending on you and other senior disciples to assist me in that mission, a mission given to all of us by our beloved Srila Prabhupada.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #138 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Bewilderment arises when we see everything connected to our self (false ego).

Duality exists on this platform only. And it’s from that platform that we always go back and forth between what is good and what is bad. Will this be good for me? Will this be bad for me? Will this make me happy? Will this make me sad? Will this bring me pleasure? Will this bring me pain? Is this right for me? Is this wrong? The word “me” exists in each of those statements.

I'm very sorry to hear that you are suffering from this bewilder-ment. I’ve been speaking a lot lately from a particular verse from the Eleventh Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam where it describes that the duality of the material world does not ultimately exist. We experience this pain and pleasure, good and bad, and experience it as real due to our conditioned intelligence. We see things separate from Krishna and therefore we suffer. This pleasure and suffering is actually an imaginary experience due to it’s connection to the body and mind, just as a person waking up from a wonderful dream, and then realizes that it was only a dream, experiences disappointment, or when he wakes up from a terrible dream, and realizes that it was only a dream, experiences great happiness and relief. Both experiences of disappointment and happiness are connected to a dream and have absolutely nothing to do with reality! In the same way when seeing things separate from Krishna we create a reality of happiness and distress that has no existence beyond our own imagination. Krishna is the only Transcendent Reality, and only when we see everything connected to Him can we actually be in touch with reality!

Whether it is a dream at night or a dream during the day, it is both the same. During the day we dream of something we would like to have or something we would like to avoid. Meditating on what we would like to have (if it is not Krishna) creates a false sense of happiness, because we think that since we want it and then we get it, it will make us happy. We tend to either forget or neglect that the fulfillment of such daydreams will also bring so much distress as a result of the attachment. Read the story in the Sixth Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam regarding Citraketu Maharaja’s attachment for having a son. He was so resolute in his desire to have a son, that he became blinded by the fact that his son was going to bring him so much distress, as warned to him by Angira Muni. He was thinking. It doesn’t matter. As long as I have a son, I will be happy. But his son was given the name Harsasoka (meaning jubilation and lamentation). He was finally given the benediction to have a son, and his ecstasy knew no bounds. But soon after the envious co-wives of the queen conspired to poison the child, because Citraketu was giving more attention to the queen who bore him this son than to all of his other queens. When they succeeded in their plans, then Citraketu Maharaja’s became thrown into an ocean of lamentation. All that happiness and all that distress was connected to his daydream. It was not connected to reality because he was thinking only of the pleasure and pain from his son in connection to himself. Only after receiving instruction from Narada Muni was he able to come to his senses and see everything in it’s proper perspective in connection with Krishna.

The 11th Canto verse goes on to describe that the tendency of the mind is to accept and reject various activities based on their connection to the gratification of the body and the mind. An intelligent person, therefore, must control the mind, and restrict it from seeing things separate from Krishna. When the mind is thus controlled one can experience complete fearlessness.

Of course, controlling the mind is easier said than done. But, still, we have to practice. You should at least know that your bewilderment arises from this duality as I’ve described to you above, and you should at least understand that that is the cause of your bewilderment. Unless and until you start controlling your mind and making a resolute decision to do what is best for pleasing Krishna, your bewilderment will only continue. You may get some temporary relief from your bewilderment, for example, if you impulsively try to avoid something that you don’t want to have (it falls into the same category, as desiring something that you want), it may bring you some temporary relief and the same false sense of happiness. But you can be sure, that very shortly thereafter, you will be facing the same distress that always follows your happiness.

Please. Do something about this. Stop unnecessarily creating so much future suffering for yourself. Take a serious look at all the good Krishna conscious instructions that you’ve received, and then take a serious step to make your decision from the platform of what would be most pleasing to Krishna. Krishna is there watching, both within your heart, and through His devotees. No efforts made by you to please Him will go unnoticed. When you decide to accept what’s best for your Krishna consciousness, you will find the only real solution to the bewilderment that you are experiencing now.

I hope that this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #139 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I have read your long explanation behind your behavior in..., and, from what I can understand of the explanation, I can forgive you.

Please, learn from this experience about the importance of being exemplary in your behavior. If you want to preach, you must be an example. Nobody wants to be preached to by a hypocrite. And neither can hypocrites be effective in their preaching. You are now married, so follow the good instructions of ideal family life. Live simply, restrict sense gratification, remain devoted to *guru* and Krishna, and do not foolishly think that married life is your opportunity for unrestricted independence. We are always dependent in all stages of our life.

Consider the following instructions I recently gave to one house-holder disciple:

\*\*\*

When I watch my disciples go through the same trials and tribulations that others before them have gone (i. e. pursuit of economic stability and the pressure that comes with it, family responsibilities, nondevotee association, etc., etc. ) it makes me worried if I have given enough of a foundation for them to carry them through. It helps if I know if a disciple feels dependent upon the instructions of their spiritual master, or if they think they’ve “outgrown” them. I always try to give instructions relevant to all stages of life, and not just something relevant to the time and place. Truth is always truth and cannot change, although our perceptions of them may change.

Please do not foolishly think that now that you are a householder, you have outgrown all the instructions I’ve already given. Every one of them still applies, except that now, there is a license for a “little” independence.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #140 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Despite your constant battles with material desires, and your inability to strictly follow, you should know that I have never for a moment rejected you. I cannot reject a disciple in whom I know there is sincerity. You may think that it’s not there in your heart, but

I know that it’s there. By the mercy of *guru* and Krishna, I am helped to know that it’s there, and I take it as my personal responsibility to kindle that sincere desire whenever possible with personal care and attention. This letter is one such attempt, and as you know, I have previously made similar attempts to keep a reserved place in your heart for devotion to *guru* and Krishna.

Although sometimes I must preach strongly, emphasizing the strictness required for advancement in Krishna consciousness, I do this out of duty. The goal must always be stressed, and without doubt, strict practices in Krishna consciousness help us to achieve that goal. But even though I preach strongly in this way, I am also very careful to be patient with those from among my disciples who are may falter from time to time in their practices.

I know how *maya* works, but I completely trust how Krishna acts from within their hearts. He’s already reserved His space in everyone’s heart, and when the disciple is sincere, Lord Paramatma helps relieve the disciple from unwanted *anarthas.*

Please remain honest, both with me and your wife. That will certainly help you to get Krishna’s mercy. In fact, I was very pleased how honest you were with me in July. You could have not said anything about your fall downs, but because you did, I took that as a very good sign.

I am always eager to see you.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #141 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Regarding to change your *ashrama,* in principle, I do not object to this idea. I am glad that you decided to first study in the *Bhakti- sastri* courses before coming to this decision. It’s always best to make this decision as most informed as possible.

It is also very important to have as complete of an understanding as possible as to the responsibilities you will face as a householder. This is best understood by taking counseling from those who have experience fulfilling their householder responsibilities along with maintaining an exemplary spiritual standard as well. Of course, for most part, it will all be theoretical for you until you are actually facing the responsibilities.

But still, if you are aware of some of the traps that you could fall into, it will certainly be helpful. I therefore strongly recommend that you do this even before making plans to approach somebody to marry (of course in an authorized way).

Moreover, I also will certainly expect you to both approach and to associate with your potential spouse in a manner befitting an initiated disciple. I personally do not approve when unmarried people live together, or, for that matter, even associate together in a secluded place or flat during their period of betrothal. This only causes disturbance both for yourself and for those who are seeking protection within the *bramachari ashrama.* Please, therefore, approach this idea about changing your *ashrama* in a mature and responsible way, and certainly I will give my blessings if everything is done in this way.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #142 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.
