I am well aware of the difficulties you are facing. You are right, this experience is common to many devotees after giving several years of their lives to book distribution.

Although I preach the importance book distribution, I believe that my lectures are well-balanced with simultaneous emphasis on the most important aspects of our lives in relation to the devel-opment of Krishna consciousness. There is no failure in Krishna consciousness. You should never think you are failure because you do not have the physical or mental endurance to continue with full-time book distribution. Even when I hear such statements (as I have recently been hearing from another disciple in ...) it causes me great pain. It goes against our philosophy.

We have to stick with the pursuit of Krishna consciousness throughout our whole lives. That is a fact. But we also must not artificially renounce other needs, which the body and mind require. Your years on book distribution have given you a foundation for Krishna consciousness in this life that you will not be able to forget. They have taught you the meaning of surrender, dependence upon Krishna, and most importantly, they have given you a realization achieved by only a few rare souls in this material world, that Krishna is our worshipful Lord and master, and that He is the Supreme Goal of our lives. Such realizations are precious, and cannot be forgotten. Having such realizations behind you, you should not fear the need to undertake other activities which may be less mentally or physically strenuous, provided you don’t become slack on the most essential practices for maintaining Krishna consciousness.

Your secondary duties in life may change—whether it be book distributor, cook, *pujari,* counselor, teacher, receptionist, or even housewife or mother. Although devotional service is eternal, the duties we perform within this body are not. Whatever duties we perform are considered so much useless labor, if they don’t provoke our attraction for hearing and chanting about Krishna— which is the primary duty of our lives! Please do not forget this, and you will never feel despondent thinking that you have failed in Krishna consciousness, just because your secondary duties have changed.

“Krishna must always be remembered and He must never be forgotten. All the rules and prohibitions given in the scriptures are meant to be a servant of these two principles. ” (Padma Purana)

Therefore please do whatever is needful to maintain a strong de-sire to associate with devotees and to practice Krishna consciousness. You will always have my support to practice Krishna consciousness, no matter what other duties you will accept in this life.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #143 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I am aware of the crisis in the economy and the affect that it is having on the devotees. Unfortunately, it is something that is beyond all of our control. We must always have to adjust our lives to such influences, without losing faith while maintaining our dependence upon Krishna.

I am not qualified to answer your question about Kurjinovo. I have not been in that region, and I have no information about what is presently going on in that region. It is not that I am some kind of psychic “seer” that I can predict what will happen if you were to relocate your family to that region. If you want to consider that as an option, then you will have to thoroughly investigate the matter yourself, and then make a decision as informed as possible, based upon the information you’ve gathered.

Although, in principle, I can appreciate your concerns that having land is a very valuable asset in the wake of an economic crisis, still, when there is an economic crisis, most others are thinking the same way, and as you can see, they are willing to fight and kill to obtain it. In my opinion, it would be best if you seek other options before making any kind of a decision. Don’t just grab something that appears to be immediately available, only to lament later that you were to hasty in your decision.

Of course, in my opinion, wherever you go, you should try your best to keep yourself close to devotees who are inclined to maintain strict practices in Krishna consciousness. Ultimately, there is nothing secure in this material world. It is only under illusion that we think we are secure when material arrangements appear to be nicely in place. It can all change in a moment. Therefore, please remain secure in practicing Krishna consciousness, for your sake, and the sake of your family. Take your strength from association, and I will leave you with a short excerpt on this subject from a letter which I had written previously this year to another disciple.

Hoping this meets you and your family in good health.

## Letter #144 (NS—&gt;)

I just recently answered a letter to Bhaktin in Moscow, where she was explaining how she sells *prashadaam* with other devotees. My letter to her was as follows:

\*\*\*

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you for sending me this report. Staying in the association of devotees is always most important, but we have found that oftentimes economic pressures tend to come into conflict with that. If you can fulfill your economic needs through *prashadaam,* in the association of devotees, then this is very good. But be careful to not base your association with devotees on the strength of cooking and selling *prashadaam* together. To remain strong in Krishna consciousness association must always be based upon hearing and chanting about Krishna. If you remain dependent upon that, then even if economic constraints arise in the future, you will not abandon your “association” with devotees.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

In other words, it is less important to keep association with devo-tees for economic purposes. It is more important to keep association with devotees for maintaining your Krishna consciousness. Even if you are “with” devotees for long periods of time “working together to maintain your bodies”, it is better even if you are with devotees for shorter periods of time, but for exclusively hearing and chanting about Krishna. In this regard, “quality” is more important than “quantity”. Of course, if you can do both, that is best. But it seems that you are too disturbed due to lack of a steady means of support, and therefore neither association for maintenance nor association for hearing and chanting is happening for you.

## Letter #145(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

The only reason why I did not answer your previous letter is because I am still digging through “hundreds” of messages that accumulated during the time I was dealing with the recent crisis in Russia. Although in principle, I should probably answer the older letters first, I have not been following any particular order in my replies. I decided to do a little bit, day by day, and even that little bit takes so much time each day.

Regarding your previous letter, there is absolutely nothing wrong with your first realization. Everything that surrounds you is Krishna’s energy, and you should always try to see it in that way—by seeing everything and everyone connected to Krishna.

Krishna wants everyone to answer His love, but until one is on the stage of pure love, we must answer his love through obedience. In *Bhakti rasamrta Sindhu*, Srila Rupa Goswami writes,

*sruti-smrti-puranadi-*  
*pancaratra-vidhim vina*  
*aikantiki harer bhaktir*  
*utpatayaiva kalpate*

“Devotional service of the Lord that ignores the authorized Vedic literatures like the Upanisads, Puranas and Narada- pancaratra is simply an unnecessary disturbance in society. ”

In other words, until one’s personal, intimate relationship with Krishna is revealed to you by a resident of Goloka Vrindavana, (which will only come when you are qualified and ready for that) you cannot render a concocted form of intimate service and think that it is acceptable devotional service (by Krishna). You have to follow the authorized Vedic literatures, and worship the Lord by adherence to the instructions given within. The best way to render intimate service to Krishna while still in the conditioned stage of devotional service is to render service to the Deity according to the authorized pancaratras. Narada Muni has given an authorized means for worshiping the Deity in the Narada-pancaratra, and most of the standards which are followed in ISKCON are in accordance to those authorized instructions. If you want to render “intimate” service as a means of reciprocating Krishna’s love, then you should take up some form of Deity worship, and cook for the Lord, bathe the Lord, dress the Lord, clean for the Lord, decorate the Lord, and pray to the Lord through an authorized form. The Deity worship handbook, Pancaratra Pradipa, gives a well-rounded approach to Deity worship, both according to strict temple standards, and to a more simplified standard at home.

I hope that this answers your inquiries, and that you will continue to protect yourself from making the same mistakes of the past.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #146(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Thank you very much for your report. I am relieved that you have been able to maintain some standard of *sadhana* and can continue to preach and serve in the temple, despite your ill health.

You are right. We cannot become slack in our efforts to render devotional service. Just before I left Ukraine about two weeks ago, I gave a class in Kiev on a verse which described that the living entity within the body does not suffer due to the body’s fatigue, and ultimately, the living entity has nothing to do with bodily pains and pleasures. Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakur in his commentary to that verse gave an example of a child heavily decorated with ornaments. Although the child’s body is very delicate, he does not feel fatigue, nor do the parents think that his ornaments should be taken away. In the same way, we may feel fatigue due to identification with the material body, but for the soul, there is no fatigue. The living entity’s eternal engagement is glorification of the Lord, and therefore if we want to realize our eternal relationship with Krishna, we must be prepared to “constantly” glorify the Lord with our body, mind and words. There are no vacations from glorifying Krishna.

So please go on rendering service in this way, and gradually the disease of material life will be cured.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #147 (—&gt;NS)

Please, accept my humble obeisances! All Glories to Srila Prabhupada!

My name is..., I have been living in temple in *brah-macharya ashram* and trying to dedicate all my free time to *Sankir-tana.*

Dear Srila Niranjana Swami, I'd like to consult You by addressing to You as a senior Vaishnava, not an official person, engaged in an important post in ISKCON.

Dear Srila Niranjana Swami, it is better to present essence of my question through the example.

It happens that little child has to go to a doctor (heal tooth or cure something else). It is rather more frightful than painful. And this child is accompanied by his father, who is certainly unable to mitigate child’s pain but he can hold his child’s hand. For the child all universe is concentrated on the father’s hand while sitting before a doctor. All child’s fears disappears by knowing that his father thinks only of him at the moment, as if the sun has just rose in child’s heart. The child keeps memories of the occasion throughout whole his life and remembers it in most difficult situations.

Dear Srila Niranjana Swami, everyone, who is going by a spiritual path, is destined to have “operations” on the heart which may be quite painful. And as far as I can see everyone who passed these operations was able to do so due to having a father in spiritual life. None would be able to tolerate pain of reject material attachments unless one feels the father nearby him and unless the father takes care of him more than of himself. Now, the essence of the question.

I feel Srila Prabhupada’s care and protection very frequently but my mind is so strong that sometimes I fall into a panic and thoughts come up as following: “Who needs me here? Who takes responsibility of me and before whom? Why none takes care of me? ” and so on. At such moments I feel an acute need in a shelter of a Person being present near me physically. I don’t doubt that there are such persons in the temple I could trust completely but after having lived in the temple for a long time I haven’t found anyone to fully trust so far. This is the problem.

Dear Srila Niranjana Swami, I want to ask You to tell whether You had similar situations in Your life?

And what to do next?

Dear Srila Niranjana Swami, while I was writing this letter I tried to keep a spirit of respect and set forth my thought clearly but because of my agitation I probably couldn’t be correct or respectful somewhere. Therefore, please, be indulgent to me.

## Letter #148(NS—&gt;response to#147)

Hare Krishna. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

I’ve read your letter and noted everything within.

\*\*\*

“Dear Srila Niranjana Swami, I want ask You to relate did You have the same situation in Your life?

And what to do next? ”

Regarding my own situation when I was living as a new *bhakta* in the temple, I always remember trusting my authorities because I trusted Srila Prabhupada. When I was in difficulty, although I did not necessarily get attention immediately, eventually, because I trusted devotees, Srila Prabhupada and ultimately Sri Sri Radha Gopivallabha, the presiding Deities of our Temple, someone ex-tended themselves to me to inquire about my spiritual welfare. I remember very much feeling cared for by my Temple President. In fact, from those formative years of my devotional life, I can always think back and favorably remember the attention that was given to me by my Temple President and other senior devotees. During those days we hardly ever sat down for long meetings in the Temple President’s office. Neither did he really sit down and counsel me very much. I never looked for that, and my authorities really did not partake in such activities so much with the devotees. But my authorities were always visible to me. They were not some big administrators always sitting behind a desk behind closed doors. There was a particular advantage in those days that most of our temples were not so big, and we were like one big family, all working and struggling together to do something to please Srila Prabhupada. So we really didn’t need to make appointments to speak with the President. He was always around, and from time to time, was just patting us on the back, smiling, and saying a few encouraging words that were always relevant to our efforts in spiritual life. That’s all we really needed in those days to feel cared for and to trust, and I can honestly say that it was certainly important for us to feel this way.

I realize that the situation in may not be as simple as that which I experienced as a new *bhakta. It* is a relatively large and departmentalized temple, and I would imagine that the Temple President is not as accessible to the devotees as my Temple President was in those early days. But, still, it is my understanding that there is a major effort presently being undertaken by the spiritual authorities to implant a system for caring, by placing the counselor system as a major priority for temple development. From my vantage point it seems that there are many senior, qualified devotees who are willing to extend themselves in a very similar way as my Temple President did in the early days. Of course, it is not exactly the same, but, under the circumstances, the impression I have from the counselor system is that it is the best possible arrangement that any new devotee could expect. It is really up to you to take advantage of the system, and to make every possible effort to build trust in someone who inspires you the most, and who seems the most inclined to encourage you with an occasional pat on the back and a few short, but very relevant encouraging words about your devotional service.

As far as what to do next... I suggest that you show this letter to a senior devotee that you trust, and ask him if he would be willing to be a “big brother” who would, from time to time, just ask how you are doing, with a positive smile on their face, and ask him if he would be willing, from time to time, to just offer a few encouraging words. And furthermore, on your part, you should also try your best to be the type of devotee who does not demand or expect more than that from those who you would like to build up your trust. I have practically seen that such a demanding mentality has also done much to place barriers between younger devotees and their well-wishers. Why would a well-wisher stop, pat you on the back, and ask how you are doing, if he knew that any time he would do that, you would launch back at him, accusing him for not doing enough for him, or you would spend two hours complaining about the management and how you expect your well-wisher to do something to change the situation? There is a limit as to how much you should expect from your well-wishers. Otherwise, you will lose out on their association, and most importantly, you will lose out on the most valuable experience, which for me, in those early years of my Krishna consciousness, gave me the foundation which I both needed and wanted to dedicate my life to Lord Caitanya’s *Sankirtana* mission.

I hope that this helps, and I sincerely hope that you will take this little pat on your back from me, and a few encouraging words from me, to enthusiastically take part in Srila Prabhupada’s book distribution marathon, which is now going on in your temple. You should have firm faith that Lord Caitanya wants each and every one of us to take up a spirit of giving Krishna consciousness to others, and He is so merciful and kind, that if we do this, in a genuine humble spirit, He will also become very inclined to reach out, pat us on the back, give us a few encouraging words, and most important of all, He will accept that which we have offered to Him, which is the culmination of everything that we do in Krishna consciousness.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #149 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

The temples cannot maintain householders, except only those who are holding very responsible administrative positions. For the most part, those who require income to live independently must earn their income themselves, either through their own business, through *prashadaam* selling, through working for Food for Life, by working with other devotee businesses, or simply by holding down an outside job. In some cases, the temple can help householders “get on their feet” for some time, by allowing them to live in the temple for some time until they can support themselves outside, and/or by providing them *prashadaam* for some time as well. I have explained this point to dasa already so many times. If both you and your husband could maintain yourselves independent from the temple, I have no doubt that your valuable service in Kiev would be greatly appreciated and encouraged. Certainly, counselors are needed and appreciated. Certainly your book distribution for the marathon is needed and appreciated, and certainly the *puja* that you do from time to time is needed and appreciated. But, as I said, the temple cannot maintain householders who are not holding important administrative positions. The whole concept of the counselor system is to get the householders to start networking and helping each other from “outside” the temple, so that they can all contribute their service to the temple in the ways mentioned above. The householders should not be thinking that “service” to the temple means that there must be payment.

## Letter #150 (NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

After rereading your frank letter, I'm finding myself somewhat at a loss for words. I'm not quite sure what I could say, given the limited amount of time available to ponder your plight, which could immediately turn you around into “a true believer”.

Unfortunately, I'm speaking about “limited time” because within 24 hours from the time I arrived in America, an emergency situation arose, and I got drawn into it. This emergency has unexpectedly pulled me away from what I had hoped to accomplish in the way of correspondence during my stay in the U. S., and it seems as though it is going to continue to pull my attention right up until my departure to India the day after tomorrow. I am therefore limited in the amount of time I can give you to you right now because I have so many other letters waiting for answers, and I'm going to have to deal with as many as possible of them tonight. So please forgive me if the following reply does not fully satisfy your hankering for an answer to your letter.

It appears to me that you simply do not want to “follow the flock”, and I can’t fault you for that. I believe that if some of my seniormost disciples were faced with the prospect of just now approaching me to become a disciple, they would probably have a very difficult time “following the flock” as well, even though I'm the same person now that they approached 10 years ago. Of course, I'm not exactly the same person. But, for whatever reason, those who’ve maintained faith in me, as a person who is representing the *guru parampara* to them, have been able to do so because they got enough association from me, which was consistent to them and which they needed to cultivate that faith.

Now that the number of my disciples has significantly increased, it is becoming increasingly difficult for me to spread that association to those who are approaching me. I have therefore been forced into the alternative of pushing forward my disciples as “representatives”, knowing full well that I have my limitations, and I cannot individ-ually reach out to everybody who approaches me for shelter. Those who are coming forward now for direction, and who do not need to push forward their individuality, if they remain honest, they can survive and serve in such an environment. Not everyone is capable of doing that, and it appears that you are one of that kind.

Thus you are finding yourself doubting not only my disciples, and the institution they serve in, but also my own ability to deliver what you need to maintain your “individuality” as a person, and not as a flock-follower who is forced to placate the desires of the flock in order to artificially gain favor for some personal recognition.

I agree. You need more attention than what I can personally give you if you are to have any hope of maintaining faith in this institution. I am not all-pervasive and omniscient, and I cannot dictate from within your heart the direction which you need to follow.

But, even though I'm being frank with you about my own limitations, I still do not see the rationale for you to not follow *sadhana bhakti.* Even though you don’t have enough faith to “become somebody’s dog”, can you find a higher purpose and justification for life than that which is espoused through the process of *bhakti?* Do you, with your higher intelligence, actually think that to hear rock and roll music is a satisfactory goal, in of itself, to achieve with this valuable human form of life? I don’t think so. I actually think that you are more intelligent than that. That’s why you are attracted to this movement. You know that there’s something to be gained through the process of *bhakti,* but you just don’t see where an individualist (and, admittedly, a proud one) can fit into this movement.

All right, then don’t follow the flock. Don’t surrender your individuality. Don’t force yourself to follow some ritual, which doesn’t seem to be stimulating your internal growth. But I strongly recommend that you don’t give up your efforts to understand *bhakti* without seriously studying Srila Prabhupada’s books, especially Cai-tanya Caritamrta. I would like to challenge you on this. You read the whole Caitanya Caritamrta, from Adi Lila, Volume 1, to Antya Lila Volume 5, and then tell me that you don’t see how you be an individual in Lord Caitanya’s *Sankirtana* Movement. Actually this is not my challenge. This is Krishnadasa Kaviraja Goswami’s challenge, “for those who are accustomed to logic and argument. ”

Text 13

*ataeva punah kahon urdhva-bahu hana*  
*caitanya-nityananda bhaja kutarka chadiya*

TRANSLATION. Therefore I say again, lifting my arms:

**O fellow human beings, please worship Sri Caitanya and Nityananda without false arguments!**

PURPORT. Because a person who performs *krsna-bhakti* but does not understand Sri Krishna Caitanya and Prabhu Nityananda will simply waste his time, the author, Krish-nadasa Kaviraja Gosvami, requests everyone to take to the worship of Sri Caitanya and Nityananda Prabhu and the Panca-tattva. He assures everyone that any person who does so will be successful in Krishna consciousness. (Adi 8. 14)

Text 14

*yadi va tarkika kahe, —tarka se pramana*  
*tarka-sastre siddha yei, sei sevyamana*

Logicians say, “Unless one gains understanding through logic and argument, how can one decide upon a worshipable Deity? ” (Adi 8. 15)

Text 15

*sri-krsna-caitanya-daya karaha vicara*  
*vicara karite citte pabe camatkara*

**TRANSLATION. If you are indeed interested in logic and argument, kindly apply it to the mercy of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. If you do so, you will find it to be strikingly wonderful.**

PURPORT. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura comments in this connection that people in general, in their narrow-minded conception of life, create many different types of humanitarian activities, but the humanitarian activ-ities inaugurated by Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu are different. For logicians who want to accept only that which is proven through logic and argument, it is a fact that without logic and reason there can be no question of accepting the Absolute Truth. Unfortunately, when such logicians take to this path without the mercy of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, they remain on the platform of logic and argument and do not advance in spiritual life. However, if one is intelligent enough to apply his arguments and logic to the subtle understanding of the fundamental spiritual substance, he will be able to know that a poor fund of knowledge established on the basis of material logic cannot help one understand the Absolute Truth, which is beyond the reach of imperfect senses. The Mahabharata therefore says, *acintyah khalu ye bhava na tams tarkena yojayet.* (Mahabharata, Bhisma-parva 5. 22) How can that which is beyond the imagination or sensory specula-tion of mundane creatures be approached simply by logic? Logic and argument are very poor in spiritual strength and always imperfect when applied to spiritual understanding. By putting forward mundane logic one frequently comes to the wrong conclusion regarding the Absolute Truth, and as a result of such a conclusion one may fall down to accept a body like that of a jackal.

Despite all this, those who are actually inquisitive to understand the philosophy of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu through logic and argument are welcome. Krishnadasa Kavi-raja Gosvami addresses them, “Please put Sri Caitanya Ma-haprabhu’s mercy to your crucial test, and if you are actually a logician you will come to the right conclusion that there is no personality more merciful than Lord Caitanya. ” Let the logi-cians compare all the results of other humanitarian work with the merciful activities of Lord Caitanya. If their judgment is impartial, they will understand that no other humanitarian activities can surpass those of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

Everyone is engaged in humanitarian activities on the basis of the body, but from the *Bhagavad-gita* (2. 18) we un-derstand, *anta-vanta ime deha nityasyoktah saririnah:* “The material body is ultimately subject to destruction, whereas the spiritual soul is eternal. ” Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s philanthropic activities are performed in connection with the eternal soul. However one tries to benefit the body, it will be destroyed, and one will have to accept another body according to his present activities. If one does not, therefore, understand this science of transmigration but considers the body to be all in all, his intelligence is not very advanced. Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, without neglecting the necessities of the body, imparted spiritual advancement to purify the exis-tential condition of humanity. Therefore if a logician makes his judgment impartially, he will surely find that Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu is the *maha-vadanyavatara,* the most magnani-mous incarnation. He is even more magnanimous than Lord Krishna Himself. Lord Krishna demanded that one surren-der unto Him, but He did not distribute love of Godhead as magnanimously as Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Therefore

Srila Rupa Gosvami offers Lord Caitanya his respectful obei-sances with the words *namo maha-vadanyaya krsna-prema- pradaya te* / *krsnaya krsna-caitanya-namne gaura-tvise namah* \[Madhya 19. 53\]. Lord Krishna simply gave the *Bhagavad-gita*, by which one can understand Lord Krishna as He is, but Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, who is also Krishna Himself, gave people love of Krishna without discrimination. (Adi Lila, Chapter 8, Verses 13—15)

Please, whatever else you do, continue to follow the principles, chant Hare Krishna, as much as possible, and study these books. Although I'm not saying that you must do these things, but even if you go back and live with your parents, you work an outside job, and you don’t associate with devotees so often (because you only find fault with what they tell you), I am quite confident that if you use your God-given intelligence to make a serious investigation of this wealth of knowledge, you will find Lord Caitanya’s mercy to be so strikingly wonderful, that you will be convinced that you must continue to follow the process of *sadhana bhakti* despite all of our limitations. Moreover, I'm also convinced that if you seriously read Caitanya Caritamrta in the manner I’ve described, that Lord Caitanya will recognize your efforts, and He will arrange the inspiration you need to go forward. Can you do it? I hope so. Don’t miss out on this opportunity. Don’t let impediments (which will naturally be there) stop you from this attempt.

For now, that is all I can offer within the limited time that’s available to me. I really hope that you will take up “our” challenge, and I look forward to hearing the results of your investigation during my next visit to when I pass through in March.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #151 (NS—&gt;Indriyesha d. )

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. Thank you very much for this report on my tape ministry. I am becoming increasingly convinced of the importance of this ministry for my disciples, and I am grateful that you are taking this service seriously. Please go on enlisting as much help as you can possibly get from my disciples for this endeavor, and be assured that it has my full support. I only hope that my disciples will grow to see more and more the importance of keeping themselves connected through this tape ministry. If there is no *shiksha* from the spiritual master, then the connection becomes weak, and disciples can not only become victims of *maya* by falling away, but they may become influenced by so many deviant philosophies as well. It is painful when I see devotees becoming so bewildered, and I believe that many of my current lectures address this concern over and over again.

\*\*\*

“During the festival in Kiev I noticed a great in-terest of our devotees to Your lectures in English, which were given by You without translation. Is anybody in America, who purposefully gathers Your American lectures? ”

I believe that Sachimata devi dasi is keeping these tapes. Her mailing address is:...

Or, you can try communicating to her through Premananda dasa who is on email in Boston at...

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Letter #152(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

First of all, to “aspire” for relationship with Krishna, simply means that we must develop an intense *“lobha”* (or greed) to un-derstand what is our particular relationship with the Lord. The means for achieving that greed is to hear the Lord’s pastimes from an authorized source. Not that we hear only about particular types of pastimes. We should simply hear authorized pastimes. The more we hear the Lord’s pastimes, the more that that greed for understanding our relationship with the Lord will increase.

After intense hearing, and intense purification, gradually one may develop an attraction to particular types of pastimes of the Lord as Srila Prabhupada writes in the following purport:

“Special natural appreciation of the descriptions of a particular pastime of Godhead indicates the constitutional position of a living entity. Adoration, servitorship, friend-ship, parental affection and conjugal love are the five primary relationships with Krishna. ” (Adi 4. 34)

But that is more advanced stage. In the neophyte stage before there can be a greed for a particular pastime, there must be intense greed to hear all the Lord’s pastimes.

One should never imagine that “I am this, I am that, Krishna is dealing with me this way, Krishna is dancing with me, etc., etc. ” this is *sahajiya.* There is no question of imagining such relationships.

Later, in the more advanced stages, one can absorb his con-sciousness in remembrance of these particular pastimes to which we are attracted. But even at this stage, there is strictly “*smaranam*”, remembrance of those pastimes with the Lord’s eternal associates. Not with ourselves! We should not think that we are actually par-ticipating in those particular pastimes. We are “remembering” those pastimes.

In your case, before you can aspire to understand your particular relationship with the Lord, you must fully understand just what these different relationships are, and the science of those relationships.

I strongly suggest that you study completely, from cover to cover, The Nectar of Devotion. There is no question of aspiring for a particular relationship, or knowing you actual relationship without first developing a comprehensive understanding of the different relationships described in the Nectar of Devotion.

Regarding your meditation on the Deities as asked in your following question:

\*\*\*

“But I didn’t understand how does the Lord want me to meditate upon the Deities?

\- Should I meditate with a particular intimate image within my mind?

\- Should I meditate while simultaneously asking the Lord about anything?

\- Should I simply serve the Deities?

\- Should I simply keep the Deities within my mind?

Or maybe I should do something else? ”

Your meditation on the Deities should be in relationship to the service rendered to the Deity. For instance, if you are *pujari,* then you should meditate on making offerings with love and devotion, keeping the Deities paraphernalia and altar clean, being punctual for the service to the Deity, making beautiful paraphernalia or outfits for the Deity and meditating on Their appearance in those outfits, meditating on Their pleasure while cooking preparations made for Them, and meditating on getting every opportunity to render personal service to Them.

Even if you are not a *pujari,* you can meditate on Their appear-ance each day, remembering the outfits They were wearing and the way They were decorated and dressed. You can meditate on bringing something to offer to the Deity, or you can meditate on expanding the glories of the Deity by speaking about Them or Their service to others. Also, whatever service is rendered can be a meditation on the Deity. The Deities are the proprietors of the temple or of one’s home. Whether one is cooking, cleaning, funding, preaching, or whatever, these are all forms of service which can be performed in meditation upon the Deity by always remembering Them as the ultimate object of one’s service. Remembrance of the Deities’ form in one’s mind is always beneficial. You can always carry the Deities in your mind.

You can also meditate upon the Deities when offering prayers before Them, praying to be always absorbed in thought of Them and Their service.

These are all different ways which one can meditate upon the Deity. But one should never “imagine” any more of a relationship that actually exists with the Deity than in the mood of a servant.

I hope that this answers your questions, and that this letter meets you in good health.

## Letter #153(NS—&gt;)

Please accept my blessings. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

Here is the reply to your first letter:

\*\*\*

“Gurudev, please forgive me that I am disturbing You again, but one question is torturing me. Would You please explain to me how can I discriminate clearly between Krishna’s desires, thoughts and feelings and my desires, thoughts and feelings? Many times I listened to Your lecture where You describing that the weeds that surround the devotional creeper may look exactly the same as the creeper, but I understood that one can discriminate only by the mercy of Krishna and one’s spiritual master.

That’s why I am begging about Your mercy. Guru Maharaja, I tried to reject all manifestations of feelings and thoughts and desires within my heart, thinking that all of them are mine, but then it became clear to me that I did something wrong. But since I am on a very law level and Krishna can give me only some small drops of His love and attention, then how can I choose His manifestations clearly in order for me to continue my devotional service properly? ”

Until one has actually “realized” the presence of the Lord within the heart, one has to confirm everything by *guru, shastra* and *sadhu.* Even for those who are Paramatma realized, everything they follow is consistent with *guru, shastra* and *sadhu.* But they are not bewildered. They know. You do not know. Therefore, you are not qualified to understand Krishna’s “desires, thoughts, and feelings” independent from *shastra, guru* and *sadhu.* Please read the following quote from Srila Prabhupada:

«So you can violate the laws on the order of Krishna. You cannot do. This is surrender. When Krishna says that “You do this, ” although it is wrong, you have to do it. There is no consideration. Just like in fight, the commander says to the soldiers, “Do this. ” His duty is to do that. He should not think at that time what is right or wrong. Just like Yudhishthira Maharaja, he was advised by Krishna that “Yudhishthira, Maharaja Yudhishthira, you go to Dronacharya and inform him that ‘Your son is dead. ’ ”

It was a false information. Because Dronacharya would not die if he is not affected with some lamentation. So Krishna asked Maharaja Yudhishthira that “You go because you are recognized truthful, Dharmaraja. So when you will say, this is also false, he will believe. ” But Yudhishthira hesitated, “How can I tell lie? ” He disobeyed the order of Krishna, and he wanted to become very truthful. For this reason he had to see hell. So in the mundane consideration there are so many things, right and wrong. So long you are on the mundane platform, you have to obey all these right and wrong. But in the spiritual platform, when it is ordered by Krishna... He is above all this duality. He is Absolute. So even if He says to do something wrong... Because He cannot say anything which is wrong. God is all-good.

If you discriminate God’s order from the mundane plat-form, then you will be misguided. Anyone who has got firm and fixed up faith that whatever God does, whatever God orders, even from mundane calculation it may be wrong, that is right... That is absolute understanding. So those who are in the spiritual platform, for them, there is no such consideration, which is right and which is wrong. Their only consideration is whether Krishna is pleased. That’s all. If Krishna is pleased by inducing me to do something wrong, I will not hesitate to do it. I will do it because it is Krishna’s order. *Tasmin tuste jagat tustam.* My principle should be, “If Krishna is satisfied, then everything is all right. ” But while we are in the mundane platform, we should not concoct the idea that “Krishna is pleased; let me do that. ” No. That you, cannot do. You have to follow the rules and regulations.

But if you think that it is... You cannot think; neither you can manufacture. So either you should hear from Krishna directly or... That is not possible because we are not in touch with Krishna at the present moment. We are trying to see Krishna through the transparent medium of spiritual master.

So we cannot manufacture that “This is Krishna’s desire. ”

No. You have to get it confirmed from the transparent medium, spiritual master. These are the principles. » (Ref. VedaBase, *Bhagavad-gita,* 1. 45-46, London, August 1, 1973)

Krishna’s desire should be manifest to you through instructions from higher authorities. And as far as Krishna’s thoughts and feelings are concerned, first you should become conversant about those from *shastra* such as “Nectar of Devotion”. These things must be studied scrutinizingly from the shastric point of view, and without a thorough examination from different works of the Six Goswamis and other previous *acharyas* such as Visvanath Cakravarti Thakur, it is simply not possible to understand what are Krishna’s thoughts and feelings. One has to first see (from *shastra*) how Krishna’s thoughts and feelings are manifested to His devotee from objective research, without imposing one’s own imperfect experiences into the research. When that research is coupled with strict adherence to *sadhana,* chanting, remembering, hearing, after one surpasses the stage of *anartha nivritti* (the stage when all *anarthas* such as lust, envy, greed, desire for recognition, etc. are completely cleansed from the core of the heart) then and only then can one begin to distinguish what are Krishna’s thoughts and feelings. Without such cleansing, you will continue to speculate on that which you are not capable of even approaching.

You really need to read more. Otherwise you will continue to live in your own subjective reality, which according to *shastra,* is a reality which has no existence beyond your imagination.

Another point is that it is too artificial for you to reject all feelings, thoughts and desires. Until the *anarthas* are cleansed from the heart, you have to expect that they will be there. You say that you reject anything that is coming from you. But you are also a person. You should not think that you can live by totally denying your own individuality and desires. For the platform you are on, it is too artificial. You will simply become an impersonalist, denying everything out of frustration, but getting no tangible attachment to Krishna’s service.

But you should not project your feelings, thoughts and desires onto the spiritual master. As I’ve told you many times, the relation-ship with the spiritual master is one of teacher and student, and it is the spiritual master’s duty to teach the disciple from this plat-form without getting emotionally involved, especially with female disciples. Whatever feelings, thoughts and desires that you do have, although still not pure, can be dovetailed in service to the Deity, in service and association with one’s godsister’s, and when one’s family consists of devotees, with one’s family members. To whatever degree you can do that with your family, you should do, but I realize that on this point, it is something which is not so easy for you.

Hoping this meets you in good health.

## Lecture #1 Nectar of Instruction

—Note—

Lecture was given sometime around 1989

King Puranjana continued thinking how, when he was in a state of bewilderment, his wife would give him good counsel and how she would become aggrieved when he was away from home. Although she was the mother of so many sons and heroes, the King still feared that she would not be able to maintain the responsibility of household affairs.

*prabodhayati mavijnam*  
*vyusite soka karsita*  
*vartmaitad grha medhiyam*  
*vira-sur api nesyati*  
*(SB 4.28.20)*

This verse describes the plight of the living entity in conditional consciousness. Although the living entity by constitutional nature is meant to always be thinking of Krishna, just as—Prabhupada gives the example—we don’t speak of shine—we speak of sunshine because the shine’s connection is to the sun. So, in the same way, when we refer to consciousness, real consciousness is Krishna consciousness. But, due to conditional consciousness, being con-ditioned by material nature, the living entity is having to struggle with the material nature simply due to the false consciousness of *aham mameti*—“I am this body and everything belongs to me”— communally, nationally, even pseudo-religiously.

These different types of consciousness, although they may appear to be selfless, are still centered around the body. Being overly concerned of the bodily needs, being overly concerned about the fulfillment of sense gratification, individually and collectively, the living entity is forced to have to accept another birth, as described how King Puranjana would be forced—because of his attachment to a woman—to come back and take the body of a woman. It is that simple. The living entity carries his different conceptions of life from one body to the next just as the air carries aromas. So, in the same way, whatever one is thinking of at the time of death, certainly that will determine his next birth. Of course, Krishna also says that *anta-kale ca mam eva* / *smaran muktva kalevaram*— “Whoever remembers Me at the time of death, he at once attains My nature. Of this there is no doubt” (Bg 8. 5)—So, because the living entity is subject to bewilderment—there is that verse — *visnu-shakti para proktah.*.. —that the potencies of Lord Vishnu are summarized in three categories, namely, the superior potency, the living entity, and ignorance. The superior potency is full of knowledge. The living entities, although belonging to the superior potency, are subject to bewilderment, and the third energy, which is full of ignorance, is always visible in fruitive activities. This is the summary of the three different energies of the Supreme Lord Vishnu.

Certainly, there is a superior energy which is, as Krishna describes in the Bhagavad Gita, the spiritual world, the *daivim prakritim,* the internal potency of the Supreme Personality of God-head, and, of course, Krishna describes *bhumir apo ’nalo vayuh* / *kham mano buddhir eva ca* / *ahankara itiyam me* / *bhinna prakritir astadha.* That is, “Earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intelligence and false ego—all together these eight constitute My separated ma-terial energies. ” (Bg 7. 4) A*pareyam itas tv anyam* / *prakrtim viddhi me param* / *jiva-bhutam maha-baho* / *yayedam dharyate jagat* (7. 5)— besides this inferior nature is the superior nature which are the living entities who are working hard. It is a struggle. Again, that struggle is simply due to identification with matter. If we do not change our consciousness in this life, whatever we do in the name of social, political, religious or communal and national welfare will be the cause of our bondage. Prabhupada explains that one has to change his consciousness in order to become free from bondage. To be released from bondage, we must change from the social concept, from the political, religious or communal. We see that all these different conceptions are all, again, ingrained with the different bodily concepts of life, and we see that there are many organiza-tions, collective institutions, societies, that are established under these certain headings, these certain banners. When we think of banners, we always think of a flag that represents a large group of people. Sometimes we call it our nation. Or, sometimes we may call it a city or a community or a town or village, whatever it may be. There are certain needs, and there are certain ideals which those people are gathered about as they stand under that particu-lar banner, and if that banner simply represents a bodily concept, simply due to some geographical location—if we are Italians, or we are Americans—or if that banner simply represents another bodily concept, it doesn’t make any difference—social, political, religious or communal, or national welfare—it will all be the cause of bondage for whatever purposes that one may work for the in-terest of that particular ideal. It is still the cause of bondage; it is material.

Therefore, the importance is established here for purifying the senses. It’s just as the example is given: Whatever the mind is thinking of, it will carry that living entity from one body to the next. Therefore, we see that under these particular headings, that the living entities are constantly struggling due to that identification. It’s due to the identification of thinking that, “I am American” that the living entity must struggle. Or due to the identification of thinking that, “I am a man”, “I am a woman”. Or, as we even see now, that the terminology which is applied towards even religions— “I am Christian”, “I am Protestant”.

I recall my visit to Ireland to see how pervasive this consciousness was of “Catholic” and “Protestant”, and how so much conflict and struggle was there due to that false conception. I even remember a devotee once telling me a story of how he was preaching to one of the residents of Ireland, and this bodily concept was so strong that he was preaching for two straight hours with this person about how one is not a Catholic and one is not a Protestant—that these are all simply designations simply imposed upon the body—and for two hours he was trying to make that distinction between the soul, and the designation of the body. So after this preaching of two hours sitting there, listening, people came forward and asked the devotee, “I understand everything you say. I just have one question to ask you, ” and he said, “What is it? ” and they said, “Are you a Catholic Hare Krishna or are you a Protestant Hare Krishna? ” So ingrained! It’s the power of the illusory energy, of the material energy, whereby one is forced to think in terms of the body. They fail to recognize the needs of the soul.

What is the soul? What is the identity of the soul? That’s what make’s it so difficult. Due to excessive attachment, people will fail to recognize that the true identity of the soul is *jivera svarupaya krsnera nitya dasa*—that the living entity is eternally, constitutionally, part and parcel; a servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. So, unless one comes to that consciousness of being an eternal servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead—until he comes to that consciousness, he’ll always be fixed in some other designation. Even religiously. Therefore, Prabhupada says, even religion has to be abandoned. We see that Srila Prabhupada established the Interna-tional Society for Krishna Consciousness for that very purpose. He established the International Society for Krishna Consciousness so that living entities could associate with the principle of transcending this bodily designation.

There are a few things from *Upadeshamrita* where Prabhupada described the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Here he says:

“When by good fortune one comes in contact with the pure devotee and hears from him patiently, one begins to follow the path of devotional service. Such an opportunity is offered to a person who is sincere. The International Society for Krishna Consciousness is giving such a chance to humanity at large. If by fortune one takes advantage of this opportunity to engage in devotional service, the path of liberation immediately opens... ” (Nol 3, Purport)

Prabhupada also says here:

“... If one also lives in the association of devotees, there is little chance of living with nondevotees. The International Society for Krishna Concsciousness is opening many centers just to invite people to live in the company of devotees and practice the regulative principles of spiritual life.

Devotional service means transcendental activity. On the transcendental platform there is no contamination by the three modes of material nature. This is called the *visuddha- sattva,* or the platform of pure goodness, or goodness from contamination by the qualities of passion and ignorance. In this Krishna Consciousness Movement, we require everyone to rise early in the morning by 4 a. m. and attend *mangala arotik,* or morning worship, then read Srimad-Bhagavatam, perform *kirtana,* and so forth. Thus, we hold continuous activities in devotional service 24 hours daily. This is called *sato vrtti* or following in the footsteps of the previous *acharyas* who expertly filled every moment of time with Krishna conscious activities... ” (Nol 3, Purport)

One more reference:

.. The International Society for Krishna Consciousness has been established to facilitate these six kinds of loving exchanges between devotees. This society was started single- handedly, but because people are coming forward and dealing with the give-and-take policy, the Society is expanding all over the world. We are glad that people are donating very liberally to development of the Society’s activities. Our peo-ple are also eagerly accepting whatever humble contribution we are giving them in the shape of books and magazines, dealing strictly with the subject matter of Krishna conscious-ness. We sometimes hold Hare Krishna festivals and invite life members and friends to participate in the feasting by accepting *prashada.* Although most of our members come from the higher rungs of society, they nonetheless come and take whatever little *prashada* we are able to offer them.

Sometimes the members and supporters inquire very confidentially about the methods of performing devotional service, and we try to explain this: in this way our Society is successfully spreading all over the world, and the intelli-gentsia of all countries is gradually appreciating our Krishna conscious activities.

The life of Krishna consciousness, the life of the Krishna conscious society is nourished by these six types of loving exchange among the members. Therefore, people must be given the chance to associate with the devotees of ISKCON, because simply by reciprocating in the six ways mentioned above, an ordinary man can fully revive his dormant Krishna consciousness... ” (Nol 3, Purport)

We see here, in these particular references of what we see as the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, the Interna-tional Society for Krishna Consciousness is a society which is meant to foster Krishna consciousness. All these activities of exchang-ing—*dadati prtigrhanti guhyam akyati prcchati*—these activities of exchanging—giving in charity, accepting charity, revealing one’s mind in confidence, inquiring confidentially, offering *prashada,* accepting *prashada*—Prabhupada says, “We have established this Society for this purpose—for the cultivation of Krishna conscious-ness. ” So, in establishing “What is ISKCON? ”, and when we see our membership to ISKCON, we have to establish in our minds, and also in the minds of others, what we should be dependent upon ISKCON for.

We must be dependent upon ISKCON as a society which nour-ishes Krishna consciousness. That is ISKCON. Any place—just as Krishna says, *“naham tisthami vaikunthe yoginam hrdiyesu va* / *tatra tisthami narada yatra gayanti mad bhaktah.*.. ” Krishna says that, “I am not in the *Vaikuntha* planets, nor am I in the hearts of yogis, but I am in that place wherever the devotees are discussing my activities and pastimes. In that place, I go there. ” (Bs 5. 37) That’s where Krishna is: where the devotees are discussing Krishna consciousness.

So, if we understand the International Society for Krishna Con-sciousness is established for that purpose. As Prabhupada explained right here, *sato vrtti*—“... this is called *sato vrtti*, a following in the footsteps of the previous *acharyas* who expertly filled every moment of time with Krishna conscious activity. ” The temples were estab-lished as a place whereby the devotees amongst themselves—when they’re fostering Krishna consciousness amongst themselves—that is the activity of ISKCON. Not only that but Prabhupada says when devotees amongst themselves are sharing Krishna Consciousness, inquiring confidentially, speaking about Krishna, talking about Kri-shna, fostering these types of Krishna conscious relationships, then members come forward to associate with ISKCON members. They also want to become part of ISKCON. What do they want to become part of? They want to become part of that association. There may be so many other banners; there may be so many other flags by which people may gather together. If it’s for communal purposes, that’s not ISKCON. Although it may be included within ISKCON. Certainly, there are communal activities within ISKCON. There are responsibilities that every member has community-wise within the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, but it’s not an end in itself. The end in itself, which is established by ISKCON, is that it is a society of people. Just as Prabhupada gives that example how people will share a common goal, therefore we see there are so many clubs, so many societies because they share a common concern. What was the example he gave of the... Right in here he gave the example of one—was it the stock market? The stock exchange, or something like that? He is the sharer—So, in the same way, what establishes the community for Krishna conscious-ness? What establishes the community for Krishna consciousness is where each and every member of the community shares that same goal: to become Krishna conscious, and, therefore, they set as the priority these activities which foster Krishna consciousness—which makes it the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. If it is simply communal concerns, there are other institutions which are established for such purposes. Therefore, what do we depend on ISKCON for providing?

We certainly depend upon ISKCON for providing this type of association. This is what we depend upon ISKCON. If we de-pend upon ISKCON for something else, and think that that is the sole purpose which ISKCON is meant to provide, then actually it is something else that we’re depending upon. It’s not ISKCON. ISKCON is meant to provide a society by which the members can cultivate Krishna consciousness. Because if we become Kri-shna conscious, then it’s understood that all these other concerns are automatically fulfilled. Without becoming Krishna conscious— *dharmah svanusthitah pumsam* / *viksvaksena-kathasu yah* / *notpadayed yadi ratim* / *srama eva hi kevalam,* that “Whatever occupation a man performs according to his own position is considered to be so much useless labor if one doesn’t become attracted and become Krishna conscious”. (Sb 1. 2. 8) We may have so many prescribed duties; we may have so many occupations—communally, collectively, within the community. But, if it doesn’t provoke Krishna consciousness, then it’s described as a waste of time. So, therefore, we have to establish our priorities as members of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. To what degree do we establish this in our minds, in the minds of others, what ISKCON is meant to provide for the rest of society? How to become free from these other des-ignations—communally, socially, politically, pseudo-religiously—it doesn’t make any difference. These are all, Prabhupada explains— if one is absorbed in that consciousness, he must come back again to the material world. Just as one might sometimes be thinking about family responsibilities. We might be thinking, “Yes, family responsibilities, religious duties. ” Arjuna in the Bhagavad Gita, he was thinking about, also, responsibilities to the family members. He was thinking about what will happen to the children if the women go unprotected. He offered so many arguments as to why he should not participate in the battlefield of Kuruksetra. Yet, Krishna es-tablished the higher principle in Arjuna’s mind. There is a higher principle. Therefore, we see that the higher principle always is in the cultivation of Krishna consciousness. That is what the Society is established for, and, because the members themselves are fostering amongst themselves Krishna consciousness, therefore, we see they actually have a positive contribution for the welfare of humanity; they have something to give to society: Krishna consciousness, which is considered to be the solution whereby one does not have to come back again in this material world to accept another body according to his bodily concept of life. So, this verse is describing this princi-ple. Therefore, Prabhupada explains right here, “... real happiness must be appreciated by one’s transcendental senses. Unless one is purified, the transcendental senses are not manifest. Therefore, to purify the senses, one must take to Krishna consciousness and engage the senses in the service of the Lord. Then there will be real happiness and liberation. ” *hrsikesa hrsikenam sevanam bhaktir ucyate* / *sarvopadhi vinir muktam tat paratvena nirmalam.* That one has to give up the *upadhis,* the designations of the body, of thinking that I am anything except *jivera Svarupa haya krsnera nitya dasa*— “I am a servant of Krishna, ” and, being a servant of Krishna, what is the best means for rendering service for the satisfaction of the Supreme Personality of Godhead? That has to be established in the minds of all of us. In this age, it is *sravanam kirtanaam visnoh smaranam.* This is the service. What is that verse? *ya idam paramam guhyam* / *mad-bhaktesv abhidhasyati.* Krishna says that, “One who explains the supreme secret to the devotees, then for him devotional service is guaranteed. At the end he comes back to me. ” *Na ca tasman manusyesu* / *kascin me priya krttamah*—“There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear”. (Nol 3)

That is the dear servant of Krishna—one who is actually telling others about Krishna. That is the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. That is what we are hankering for; that is the nectar that we’re all hankering for, is Krishna consciousness, and if we forget Krishna, then we see that that is the cause of so much suffering. That is the struggle. We must struggle simply by forgetting Krishna. What is that verse in the Caitanya Caritamrta which describes that?.. “... one who remembers Lord Caitanya, then the most difficult thing becomes very easy to perform. But if he forgets, the most simple thing becomes extremely difficult. ” The simplest thing becomes... simply because he’s forgetting that he’s a servant of Krishna. Even the simplest tasks, *bhogaisvarya-prasaktanam* / *tayapahrta-cetasam* / *vyavasayatmika buddhih* / *samadhau na vidhiyate*—“In the minds of those who are too much attached to sense gratification and material opulence, and who are bewildered by such things, there can be no resolute determination for engaging in devotional service. ” (Bg 2. 44) Due to attachment—if one is not resolute in purpose. Therefore, Krishna says, *vyavasayatmika buddhir/ ekeha kuru-nandana*—“... the intelligence of those who are irresolute is many-branched. ” (Bg 2. 41) We have to be resolute in purpose. Our “resolute in purpose” is: We have to become Krishna conscious. And the way that we become Krishna conscious is by applying our membership to the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, depending upon the society of devotees for the cultivation of Krishna consciousness. Therefore, wherever devotees are gathered for such purposes, that is the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Prabhupada says that it is the spiritual world. That is this temple. The temple wherever devotees are established for that purpose of worshiping the Deity, of glorifying the Supreme Personality of Godhead, then we see that all these activities—communally, how do they become purified? By fixing the mind on the Deity. Communally we purify our activities by fixing our consciousness on rendering service to the Deity. Not that we’re thinking about our own personal concerns, but actually the Deity is first. We establish in our minds that actually this is Radha-Saradhvihari’s temple. Then, that same way, everything we do in that consciousness—we’re cleaning, we’re cooking, we’re purchasing *bhoga,* we’re remodeling, we’re fixing—if we take away our own consciousness, our own identification, thinking in terms of I and mine—which is going to be the cause of bondage—and apply it instead to the service of the Lord, then, what happens is one becomes purified. One’s senses become purified. This is purified senses—fixing the mind on Krishna. Therefore, it is so essential— we see that every morning, that we have to hear about Krishna and glorify Krishna. That is what the International Society for Krishna Consciousness... Prabhupada says, “We have established single-handedly this purpose to associate with members for the development of Krishna consciousness, and we see that by doing so, people come forward with the give-and-take policy. Whatever humble offering we can give, they accept it, and they come forward and give in charity. ” (Nol 4, Purport) That is the International Society for Krishna Concsciousness. Whatever humble offering we give in the form of Krishna consciousness, by giving *prashadaam,* by giving books, by giving association for the development of Krishna consciousness, the result if people voluntarily come forward and give their services to ISKCON. Because this is a wonderful institution because people are becoming Krishna conscious. Because, without becoming Krishna conscious, I have to struggle so hard with material life; it is a constant struggle. That is explained right here, how one must struggle. “In this material world of conditional life, the fragmental portion of the Supreme Lord, the individual soul is struggling due to his contaminated mind and consciousness. As part and parcel of the Supreme Lord, the living entity is supposed to think of Krishna, but here we see that King Puranjana the living entity is thinking of a woman. Such mental absorption with some sense object brings about the living entity’s struggle for existence in this material world. ” That is the struggle. So, Prabhupada also explains that the struggle is so difficult that yet, sometimes because of the sense of struggling, people think—they develop a false sense of happiness— thinking that, “Yes, I’ve been struggling so long that now that I’ve gotten the fruit, I’m calling it happiness because I’ve achieved the fruit. ” But, actually, that’s not happiness; it’s temporary. The fruits are temporary, and they’ll simply come and go. The real fruits of our struggles is: “How much are we becoming Krishna conscious? ” That’s the real fruit. How much are we depending on Krishna? That’s the real fruit of our activities. Without actually bringing that to fruition then, just like Prabhupada explains in one purport in the Srimad- Bhagavatam 3rd Canto, he describes how the living entity (this is in relationship to Lord Brahma—how Lord Brahma in the process of creating the universe, he was offering prayers to the Lord so how that he could become dependent upon the Lord)—and Prabhupada explains that, “fortunate is that person who has been entrusted with responsible work, and if he executes that responsible work in the consciousness that I am dependent upon the Lord, subordinate to the Lord always, then whatever he does is done nicely. But, if he executes in the consciousness that I am not subordinate, then whatever he does cannot be done nicely. ” Whatever the fruit, it’s not nicely done. It’s not nicely performed because he’s forgotten that he’s a servant of Krishna. But, if he remembers that he’s a servant of Krishna, that he’s subordinate to Krishna, then it doesn’t make any difference. That’s the real fruit. Our service is meant to cultivate Krishna consciousness—without which, it is described, we’re wasting our time, whatever occupation we are given. We have to develop that sense of dependence upon the Lord in all our activities. Krishna says, “... just depend upon Me and always work under My direction. If you do this, you will be able to cross over all the obstacles of material life, but if you don’t, not hearing Me, you will be lost. ” You will be lost. “... if you don’t depend upon Me and work under My direction”, He says, “if you’re not hearing Me”— that was His concluding statements in the 18th chapter of Bhagavad Gita. “... you will become lost. ”

So, we must depend on Krishna, and the way that we depend upon Krishna is by the cultivation of Krishna consciousness. This is the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, and this is what Srila Prabhupada is describing. We can stand under that banner and not be disillusioned into thinking that we’re something besides an eternal servant of Krishna. Therefore, Prabhupada says, “Let there be one banner. Let there be one mantra—the Hare Krishna mantra. Let there be one banner—the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. ” That banner is not something that is imposing a bodily designation. That banner is that we all stand under the banner as eternal servants of the Supreme Lord. That is the banner by which we stand under. That banner is universal and can include all living entities throughout all universes, throughout the entire cosmic manifestation and the spiritual world. That banner we can all stand under comfortably because there is no conflict in that banner. But, any other banner that we wave, even if we force our material conception on ISKCON, to think it’s something else besides that... We have to be very careful that we don’t impose our own... Just like we say that there is not a Boston sun, and there’s not a Philadelphia sun, and not a London sun; we do not impose our relative conceptions upon the sun. So, in the same way, we should not impose our conceptions upon ISKCON to think it is something besides that society which is meant to foster Krishna consciousness amongst devotees and members. Sometimes we hear people with grievances: “Well, ISKCON didn’t give me this. ” or “ISKCON didn’t give me that. ” No. ISKCON is meant to give Krishna consciousness. If you become Krishna conscious, then all your problems are solved. Now, granted the responsibility is on all the members of ISKCON to be Krishna conscious to provide that. We all have the onus on us to become Krishna conscious to make others Krishna conscious, and for ourselves to become Krishna conscious. That is ISKCON. That is the society that Srila Prabhupada established, and that is the Society that will continue for the next 10, 000 years if we keep Krishna at the center. If we don’t then we’ll have to fall under some other banner, and, unfortunately, that banner will not make us happy. Any questions or comments?

\[Response to question\] NS: Well, all these are very important. Certainly, accepting gifts and offering gifts in charity is very im-portant. This is exchange amongst one devotee and another. But, certainly I feel that the most important is revealing one’s mind in confidence and inquiring confidentially. I feel right now what’s happened in our Society is there’s been a breach of faith. A breach of faith; and if we don’t have faith, if we don’t have trust in devotees then it becomes every man for himself. “Who’s gonna take care of me? ”, “Who’s gonna take care of my problems? ” Therefore, I say that if we have faith in the devotees in terms of their Krishna consciousness, then that faith is so strong and so powerful, because if we have faith in somebody’s Krishna consciousness, then we’re willing to sacrifice... even our own material things. We saw that in Srila Prabhupada. Why did everybody come forth and give so much to Prabhupada? Because they had faith in Prabhupada’s Krishna consciousness. Prabhupada’s Krishna consciousness was so profound that they didn’t care. They were giving to Prabhupada, they were giving to Krishna. But that faith has been lost in the minds of many of the devotees. So we have to learn how to cultivate that faith amongst the devotees, and the way that we cultivate that faith is, again, by sharing Krishna consciousness, keeping Krishna consciousness in the center, and by ourselves seeing to the Krishna conscious concerns of others. The leaders have a responsibility to society to see that others are becoming Krishna conscious, and because people are thinking, “Because I was exploited, because I was used for other concerns... ”, “People are too much concerned about money, therefore they’re using me for communal concerns. ”, or, “They’re using me for other concerns, ” therefore they are thinking, “When this happened, this person fell down... ” Everything is just gone. Nobody has faith. Now, I'm not saying that that is a state which there is no faith. I personally experience faith—that there is many places where there is faith. But I say that this faith must be strengthened. This faith must be strengthened by the Krishna conscious leadership of this Society. And that the onus is on us to become Krishna conscious. Without Krishna consciousness, there will be no faith, and therefore, we’ll see that no one will inquire confidentially because they will be thinking, “Well, if I reveal my mind in confidence then I know what’s gonna happen—I'm gonna reveal my weakened state and I'm gonna get raked across the coals. ” That’s what’s gonna happen. That’s what happens in the material world. That’s why nobody reveals their mind in confidence in the material world. They say, “Oh, my dear sir, I have this problem and that problem. I am so weak... ” and they rake you across the coals. \[Conchshell\] Sorry, I have to end here. All glories to Srila Prabhupada.

## Lecture #2 \[no title\]

—Note—

The lecture was given sometime around 1990. Sorry, I don’t know what happened to the rest of the lecture.

So on behalf of our spiritual master, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhak-tivedanta Swami Prabhupada, and the *Vaishnava* residents of the Toronto Mandir, I would like to welcome all of you on this auspicious occasion. Actually it is very wonderful to see so many supporters gathered at one temple. It is my understanding that all of you are supporters, and therefore this banquet has been prepared as recip-rocation for your support, with hope of your continued support so that we can carry on with our contribution to all of you, and society at large.

Certainly there is so much here to attract anyone who under-stands Krishna culture. All of you are so fortunate to have taken your birth in the land of Bharatvarsa. Whenever Lord Krishna appears within this material world, He chooses to appear in the that sacred land. And certainly everyone there knows of Lord Krishna. And if it were not for Lord Chaitanya and Srila Prabhupada, who also both appeared in that sacred land, those of us who were less fortunate, having been raised in the West, amidst the modern tech-nology and degrading Western influences would have never known of this wonderful culture and the principles of *sanatana-dharma.*

But for many of you, you have now gotten the opportunity to learn for yourselves just how demeaning Western technology can be without Krishna consciousness. The culture of India was based on a simple economy due to the presence of great sages and saints who gave spiritual direction for human society so that others could fulfill their material needs, but at the same time achieve spiritual enlightenment, the primary goal of human life. Conversely our Western society dictates an artificial standard of living due to a lack of spiritual preceptors. Everyone is driven to increase their labor for material gain, only to find a decrease in satisfaction by missing the most essential ingredient in life; the eternal soul.

It is described in the Srimad-Bhagavatam, how the age of Kali *yuga* is compared to the clouds in the sky during the rainy season. Due to the presence of the clouds the luminaries in the sky cannot be seen, and thus those with imperfect vision accept the glowworms in the tress as the luminaries in the sky. Similarly, due to the influence of this age of Kali *yuga,* people will accept the material leaders of society, who dictate such artificial goals for material fulfillment as the leaders and preceptors, and the real luminaries, the sages and saints who carry the message of Godhead, will not be seen or appreciated. This is very lamentable.

Our spiritual master also gives an example comparing our modern civilization to a complex mathematical equation. In a mathematical equation we may put in the beginning of our equation that 2 + 2 = 5. And no matter how far we carry out that equation the conclusion will always be false. Similarly we have created such a complex civilization, but if in the beginning we make such a significant error, by accepting this body as the self, than no matter how complex and advanced we become, the conclusion will always be false. A society that does not recognize the needs of the soul can produce nothing but dissatisfaction.

So now something has brought all of you here in the West with us but you have a duty that you cannot neglect. Lord Chaitanya has declared “*bharata bhumite haila manusya janma jara* / *janma sarthaka kari kara para-upakara. ” (CC.* Adi 9. 41) Everyone who has taken his birth in the land of Bharatavarsa has a duty to perform welfare work for all the people in general. You all have been raised in this Krishna culture and can appreciate the wonderful merits it has to offer. This Krishna consciousness is real welfare work because it provides the remedy for all material ailments by going right to the soul. We may have so many comforts for the body, but still we must suffer.

Prahlada Maharaja, in his prayers to Nrsmhadeva offered this following prayer to him. B*alasya neha saranam pitarau nrsmha/e*tc. “O Lord Nrsmhadeva, O Supreme. Because of a bodily conception of life, embodied souls, neglected and not cared for by You cannot do anything for their betterment. Whatever remedies they may accept, although perhaps temporarily beneficial are certainly impermanent. For example, a mother and father cannot protect their child, a physician and medicine cannot relieve a suffering patient, and a boat on the ocean cannot protect a drowning man. Prahlada Maharaja is telling us that without Krishna’s protection, we may make all the arrangements in the world, but still we cannot do anything to help someone who is neglected by Krishna.
